# Federated Clash Test & Results — Design Spec

Status: **DESIGN SPEC — not an implementation plan.** No code, tests, or public library contract is changed by this document. Source of truth is the landed repository state (branch `feature/m4-performance-worker-decision-design`, HEAD `959a8a8` at authoring time), not older design text where the two differ.

Governing inputs (all read for this spec; claims about landed behavior are sourced from code and `collision/README.md`, then cross-checked against the specs):

- Roadmap: `docs/superpowers/specs/collision-library-roadmap.md` (§3 responsibility boundary, §4 binding compatibility constraints, §6 the scope list this spec formalizes, §8 architecture-change discipline).
- Latest status authority: `docs/research/m4-phase3-closeout-disposition.md` (M4 closed, **Verdict D**, Phase 4 not started/authorized) and `docs/handoffs/latest.md`.
- Library: `collision/index.js`, `collision/CollisionWorld.js`, `collision/scheduler.js`, `collision/errors.js`, `collision/constants.js`, `collision/viewer/ViewerModelAdapter.js`, `collision/README.md`.
- Specs: M1 (`2026-08-06-…-milestone1-design.md`), M2 (`2026-09-05-…-milestone2-design.md`), M2.1, M3 (`2026-09-07-…-milestone3-clearance-design.md`), M4 (`2026-09-08-…-milestone4-performance-worker-decision-design.md`), and the two 2026-09-11 fix specs (canonical ordering / operand symmetry; deterministic crossing budget).
- Consumer reference: `wwwroot/extensions/ClashFinderExtension.js`, `test/acceptance/clash-finder.acceptance.mjs`.

**Revision note (review round 1).** An independent `codex-review spec` pass (medium effort, request `20260920-034058-96286-27495`) found 5 critical/high and 9 further issues; every finding was checked against the repository before acceptance. The two most consequential (the second later superseded, see round 2 below) were confirmed as real defects in the first draft: the library's extraction walks each requested dbId's **whole subtree**, so dbId-set disjointness does not imply fragment-set disjointness (now §5.6 and §6, with a plan-time `hierarchy-overlap` rule), and whole-model *target* scope is re-extracted on every call, so a run-start snapshot cannot be promised for it (now §5.2/§16.3, with a drift guard). Others tightened the claim rule (§8.3), `ResultId` (§4.4), pair-level comparison (§12.5/§15.4), the snapshot boundary and world-scoped coordinator (§7.3/§16.1), pre-plan cancellation (§10.2), persistence settlement (§13.5.1), the validation matrix (§5.3), digest format (§5.4/§13.1), the result-cap boundary (§17.1), and executor injection (§18). Dispositions are in §25.

**Revision note (review round 2 — Work review).** Five findings from an independent Work review were addressed: (1) target-scope equality is now *proven per unit* by bracketed scope digests instead of a cumulative `placementCount` (§6.2.1, §16.3, F18); (2) unattributable `dbId: null` diagnostics are first-class and taint pair/run coverage (§12.2.1, §12.5, F17); (3) `modelKey` is defined as one immutable model version with mandatory `modelVersion` witness and three enforcement points (§4.2); (4) resident result sets are hard-bounded with explicit backpressure and no silent drop of unsaved state (§13.5.2); (5) `RunHandle.done`/`persisted` have disjoint one-shot semantics (§13.5.1). Dispositions are in §25.

**Revision note (review round 7 — full independent design review, 7 High findings).** The review at `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-design-review.md` (verdict: revise before implementation planning; 0 Critical, 7 High, 5 Medium, 4 Low) was checked against the repository (`collision/CollisionWorld.js`: owner-level pair key at the aggregation step, per-call whole-model target extraction with per-call diagnostic accumulation, cumulative per-epoch admission in `admitCollisionInstances`) and against this text before acceptance. All seven High findings are accepted and remediated: (H1) mixed reachable/unreachable owners are conservatively tainted, and "not required" is reserved for owners absent from the root-owner population (§6.2, §12.5); (H2) target-side fan-out is bounded before the first collision call by per-unit target model/fragment caps (§6.4, §17.1); (H3) determinism is narrowed to input-only *unless* a unit fails on the cumulative epoch cache, which is recorded as an explicit environmental input (§6.4 P1, §15.1, §13.2); (H4) hierarchy-internal exclusions are claim-blocking until a product acceptance record is supplied (§5.6, §6.6, §8.3); (H5) pair-semantics changes are declared incomparable and are never joined on `PairId` (§4.4, §15.4); (H6) synchronous validation is excluded from the 8 ms slice guarantee, given a lowered, measured ceiling and a bounded issue list, with normalization and digesting moved into sliced work (§5.3, §17.1, §17.3); (H7) oversize-reason rejection is separated from legal-length high-cardinality overflow, with exact totals, exact counts only for retained reasons, and one explicit overflow count (§7.1, §12.4). Dispositions are in §25 (Round 7). The five Medium and four Low findings are **not** part of this remediation (two Low wording fixes were made in passing; see §25).

**Revision note (review round 8 — follow-up independent `codex-review`, 4 High + 5 Medium; original Medium findings dispositioned).** The committed remediation verification review (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-remediation-review.md`: 3 High NH1–NH3, 8 Medium M1–M8) is the review authority for this round; it adjudicated a second independent Codex pass (request `20260920-153241-74421-15823`) did not report round 7's H1–H7 as unresolved but raised four further High findings, each checked against the repository and this text: (1) the durable `modelKey → modelVersion` index must be claimed atomically with the Result Set insert across different `runId`s (§4.2, §13.5, AC-11); (2) terminal-status precedence when cancel, disposal, reload, drift and core failures coincide is now a normative order, which also resolves a conflict between §16.1 and §16.3 over `bindingGeneration` (§16.7, §16.3, AC-21); (3) transient geometry readiness is recorded as an environmental input and the determinism/freshness statements are narrowed accordingly — the false-clean risk it described was already closed by the completeness-affecting `geometry-not-yet-available` diagnostic (§1.3.6, §15.1, §16.4, AC-19 (e)); (4) hard `delete` now tombstones the `testId` permanently (§5.5, AC-1). The five Medium findings of the original review (M1–M5) and the five Medium findings of this pass were dispositioned; see §25 Round 8 (all are folded in except that the live Viewer owner-mapping evidence still does not exist).

**Revision note (review round 9 — round 8 verification review: NH3 and M2 remained).** The round 8 review (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round8-review.md`) found NH1/NH2 and seven Medium dispositions resolved and left two open, both accepted: (NH3, High) the `testId` tombstone lived only in `ClashTestStore`, while direct definitions and embedded reruns never touch that store, so a deleted `testId` could still persist byte-identical `ResultId`s — the reservation is now also held and enforced by the `ResultStore` (the durable authority) and by an engine admission check, with `engine.deleteTest` ordering the two writes fail-closed (§5.5, §4.2, §13.5, AC-1, AC-11); (M2, Medium) `maxLedgerRecords` exhaustion is now specified for **every** runtime record class, backed by `maxUnitsPerRun` reserved overflow slots, with a fail-closed fallback per class (§6.5.1, §12.2.1, §6.4, AC-16). No other section's decisions changed; dispositions are in §25 Round 9.

**Revision note (review round 10 — round 9 verification review: R9-M1 and R9-M2).** The round 9 review (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round9-review.md`) found the NH3 and M2 designs resolved and two Medium cross-section defects, both accepted: (R9-M1) AC-1(b) required `retryPersist` from a `persist: false` (`skipped`) set, which §13.5.1 and §18 forbid — `retryPersist` stays legal only from `failed`, and the acceptance case now retrieves the sealed set and calls `ResultStore.put` directly, with a separate legal `retryPersist` path (§5.5, AC-1); (R9-M2) the reserve proof treated `maxPlanReferences` as bounding all planning records although it named only some of them — `maxPlanReferences` now counts **every** planning-time record (`planRecords`), which makes the proof `planRecords ≤ maxPlanReferences ⇒ D ≥ maxUnitsPerRun` sound, and AC-16's fixture is sized so planning succeeds and runtime exhaustion is actually reached (§6.5.1, §17.1, AC-16). No design decision changed. Dispositions in §25 Round 10.

**Revision note (review round 11 — internal Codex review of the round 10 draft: 1 Critical, 3 High, 3 Medium).** The internal `codex-review spec` pass (request `20260920-202255-26792-26384`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round10-internal-codex-review.md`) is the review authority for this round; it raised findings that the earlier rounds' criteria did not cover. Each was checked against the spec text and (for the two library-behavior claims) `collision/CollisionWorld.js`, and all seven were accepted in whole or in part: (R11-1) inbound definitions, options, store/migration results are **captured as detached data-only copies in the same single synchronous pass that validates them** (§5.3, V1, §13.3, §17.3, AC-1); (R11-2) `admissionBaseline` is a per-epoch *count*, so it is restated as an **observational witness, not a reproduction key**, with the flag following the *outcome* (§1.3.5, §6.4, §13.2, §15.1, AC-19 b′); (R11-3) exhausted foreign placement retries become the environmental cause **`placement-contention`** (§7.5, §8.3, §13.1, §15.1, AC-8, AC-19 d′); (R11-4) a run sealed before its definition is resolved has one **pre-resolution representation** for both overloads and both terminal statuses (§13.1, §8.2, §10.2, §15.3, AC-7); (R11-5) `getPersistenceState` and the run-scoped operations are defined for **nonresident** ids without retaining history (§13.5.2, §18, AC-16); (R11-6) projection caps travel in `ExecutionRequest.limits` and the executor emits **overflow markers** instead of oversized arrays (§7.1, §11.2, AC-3); (R11-7) a **memory-ceiling calibration gate** makes the count caps' bytes an explicit pre-freeze pass condition (§17.1, AC-12). No earlier decision changed. Dispositions in §25 Round 11.

**Revision note (review round 12 — second internal Codex review of the round 11 draft: 1 Critical, 2 High, 2 Medium, 4 questions).** The internal `codex-review spec` pass (request `20260920-203425-37567-21881`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round11-internal-codex-review.md`) was checked against the spec and `collision/viewer/ViewerModelAdapter.js`; all five findings were accepted, two in part. (R12-1, Critical) the tree-vs-population reconciliation covered only loaded, owner-resolvable fragments, so a `not-loaded`/`owner-unresolvable` fragment absent from the instance tree was tainted as a whole-model **target** (the library's `dbId: null` diagnostic) but silently clean as a whole-model **source** (the tree walk never sees it) — it is now recorded orientation-independently as `unreachable.unattributable`, claim-blocking and pair-tainting (§6.2, §12.5 rule 7, AC-17 g), while the *explicit-scope* under-enumeration limit (library "Mode 2") is restated as an inherited trust boundary, not closed; (R12-2, High) the round 11 capture rule promised proxy detection, which JavaScript cannot deliver, so it is replaced by one concrete reflection algorithm with an honest trust boundary and cost statement (§5.3, §17.3, AC-1); (R12-3, High) stored Result Sets get a full untrusted-read contract, `validateResultSet` with typed `CorruptResultSetError` and stated forgery limit (§13.6, AC-11); (R12-4, Medium) annotation vs. eviction/release/discard/dispose is defined by a residency token, atomic publish and an all-or-nothing source contract (§14.5, AC-13); (R12-5, Medium) both stores' `list` get a total order, bounded `limit`, opaque versioned keyset cursors (§13.5, AC-11). Question 2 (what a hierarchy acceptance authorizes) is answered in §8.3; questions 1 and 4 by R12-3/R12-4; question 3 (OQ-4) is unchanged. No earlier decision changed. Dispositions in §25 Round 12.

**Revision note (review round 13 — third internal Codex review, of the round 12 draft: 1 Critical, 4 High, 3 Medium).** The internal pass (request `20260920-204249-45015-26623`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round12-internal-codex-review.md`) was checked against the spec text. The Critical (bracketed scope proof) restates a residual **already declared and decided in round 2** (§16.3 item 5) and is therefore *dispositioned, not redesigned*: the residual is now stated at the claim itself (§8.3) and raised as product question OQ-19 — it is the one item in this loop that needs a product answer, and it has a defined interim. The rest were accepted: (R13-2) `dispose()` versus the terminal-event contract (§10.2, §13.5.1); (R13-3) one aggregate `ResultSet.schemaVersion` for every nested structure (§13.6); (R13-4) grouping/filter are asynchronous with facet, clause and membership bounds (§14.4); (R13-5) `ClashTestStore.resolveForRun` returns revision and archive state from one linearizable read (§5.5); (R13-5b) `validateResultSet` uses fixed schema ceilings, not the reading engine's run options, plus `ResultSetTooLargeError` (§13.6); (R13-6) `ModelRegistry.snapshotBindings` gives the atomic snapshot an API (§4.3, §5.2); (R13-8) annotation bytes are an exact UTF-8 canonical-JSON count (§14.5). OQ-4 (finding 7) is unchanged — its gating, fallback and owner are already specified. Dispositions in §25 Round 13.

**Revision note (review round 14 — fourth internal Codex review, of the round 13 draft: 1 Critical, 2 High, 2 Medium).** The internal pass (request `20260920-204924-51205-10583`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round13-internal-codex-review.md`) was checked against the spec text. Its Critical is a **contradiction introduced by round 12's own `validateResultSet`** — the invariant "every result's owners are in `rootOwners`" rejected the engine's own valid `unreachableOwner` positives after a save/evict/read-back — and is fixed by making the stored-result invariant identical to the production-time rule (§13.6, AC-11). Also accepted: (R14-2) store read-through now has bounded read slots and a FIFO so transient read/validate/freeze work cannot bypass the memory bound (§13.5.2, §17.1, AC-16); (R14-3) `UnitOutcome` is a closed, bounded schema (§7.1, AC-3); (R14-4) derivations take an `AbortSignal` and are concurrency-bounded, because dropping a Promise does not cancel it (§14.4, AC-13). The remaining High re-raises OQ-4 (third time, no new content): only the closing sentence of §24 was clarified so the gate is stated at its point of use. Dispositions in §25 Round 14.

**Revision note (review round 15 — fifth internal Codex review, of the round 14 draft: 2 Critical, 3 High, 2 Medium, 3 questions).** The internal pass (request `20260920-205504-56220-8570`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round14-internal-codex-review.md`) was checked against the spec text; all seven findings were accepted. Both Criticals are gaps in the *newer* machinery rather than in the core design: (R15-1) stored/caller-supplied Result Sets get the same **capture-first** boundary definitions already had — one atomic detached clone before migration or any sliced validation (§13.6); (R15-2) `getFreshness` is a **two-pass bracketed operation** over all scopes and both registry snapshots, so a model changed while another is digested cannot yield `current` (§16.4). Also accepted: (R15-4) `compareResultSets` is asynchronous, O(n+m), abortable and bounded (§15.4); (R15-3) an exhaustive **RunOption vs engine-limit ownership** split, with engine limits no longer claimed to be recorded in Result Sets (§17.1.1, §18); (R15-6) closed, validated listing summaries and `listTests`/`listResults` (§13.5, §18); (R15-5) facet bucket indices must be safe integers (`FacetRangeError`) (§14.2); (R15-7) `put`-vs-abort semantics (§13.5). Question 3 (missing stores) is answered with `StoreNotConfiguredError` (§5.5). Dispositions in §25 Round 15.

**Revision note (review round 16 — sixth internal Codex review, of the round 15 draft: 0 Critical, 4 High, 1 Medium, 4 questions).** The internal pass (request `20260920-210316-63016-12453`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round15-internal-codex-review.md`) found **no Critical**. Three of its four High findings are defects in machinery added in rounds 12–15, checked against the spec text and accepted: (R16-1) round 15's "`structuredClone`-equivalent" capture was wrong — native `structuredClone` invokes getters — so it is replaced by the §5.3 descriptor-based algorithm with ceilings charged before allocation and `schemaVersion` read first (§13.6); (R16-4) the listing summary's 256-model cap could reject the summary of a valid run — raised to the definition-implied maximum of 20,000 (§13.5); (R16-3) a remote store cannot always know whether a write committed — a typed `PersistenceOutcomeUnknownError` plus automatic reconcile, with idempotent `retryPersist` making every outcome safe, and no new persistence state (§13.5). The fourth High (R16-2) is a real API trap: a boolean `canClaimNoClashes` could not carry the hierarchy-acceptance qualification, so it now returns `true` only for an *unqualified* claim and a new `assessNoClashClaim` returns the qualifiers (§8.3, §18, AC-15/AC-20). (R16-5) comparison records both sides' determinism class and the weaker one. Clarifications from the questions: `validateTest` never throws for data errors; filter truth rules for multi-valued/null/unavailable facets; non-finite annotation numbers become `null`; the OQ-1/4/16/17/19 gates are now a named prerequisites table with owner, artifact and pass/fail (§24). Dispositions in §25 Round 16.

**Revision note (review round 17 — seventh internal Codex review, of the round 16 draft: 1 Critical, 3 High, 1 Medium, 3 questions).** The internal pass (request `20260920-211118-69383-30495`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round16-internal-codex-review.md`) was checked against the spec text; all accepted. (R17-1, Critical) round 15's bracketed freshness had no registry snapshot *after* the second digest pass, so a placement change landing during it (invisible to scope digests) could still yield `current` — it now takes `snapshot₃` after `digest₂` and compares every touched binding across all three (§16.4, AC-8). (R17-2) round 16's ambiguous-write reconciliation contradicted the one-shot persistence observables — reconciliation is now *inside* the same attempt, which stays `pending` until it settles (§13.5, AC-11). (R17-3) the external Result Set capture keeps its deliberate atomic, synchronous form but its worst-case duration and peak bytes are now measured, and the schema ceilings frozen only after that measurement (§13.6, AC-12). (R17-4) derivation functions moved onto a `DerivationContext` (`engine.derivations` / `createDerivationContext`) so their limits and scheduler have an owner (§14.4, §17.1.1, §18). (R17-5) `engine.assessNoClashClaim` added to the engine contract. Questions: `locationCell` is array-valued and not orderable (§14.4); an attribute-source `Map` is captured on resolution (§14.5). Dispositions in §25 Round 17.

**Revision note (review round 18 — eighth internal Codex review, of the round 17 draft: 0 Critical, 4 High, 1 Medium, 4 questions).** The internal pass (request `20260920-211547-73518-24910`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round17-internal-codex-review.md`) reported **no false-clean or pair-coverage defect in the primary design**; its findings are operational-lifecycle gaps in the newer machinery, all accepted: (R18-1) annotation jobs are now actively cancellable and concurrency-bounded, with their memory in the hard bound (§14.5); (R18-2) the ambiguous-write reconciliation gets its own `reconcileTimeoutMs` (§13.5); (R18-3) the external Result Set path gets a store-boundary serialized-size guard, an exact `ResultSetTooLargeError` predicate and a lower provisional node ceiling, while the product-approved memory/stall budget stays an open product input (§13.6); (R18-4) `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic`, which shapes persisted diagnostics, moves to the recorded RunOptions (§17.1.1); (R18-5) core-engine vs Viewer-adapter readiness is explicitly divided (§24). Dispositions in §25 Round 18.

**Revision note (review round 19 — ninth internal Codex review, of the round 18 draft: 0 Critical, 3 High, 2 Medium, 3 questions).** The internal pass (request `20260920-212147-79302-5443`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round18-internal-codex-review.md`) again found **no exact-cover or false-clean defect in the planner**; its findings are boundary-text gaps, all accepted: (R19-1) the capture-first rule now covers *every* raw Result Set entering an asynchronous engine operation (`getFreshness`, both claim methods, `rerun`, derivation methods), with uniform synchronous-capture / rejected-Promise / read-slot rules (§13.6); (R19-2) `results = ∅` is now inside the single normative claim predicate, so `allowed` cannot be read as a coverage assessment for a run that has clashes (§8.3); (R19-3) an ambiguous write that rejects *after* `dispose()` settles `failed` (`ambiguous`, `disposed`) without reconciliation, retaining no disposed-engine state (§13.5); (R19-4) the §18 signatures list `annotate`'s `{signal}` and `compareResultSets`' tolerances; (R19-5) `persist` defaults to `true` (`skipped`/`no-store` without a store) (§17.1). Dispositions in §25 Round 19.

**Revision note (review round 20 — tenth internal Codex review, of the round 19 draft: 1 Critical, 4 High, 1 Medium, 3 questions).** The internal pass (request `20260920-212650-83795-16541`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round19-internal-codex-review.md`) found a **genuine planner-level false-clean path**, the first since round 12: (R20-1, Critical) a whole-model **source** selection bracketed only its source-root subtree digests, so a fragment appearing *outside* the instance-tree roots (and reverting before claim-time freshness) changed nothing that was checked — the whole-population digest is now bracketed around every unit that uses a `kind:'model'` source, exactly as it already was for whole-model targets (§16.3.2, AC-8). The four High findings are contradictions in the newer text, all accepted: (R20-2) raw-set captures no longer queue behind read slots — a slot is reserved synchronously or the call rejects `ReadCapacityError` (§13.6, §13.5.2); (R20-3) round 12's `completed ⇒ no failed reason` invariant would have rejected the engine's own completed-with-gaps sets — it now forbids only fatal/input-change/cancel causes (§13.6, AC-11); (R20-4) OQ-19 was both a standing policy and a prerequisite gate — **one policy is now chosen: the bracketed proof is accepted (standing since round 2); OQ-19 is a non-gating tightening review** (§8.3, §24); (R20-4b) the store-side byte guard is a *required* capability with a conformance test, not an option (§13.5, AC-11). Also: `broadPhase`/`interUnitYield` defaults (R20-5), `retainResultSet` on a held set, and `getRun` for terminal-resident runs (§13.5.2, §18). Dispositions in §25 Round 20.

**Revision note (review round 21 — eleventh internal Codex review, of the round 20 draft: 0 Critical, 4 High, 1 Medium, 3 questions).** The internal pass (request `20260920-213243-89190-2834`; raw output `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round20-internal-codex-review.md`) reported **no new false-clean path and no Critical finding; the planner and the round-20 source-population bracket held**. All five findings concern API and memory contracts and were accepted: (R21-1) `rerun` is now `Promise<RunHandle>` (a synchronous handle cannot report capture/validation failures) (§15.3, §18); (R21-2) the writer/reader ceilings could disagree — a **fixed schema ceiling** (`SCHEMA_MAX_NODES`, corrupt above it) is now separate from an **operational reader limit** (`maxCapturedNodes`, `ResultSetTooLargeError` above it), a normative *producer/reader invariant* rejects run options whose worst case the same engine could not read back, and the provisional run defaults were lowered (`maxPlanReferences` 500,000; `maxLedgerRecords` 1,000,000) so the defaults satisfy it (§13.6, §17.1, §17.1.1); (R21-3) the memory formula now includes detached input clones, indexes and outputs of concurrent reads and derivations, with the derivation slot taken before capture (§14.4, §17.1); (R21-4) `validateResultSet` now links every result to its evaluated leaf unit, orientation and mode (§13.6); (R21-5) `dispose()` hands the unsaved sets back instead of merely naming them, reconciling the "never destroyed" guarantee (§10.2, §13.5.2). Swapping `selectionA`/`selectionB` is confirmed as a different question (§5.4). Dispositions in §25 Round 21.

This spec is the roadmap §6 "post-M4 formal specification". It does **not** reopen M1–M4, does **not** begin Phase 4 or any worker implementation, and does **not** define Viewer Clash Panel UI. Where it depends on a library behavior that is not yet a documented contract, it says so (§1.3) instead of assuming.

---

## 0. Decision summary

Every numbered decision below is normative and is developed in the section named. "Provisional" means chosen by reasoning, revisable by measurement, the same posture `collision/constants.js` already documents for its budgets.

| # | Decision | § |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | The layer is a **pure consumer** of `CollisionWorld`'s existing public surface. **No collision-core change is required for the execution path.** (Strict input-only determinism *would* need one — cumulative epoch-cache isolation — so determinism is instead narrowed and the environmental input recorded, §15.1, §21.2 (h).) It lives in a new sibling module (proposed `clashtest/`), Viewer-neutral at its core. | 2, 19 |
| D2 | A **Clash Test definition** is an immutable, revisioned, plain-data value (two selections + one library mode + `clearance` when soft). It holds **no** `Model`, `dbId`-of-a-different-model, epoch, or `modelId`. | 5 |
| D3 | Durable model identity is a host-supplied opaque **`modelKey`**, bound to the live `Model` object by a **`ModelRegistry`** at run time. **A `modelKey` identifies exactly one immutable model version/content identity, forever** (a retranslated or replaced model must get a new `modelKey`); the mandatory `modelVersion` content token is the enforcement witness, and the key→version pairing is checked at `register`, at `ResultStore.put`, and at compare/rerun time (§4.2). The engine never persists a `Model`, `loadEpoch`, `modelId`, `fragmentId`, or `geometryIndex` as identity. | 4 |
| D4 | Pair identity is **`PairId`**: canonical over `(modelKey, dbId)` refs, **unordered** for symmetric modes (`volume-overlap`, `surface-intersection`, `clearance`), **ordered** for `containment`. Result identity is `(testId, semanticDigest, PairId)`: stable across runs and sessions **of the same question over the same model versions**, and deliberately *different* when the mode, clearance or selections change (or, via a new `modelKey`, when model content changes), so workflow state can never silently transfer to a materially different question or alias across a retranslation. | 4.4 |
| D5 | Execution is **one `findClashes` call per Work Unit**, planned deterministically from the definition over **subtree roots** (the library extracts whole subtrees), using a **three-cell decomposition** that covers every required pair exactly once and never relies on same-model source/target overlap (the library's fragment-membership self-exclusion would silently drop it). **Both sides of every unit are bounded before the first call**: source roots per unit, and target models and target fragments per unit (targets are sliced by whole roots, never inside one root). Nested (ancestor/descendant) selection across the two roles is **not supported in v1** and fails planning with `hierarchy-overlap` rather than mis-covering. | 5.6, 6 |
| D6 | Units run **strictly sequentially, one engine run at a time per world** (a world-scoped coordinator shared by every engine over that world), yielding to the scheduler between units. The library's foreign-revision abort (`ModelPlacementChangedError` on growth by a concurrent caller) makes concurrent `findClashes` against one world unsafe for a batch. Direct `findClashes` callers cannot be serialized by this layer; that residual hazard is handled by bounded retry and documented (OQ-11). | 7 |
| D7 | Run state has **four terminal statuses** (`completed`, `cancelled`, `failed`, `invalidated`) and, orthogonally, **`coverage`** (`full`/`partial`), **`integrity`** (`clean`/`caveated`) and **`freshness`** (`current`/`stale`/`unverifiable`). "No clashes found" may be claimed only for `status: completed ∧ coverage: full ∧ integrity: clean ∧ freshness: current ∧` a non-empty required pair space `∧ ¬emptyOperand ∧ unresolvedSelections = ∅ ∧ unreachablePopulation = ∅ ∧` (`hierarchyInternal = ∅` **or** a recorded product acceptance — the latter only as a *qualified* claim through `assessNoClashClaim`; the boolean `canClaimNoClashes` requires `hierarchyInternal = ∅`) (§8.3). | 8, 12 |
| D8 | **Cancellation is cooperative at Work-Unit boundaries.** The library has no abort seam; an in-flight unit is drained, not discarded, and its valid result is kept. Cancelled runs retain partial results, flagged `coverage: partial`. | 10 |
| D9 | Progress is a monotone, pair-space-weighted fraction plus counters, delivered by snapshot and by throttled events. `unitsTotal` may grow (deterministic splitting); the weighted fraction never regresses. | 9 |
| D10 | Library diagnostics are normalized to plain data, deduplicated at batch scale, capped, and classified by one `isCompletenessAffecting(reason)` predicate. **Unknown reasons count as completeness-affecting** (fail-safe). Diagnostics that cannot be attributed to an element (`dbId: null`: `fragment-not-loaded`, `fragment-owner-unresolvable`) are first-class: they carry an `attribution` class and taint every pair of the affected unit/target model, so they can never yield a false `covered-clean`. | 12 |
| D11 | A **Result Set** is sealed at terminal state, canonically ordered by `PairId`, structured-clone/JSON safe (no live refs), and persisted through a `ResultStore` port. The M1-gate structured-clone crash class is excluded by construction. **Resident sets are hard-bounded** (`maxResidentResultSets` counts in-flight runs and sets with in-flight writes until settlement (§13.5.2); unsaved sets are never auto-evicted; a full engine refuses new runs with `ResultCapacityError` rather than grow or drop state). `done`/`persisted` have disjoint one-shot meanings: `done` resolves at seal + first-persistence-or-timeout, `persisted` resolves with the eventual persistence outcome. | 11, 13 |
| D12 | Aggregation/grouping foundations are **pure, deterministic derivations** over a sealed Result Set (facets, group identity, filters) plus an optional host-supplied attribute snapshot. No UI, status, or workflow state. | 14 |
| D13 | Repeatability is contractual **per mode**: deterministic for `volume-overlap` and `clearance`; **time-sensitive** for `surface-intersection` and `containment` (library-declared exceptions). Determinism is **input-only only while no unit fails on the cumulative per-epoch instance cache** (`bucket-capacity`) **no `geometry-not-yet-available` occurs** (transient renderer readiness, which no scope digest covers) **and no unit exhausts its placement retries against foreign placement activity** (`placement-contention`); such a run is flagged `environmentSensitive` with its cause(s) and the starting admission counts are recorded as an observational witness, not a reproduction key (§15.1). Reruns are new runs; comparison is a defined diff with an explicit `unverified` class, and **never joins across a change of pair semantics** (§15.4). | 15 |
| D14 | Stale-input handling is explicit: evicted/reloaded/re-placed models never produce silently mixed results; affected units become gaps, tainted units are discarded, and the run may become `invalidated`. **Executed-scope equality is proven, not assumed**: every unit's whole-model-target population and root subtrees are re-digested immediately before and after the call and compared with the planning snapshot; a unit is `evaluated` only if equal (invariant I-scope), so a completed/full run implies demonstrated equality. | 16 |
| D15 | Memory/performance: provisional caps (§17), engine-owned synchronous spans bounded by `frameBudgetMs`, and a **`UnitExecutor` port** whose inputs/outputs are plain data — the only worker seam this spec commits to (it does not adopt a worker). | 7.1, 17 |
| D16 | Verification is tiered (unit → differential on the real `CollisionWorld` → live Viewer), with **responsiveness figures characterization-only** because M4 Verdict D left the target unconfirmed. | 22 |

---

## 1. Baseline: what the library gives and what it does not

### 1.1 Facts this design builds on

Each fact is verified against landed source (file named) and, where a spec or the README states it, that doc.

| # | Fact | Source |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | `findClashes` takes **exactly one source model** and explicit `source.dbIds`; targets are `{targets:[{model, dbIds?}]}` where omitted `dbIds` means whole-model scope. `groupBy` must be `'dbId'`. One mode per call. | `CollisionWorld.js` `findClashes` entry validation |
| F2 | **Source self-exclusion is by fragment membership**: a target fragment is dropped iff its `(loadEpoch, fragmentId)` is in the source's own extracted set (assembly-rooted rule). A whole-model source against the same model as target therefore yields **zero** same-model pairs. | `isSourceOwnFragment`, M2 §4.4 |
| F3 | Pair dedup key is `${sourceEpoch}:${sourceOwnerDbId}:${targetEpoch}:${targetOwnerDbId}` — ordered, epoch-qualified, `ownerDbId` (the fragment-owning node, possibly a descendant of the requested dbId). `pairs` are in **insertion order, not canonical**; TLAS and linear differ in order. | `findClashes` aggregation, README |
| F4 | `ClashPair` carries the **live `Model`** on each operand (`source.model`, `target.model`) plus diagnostic-only `modelId`. A raw `ClashPair[]` is not structured-cloneable (circular reference to `window`) — the tracked M1-gate crash. | `CollisionWorld.js`, closeout follow-up 3 |
| F5 | Lifecycle: `admitModel(model)` → frozen `{model, epoch}` lease (idempotent while live); `loadEpoch` is global to the world, monotonic, never reused; a Model ever evicted can never be re-admitted (a reload is a new object and a new epoch); `isLeaseValid` is total/non-throwing; `evictModel` never throws; `dispose()` is terminal. | `CollisionWorld.js`, M1 |
| F6 | **No per-call cancellation, `AbortSignal`, deadline, or progress hook exists** (`AbortController` appears zero times in `collision/`). Checkpoints throw only `WorldDisposedError`/`ModelEvictedError`/(TLAS) `ModelPlacementChangedError`. Abort granularity is one atomic unit. | `scheduler.js`, M1 §"cancellation", M4 §2.3/§11 |
| F7 | A call that hits a lifecycle/capacity/extraction failure **throws and returns no partial result**. Per-fragment/pair problems are **diagnosed** (`statistics.diagnostics`) and the call resolves. | README "Diagnostics", `errors.js` |
| F8 | Thrown-failure vocabulary: `WorldDisposedError`, `ModelEvictedError`, `ModelPlacementChangedError`, `CapacityExceededError` (`kind` ∈ `call-wide-unique-fragment-precheck`, `bucket-capacity`, `tlas-candidate-budget`, `whole-model-enumeration`, `clearance-narrow-phase-budget`), `ExtractionLimitExceededError`, `ObjectTreeUnavailableError`, plain `Error` (entry validation; linear `maxBroadPhaseInstancePairs`). | `errors.js`, `ViewerModelAdapter.js`, `index.js` |
| F9 | Budgets that bind a batch: `maxFragmentsPerDbId` 5,000 and `maxChildrenPerNode` 10,000 (per requested dbId); `maxCollisionInstancesPerEpoch` 20,000 (cumulative **per epoch**, bucket persists across calls, only released by evicting that model); `maxTlasCandidatePairs` 200,000 and `maxClearanceNarrowPhasePairs` 20,000 (per call); `frameBudgetMs` 8. All provisional. | `collision/constants.js` |
| F10 | Modes: `containment` is **directed** (source surface inside target solid); `volume-overlap`, `surface-intersection`, `clearance` are symmetric in meaning. `clearance` requires a caller-supplied `clearance` (finite, > 0); the library has **no default** (`DEFAULT_CLEARANCE = 0.05` is a Viewer-extension-layer default). | `ContainmentModeQuery.js`, README M3 |
| F11 | Determinism: `volume-overlap` and the clearance Hard-Clash precheck terminate on deterministic operation counts only (PC fix landed). `surface-intersection` and `containment`'s witness term still let wall-clock elapsed time set `aborted` — results can depend on machine speed (README "known exceptions"). Operand-swap invariance holds through `volumeOverlapQuery` **except the rank-6 residue** (pairs tying through rank 5, including byte-identical geometry). | README, canonical-ordering spec |
| F12 | Diagnostics: non-empty `statistics.diagnostics` means the result **may be incomplete**; `witness-unavailable` is the one carve-out (verdict complete, marker missing); all four clearance reasons **do** count. Diagnostic `modelId` is diagnostic-only and not unique. R1 alone emits ~1,709 diagnostics per single query on the M4 fixture. | README, M4 evidence |
| F13 | Placement: TLAS placement data is static until the **caller** calls `invalidateModelPlacement(model)`; `broadPhase: 'linear'` re-reads placement every call. Any foreign placement-revision bump (an explicit invalidation **or** growth by another caller's extraction) aborts an in-flight TLAS query with `ModelPlacementChangedError`; the model stays live and the caller should retry. | README, M2 §6.7.5 |
| F14 | Batch-relevant measurements (M4, one fixture, one dev machine, single `findClashes` calls — **never batches**): tasks stay ~9–25 ms yet frames starve 100–283 ms per query; candidate cost is driven by per-source-instance density, not source count (whole-Structure source, 543 instances → 5,284 clearance candidates, 26% of the 20,000 cap); typed-buffer memory 4–25 MB; per-instance retention 280–388 B; **no** multi-process wall-clock makespan was recorded; no worker overhead was ever measured. | M4 checkpoint/closeout, digest of `m4-gate-1789180565018.json` |
| F15 | Instance-tree readiness: an under-enumerated tree yields a walk that finishes normally with **no diagnostic** ("Mode 2", explicitly left open). Whole-model **source** enumeration is not a library primitive (only whole-model **target** scope, `extractAllFragments`, exists). | `instance-tree-readiness-decision.md`, `findClashes` |
| F16 | **Extraction is subtree-recursive per requested dbId** (an explicit-stack walk of the node and all descendants; each fragment is attributed to the node that *directly* owns it, first-discovery-wins). Two disjoint dbId lists therefore do **not** imply disjoint fragment sets whenever one dbId is an ancestor of another; and the fragment-membership self-exclusion (F2) drops a target fragment that any source subtree walk touched. There is no exact-owner (non-recursive) extraction scope. | `ViewerModelAdapter.js` `extractFragments` |
| F17 | **Two diagnostics are unattributable by construction.** Whole-model *target* extraction (`extractAllFragments`) emits `fragment-not-loaded` (checked before any owner lookup) and `fragment-owner-unresolvable` (`getDbIds` returned `undefined` or an array) with `fragment.dbId` **hard-coded `null`**, `role: 'target'`; the fragment is **dropped** (`continue`, no record), so a clash involving it is silently not found. Every other library diagnostic carries a numeric `ownerDbId`. `extractFragments` (explicit dbIds) emits no diagnostics. | `ViewerModelAdapter.js` `extractAllFragments`; `CollisionWorld.js` `fragmentRef` |
| F18 | **The library exposes no per-call evidence of what target population a call saw.** `statistics` (`emptyStatistics()`) has no target/source fragment count or enumeration identity (`targetTotalCount` is a local). `getEpochPlacementDiagnostics(epoch).placementCount` is a **cumulative, write-once-per-fragmentId epoch table** (union of everything ever extracted for that epoch across all calls; it never shrinks while the epoch is live), so it cannot prove any single call's population — it does not change on replacement of one fragment by another, nor on a fragment that stopped/started being eligible. | `CollisionWorld.js` `emptyStatistics`, `getEpochPlacementDiagnostics`, `_recordPlacement` |

### 1.2 What the roadmap assigns to this layer

Roadmap §3/§6 give this layer: batch/many-object orchestration, test definitions and lifecycle, result-set semantics/lifecycle, aggregation/grouping/filtering-ready data, progress, cancellation/abort with terminal-state semantics, execution state, diagnostics/incomplete-result semantics at batch scale, and interaction with the Hard and Soft Clash modes. Roadmap §4 makes durable model/version identity this layer's to define. Roadmap §4/§6 explicitly hand cancellation policy (reject vs partial vs discard) and progress to this layer. §21 traces each roadmap constraint to where this spec satisfies it.

### 1.3 Library behaviors this spec relies on that are not yet documented contract

Stated so the implementation plan can pin or test them rather than assume:

1. **Concurrency posture.** M4 records concurrent `findClashes` calls as "an existing, handled scenario", while M2 §6.7.5 records that a concurrent caller's *growth* revision bump aborts another in-flight TLAS query. This spec takes the conservative reading (D6). It is not a claim that concurrency is broken.
2. **`getEpochPlacementDiagnostics(epoch).placementRevision`** is a public diagnostic accessor but its revision counter moves on growth as well as invalidation, so it **cannot** by itself detect "the model was moved". The engine therefore owns a separate placement generation (§16.2).
3. **The library exports no version constant.** Provenance records a host-supplied `libraryVersion` (§13.2); absent, repeatability across builds is not asserted.
4. **Whole-model source enumeration** needs an `ElementEnumerator` port (§6.2) because no library primitive exists; the Viewer implementation of that port reads the same `fragList.getDbIds(fragId)` owner mapping `extractAllFragments` already uses (accessor resolved in `docs/superpowers/specs/m2-extractAllFragments-viewer-evidence.md` and exercised live by the M2/M3 whole-model-target acceptance). What is **new** here is consuming that mapping to enumerate distinct *source* owners and gating on tree readiness; the plan must verify that use against a real Viewer session (OQ-4).
5. **Epoch admission is cumulative world state, not a function of one call's inputs.** `admitCollisionInstances` (and its side-effect-free preflight mirror) checks `currentBucketSize + genuinely-new fragment ids ≤ maxCollisionInstancesPerEpoch` against the epoch's **existing bucket**, which any earlier query — this engine's, the Clash Finder extension's, or a direct caller's — may already have populated and which only `evictModel` releases (F9). Whether a call throws `CapacityExceededError{kind:'bucket-capacity'}` therefore depends on the world's history, not only on the call's inputs. A *successful* call's verdicts do not depend on the bucket (admission only gates), so the dependence is confined to *which units become `bucket-capacity` gaps*. This spec treats the starting admission state as an explicit environmental input (§6.4 P1, §13.2, §15.1) rather than claiming it away — but records only its per-epoch *count*, which is an observational witness, not a reproduction key: admission depends on cache membership, which the public surface does not expose (§15.1).
6. **Geometry availability is transient renderer state, not part of any scope digest.** `resolveGeometryRecord` turns a missing render proxy (`getRenderProxy(model, fragId)` returning nothing, or a proxy with no geometry) into a per-fragment `geometry-not-yet-available` diagnostic and does *not* settle a permanent entry, so a later call can succeed for the same fragment (`collision/CollisionWorld.js`, `RenderProxyUnavailableError` handling). The fragment's loaded flag, owner and `geometryId` — everything the scope digests of §6.2.1 cover — can be identical before and after. Two runs with the same inputs can therefore differ *only* because a proxy became (un)available. The fail-closed side is already covered: that diagnostic is completeness-affecting (§12.3), so the affected elements are `element`-tainted and the run is `integrity: caveated`, never a clean negative. What this spec adds is the honest scope of the *determinism* and *freshness* statements (§15.1, §16.4).

---

## 2. Scope, layering, non-goals

### 2.1 Layering

```text
Collision Core (collision/)            geometry, query execution, diagnostics, budgets, checkpoints   [unchanged]
        ▲ public CollisionWorld surface only
Federated Clash Test & Results         definitions, planning, orchestration, run lifecycle, results,
(this spec; proposed clashtest/)       diagnostics at batch scale, durable result sets, grouping foundations
        ▲ stable engine contract (§18)
Viewer Clash Panel (future)            presentation, navigation, interaction state, workflow/status
```

The engine's core imports **nothing** from the Viewer, `three`, or `collision/` internals. It touches `CollisionWorld` only through the injected world and through the two ports that isolate Viewer specifics (`ElementEnumerator`, `ModelRegistry`), mirroring how `collision/` itself keeps Viewer specifics in `collision/viewer/`. A Viewer adapter (proposed `clashtest/viewer/`) supplies those ports.

### 2.2 In scope

Test definitions and their lifecycle; model/model-set operands; deterministic planning and batch orchestration over `findClashes`; deterministic pair/result identity; run lifecycle, progress, cancellation; partial/incomplete semantics and batch-scale diagnostics; sealed durable result sets and their persistence port; aggregation/grouping/filter foundations; rerun and comparison; stale-model/epoch/placement handling; memory/performance boundaries; acceptance criteria.

### 2.3 Explicit non-goals

See §23 for the full list. Headline items: no UI (Panel), no issue/approval/assignment workflow, no persistence backend, no worker/off-main-thread execution, no change to collision-core contracts, no Group-By UI, no resume-of-cancelled-run, no user-tunable geometric tolerances.

---

## 3. Vocabulary

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Clash Test** (definition) | Immutable revisioned value: `selectionA`, `selectionB`, one `mode` (+ `clearance`). The *question*. |
| **Selection** | A set of model scopes: whole model, or an explicit dbId list within one model. Union semantics. |
| **`modelKey`** | Host-supplied durable, opaque model identity. The only model identity that appears in durable data. |
| **`ElementRef`** | `{modelKey, dbId}` — the durable element identity. |
| **Binding** | The runtime association `modelKey → live Model` (+ lease, generations), held by the `ModelRegistry`. |
| **Run** | One execution of one definition revision against one input snapshot. Has a lifecycle. |
| **Work Unit** | One planned `findClashes` call: one source model, an explicit source dbId set, a target scope. |
| **Cell** | One of three disjoint pair-space regions the planner decomposes a test into (§6.3). |
| **Ledger** | The per-run record of every unit's outcome and every declared gap. |
| **Result Set** | The sealed, immutable, plain-data outcome of a run. |
| **Root** | A subtree root: the unit of selection granularity. Selecting a dbId selects its whole subtree; a selection is normalized to an antichain of roots (§5.6). |
| **`PairId` / `ResultId`** | Canonical pair identity / `(testId, semanticDigest, PairId)`. |
| **Gap** | Pair-space the run was required to cover but did not evaluate, with a reason. Absence of a result inside a gap is *unknown*, never *negative*. |
| **Caveat** | A completeness-affecting diagnostic inside evaluated pair-space. |

---

## 4. Durable identity model

### 4.1 Why runtime identity cannot be persisted (F4, F5, F3)

`Model` objects are live, non-serializable, and dead after unload; `loadEpoch` is per-world; `modelId` is diagnostic-only and reuse-prone; `fragmentId`/`geometryIndex` are session-local (`fragmentId` is only empirically stable across identical reloads). The roadmap (§4) makes a durable identity **this layer's** to define and forbids persisting a `Model` or `modelId` as one. Everything durable in this layer is therefore keyed on the identities below, and the runtime identities exist only inside a live run's bindings.

### 4.2 `modelKey` and `modelVersion`

- **Identity contract (chosen, binding): a `modelKey` names one immutable model version — one source document *and* one specific translation/content state — for all time.** `dbId`s are stable only within one such version (§4.4), so every durable identifier built on `(modelKey, dbId)` is only sound if the key can never denote two different contents. The alternative — carrying a version inside every `ElementRef`/`PairId`/`ResultId` — is deliberately **not** chosen: it would make every durable id longer and every definition version-pinned, and it would still rest on the same host honesty about what a "version" string means. A host that re-translates, replaces, or otherwise changes a model's content **must mint a new `modelKey`**; carrying workflow state from the old key to the new one is an explicit host/Panel reconciliation (§23), never an implicit aliasing.
- `modelKey`: non-empty string, **host-supplied**, opaque to the engine (compared by exact code-unit equality; total order by code-unit comparison). The engine never derives, parses, or normalizes it. The same physical model *version* must yield the same `modelKey` across sessions (a reload of unchanged content keeps its key); two different versions must never share one.
- `modelVersion` (**mandatory**): non-empty opaque string that is a **content fingerprint/witness** of that version (for example the translation/derivative revision or a content hash — never a display label and never a constant). The engine does not interpret it; it records it and compares for exact equality. It exists so the identity contract is *checkable*, not so identity can vary within a key.
- **Enforcement points** (a violation is always a loud, typed failure, never silent aliasing):
  1. **`ModelRegistry.register`** — keeps a `firstSeenVersion` per `modelKey` for the registry's lifetime (surviving `unregister`). Registering a `modelKey` with a different `modelVersion` than first seen throws `ModelKeyVersionConflictError{modelKey, seen, offered}`; a missing/empty `modelVersion` throws `ModelBindingError`.
  2. **`ResultStore.put`** — the port contract requires the store to reject with `IdentityConflictError` any Result Set whose `provenance.models[]` maps a `modelKey` to a `modelVersion` different from one ever accepted by that store (the index survives `delete`, §13.5). **The whole of `put` is one linearization point (or one transaction):** (i) same-`runId` idempotency/conflict validation, (ii) validation of *every* supplied `modelKey → modelVersion` against the index, (iii) claiming every previously unseen mapping, (iv) rejecting a **new** insertion whose `testRef.testId` is tombstoned in that store (`TestDeletedError`, §5.5, NH3), and (v) inserting the Result Set — either all take effect together or none does. A backend that checks the index and inserts in separate steps is non-conforming: two concurrent `put`s of *different* `runId`s that map one `modelKey` to two `modelVersion`s must produce exactly one success and one `IdentityConflictError` (AC-11). This is the durable, cross-session enforcement point (all durable `ResultId`s pass through it). A rejection is a persistence failure (`failed`, `error.name = 'IdentityConflictError'`, §13.5.1), never a change to the run outcome.
  3. **`compareResultSets` / `rerun` / `getFreshness`** — if the two sets (or a set and the live binding) record the same `modelKey` with different `modelVersion`s, the contract has been violated by the host: `compareResultSets` classifies **no** pair involving that `modelKey` as `unchanged`/`changed`/`added`/`resolved` and lists them under `identityConflicts` (§15.4); `rerun` against a live binding whose `modelVersion` differs from the base's recorded one **rejects** its returned Promise with `ModelKeyVersionConflictError` (A8); `getFreshness` reports `stale` with reason `model-version-conflict:<modelKey>`.
- **Residual trust boundary, stated plainly:** if a host reuses a `modelKey` *and* a `modelVersion` for different content, no engine check can detect it. `modelVersion` must therefore be a genuine content witness; OQ-1 covers how the Viewer host derives it.
- The engine does not choose the derivation. A Viewer adapter may propose one (for example from the loaded document's URN plus derivative version), but that is a host/Panel decision (OQ-1).

### 4.3 Binding: `ModelRegistry`

```text
register(modelKey, model, { modelVersion })     // modelVersion MANDATORY (§4.2); idempotent for the same (key, model, version); throws ModelKeyVersionConflictError on a version change for a known key
unregister(model)                                // wired to BEFORE_MODEL_UNLOAD_EVENT, beside world.evictModel
bindingFor(modelKey) → Binding | null
snapshotBindings(modelKeys) → { registryRevision, bindings: Array<Binding|null> }   // R13-6: ONE synchronous, all-or-nothing read of every listed key (no await inside, no callback that can mutate the registry); `registryRevision` is a counter bumped by every register/unregister/notifyPlacementChanged, so two snapshots are comparable
keyOf(model) → modelKey | null
registrySessionId                                // read-only nonce, fixed for the registry's lifetime (freshness, §16.4)
notifyPlacementChanged(modelKey)                 // §16.2
```

`Binding = { modelKey, model, modelVersion, lease, bindingGeneration, placementGeneration }`.

- `lease` is obtained lazily via `world.admitModel(model)` (F5).
- `bindingGeneration` increments when the same `modelKey` is registered against a **different** `Model` object *with the same `modelVersion`* (a reload of unchanged content). A reload whose content changed must use a new `modelKey` (§4.2); re-registering the old key with a new `modelVersion` throws. `placementGeneration` is an engine-owned counter the host increments via `notifyPlacementChanged` (§16.2).
- `unregister` must never throw (it runs inside an unload-event handler; an uncaught throw there aborts the Viewer's teardown of every remaining model — M1 §"BEFORE_MODEL_UNLOAD_EVENT"). It marks the binding dead **only if** the binding's current `Model` is the exact object being unregistered (object identity, never `modelKey` alone), so a late unload event for an *old* model cannot kill a newer binding registered under the same `modelKey`. Active runs observe the dead binding at their next boundary (§16).
- One live `Model` maps to at most one `modelKey`, and one `modelKey` to at most one live `Model`. Violations **throw** at `register` (a synchronous call) with a typed error.
- **The registry is world-scoped, not engine-scoped.** Bindings, `bindingGeneration`, `placementGeneration`, key uniqueness and the `firstSeenVersion` ledger are shared state; serializing runs per world (§7.3) is useless if two engines over one world could hold different generations. The world coordinator (§7.3) therefore **owns the sole `ModelRegistry` for its world**: `createClashTestEngine({world})` joins it, `engine.registry` is that same object for every engine over the world, and `engine.invalidateModelPlacement` / `register` / `unregister` / `notifyPlacementChanged` all act on it. A `registry` option that is not the coordinator's registry for that world throws `ModelBindingError('registry-mismatch')` at construction. A placement change made through one engine is therefore visible to a run in progress in another (invalidating it, §16.3); AC-8 tests exactly that.

### 4.4 `ElementRef`, `PairId`, `ResultId`

- `ElementRef = { modelKey: string, dbId: number }`. `dbId` is model-local (a non-negative safe integer); it is meaningful only alongside its `modelKey`. **Element identity is `(modelKey, dbId)`; nothing stronger is claimed — and it is sound *because* a `modelKey` is one immutable model version (§4.2).** dbIds are stable only within one model version; a re-translated model may renumber, which is exactly why a re-translated model gets a **new `modelKey`** and therefore new `ElementRef`s, `PairId`s and `ResultId`s that cannot alias the old ones (open question OQ-2 covers an optional durable per-element identity such as a Revit unique id for continuity *across* keys; `ElementRef` is a **closed** persisted type in schema version 1 — exactly `{modelKey, dbId}`, §13.6 — so no `externalId` field is reserved in this version and adding one would be a `schemaVersion` bump with a migration, never an additive optional field).
- **Element order** (used for every canonical ordering): `modelKey` by code-unit order, then `dbId` numerically.
- **`PairId`**: a pair of `ElementRef`s `(first, second)`.
  - *Unordered* semantics (`volume-overlap`, `surface-intersection`, `clearance`): `first` is the lesser ref in element order, `second` the greater. `first ≠ second` always.
  - *Ordered* semantics (`containment`): `first` = the contained (library *source*) element, `second` = the container (library *target*). No reordering.
- **`ResultId` = `(testId, semanticDigest, PairId)`**, with canonical string form `JSON.stringify([testId, semanticDigest, first.modelKey, first.dbId, second.modelKey, second.dbId])`. Including `semanticDigest` (§5.4) is deliberate: a revision that changes the mode, the clearance, or either selection asks a *different question*, so its results must not inherit an identity that a workflow layer may have attached approval/assignment state to. A revision that changes only `name`/`description` keeps the same `semanticDigest` and therefore the same `ResultId`s. Model content changes are handled one level down: a changed model has a **new `modelKey`** (§4.2), so every `ElementRef`/`PairId`/`ResultId` involving it is different by construction; and because the `semanticDigest` covers the selections' `modelKey`s, a test re-pointed at the new key is also a different question. **Cross-question and cross-version carry-over is never implicit**: a workflow that wants it must go through an explicit reconciliation using `compareResultSets` (§15.4) and `getPairStatus` (§12.5), which compare by `PairId`, and (for a key change) a host-supplied element mapping that is outside this layer (§23). This form is a stable, delimiter-safe encoding; it deliberately avoids the "modelId in a delimited string key" hazard M2 §4.1 calls actively wrong.
- **`PairId` is comparable only within one pair-semantics class.** An *unordered* `PairId` (`first < second` by element order) and an *ordered* `PairId` (`first` contained, `second` container) are different kinds of identity even when they name the same two elements: the same physical pair can carry `(x, y)` in one and `(y, x)` in the other, and the two say different things (symmetric proximity vs. "x lies inside y"). A `PairId` is therefore **never** used as a join key across a change of `pairSemantics` (§4.5); §15.4 declares such comparisons `incomparable`. Within the *ordered* class, `(x, y)` and `(y, x)` are distinct pairs by definition (a reversal of `selectionA`/`selectionB` in a `containment` test asks the converse question), and §15.4 reports it as such rather than unifying them. This spec defines **no** cross-semantics unordered comparison key.
- **`testId` reservation (NH3).** Because `testId` is part of `ResultId`, a hard-deleted `testId` is reserved forever and re-use is refused at *both* the definition store and the result store, on every run path (direct definitions and embedded reruns included) — §5.5. The reservation never depends on a definition still existing.
- Both forms are **independent of run, epoch, unit boundaries, broad-phase choice, and evaluation orientation**. A pair keeps the same `ResultId` across reruns of the same question, across sessions, and across different unit partitionings — which is what makes rerun diffing (§15) and any later status carry-over (Panel/workflow layer) well-defined.

### 4.5 Symmetry class is derived, not chosen

`pairSemantics` is a function of `mode`: `containment → ordered`; all others `→ unordered`. It is not a definition field; the validator rejects any attempt to supply one. (`clearance` distance is swap-invariant per the canonical-ordering spec; `volume-overlap` is swap-invariant except the rank-6 residue, which §11.4 handles.)

---

## 5. Federated Clash Test definition

### 5.1 Schema

```js
/** @typedef {{ kind: 'model', modelKey: string }
 *          | { kind: 'elements', modelKey: string, dbIds: number[] }} SelectionEntry */
/** @typedef {{ entries: SelectionEntry[] }} SelectionSpec */

/** @typedef {Object} ClashTestDefinition
 *  @property {1} schemaVersion
 *  @property {string} testId              opaque, unique, immutable across revisions
 *  @property {number} revision            integer >= 1, strictly increasing per testId
 *  @property {string} name                display only
 *  @property {string} [description]       display only
 *  @property {SelectionSpec} selectionA
 *  @property {SelectionSpec} selectionB
 *  @property {'volume-overlap'|'surface-intersection'|'containment'|'clearance'} mode
 *  @property {number} [clearance]         world units; REQUIRED iff mode === 'clearance', forbidden otherwise
 */
```

### 5.2 Operand semantics

- A selection is a **union over its entries, per model**. `kind: 'model'` = every fragment-owning element of that model (dynamic: enumerated at run start, §6.2). `kind: 'elements'` = exactly those dbIds (static). If both appear for one `modelKey`, `model` wins. Duplicate entries for one model merge.
- **Snapshot boundary.** Bindings, model versions and generations are captured **atomically at the `queued → planning` transition** (§16.1) by **one** call to `registry.snapshotBindings` over every referenced `modelKey` (a single synchronous read that cannot interleave with a registry mutation; the returned `registryRevision` is recorded in the run and a later refactor that reads keys one-by-one across an `await` is non-conforming, AC-8), not at `startRun` and not at unit start. Whole-model *source* selections and every explicit list are enumerated once, at planning, and fixed for the run; models registered after that transition are out of scope (the same snapshot rule M2 §9.2.3 gives `findClashes`). Whole-model **target** scope is the exception the library imposes: `findClashes` re-extracts a whole-model target on every call (F1), and explicit root lists are re-walked on every call too, so the executed fragment sets can differ from the planned ones if the Viewer's fragment population changes mid-run (e.g. a still-streaming model). The engine therefore does **not** promise a snapshot of executed inputs; it promises a **proof or a gap**: the planning-time scope digests (§6.2.1) are the baseline, and a unit is `evaluated` only if the live scope is shown equal to it immediately before and immediately after the call (§16.3). If equality is not demonstrable the unit is a gap or the run is invalidated — a completed/full run therefore *implies* demonstrated scope equality (invariant I-scope, §16.3). The resolved model list, plan-time digests and the proof method are recorded in provenance (§13.2).
- **Hierarchy is subtree-granular** and is specified in §5.6. The library walks a requested dbId's whole subtree and reports results at the fragment-owning `ownerDbId` (F3, F16); the engine reports results at that owner dbId and defines **no roll-up**.
- **Selection shapes supported** (the roadmap §6 list): model-vs-model (`A={model X}`, `B={model Y}`), model-vs-many (`A={model X}`, `B={elements/models…}`), many-vs-many, full-model enumeration, and same-set self tests (`A = B`), all through the one schema — no special-case shapes. Same-model overlap between `A` and `B` is fully supported **when it is root-identical** (§5.6, §6.3) and is where the design is most careful; nested overlap is rejected at planning.

### 5.3 Validation

One pure function, `validateTest(def) → ValidationResult = {issues: Issue[], issuesTruncated: boolean}`, is used identically by `validateTest`, `saveTest` and `startRun` (R16 Q1: **`validateTest` never throws for a data error** — an unsupported `schemaVersion` is the issue `unsupported-schema-version` at `/schemaVersion`; `saveTest`/`startRun` turn a non-empty result into `InvalidTestDefinitionError{issues, issuesTruncated}`, or into `UnsupportedSchemaVersionError` when that is the *only* issue — the validation itself always runs synchronously, but the failure is **thrown** by `startRun` (which returns a `RunHandle`) and **delivered as a rejection** of `saveTest`'s returned Promise, A8). It **collects issues** as `{path, code, message}` in a single pass, performs **no coercion**, and never stops at the first issue *until* it has collected `maxValidationIssues` (provisional 100, ceiling 1,000): beyond that it stops scanning and the result carries `issuesTruncated: true`, so the issue list, like everything else the synchronous path touches, has a constant memory bound. **Tiering (H6):** validation is the *only* work done synchronously in the caller's turn. It is a single linear pass over the definition (O(entries + explicit elements + own keys of the visited objects), see the trust boundary in the capture rule below) with **no sorting, de-duplication, normalization, or digesting** — those run later, in the sliced work of the `queued` step (`startRun`) or inside the sliced async body of `saveTest`. The synchronous span is bounded by the *lowered* ceilings `maxEntriesPerSelection` and `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` (§17.1) and is **excluded from the 8 ms engine-slice guarantee** of §17.3 (it cannot be cooperatively sliced while preserving synchronous error delivery); §17.3 and AC-12 define how it is measured instead. Only if the issue list is empty does normalization run, in slices (de-duplicate and ascending-sort dbId lists; merge same-model entries with `model` winning over `elements`; **order each selection's entries ascending by `modelKey` code-unit order, one entry per model**; compute `semanticDigest`). Failure throws `InvalidTestDefinitionError{issues, issuesTruncated}`; a `schemaVersion` greater than the engine understands throws the distinct `UnsupportedSchemaVersionError` and is never partially interpreted.

**Capture at the boundary (R11-1).** Every value that crosses an asynchronous boundary is **captured as a detached, data-only copy in the same single pass that validates it**, so the value that is validated, normalized, digested, planned, embedded and persisted is one and the same. **The capture algorithm (one concrete, reflection-based procedure — no exotic-object detection is promised).** For each object the validator (1) reads its own keys once with `Reflect.ownKeys` and rejects any symbol key or any key outside the schema (unknown-property issue); (2) reads each schema key's **own property descriptor once** and rejects an accessor descriptor (`get`/`set` present) or a missing required key — the value is taken from the descriptor's `value`, so no getter on the caller's object is ever *invoked*; (3) for an array, reads `length` from its descriptor once, reads each index `0..length-1` by descriptor once (a hole is a `sparse-array` issue) and rejects any other own key (extra array properties); (4) writes what it read into a fresh plain copy and validates *that copy's* fields, and never touches the caller's object again after it returns. A `Proxy` is **not** detected and no promise is made about it: it is treated as whatever its traps report, its reported values are captured once like any other, and a trap that throws (or an object whose reflection throws, or a revoked proxy) is caught and reported as a V1 issue `unreadable` — never an internal failure and never an escape of the thrown value. V1 still requires plain-object *shape* — prototype `Object.prototype` or `null` as reported by `Reflect.getPrototypeOf` — but this is a shape rule for honest callers, not a defence against a hostile same-realm caller. **Trust boundary and cost, stated plainly:** the caller is in-process host code. The synchronous cost is O(entries + elements + *own keys of the visited objects*); the `maxEntriesPerSelection`/`maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` ceilings bound the schema-shaped part, but an object carrying millions of *unknown* own keys is enumerated by `Reflect.ownKeys` before it can be rejected and is **not** bounded by any ceiling. A host that passes definitions from an untrusted source must first bring them through a structured-clone/`JSON.parse` boundary (which bounds key counts by payload size and cannot carry accessors, symbols or proxies) — the engine's own stores, persisted sets and any worker message are already of that form. The captured copy is what `startRun(definition)` and `saveTest` use after they return (the caller keeps its own object and may mutate it freely), and it is deep-frozen at the end of the first slice. **Scope of the rule:** (i) `startRun(definition)` — the definition **and** `runOptions` (an O(constant) copy); (ii) `startRun({testId, revision?})` — the two reference fields; (iii) `saveTest(def)` — captured **before** the promise is returned, and the store receives only the frozen copy; (iv) a definition **returned by a store or by a registered migration** (§5.5, §13.6) — captured and validated in one uninterruptible pass on receipt, before any use, and treated as untrusted exactly as before; (v) `rerun` — the base set's embedded definition is already a frozen engine value (§13.3) and only the new `definitionRevision`/`runOptions` are captured. The copy allocates O(input) in the synchronous span, bounded by the same lowered ceilings as validation (§17.1) and measured with it (§17.3, AC-12); it performs **no** sorting, de-duplication, normalization or digesting (those stay in the sliced work and run over the captured copy). A caller mutation made after `startRun`/`saveTest` returns — including immediately after, and while the engine is yielded mid-slice — therefore cannot alter validation, the `semanticDigest`, the plan, the embedded definition or the persisted identity (AC-1).

| Rule | Field(s) | Check |
|---|---|---|
| V1 | definition | A plain object with exactly the keys of §5.1: required `schemaVersion, testId, revision, name, selectionA, selectionB, mode`; optional `description, clearance`. **Unknown properties are rejected at every nesting level** (this also rejects `pairSemantics` and any run/execution option, which are not definition fields). Every object must be plain-shaped (prototype `Object.prototype` or `null`, own data properties only — no accessors, no symbol keys) and every list a real `Array` without holes or extra own keys; a violation is a V1 issue, read and reported by the capture algorithm above (which promises no `Proxy` detection). |
| V2 | `schemaVersion` | Exactly `1`. |
| V3 | `testId`, `revision`, `name`, `description` | `testId`: string, 1–256 code units. `revision`: integer, 1 ≤ n ≤ 2^53−1. `name`: string, 1–256. `description`: string ≤ 4,096 when present. |
| V4 | `selectionA/B` | `{entries: SelectionEntry[]}` with 1 ≤ length ≤ `maxEntriesPerSelection` (provisional 1,024). Each entry is exactly `{kind:'model', modelKey}` or `{kind:'elements', modelKey, dbIds}`; `kind` is one of the two discriminators. |
| V5 | `modelKey`, `dbIds` | `modelKey`: string, 1–1,024 code units. `dbIds`: non-empty array of non-negative safe integers, total explicit elements per selection ≤ `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` (provisional **10,000**, §17.1 — deliberately low because this check runs synchronously; the array `length` is tested first, so an over-cap array costs O(1), not O(n)); duplicates are permitted on input and removed on normalization. |
| V6 | `mode`, `clearance` | `mode` ∈ the four library modes. `clearance` present ⇔ `mode === 'clearance'`, and then a finite number > 0 — the same two-directional rule `findClashes` enforces (F10). **The engine supplies no default clearance**; the Panel/host chooses one. |

Emptiness that can only be known at run time (a `kind:'model'` entry enumerating to zero elements) is **not** a validation error; it is a run-time outcome (§6.6). Hierarchy conflicts between the two selections likewise need the instance tree and are detected at planning (§5.6), not here.

### 5.4 Revisioning, identity, digest

- Editing produces a **new revision**; revisions are immutable. `testId` + `revision` identify a definition.
- `semanticDigest` = digest of the canonical (sorted-key JSON, normalized selections) form of `{mode, clearance, selectionA, selectionB}` — **excluding** `name`, `description`, `revision`. Two revisions with equal `semanticDigest` ask the same question. **Swapping `selectionA` and `selectionB` is a different question for every mode, including the unordered ones (R21 Q3, decided):** the two selections are role-tagged in the canonical form, and even for a symmetric mode the definition-derived role assignment decides the rank-6 tie-break residue (§11.4) and the orientation of cell scheduling, so the swap changes `semanticDigest` and every `ResultId`; AC-1 pins this with a golden-vector pair. The digest format is fixed here: `"sha256:" + lowercase hex` of SHA-256 over the UTF-8 bytes of a **canonical JSON** encoding — object keys sorted by UTF-16 code-unit order, no insignificant whitespace, strings serialized by the ECMAScript well-formed `JSON.stringify` algorithm (ES2019: lone surrogates are escaped as `\uXXXX`, control characters as their short or `\u00XX` forms, nothing else escaped, so each string has exactly one encoding), numbers rendered as the shortest round-trip `Number#toString` (finite only; `-0` normalized to `0`), arrays in their normalized order. Every durable digest in this spec (§13.1) uses this format and records `digestVersion: 1` beside it; digests of different versions are never compared (the comparison reports `unverifiable`). Golden byte-level vectors (AC-1) cover entry permutations, Unicode, control characters, lone surrogates, `-0`, and exponent-form numbers. The *implementation* (Web Crypto `SubtleCrypto`, or a sliceable pure-JS SHA-256 for the ≤ `frameBudgetMs` span rule, §17.3) is a plan-level choice; the output bytes are not.
- Results reference `(testId, revision, semanticDigest)` and **embed a full copy of the definition** (§13.1), so a Result Set stays self-describing after its definition is edited, archived, or deleted.

### 5.5 Definition lifecycle

`active → archived` (soft) and hard `delete`. **Archive and delete apply to a test as a whole (`testId`, all its revisions)**; neither touches Result Sets. **A hard `delete` removes the definition bodies but permanently tombstones the `testId`:** the store must retain at least `{testId, latestRevision, deleted: true}` forever, and every later `put` for that `testId` — including `revision: 1` — rejects with `TestDeletedError{testId}` and changes nothing. This is required because `ResultId` embeds `testId` (§4.4) and Result Sets (and any workflow state keyed by `ResultId`) deliberately survive definition deletion: re-using a deleted `testId` could otherwise make a different test produce byte-identical `ResultId`s. The tombstone is written in the **same linearization point** as the delete (so a racing `put` sees either the live test or the tombstone, never neither). A tombstoned id reads as not found (`get` → `null`, not listed) but is observable through `isTombstoned(testId) → Promise<boolean>` (linearizable with `delete`). **The `ClashTestStore` tombstone alone does not protect `ResultId`s** — direct definitions and embedded reruns never call `put` on this store — so it is paired with the `ResultStore` reservation below. Like the identity index of §4.2, the tombstone set is independent of any other retention. An archived test remains readable (`get`, `list`) but cannot start *stored-reference* runs or accept new revisions (`TestArchivedError`). **Archive and delete are governed differently for direct definitions (NH3, round 9).** *Archive* is soft and advisory: `startRun(definition)` does not consult the store's archive state, so a caller who retains a copy of an archived definition can still run it (the result records `testRef`; hosts that need archive enforcement start runs by reference), and `rerun` from an embedded definition is likewise ungoverned by archive state. **Delete is a permanent identity reservation and is enforced on *every* run whose Result Set may be persisted — stored-reference, direct-definition and embedded-definition `rerun` alike — because `ResultId` is derived from `(testId, semanticDigest, PairId)` and every durable `ResultId` reaches storage through `ResultStore.put`, not through the `ClashTestStore`.** The reservation is therefore held, and enforced, at **two layers**:

1. **Durable authority — `ResultStore` (the guarantee).** The `ResultStore` port keeps a permanent **test tombstone set**, written by `tombstoneTest(testId)` (idempotent, permanent, independent of `delete(runId)` and of every other retention, like the model-identity index of §4.2). `ResultStore.put` — inside the same linearization point as the identity-index checks (§4.2 point 2) — rejects with `TestDeletedError{testId}` any **new** insertion whose `testRef.testId` is tombstoned, changing nothing (no result inserted, no `modelKey` claimed). A `put` of a `runId` that is *already stored with identical canonical content* still succeeds idempotently (it creates no new durable identity), and Result Sets stored before the tombstone stay readable, listable and deletable by `runId`. **Guarantee:** no `ResultStore` honoring the port ever holds a Result Set *newly inserted* after `tombstoneTest(T)` took effect whose `testRef.testId` is `T`, on any run path (stored reference, direct definition, embedded rerun, `retryPersist` of a `failed` set, a host retrieving a sealed set and calling `put` itself, or a persisting run admitted before the delete and finishing after it — that last case seals normally and its persistence ends `failed` with `error.name = 'TestDeletedError'`, run outcome unchanged, set `held`, §13.5.1).
