2. **Admission check — engine (fail-fast, so a doomed persisting run does no work).** In the first `queued` step, **for every run with `runOptions.persist !== false` when a `testStore` is configured**, the engine calls `testStore.isTombstoned(testId)` (`testId` of the reference, the direct definition or the embedded definition). `true` ⇒ terminal planning failure `test-unavailable` (`test-deleted`); a rejected/unavailable lookup ⇒ `test-unavailable` (`test-lookup-failed`) — **fail closed**, so a persisting direct run depends on the store being reachable. In both cases the sealed set has `definition: null` and `testRef: {testId, revision: null, semanticDigest: null}`, no unit runs, no `findClashes` call is made. Runs with `persist: false` skip this check (they create no durable identity) but remain subject to layer 1 if anyone later persists them — a host retrieving the sealed set with `getResultSet` and calling `ResultStore.put` itself (the engine's `retryPersist` is legal only from a `failed` set, §13.5.1, and is **not** an entry point for a `skipped` one).

**`engine.deleteTest(testId)` is the only supported delete and orders its steps fail-closed:** (i) `resultStore.tombstoneTest(testId)` (if a `resultStore` is configured), *then* (ii) `testStore.delete(testId)` (which writes the `ClashTestStore` tombstone in its own linearization point, above). Both steps are idempotent; if (i) succeeds and (ii) fails the promise rejects, the reservation **stays** (durable persistence is already blocked) and a retry completes the delete; the reverse order is never used. **Residual trust boundaries, stated:** a host that calls `testStore.delete` directly (bypassing the engine) leaves each `ResultStore` unaware — the admission check still blocks persisting runs through that `testStore`, but the durable guarantee holds only for stores that received `tombstoneTest`; each independent `ResultStore` needs its own `tombstoneTest`; and two stores cannot enforce `testId` uniqueness against each other, so a host sharing Result Sets across stores must mint globally unique `testId`s. Persistence goes through a `ClashTestStore` port (`put/get/resolveForRun/list/archive/delete/isTombstoned`; the plan supplies an in-memory reference implementation; storage backends are a non-goal, §23).

**Run-admission read: `resolveForRun` (R13-5).** `get` returns a bare `ClashTestDefinition|null` (a closed schema that carries no archive flag), so a stored-reference run reads the store through one dedicated operation, **`resolveForRun(testId, revision?) → Promise<{status: 'live'|'archived'|'deleted'|'missing', definition: ClashTestDefinition|null, latestRevision: number|null}>`**, which is linearizable per `testId` and returns the revision *and* the archive/tombstone state from **one point** (`definition` is non-null only for `live` and `archived`; `latestRevision` is the store's current latest, `null` for `deleted`/`missing`; a requested revision that does not exist is `missing`). The engine maps `status` to the planning cause — `live` ⇒ continue, `archived` ⇒ `test-archived`, `deleted` ⇒ `test-deleted`, `missing` ⇒ `test-not-found` — and treats the returned `definition` as untrusted (§5.5). No store adds fields to the definition, and no store communicates archive state through any other channel; `list` summaries carry `{testId, latestRevision, archived, name}` (the closed `TestSummary` shape of §13.5). `get` stays the plain read for hosts.

**Missing stores (R15 Q3).** Definition operations need a `ClashTestStore`: with none configured, `saveTest`, `getTest`, `listTests`, `archiveTest` and `startRun({testId})` (a stored reference) fail with `StoreNotConfiguredError{store:'testStore'}` — the four Promise-returning operations **reject**, and `startRun({testId})` **throws synchronously**, before slot admission; `validateTest` and `startRun(definition)` are unaffected. `deleteTest` performs each step whose store is configured — `resultStore.tombstoneTest` and/or `testStore.delete`, in the §5.5 order — and **rejects** with `StoreNotConfiguredError` only when **neither** is configured; with just one configured it completes that step (the residual trust boundary of §5.5 then applies to the other, unconfigured, side).

**Store consistency contract.** `ClashTestStore` operations (`put`, `get`, `resolveForRun`, `list`, `archive`, `delete`, `isTombstoned`) are **linearizable per `testId`**: each takes effect at a single point between its invocation and response (for a server or IndexedDB backend, its committed transaction), and every `get` observes a consistent state — revision *and* archived flag from the same point — so `startRun({testId})` racing `archive(testId)` sees either the world before it or after it, never a mix. The in-memory reference implementation must satisfy this and be tested with interleavings (AC-1).

**Revision model (chosen): the caller assigns the revision; the store enforces strict monotonicity atomically.** `put(def)` accepts a definition iff `(testId` has **never been seen by the store — neither live nor tombstoned** ∧ `revision === 1)` or `revision === latest(testId) + 1` (for a live test); a tombstoned `testId` rejects with `TestDeletedError` (§5.5); anything else — a duplicate `(testId, revision)`, a gap, or an out-of-order save — rejects with `RevisionConflictError{testId, latest, offered}` and **never overwrites**, so a saved revision is immutable and histories are gap-free. `get(testId)` without a revision returns the **latest** revision (archived or not); `get(testId, n)` returns exactly revision `n` or `null`. `saveTest(def)` = `validateTest` + `put` (it neither assigns nor rewrites revisions); `startRun` has two overloads with explicit semantics (a stored reference is subject to the store's archive state, a direct definition is not, §5.5). **`startRun(definition)`** validates and admits *synchronously*: `InvalidTestDefinitionError`, `InvalidOptionsError`, `EngineDisposedError` and `ResultCapacityError` all throw before a handle exists. **`startRun({testId, revision?})`** (a stored reference) validates the reference shape and applies **exactly the same synchronous capacity admission as the direct overload** (§13.5.2: reserve a slot, else evict an `evictable` set, else throw `ResultCapacityError`); once admission has succeeded it **always returns a handle**, and only the *asynchronous* lookup can fail after that. The store lookup is the first step of `queued`. **Every definition a store returns is untrusted** (a store is a port, and may hold data written by another writer or version): it is passed through `validateTest` (after the registered migration, if its `schemaVersion` is lower, §13.6) before it is used. A missing or archived test, or a returned definition that cannot be used, is a terminal planning failure — the run seals `failed` with planning gap `test-unavailable` (`test-not-found` / `test-archived` / `test-deleted` / `test-lookup-failed`), `definition-invalid` (the returned value fails `validateTest`; **no field-level issue detail — no path, code or value — is recorded in the sealed set**, which carries only the planning-gap `cause`; the planning-gap shape is unchanged, owner disposition A1), `schema-unsupported` (`schemaVersion` greater than the engine understands, no partial interpretation), or `migration-failed` (a registered migration threw or produced an invalid definition). In all four cases the sealed set has `definition: null` and `testRef.semanticDigest: null` (only the `testId` the caller supplied is recorded — the *pre-resolution seal* of §13.1, shared with cancellation before resolution and with direct-definition runs); the slot it was **already admitted with** becomes an ordinary resident set (never leaked, never exceeding the cap). Once the definition is resolved and embedded, a later `archiveTest`/`deleteTest` does not affect the run's *execution* (a later delete does still block its *persistence*, layer 1 above). Lookup consistency is the store's linearizability contract (§5.5): a run sees either the state before or after an archive, never a mix. Two hosts racing on the same next revision are resolved by the store's atomic `put`: exactly one wins, the other gets `RevisionConflictError`.

### 5.6 Subtree granularity and the roots contract

The library cannot evaluate "this node's own fragments only" (F16): a source dbId contributes its **entire subtree**, and any target fragment inside a source subtree is dropped by self-exclusion (F2). A planner that reasoned over dbId sets alone would prove disjointness it does not have. This spec therefore makes **subtree roots** the unit of selection and states the consequences explicitly.

1. **Selecting a dbId selects its entire subtree.**
2. **Root normalization** (per model, per selection) via `ElementEnumerator.resolveRoots` (§6.2): a *whole-model* scope resolves to the **topmost fragment-owning nodes** (every **tree-reachable** fragment then lies in exactly one root's subtree; fragments the instance tree does not reach are the separate `unreachable` population of §6.2 and lie in no root); an *explicit* list resolves to the listed dbIds that have **no listed ancestor**. Each root carries its subtree `fragmentCount` and the **complete ascending list of fragment-owning dbIds in its subtree** (`ownerDbIds`), recorded in the ledger as the `rootOwners` table (§6.6). Its size is bounded by the number of fragments in scope (≤ the library's per-epoch fragment ceiling for a whole model, F9; ≤ `maxFragmentsPerDbId` per explicit root) and it is covered by the root's `subtreeDigest` (§6.2.1). **Persisted row shape (v1, normative).** Each `rootOwners` row is `{rootDbId, ownerDbIds, cls}` — the table is grouped per model, so a row is reached as `rootOwners[].owners[]` — where `cls` is that root's **planning class** (§6.3): `'A'` for a root of `A'_m = A_m \ B_m`, `'B'` for `B'_m = B_m \ A_m`, and `'C'` for `C_m = A_m ∩ B_m` (the hierarchy-internal candidate class, item 3). All three fields are **required**, and all three are **digested** (§13.1): `cls` is what makes “is this root in `A ∩ B`?” answerable from the sealed set alone rather than from the bounded `hierarchyInternal.examples` list (§6.6 item 3), which would silently under-report element-level caveats (§12.5). This is the **initial `schemaVersion` 1 contract** for the row, not a field added to a shipped one — no Result Set of this schema has been produced or persisted — so §13.6's version-bump rule for a *required new* field does not apply and no migration is involved (erratum **A9**).
3. **The required pair space is defined over roots** (§6.1): a pair is a pair of *distinct* roots. Fragment pairs *inside* one root's subtree (e.g. a fragment-owning parent versus its own fragment-owning descendants, or two fragment-owning siblings under a non-owning explicit root) are **hierarchy-internal**: the library cannot evaluate them from the parent (F2, F16). They are not in the required pair space `P`, but they are **not harmless**: whenever a root lies in **both** selections (`x ∈ A ∩ B`, the only case in which a pair between two of its own owners answers the user's question) and its subtree has **two or more distinct fragment-owning nodes**, a whole-model self-test silently omits real geometric intersections. This is a **declared exclusion** (§6.6) that is **claim-blocking**: `canClaimNoClashes` is false whenever the run's `hierarchyInternal` table is non-empty, *unless* a product acceptance record has been supplied (§8.3, OQ-16). Until that acceptance exists, the unresolved state is treated as a coverage limitation, never as "no clashes". (A root in `A` only or `B` only has no internal pair the question asks about, so it does not enter the table.)
4. **Results remain at owner level.** A result's `ElementRef`s are fragment owners (`ownerDbId`), which may be descendants of a root. `PairId` is owner-level (§4.4); pair-space accounting is root-level; the ledger's `rootOwners` table maps **every** fragment owner in a root's subtree — listed or not — to its root, so an owner such as a non-listed descendant `105` of explicit root `100` is always mappable (§12.5 `getPairStatus`), and a base result at `105↔220` can be proven `resolved` by a clean `100↔200` evaluation.
5. **Cross-role nesting is unsupported in v1.** For each model let `RA`, `RB`, `RU` be the root sets of `A_m`, `B_m` and `A_m ∪ B_m`. The selections are *supported* iff `RU = RA ∪ RB` as dbId sets (roots identical in both roles are fine; no root in one role may be an ancestor of a root in the other). Otherwise planning fails: run `failed`, reason `hierarchy-overlap`, with the first 20 offending (ancestor, descendant) pairs recorded, **before any unit runs**. This is deterministic and cheap, and it is the honest alternative to a plan that silently mis-covers.
6. **Remedies are outside this milestone.** Supporting "whole model vs. one nested element", or a parent tested against its own descendants, needs an exact-owner extraction scope in collision core (a collision-core change under roadmap §8, OQ-15). How common fragment-owning nodes with fragment-owning descendants are in the target Revit content is **unmeasured**; the plan should count them on the acceptance fixture.

---

## 6. Planning: coverage, Work Units, decomposition

### 6.1 The required pair set

Let `A_m`, `B_m` be the **root sets** (§5.6) of model `m` in `selectionA`, `selectionB`, after enumeration. The **required pair space** `P` is over distinct roots:

- *Unordered modes:* `P = { {x,y} : x ≠ y, ((x ∈ A) ∧ (y ∈ B)) ∨ ((y ∈ A) ∧ (x ∈ B)) }` — each unordered pair of roots once.
- *Ordered mode (`containment`):* `P = { (x,y) : x ∈ A, y ∈ B, x ≠ y }`.

Pairs of fragments within one root's subtree are hierarchy-internal and are **not** in `P` (§5.6.3). The planner's job is to cover `P` **exactly once** with Work Units, every unit legal under the library's rules, and to account for anything it could not cover.

### 6.2 Enumeration and the `ElementEnumerator` port

`findClashes` requires an explicit source `dbIds` array; only *target* scope has a whole-model sentinel (F1, F15). The engine therefore resolves roots itself:

```text
ElementEnumerator.resolveRoots(binding, scope: 'whole' | { dbIds: number[] }, ctx: EnumerationContext) → Promise<{
  roots:    Array<{ dbId, fragmentCount, ownerDbIds, subtreeDigest }>,   // ascending by dbId; fragmentCount = fragments in the root's SUBTREE; ownerDbIds = ascending distinct fragment-owning dbIds in that subtree (the owner→root map, §5.6.4); subtreeDigest = identity of that subtree's extracted fragments (§6.2.1)
  population: { count: number, digest: string } | null,       // scope 'whole' only: identity of the whole-model TARGET population (§6.2.1); null for explicit lists
  ownerMismatch: { count: number, first20: Array<{ fragId, treeOwner, fragListOwner }> } | null,   // scope 'whole' only (§6.2)
  unreachable: { count: number, ownerDbIds: number[], unattributable: { notLoaded: number, ownerUnresolvable: number } } | null,   // scope 'whole' only: (i) loaded, owner-resolvable population fragments that lie in NO root subtree (tree under-enumeration); ownerDbIds = their ascending distinct owners; (ii) `unattributable` (R12-1): population fragments of class `N` (not loaded) or `U` (owner-unresolvable) that lie in no root subtree — they have no owner, so they cannot be classified by owner and are counted only
  unresolved: Array<{ dbId: number, reason: 'not-found' | 'no-fragments' }>   // explicit lists only: requested dbIds that yielded no root. 'not-found' = not a node of the instance tree; 'no-fragments' = exists but its subtree owns no collision-eligible fragment
}>

ElementEnumerator.digestScope(binding, scope: 'whole' | { dbIds: number[] }, ctx: EnumerationContext) → Promise<
  { kind: 'population', count: number, digest: string }                            // scope 'whole'
  | { kind: 'roots', roots: Array<{ dbId, fragmentCount, subtreeDigest }> }        // scope { dbIds } (root dbIds)
>   // re-reads the LIVE model now, with exactly the same function resolveRoots used at planning; never cached
```

- `'whole'` resolves to the **topmost fragment-owning nodes**; `{dbIds}` to the listed dbIds with no listed ancestor (§5.6.2). **Ownership is role-specific and must match the library exactly** (F3, F17): for **source roots and explicit target roots** the owner of a fragment is the instance-tree node whose `enumNodeFragments` callback first produced it (the `extractFragments` rule — first-discovery-wins); for a **whole-model target** it is `fragList.getDbIds(fragId)` (the `extractAllFragments` rule). For a whole-model scope the enumerator computes *both* for every fragment reachable from the tree and returns any disagreement as `ownerMismatch: {count, first20: [{fragId, treeOwner, fragListOwner}]}`; a non-zero count **fails planning** with planning gap `owner-mapping-mismatch` (fail closed — otherwise the same physical pair could receive different `PairId`s depending on which role the planner assigned it, and `validateUnitOutcome` would reject valid pairs). Verifying that the two mechanisms agree on the real Viewer is a **gate on accepting the Viewer adapter implementation** (AC-15 (g), OQ-4), not a property this spec assumes.
- **Must wait for the model's object tree to be ready** and must fail, not return a partial list, when the Viewer reports the tree unavailable (`ObjectTreeUnavailableError`-class). The library's own "Mode 2" (F15) means a *complete-looking* under-enumerated tree is undetectable; the engine records the plan-time `population` and per-root `subtreeDigest`s in provenance so a rerun can at least detect that the model's enumerated content changed (§15.4) and so execution can prove it saw the same content (§6.2.1, §16.3). This is a declared, inherited limitation, not a solved one (OQ-4).
- **`EnumerationContext = { checkpoint(): void | Promise<void> }`** is supplied by the engine to both methods. The enumerator must call `checkpoint()` at least once per slice (§17.3) and `await` its result; `checkpoint()` yields to the scheduler and **throws `EnumerationCancelledError`** when the run has been cancelled, its lease has died, or the world/engine is disposed. The enumerator must propagate that error (the engine maps it to planning gap `cancelled` or `world-disposed`; a dead lease during planning is planning gap `model-not-bound`). An enumerator that ignores the context weakens the guarantee to "cancellation takes effect when the current `resolveRoots`/`digestScope` invocation settles"; the engine still never *starts* another invocation after cancellation, and AC-7 tests both a cooperating and a non-cooperating enumerator.
- Enumeration is `await`ed **before** planning, checks the lease between models, and is chunked so no engine-owned synchronous span exceeds `frameBudgetMs` (§17.3).
- The engine calls `resolveRoots` for `A_m`, `B_m` and `A_m ∪ B_m` to apply the cross-role rule (§5.6.5). Because these calls `await`, the three answers for one model must agree — equal `subtreeDigest`/`ownerDbIds` for every shared root and equal `population` — otherwise the model changed *during planning* and planning fails with planning gap `enumeration-failed`; the run never plans over mutually inconsistent enumerations.

**Tree-unreachable population is never a silent clean negative either.** The two enumerations the library uses differ (`extractFragments` walks the instance tree; `extractAllFragments` scans every `fragId`), so a *loaded, owner-resolvable* fragment can be in the whole-model target population yet in no root subtree. For a `'whole'` scope the enumerator reconciles the two: every population fragment with a numeric owner must appear in exactly one root's subtree records with the same owner and geometry identity; those that appear in none are returned as `unreachable`. **Unattributable unreachable fragments (R12-1).** The same reconciliation also counts population fragments of class `N` (`fragment-not-loaded`) or `U` (`fragment-owner-unresolvable`) that lie in **no** root subtree, as `unreachable.unattributable = {notLoaded, ownerUnresolvable}`. They matter because the two orientations see them differently: as a whole-model **target** the library drops them and emits the `dbId: null` diagnostic (F17), which taints the unit (§12.5 rule 2); as a whole-model **source** the library walks only the instance tree, never visits them, and emits **nothing** — so without this record the same model would be tainted in one orientation and silently clean in the other, and a stable-but-incomplete tree enumeration would still satisfy every scope digest (digests prove *stability*, not completeness). They are recorded, orientation-independently, and are claim-blocking and pair-tainting (§12.5 rule 7).

The reconciled populations above are **declared, claim-blocking exclusions**, not silent omissions: recorded in the ledger as `unreachablePopulation` per model (`{modelKey, count, ownerDbIds, mixedOwnerDbIds, unattributable: {notLoaded, ownerUnresolvable}}`; an entry exists iff `count > 0` or either `unattributable` count is > 0; bounded by the fragments in scope), listed in `libraryExclusions` as `tree-unreachable-population`, and `canClaimNoClashes` is false whenever any exists (§8.3).

**Owner classes (H1).** The library's result is aggregated to *owner* level (F3): a returned pair names an owner `dbId` but not which of that owner's fragments produced it, so a result cannot prove whether it arose from a tree-reachable or a tree-unreachable fragment. One owner can own both kinds. The planner therefore classifies every fragment-owning `dbId` of a model with an `unreachable` population, purely from data the enumerator already returns (`rootOwners` ∪ `unreachable.ownerDbIds`, both recorded), into exactly one class:

| Owner class | Definition | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| **root-only** | ∈ some root's `ownerDbIds`, ∉ `unreachable.ownerDbIds` | Ordinary: every fragment is tree-reachable and inside the required pair space. |
| **unreachable-only** | ∈ `unreachable.ownerDbIds`, ∉ every root's `ownerDbIds` (absent from the root-owner population) | Outside `P`. As a whole-model **source** it is never evaluated (sources are tree roots — the hole the exclusion flags); as a whole-model **target** a clash against it is reported but retained with `flags.unreachableOwner: true`, and `getPairStatus` reports it `not-required` (⇒ unverified in comparison). **`not-required` is reserved for this class.** |
| **mixed** | ∈ `unreachable.ownerDbIds` **and** ∈ some root's `ownerDbIds` (recorded as `mixedOwnerDbIds`) | Some fragments are inside `P`, some are not, and a result cannot say which contributed. **Every pair with a mixed owner on either side and in either orientation is conservatively tainted**: it is never `covered-clean` (taint rule 6, §12.5), a result retained for it carries `flags.mixedOwnerProvenance: true`, and an *absence* is never a proven negative because the unreachable fragments of that owner were not evaluated as a source (or were evaluated only as a target, unattributably). The required root-pair coverage stays mandatory — the mixed owner's *reachable* fragments are still evaluated — only the *clean* claim is withheld. |

The classification is a pure function of the recorded plan data, so it is identical for both role assignments of the same pair (the two cell orientations of §6.3), and it never depends on which unit produced a result. It also cannot be bypassed by a whole-model source or target choice: the taint attaches to the owner, not to the unit's role. Duplicated or inconsistent records (a fragment under two roots, or the same fragment with different owner/geometry) are an enumerator defect and fail planning (`enumeration-failed`).

**Residual limit for explicit scopes (stated, R12-1).** An explicit `{dbIds}` scope has no independent population to reconcile against (`population` is `null`), so a subtree that the Viewer's instance tree under-enumerates while still returning at least one fragment is **undetectable** — the library's own "Mode 2" (F15). This is an inherited trust boundary, not a solved one: `canClaimNoClashes` for a test with explicit scopes means "no clashes among what the instance tree enumerated at planning, proven stable through the run" (§6.2.1), and the adapter's readiness gate (OQ-4) is what makes the tree trustworthy. The ledger records `scopeKinds` per model so a consumer can tell which operands had a population witness (`model`) and which did not (`elements` only, §13.2).

**Unresolved explicit elements are never a silent clean negative.** A requested dbId that yields no root contributes **no pair space** but is never dropped: `not-found` is an invalid reference (a stale or mistyped dbId) and **fails planning** (`failed`, planning gap `element-unresolvable`, first 20 offenders recorded, no unit runs); `no-fragments` is a real node with no geometry and is recorded in the ledger as a declared, **claim-blocking** entry in `unresolvedSelections` (`{modelKey, dbId, role}`) — the run still evaluates the rest, but `canClaimNoClashes` is false whenever `unresolvedSelections` is non-empty (§8.3), because the selection was never fully resolved to geometry. (A `kind:'model'` entry cannot produce either case: it enumerates existing owners.)

#### 6.2.1 Scope digests (the evidence for target-scope equality)

The library gives no per-call evidence of what population a call extracted (F18), and `placementCount` cannot supply it. The engine therefore takes its own evidence, **from the same accessors the library reads**, in a form that is comparable across time:

- **Population digest (scope `'whole'`).** For every `fragId` in `0..fragList.getCount()-1`, in ascending order, one record: `[fragId, 'N']` if `fragList.isNotLoaded(fragId)`; else `[fragId, 'U']` if `fragList.getDbIds(fragId)` is `undefined` or an array; else `[fragId, 'L', ownerDbId, geometryId]` (`getGeometryId(fragId)`, the identity the library itself records). The digest (format §5.4, `digestVersion` recorded) covers `count` and the record list. It is exactly the population `extractAllFragments` enumerates **including its two dropped classes** (F17): a fragment that becomes loaded, becomes unloaded, changes owner, or is *replaced by a different geometry under the same `fragId`* changes the digest — none of which a cumulative count can see (F18). Fragments unreachable from the instance tree are *in* the population (they are eligible whole-model targets), so they cannot cause false drift.
- **Subtree digest (explicit root scope, and every source root).** For each root, the digest of the sorted `[fragId, ownerDbId, geometryId]` records of the fragments `extractFragments` would take from that root's subtree (first-discovery-wins owner). A unit's source roots and explicit target roots are checked root-by-root against the plan's `subtreeDigest`s.
- **Not covered, by design:** world matrices/bounds (placement is its own axis: `placementGeneration`, §16.2, and `placementVerified: false`, §13.2).
- **Conformance.** The enumerator's records must equal what `extractAllFragments`/`extractFragments` produce for the same model. AC-2 (T1/T2) runs a differential over mock models (count-preserving replacement, status flips, growth, shrink, owner change, hierarchy) against the library's own exported extraction functions (test-only import; the engine core imports neither).
- **Cost.** One pass over the scope's fragments per digest, sliced (§17.3); a whole-model digest is bounded by the library's own `maxCollisionInstancesPerEpoch` ceiling on `getCount()` (above it enumeration fails, matching `whole-model-enumeration`). Because §16.3 takes two digests per unit, the added cost per unit is of the same order as the library's own per-call whole-model extraction; it is measured (AC-12) and not asserted.

### 6.3 Three-cell decomposition (exact cover, same-model-safe)

For each model `m`: `A'_m = A_m \ B_m`, `C_m = A_m ∩ B_m`, `B'_m = B_m \ A_m`. Cells (unordered modes):

| Cell | Sources | Targets | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | `A'_m` (per source model `m`) | `B_n` for **every** model `n` — *as a set of target slices* (§6.4): each unit's `targets:[…]` carries at most `maxTargetModelsPerUnit` models and `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit` fragments, so cell 1 is one **or more** units per source chunk, never one unbounded call | A source root in `A\B` cannot be inside a target root, so same-model targets are safe. The slices partition the target roots, so every (source root, target root) pair is in exactly one unit. `B_n` is the whole-model sentinel **iff `A_n = ∅`** and its population fits the target caps — the same eligibility condition as cell 2, because a sentinel for a model with non-empty `A_n` would also cover that model's `A'`/`C` roots and **over-cover** the required pair space (exact cover, AC-2); otherwise an explicit root list. |
| **2** | `C_m` | `B'_n` for all `n`, sliced the same way (§6.4) | `B'_n` is the whole-model sentinel iff `A_n = ∅` **and** its population fits the target caps (call-time scope proven equal to the planning population by §16.3, or the unit is a gap); otherwise an explicit root list. |
| **3a** | `C_m` | `C_n` for each model pair `m < n` (by `modelKey` order) | Cross-model overlap region, once per unordered model pair. |
| **3b** | intra-`C_m` (same model) | — | **Recursive bisection**, below. |

**Exactness (unordered).** For a required `{x,y}` with `x ∈ A_m`, `y ∈ B_n`: if `x ∈ A'_m`, cell 1 covers it and no other cell can (`y` is either not in `A`, or in `A` with `x ∉ B_m`, so `x` is never a target of `y`'s cell-1/2 sources). If `x ∈ C_m` and `y ∈ B'_n`, cell 2 covers it once. If both are in `C`, cell 3a (cross-model, `m<n`) or 3b (same model) covers it once. Each cell's source and target **root** sets are disjoint by construction, and roots are subtree-disjoint (§5.6, enforced by the `hierarchy-overlap` rule), so a source subtree walk never contains a target fragment and the library's fragment-membership self-exclusion (F2) never suppresses a required pair.

**Ordered mode (`containment`).** Cells 1 and 2 are unchanged (already ordered `A → B`). Cell 3a runs **both** orientations for every ordered model pair, and 3b bisection runs both `L→R` and `R→L` at each node.

**Cell 3b — recursive bisection.** For `C_m` sorted ascending, split into halves `L`, `R`; emit a unit set `L → R` (and `R → L` for ordered mode); recurse on `L` and on `R` until a set has ≤ 1 element. Each unordered same-model pair is evaluated exactly once, at the unique node where its members are split apart. This is required because the whole-set-vs-itself alternative is *silently empty* (F2). It is the reference strategy; the plan may substitute a block-triangular scheme **provided the coverage invariants and AC-2 (§22) still hold** (OQ-5 records the unmeasured unit-count cost) **and every block is a contiguous run of `C_m` under the canonical root order** — defined here, per model, as the three §6.5.1 root tables (`A'`, `C`, `B'`) concatenated in that fixed sequence, each already ascending by `dbId`. This is a single, fixed, writer-derived order, not a search over possible orderings: a layout is legal only if it is contiguous under THIS order, never merely "contiguous under some ordering." A block that is an arbitrary subset of `C_m` under the canonical order is not a legal persisted layout, and stored-set validation rejects it (§13.6).

**Common cases collapse cleanly:** cross-model `A={model X}`, `B={model Y}` (disjoint) ⇒ only cell 1; `A = B = {model X}` ⇒ only cell 3b; single source element vs a federation whose target population fits the per-unit target caps (the shipped "Find Clashes") ⇒ one cell-1 unit — exactly today's `findClashes`. (Cell 1 describes the role of each root exactly: a root of `A\B` is a source and is a target only of other roots' cell-1/2 units through `B`; it is not "one source against the whole federation" in a single exhaustive call — the source model's own `C`/`B'` roots reach it through cells 2–3, and every unit is bounded by §6.4.)

### 6.4 Unit sizing and deterministic splitting

- Each cell's source set is packed into units in ascending dbId order, greedily, bounded by `maxSourceFragmentsPerUnit` and `maxSourceDbIdsPerUnit` (provisional, §17), using `fragmentCount` from the enumerator (or 1 when unknown).
- **Target-side bounds (H2), applied at planning, before any collision call.** The library accumulates whole-model diagnostics and extracts every target of a call before anything can be projected or capped (F1, F12: `findClashes` runs `extractAllFragments`/`extractFragments` for *every* `targets` entry, pushing their diagnostics, before the broad phase), so a target-heavy unit would allocate and run in proportion to the whole federation no matter how small its source side is. The planner therefore slices the **target side** deterministically, exactly as it packs the source side:
  1. **Target weight.** A whole-model target entry weighs its `population.count` (every `fragId`, including the two dropped classes — a conservative upper bound on what `extractAllFragments` visits); an explicit root entry weighs the sum of its roots' `fragmentCount`.
  2. **Caps per unit:** at most `maxTargetModelsPerUnit` (provisional 4) distinct target models **and** total target weight ≤ `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit` (provisional 20,000 — the library's own per-epoch instance ceiling, F9, so a whole-model target that the library can cover at all normally fits in one unit alone).
  3. **Slicing.** Target entries are taken in ascending `modelKey` order and packed greedily into target slices. An **explicit-root** entry whose weight exceeds the remaining room is cut **between roots** (ascending dbId; a root is never split, so every root pair still lands in exactly one unit). A **whole-model sentinel** is not partitionable (partitioning it would change extraction semantics, §6.4 split order): it is placed alone in a slice. **`maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit` is a hard per-unit bound, not a packing target (M7):** if a *single target entry that cannot be cut further* — a whole-model sentinel whose `population.count`, **or a single explicit root whose `fragmentCount`**, exceeds it (only possible when a caller lowers the cap below what one root or model needs; at the default 20,000 no single root can, since one root is bounded by `maxFragmentsPerDbId` 5,000, F9) — every unit that would carry it is created at planning as a **`not-run:target-too-large` gap**: never issued, its `pairSpaceWeight` recorded as a gap, `coverage: partial` — a deterministic, pre-call outcome rather than an over-cap call. The rule is identical for both entry kinds. (The *source* side is deliberately different: `maxSourceFragmentsPerUnit` is a **packing target** — its default 200 is far below a legal root's 5,000 fragments, so a single source root heavier than it forms its own unit, bounded absolutely by `maxFragmentsPerDbId`/the library's per-epoch ceiling (F9). Source size drives latency, an operational lever; target size drives the diagnostic/memory fan-out this section exists to bound.)
  4. **Units** are the cross product *(source chunk) × (target slice)* within a cell. Because source chunks partition the source roots and target slices partition the target roots, the required pair space is still covered exactly once (§6.3). The planned unit count feeds the same `maxUnitsPerRun` / `maxPlanReferences` guards, so a target-heavy federation that would need too many slices fails `plan-too-large` before any call.
  5. **What is bounded where.** *Before the call:* source roots/fragments, target models, target fragment weight, plan references and unit count (planner-enforced). *During the call:* extraction and diagnostic accumulation are bounded by the unit's own (source + target) fragment caps; broad-phase/narrow-phase work is bounded by the library's own per-call budgets (`maxTlasCandidatePairs`, `maxClearanceNarrowPhasePairs`, `maxFragmentsPerDbId`, F9), whose errors abort the call. *After the call:* the executor's projection enforces the two whole-array caps `maxPairsPerOutcome` and `maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome` **while projecting**, from `ExecutionRequest.limits` (§7.1, R11-6), and the distinct-reason cap `maxDistinctReasons` is applied afterwards by the engine's run-level diagnostic aggregation (§12.4). The engine cannot interpose *inside* the core call, so the pre-call caps are the only lever on in-call allocation and non-preemptible latency; the spec asserts no numeric bound on raw in-call diagnostics beyond "proportional to the unit's caps and the library budgets" and requires AC-18 to measure a target-heavy unit's raw diagnostic count and peak retained size rather than assume them.
- **Plan determinism (invariant P1), narrowed (H3):** the *initial plan* is a pure function of *(definition semantics, sorted enumerations, run sizing options)*; capacity splits (below) are deterministic consequences of the library's deterministic budget errors. The **final ledger is a pure function of those inputs plus the library's budget constants only while no unit fails on the cumulative epoch cache** (`CapacityExceededError{kind:'bucket-capacity'}`, §1.3.5): that failure depends on the world's history, not on the call's inputs. The engine therefore treats the **starting admission state** — each touched epoch's `getEpochCollisionInstanceCount` at the moment leases are taken — as an explicit **environmental input**, records it in `provenance.admissionBaseline` (not digested, §13.2), records each `bucket-capacity` gap's `{currentSize, missing, limit}` (§11.2 `failure.capacityDetails`), and sets `RunOutcome.environmentSensitive = true` (cause `bucket-capacity`) whenever any unit failed that way (§8.3). A second, independent cause is renderer readiness (`geometry-readiness`, §1.3.6, §15.1), and a third is foreign placement activity that exhausts the bounded retry (`placement-contention`, F13, §7.5). P1 and AC-6 are stated for runs with `environmentSensitive = false`; for a run with `environmentSensitive = true` this spec promises only that *evaluated units' verdicts are unchanged* (admission gates a call, it does not alter its verdicts, §1.3.5), not that the same gaps recur. **Wall-clock and load never influence planning or any verdict.** Sizing options are recorded in provenance and folded into `ledgerDigest`. Strict input-only determinism regardless of world history would need a collision-core change (per-call admission isolation or an explicit cache reset) — recorded as an optional additive change, §21.2 (h), and not pursued.
- **Capacity-driven splitting.** If a unit fails with `CapacityExceededError` of kind `tlas-candidate-budget` or `clearance-narrow-phase-budget` (per-call budgets that scale with source count) the engine splits it into two halves (child ids `${unitId}.0` and `${unitId}.1`) by the **deterministic split order**: (1) if the source set has > 1 root, bisect the sources (ascending dbId); (2) else if the unit has > 1 target *entry* (i.e. more than one target model), partition the target entries into two halves in ascending `modelKey` order, both children keeping the single source root — never separating fragments of the same target root, so every root pair still lands in exactly one unit and library aggregation is unaffected; (3) else, if the single target entry is an **explicit root list with more than one root**, bisect that ascending root list (never splitting a root, so each root pair still lands in exactly one unit); (4) else the unit is fundamentally unsplittable — one source root against one explicit target root, or against a whole-model sentinel (which cannot be partitioned without changing extraction semantics) — and is a gap. At every step the children's weights partition the parent's exactly. The engine, re-queues both, and records the split in the ledger. **Ledger lifecycle of a split:** the failed attempt's record ends with the normative outcome **`split`** ("superseded"): it is retained for audit with `children: [`${unitId}.0`, `${unitId}.1`]` (two distinct, deterministic child ids) and its `pairSpaceWeight` is **transferred exactly once to its leaf descendants** (the children's weights sum to the parent's). Only **leaf** units count toward `pairSpaceTotal`, `unitsTotal`, progress and coverage; `split` records are excluded from every coverage-gap calculation, so full evaluation of all leaves is `coverage: full` (a nested split is just a deeper leaf). `unitsTotal` = current leaf units (grows by 1 per split); `unitRecords` = leaves + `split` records (grows by 2 per split) and is what `maxUnitsPerRun` bounds. On cancel, queued children become `not-run:cancelled` leaves and the parent stays `split`. The split tree is part of `ledgerDigest`. Because those budgets are deterministic functions of the geometry (F11 deterministic-budget posture), the same inputs split the same way. A unit with one source root and one target root (or a whole-model sentinel) that still fails is a `failed:capacity-exceeded` gap. **`maxUnitsPerRun` bounds the total number of unit records — live and terminal — including those created by splits.** A split that would push `unitRecords` above the cap — **or the run's `maxLedgerRecords` budget (§6.5.1: the two child unit records plus their range records must fit in the discretionary capacity)** — is not performed: the unit becomes a `failed:unit-cap` leaf gap (ledger detail `capDetail: 'unit-records'` or `'ledger-records'`) (its pair-space weight recorded as a gap, `pairSpaceTotal` unchanged), so the ledger, `unitsTotal` and the resident-set bound (§13.5.2) can never exceed the cap.
- **Not splittable by the engine (become gaps):** `bucket-capacity` / `call-wide-unique-fragment-precheck` / `whole-model-enumeration` (the per-epoch bucket is cumulative and released only by evicting the model — F9; a `bucket-capacity` gap additionally marks the run `environmentSensitive`, because it depends on the world's prior history, §1.3.5, P1 above), and `ExtractionLimitExceededError` (a single requested dbId over `maxFragmentsPerDbId`/`maxChildrenPerNode`). A model with more than `maxCollisionInstancesPerEpoch` (20,000) fragments cannot be fully covered by any run under today's library; this is reported as a gap, and raising the ceiling is a collision-core decision requiring the roadmap §8 process (OQ-6).

### 6.5 Unit description (plain data)

```js
/** @typedef {Object} WorkUnit
 *  @property {string} unitId               deterministic: `${cell}.${seq}` in canonical plan order
 *  @property {'1'|'2'|'3a'|'3b'} cell
 *  @property {string} sourceModelKey
 *  @property {Array<{table: string, start: number, end: number}>} sourceRanges   index ranges into run-scoped ROOT TABLES (§6.5.1); NOT copied dbId arrays
 *  @property {Array<{modelKey: string, scope: 'whole'|{ranges: Array<{table: string, start: number, end: number}>}}>} targets   whole-model sentinel, or ranges into the root tables
 *  @property {'A->B'|'B->A'|'canonical'} orientationRole  which selection role the source stands for
 *  @property {number} pairSpaceWeight      |source roots| × |target roots|, for progress (a whole-model target contributes its run-start root count)
 */
```

Units carry only `modelKey`s and table ranges — never `Model` objects or copied dbId arrays. The self-contained, postable form is the **`ExecutionRequest`** (§7.1), which materializes the ids; the `UnitExecutor` resolves keys to live models, which is what keeps requests postable to a worker later and structured-clone safe now.

#### 6.5.1 Run-scoped plan tables (bounded plan memory)

The plan is **normalized, not copied**. Per model and per class (`A'`, `C`, `B'`) the planner builds one immutable, ascending **root table** (`dbId`, `fragmentCount`, `subtreeDigest`, and the `ownerDbIds` range) once; every unit, split child and ledger record refers to it by `{table, start, end}` ranges (cell 1's `B_n = C_n ∪ B'_n` is two ranges). A split produces sub-ranges, never copies. The transient `ExecutionRequest` **materializes** the unit's explicit `sourceDbIds`, target lists and planning-time `scopeExpect` digests (`{modelKey, role, scope: 'whole'|'roots', population?, roots?}`) only when the unit is issued, and they are released when the attempt settles. Plan memory is therefore O(roots + owners + unit records), **not** O(units × targets), and the earlier "ledger is O(elements)" claim holds only in that sense. A hard guard `maxPlanReferences` (default in §17.1) counts **every planning-time record class** — root-table entries, `rootOwners`/`ownerDbIds` entries, `unreachablePopulation`/`mixedOwnerDbIds` entries, per-scope digest rows, `unresolvedSelections` entries, `hierarchyInternal` rows and examples, planning gaps, and the initial unit records and unit range records (counted per expanded root id, erratum **A14**, below) — and its running total is **`planRecords`** (round 9: it previously named only table entries and range records, which made the reserve proof below unsound). The guard is applied **incrementally while planning, before allocation**; exceeding it fails planning `plan-too-large` (same as `maxUnitsPerRun`). By construction `planRecords ≤ maxPlanReferences` always. AC-12/AC-16 include a cap-sized many-vs-many planning case. Independently of that budget, a model whose union roots `RU = RA ∪ RB` number more than the `maxEntriesPerSelection` hard ceiling (10,000, §17.1.1) fails planning `plan-too-large` before any of its root rows is built, because each union root is one row of that model's `provenance.models[].scopeDigests.roots` and `ledger.rootOwners[].owners`, which §13.6's schema ceilings cap at that value (erratum **A15**).

**One run-wide accounting budget (`maxLedgerRecords`).** `maxPlanReferences` covers every planning-time record (`planRecords`) and nothing that is created later; the ledger keeps growing during execution (taints, gaps, splits), and per-unit caps do not bound the *product* (10,000 units × 2,000 element taints = 20,000,000 entries at the defaults). Every retained plan **and** ledger record therefore counts **1** toward a single per-run budget `maxLedgerRecords` (default in §17.1; checked incrementally, *before* allocating the record): root-table entries; `rootOwners`/`ownerDbIds` entries; `unreachablePopulation` and `mixedOwnerDbIds` entries; per-scope digest rows (`scopeDigests.population` and each root row); `unresolvedSelections` entries; `hierarchyInternal` rows and examples; unit records and unit range records (including splits) — a unit's range records are counted **one per expanded root id its persisted unit record carries** (each source root id and each explicit target root id, a split child's copied target ids included) **plus one per whole-model target sentinel**, not one per `{table, start, end}` range, because the sealed `UnitRecord` stores expanded ids (§13.1; erratum **A14**); planning gaps; and every per-unit taint entry. Retained diagnostics are bounded separately (`maxRetainedDiagnostics`); results by `maxResultsPerRun`. **Exhaustion contract for every runtime record class (M2, round 9).** The run **never allocates beyond `maxLedgerRecords`** and **never fails merely because the budget is full**. Two mechanisms make that provable rather than assumed:

1. **Reserved overflow slots.** At the end of planning — before the first unit runs — the engine **reserves `maxUnitsPerRun` slots**, one per possible leaf unit (unit records, splits included, are capped at `maxUnitsPerRun`, §6.4, and only leaves execute and can carry taints). Reserved slots count toward the budget from that moment (`used = planRecords + maxUnitsPerRun`) and are consumed *in place* — `used` does not change — when a unit first needs its single unit-wide `taint-overflow` taint (§12.2.1). **Proof that the reserve always fits:** `planRecords ≤ maxPlanReferences` (guard above, over *all* planning classes), and option validation enforces `maxLedgerRecords ≥ maxPlanReferences + 2 × maxUnitsPerRun` (§17.1.1); hence `planRecords + maxUnitsPerRun ≤ maxLedgerRecords − maxUnitsPerRun`, so the reservation cannot fail and discretionary runtime capacity `D = maxLedgerRecords − (planRecords + maxUnitsPerRun) ≥ maxUnitsPerRun`, even for a plan at `maxPlanReferences`. The engine still asserts `planRecords + maxUnitsPerRun ≤ maxLedgerRecords` at the end of planning as an internal invariant (unreachable under validated options; a violation is `failed:internal-error`, not a silent overrun).
2. **A per-class rule, so nothing is left unspecified.** Every record class that can exist after planning is listed; each either cannot exhaust the budget or has a fail-closed fallback that is a *superset* of the detail it replaces:

| Runtime record class | Counted? | When `D` cannot admit it |
|---|---|---|
| `element` taint entry (§12.2.1) | 1 each | **Not recorded individually**; collapsed into the unit's reserved `taint-overflow` taint. |
| `target-model` taint entry | 1 each | Same collapse. |
| `unit` taint entry (any reason other than `taint-overflow`, incl. `diagnostic-overflow`, `diagnostic-reason-overflow`, `statistics-overflow`) | 1 each | Same collapse (`taint-overflow` is itself a unit taint, so the fallback covers every pair the collapsed entry would have covered). |
| the unit's single `taint-overflow` entry | **0 extra** — consumes its reserved slot | Always fits (proof: leaves ≤ `maxUnitsPerRun`, one reserved slot each). |
| split: the two child unit records **and their range records** | 2 + the children's range records (per expanded id, A14), checked as one block *before* allocating | **The split is not performed**: the unit becomes a `failed:unit-cap` leaf gap with `capDetail: 'ledger-records'` (as for `unit-records`, §6.4), its `pairSpaceWeight` recorded as a gap, `pairSpaceTotal` unchanged. No partial split is ever recorded. |
| unit outcome, cause, `scopeProof`, attempt count, `secondaryCauses` (≤ 8), `capacityDetails`, per-unit diagnostic counts, `not-run:cancelled` conversion, retry bookkeeping | **Inline, fixed-size fields of the unit record, counted once with it** (which already exists at planning or was admitted as a split child) | Cannot exhaust: they modify an existing record and allocate no new one. |
| results, retained diagnostics, `countsByReason` keys, run counters, telemetry, annotation snapshots | Not counted here; each has its **own cap** (`maxResultsPerRun`, `maxRetainedDiagnostics`, `maxDistinctReasons`, `maxAnnotationBytes`) with its own fail-closed rule | — |
| planning-only classes (root/owner/digest tables, `unresolvedSelections`, `unreachablePopulation`, `hierarchyInternal`, planning gaps, initial unit and range records) | Counted at planning | Exhaustion is `plan-too-large` (no unit runs). |

**Determinism and conservatism.** Within one unit, taint entries are admitted in canonical order (`kind`, then `modelKey`, then `dbId`, then `reason`, code-unit order), so *which* entries collapse is a pure function of the ledger state, itself a pure function of the run's inputs. Every fallback only enlarges what is tainted or turns a split into a gap: a unit that collapsed any taint makes **every** pair of that unit `covered-caveated` (§12.5 rule 3), a refused split is a gap (never `covered-clean`), so budget exhaustion can only make results *more* conservative. The ledger records `ledger.budgetExhausted` (boolean) and the id of the first unit at which the budget first refused a record; both are covered by `ledgerDigest`. The bytes-per-record figure is not measured (AC-12); this is a *count* bound, and AC-16 exhausts the budget with **every** class above, not with element taints alone.

### 6.6 Coverage ledger and declared exclusions

The plan and the run maintain a **ledger**: every unit, its outcome, `scopeProof` (§16.3), attempts, splits, gap reason, diagnostic counts, its exact `taints` table (§12.2.1) and required pair-space weight; the `rootOwners` table (§5.6.4); the claim-blocking `unresolvedSelections` list, `unreachablePopulation` table (with its `mixedOwnerDbIds`, §6.2) and `hierarchyInternal` table (§5.6.3, item 3 below); and **planning-level gaps**. Nothing is silently dropped:

1. **Unit gaps** — unit failed/skipped/discarded/not-run (§8.3). Pair-space inside a gap is *unknown*.
2. **Planning-level gaps** — a run that fails or is cancelled **before a plan exists** has no units. It records a planning gap `{phase, cause}` (**the exhaustive planning-cause enum**, referenced by the ledger, §8.2, `RunOutcome.reasons` and the tests: cause ∈ `test-unavailable | definition-invalid | schema-unsupported | migration-failed | model-not-bound | enumeration-failed | element-unresolvable | owner-mapping-mismatch | hierarchy-overlap | plan-too-large | cancelled | world-disposed`; `RunOutcome.reasons` carries `planning:<cause>`) and is `coverage: 'partial'` (never vacuously `full`), with `unitsTotal = 0`, `pairSpaceTotal = 0`, and `ledgerDigest` computed over the planning gaps alone.
3. **Declared exclusions** — `hierarchy-internal`: fragment pairs inside one root's subtree are never evaluated (§5.6.3). Not a *unit* gap (no unit was skipped), but **not harmless either (H4)**: for every model, the planner records in the ledger's `hierarchyInternal` table the roots of `C_m = A_m ∩ B_m` whose subtree has ≥ 2 distinct fragment-owning nodes — `{modelKey, rootCount, ownerCount, examples: first 20 {rootDbId, ownerDbIds: first 8}}` (`rootCount`/`ownerCount` exact, examples bounded, all derived from the recorded `rootOwners`, hence deterministic). A non-empty table is **claim-blocking** unless a product acceptance record is supplied (§8.3). Stated in provenance as `libraryExclusions: ['hierarchy-internal']` **iff the table is non-empty**; the acceptance record, when supplied, is stored in `provenance.hierarchyInternalAcceptance` (§13.2). `getPairStatus` reports a pair whose two owners map to the same root as `not-required` with reason `hierarchy-internal` (never `covered-clean`).
4. **Empty operands** — a selection whose roots are empty in every model yields `pairSpaceTotal = 0`, zero units, `coverage: 'full'` with `emptyOperand: true` and a `warnings[]` entry. It is **never claimable** as "no clashes" (§8.3 claim rule requires a non-empty required pair space). A `modelKey` with no live binding at the `queued → planning` snapshot fails the run at planning (`failed`, planning gap `model-not-bound`).

Plan-size guard: an *initial* plan of more than `maxUnitsPerRun` (provisional 10,000; the same cap also bounds runtime splits, §6.4) fails at planning with reason `plan-too-large` and **no units run** — deterministic, inexpensive, and tells the caller to narrow the selections.

---

## 7. Execution: orchestration over `findClashes`

### 7.1 `UnitExecutor` port

```js
/** @typedef {Object} ExecutionRequest        self-contained, plain data
 *  @property {WorkUnit} unit                   ranges only (§6.5, §6.5.1)
 *  @property {{sourceDbIds: number[], targets: Array<{modelKey: string, scope: 'whole'|'roots', dbIds?: number[]}>, scopeExpect: Array<{modelKey: string, role: 'source'|'target', scope: 'whole'|'roots', population?: {count: number, digest: string}, roots?: Array<{dbId: number, subtreeDigest: string}>}>}} materialized   transient expansion of the unit's ranges, planning-time digests included (§16.3); released when the attempt settles
 *  @property {string} runId
 *  @property {number} attempt
 *  @property {'volume-overlap'|'surface-intersection'|'containment'|'clearance'} mode
 *  @property {number} [clearance]              present iff mode === 'clearance'
 *  @property {'tlas'|'linear'} broadPhase
 *  @property {Array<{modelKey: string, modelVersion: string, bindingGeneration: number, placementGeneration: number}>} snapshot   the run snapshot for every model the unit touches (§16.3)
 *  @property {{maxPairsPerOutcome: number, maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome: number, maxDistinctReasons: number}} limits   the run's projection caps; the executor enforces the two WHOLE-ARRAY caps (`maxPairsPerOutcome`, `maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome`) WHILE projecting (below), so an oversized array never crosses the port (R11-6); `maxDistinctReasons` is carried for reference and is applied by the ENGINE's run-level diagnostic aggregation (§12.4)
 */
/** @typedef {(request: ExecutionRequest) => Promise<UnitOutcome>} UnitExecutor */
```

`UnitOutcome` is **plain, structured-clone-safe data** (§11.2, §12). **Division of responsibility.** The *executor* owns everything that touches live models: it resolves bindings (from the registry it is constructed with), checks leases (§7.4), runs the pre/post **scope-digest checks** (§16.3.2) via the `ElementEnumerator`, reads the generations at start and end of the attempt, builds and awaits the library call (§7.2), maps the thrown/resolved result to a structured outcome, and **projects to plain data before returning** (live `Model` references never escape), **enforcing `request.limits` while projecting (R11-6)**: if the library returns more than `limits.maxPairsPerOutcome` pairs, or more than `limits.maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome` diagnostics, the executor does **not** project or transfer them — it returns `pairs: []` with `pairsOverflowLength: n` (resp. `diagnostics: []` with `diagnosticOverflowLength: n`, `n` = the exact array length, an O(1) read) and nothing else from that array; the engine applies exactly the over-cap rules below to those markers. This bounds the executor's own projection allocation, serialization cost and any worker message size; it **cannot** bound the library's own raw arrays, which `findClashes` has already materialized when it resolves (F12, §6.4 item 5) — that in-call volume is limited only by the pre-call unit caps and measured by AC-18. The *engine* owns planning, split and retry **decisions** (from the structured `failure` data), merging, the ledger, and every status/coverage/integrity derivation. Because the request carries mode, clearance, broad phase and the run snapshot, and the outcome carries the structured `failure`, `scopeProof` and generations, any conforming executor yields the same plan and coverage. **Trust boundary (R11 Q1).** A `UnitExecutor` is a **trusted semantic implementation port**: what the engine validates is *data integrity and internal consistency*, not that the executor actually queried the library faithfully. A structurally valid executor that returns an `evaluated` outcome with no pairs and `scopeProof: 'equal'` for a unit it never ran would be accepted and produce a false clean negative; the engine cannot detect that (it has no independent evidence of the library call, F18), exactly as it cannot detect a lying `ElementEnumerator` or `ModelRegistry`. The default `LocalUnitExecutor` is the reference implementation and the only one this spec defines; a host-supplied executor is trusted to the same degree as the other ports (differential conformance against it is AC-3). **Boundary validation.** The engine never trusts an executor *to be structurally well-formed*: every returned outcome passes the pure function `validateUnitOutcome(request, outcome)` before anything is merged. It rejects — treating the outcome as `failed:internal-error`, which fails the run (§7.5) — any of: `unitId`/`attempt` not equal to the request's; `outcome`/`failure`/`scopeProof` inconsistent (`evaluated` requires `failure: null` **and** `scopeProof: 'equal'`); any non-plain value or non-finite number anywhere (`-0` is normalized to `0`); a pair or diagnostic naming a `modelKey` that is not a participant of the unit; a pair whose source owner is not in the `rootOwners` of the unit's source roots, or whose target owner (for an explicit-list target) is not in that root's `rootOwners` (a whole-model target owner must be in that model's `rootOwners` **or** its `unreachablePopulation.ownerDbIds` — anything else is out of scope and rejected); evidence not matching the mode's shape (§11.1); and **size limits on every executor-controlled field** — `pairs.length ≤ maxPairsPerOutcome` (checked *before* any deep processing; over-cap — whether `pairs.length` exceeds it or the executor reports `pairsOverflowLength > limits.maxPairsPerOutcome` with `pairs: []` — is a whole-unit `failed:result-cap` under the §17.1 result-cap rule, not a partial merge), an overflow marker that is inconsistent (`pairsOverflowLength`/`diagnosticOverflowLength` not a non-negative safe integer above the request's cap, or present together with a non-empty array, or on a non-`evaluated` outcome) or an array that exceeds the cap with no marker (the engine still re-checks `length`, since an executor is untrusted), diagnostic `reason` ≤ 128 code units and `failure.message` ≤ 512 (**an over-length value is a malformed outcome, rejected here — it never reaches the overflow mechanism below**); statistics restricted to the known numeric schema (unknown keys rejected). **`UnitOutcome` is a closed schema — every field is bounded or enumerated (R14-3):** `failure.code` ∈ the §8.3 cause enum; `failure.capacityKind` ∈ the library's `CapacityExceededError.kind` set (F8); `failure.errorName` ≤ 128 code units; `failure.modelKeys` a **distinct** subset of the unit's participating `modelKey`s (so ≤ their count); `generations.start` and `generations.end` each list **exactly** the unit's distinct participating models once (no missing, duplicate or extra entry) with non-negative safe-integer generations; every pair `type` is the mode's fixed enumerated evidence type (§11.1), and every other nested string is ≤ 128 code units (`failure.message` ≤ 512 as above); `durationMs` a finite number ≥ 0; no array is unbounded — the only free-length arrays are `pairs`, `diagnostics` and `failure.modelKeys`, each bounded above. A violation of any is a malformed outcome — `failed:internal-error`, nothing merged. **Diagnostic bounds (H7) — two separate mechanisms, never conflated:**
  1. **Rejection (illegal data).** A `reason` longer than 128 code units, a non-string `reason`, a non-plain diagnostic, or an unknown shape that cannot be classified → the whole outcome is `failed:internal-error` (as above). Nothing from it is merged.
  2. **Overflow (legal data, high cardinality).** Diagnostics whose every field is legal are never rejected for being many or varied; they are bounded by *collapsing detail while keeping totals exact*:
     - `diagnostics.length > maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome` **or `diagnosticOverflowLength` reported by the executor** (provisional 200,000, checked with the O(1) `length` / marker *before* iterating; `occurrences` = the array length or the marker value): the array is **not** examined further; the unit receives one unit-wide `diagnostic-overflow` taint (completeness-affecting) with `occurrences = length`, the run-level `total` grows by exactly `length`, and the run-level **`unclassifiedOccurrences`** grows by exactly `length`. These occurrences are **not** added to `completenessAffectingCount` and **not** to `countsByReason`: they were never classified, so counting them as completeness-affecting would label a conservative guess "exact" (M8). Instead the run reports `completenessAffectingUpperBound = completenessAffectingCount + unclassifiedOccurrences`, and **`integrity` is `caveated` whenever that upper bound is > 0** (§12.4) — fail-safe, so an oversize array can only make integrity worse, never better, even if every hidden item was benign.
     - Otherwise every diagnostic is examined once. **`total` and `completenessAffectingCount` are exact sums** over every examined diagnostic, computed *during* the pass by classifying each one with `isCompletenessAffecting` — independent of whether its reason string is retained. **The distinct-reason cap is not applied here.** The executor enforces only the two *whole-array* caps above and returns the unit's diagnostics un-thinned and **before any de-duplication** (§11.2), so `UnitOutcome` stays a **closed** schema and needs no reason-overflow field; `maxDistinctReasons` (provisional 64) and the single `omittedReasonOccurrences` counter belong to the **engine-side run-level aggregation of §12.4**, where that counter already lives. There the engine admits distinct `(scope, reason)` keys in §12.4's deterministic order, adds every occurrence of a key it could not admit to that run-level counter, and, if any omitted occurrence was completeness-affecting, gives the unit that reported it one unit-wide `diagnostic-reason-overflow` taint (fail-safe; the exact per-occurrence element attribution of omitted reasons is not retained).
     - `statistics` overflow collapses to a `statistics-overflow` sentinel as before.
  **What is exact and what is not.** Exact: `total`; `completenessAffectingCount` (over *examined* diagnostics); `unclassifiedOccurrences` (the exact number of unexamined ones); the per-`(scope, reason)` counts *for retained reasons*; and `omittedReasonOccurrences` (one number for **all** omitted reasons together). An *upper bound*, not exact: `completenessAffectingUpperBound`, the only figure that covers unexamined diagnostics (§12.4). Not retained: a per-original-reason count for an omitted reason, and the *number* of distinct omitted reasons (counting distinct values of an unbounded set is itself unbounded). Integrity can only get *worse* through overflow, never better. AC-3 has a separate test for rejection and for each overflow path. The default `LocalUnitExecutor` is the only implementation this spec defines: A future off-main-thread executor could implement the same port without changing the planner, aggregator, lifecycle, or result model — but extraction is main-thread-only in the Viewer (M4 §8.5), so this spec adopts no worker and makes no performance claim about one (D15, §17.4).

### 7.2 The call the executor makes

```js
world.findClashes({
  source: { model: sourceModel, dbIds: request.materialized.sourceDbIds },
  target: { targets: request.materialized.targets.map(t => t.scope === 'whole' ? { model } : { model, dbIds: t.dbIds }) },
  mode: definition.mode,
  groupBy: 'dbId',
  ...(definition.mode === 'clearance' ? { clearance: definition.clearance } : {}),
  broadPhase: runOptions.broadPhase,          // 'tlas' (default) | 'linear' (oracle; §15, §22)
})
```

- A `scope: 'whole'` target is the library's whole-model sentinel and is **re-extracted by the library on every call** (F1); its executed population is therefore *proven equal to the planning snapshot by the bracketed scope digests of §16.3, or the unit is not `evaluated`*. Explicit target lists are root dbIds fixed at planning and are proven the same way (subtree digests). The executor runs the pre/post digest checks around this call; the call itself is unchanged.
- Always the **`targets` shape** (never legacy `{model, dbIds}`), so the broad phase defaults to `'tlas'`, cross-model is legal, and `maxBroadPhaseInstancePairs` does not apply (F1, README M2). `'linear'` is an explicit oracle/verification option, subject to its own ceiling (a plain `Error` above 1,000,000 pairs per target epoch group — recognized by a **pinned matcher**, `error.message` starting with `findClashes: broad-phase candidate count exceeds maxBroadPhaseInstancePairs`, plus a T2 compatibility test that fails if the library stops throwing a matching error for an oversized linear call; a typed capacity error is listed as optional additive change §21.2 (g)); it is recorded in provenance.
- One mode per call (F1); composite Hard+Soft tests are two tests (§19.1).
- The engine passes fresh arrays (the library defensively copies, but the engine does not mutate plan data after issue).

### 7.3 Sequential execution and serialization

- **Strictly one `findClashes` in flight per world** from the engine layer, and **one running run per world**; further runs queue (`queued`, FIFO, bounded by slot accounting, §13.5.2, and `maxQueuedRuns`, default `maxResidentResultSets − 1`; slot accounting and `maxQueuedRuns` are **per engine**, while the FIFO queue and the single in-flight slot are **per world**). The queue **and the model registry (§4.3)** are owned by a **world-scoped coordinator** (a `WeakMap<CollisionWorld, Coordinator>` **stored on `globalThis` under `Symbol.for('clashtest.coordinators')`** so duplicate bundles or module copies in one page join the same coordinator rather than each creating their own; a coordinator carries a `protocolVersion`, and an engine copy with an incompatible version throws `ModelBindingError('coordinator-version-mismatch')` instead of running beside it) that every `createClashTestEngine({world})` joins, so two engines over one world share one queue and one in-flight slot rather than each believing it is alone. The guarantee is scoped honestly: the coordinator serializes *engine* runs; it cannot serialize direct `findClashes` callers (§19.1, OQ-11).
- Rationale (F13, F6): the library does not serialize callers' queries against each other (it single-flights only shared builds); a foreign growth/invalidation revision bump aborts an in-flight TLAS query; batch progress, cancellation and ledger accounting are all defined at unit boundaries. The engine cannot control *other* callers (e.g. the Clash Finder extension's single queries) — a `ModelPlacementChangedError` from foreign activity is handled by bounded retry (§7.5), and running the two concurrently is documented as unsupported for reliable batch throughput, not forbidden.
- Between units the engine yields to the scheduler (`interUnitYield`: animation-frame-aligned when available, else a macrotask). The library already yields *inside* a call at `frameBudgetMs`; the between-unit yield exists because M4 showed frames starve for 100–283 ms per query even when every task stays under ~25 ms (F14). Whether this yield is *sufficient* is a characterization item (AC-12), not a claim.

### 7.4 Lease handling

At run start the engine `admitModel`s every bound model it will touch and keeps the leases. Before every unit it checks `world.isLeaseValid(lease)` for each model the unit touches; an invalid lease turns the unit into `skipped:model-evicted` **without calling `findClashes`** (avoiding a `ModelEvictedError` from `admitModel`, F5, and wasted work). After any thrown `ModelEvictedError`, the engine identifies *which* binding died by re-checking leases — never by trusting the error's `modelId` (F12: not unique).

### 7.5 Error taxonomy → unit outcome

| Library signal | Unit outcome | Retry | Run effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| resolved, any diagnostics | `evaluated` (diagnostics normalized, §12) | — | continues |
| `ModelPlacementChangedError`, `placementGeneration` **unchanged** | retry same unit | ≤ `maxPlacementRetries` (provisional 2) | after limit: `failed:stale-placement` gap; records `failure.attempts` (= `maxPlacementRetries + 1`) and adds cause `placement-contention` to `environmentSensitiveCauses` (§8.3, §15.1) — a foreign caller's growth/invalidation, not a property of the inputs. A unit that *succeeds on a retry* is an ordinary `evaluated` unit (a call's verdicts do not depend on how many attempts it took) and adds no cause |
| `ModelPlacementChangedError`, `placementGeneration` **changed** (host called `notifyPlacementChanged`) | `discarded:input-changed` | none | run → `invalidated` after this unit (§16) |
| pre-check digest ≠ planning digest (§16.3.2) | `skipped:scope-drift` (no call issued) | none | run → `invalidated` at the next boundary |
| post-check digest ≠ planning digest (§16.3.2) | `discarded:scope-drift` (pairs/diagnostics not merged) | none | run → `invalidated` at the next boundary |
| digest could not be computed, pre or post (§16.3.2) | `skipped:` / `discarded:scope-unverifiable` (fail closed) | none | continues; unit is a gap |
| `ModelEvictedError` / invalid lease | `failed:model-evicted` (or `skipped:` if pre-checked) | none — an evicted Model can never be re-admitted (F5) | continues; units not touching that model still run |
| `WorldDisposedError` | `failed:world-disposed` | none | run → `failed`; all pending units `not-run` |
| `CapacityExceededError` `tlas-candidate-budget` / `clearance-narrow-phase-budget` | split (§6.4) or `failed:capacity-exceeded` | via split | continues |
| `CapacityExceededError` other kinds; plain `Error` from linear ceiling | `failed:capacity-exceeded` | none | continues; kind `bucket-capacity` additionally sets `RunOutcome.environmentSensitive` and records `failure.capacityDetails` (§6.4 P1, §11.2) |
| `ExtractionLimitExceededError` | `failed:extraction-limit` | none | continues |
| `ObjectTreeUnavailableError` | `failed:object-tree-unavailable` | none | continues |
| any other error (validation `Error`, `TypeError`, …) | `failed:internal-error` | none | **run → `failed`** (fail-fast: it means the engine built an illegal call; continuing would hide a defect) |

`continueOnUnitFailure` is fixed **true** for the non-fatal rows above; a run never stops on a single unit gap, but never reports a gap as covered (§12).

---

## 8. Run lifecycle

### 8.1 States

```text
                        ┌────────────── cancel ──────────────┐
queued ──► planning ──► running ──► (all units terminal) ──► completed
   │           │           │
   │           │           ├──► cancelling ──► cancelled
   │           │           ├──► invalidated        (input changed under the run)
   └── cancel ─┴───────────┴──► failed             (fatal: world-disposed, internal-error, plan/bind failure, result-cap)
```

Non-terminal: `queued`, `planning`, `running`, `cancelling`. Terminal: `completed`, `cancelled`, `failed`, `invalidated`. Terminal states are absorbing; a Result Set is sealed **exactly once**, at the transition into a terminal state (§13).

### 8.2 Transition rules

| From → To | Trigger |
|---|---|
| `queued → planning` | run reaches the head of the world's queue |
| `planning → running` | bindings resolved, leases held, enumerations done, plan within `maxUnitsPerRun` |
| `queued|planning → failed` | any planning gap of the **exhaustive planning-cause enum** (§6.6.2) other than `cancelled` (which goes to `cancelled`, below) — e.g. `test-unavailable` / `definition-invalid` / `schema-unsupported` / `migration-failed` (stored-reference lookup and validation, first step of `queued`), `model-not-bound`, `enumeration-failed`, `element-unresolvable`, `owner-mapping-mismatch`, `hierarchy-overlap`, `plan-too-large`, `world-disposed`. Sealed with zero units and `coverage: partial`. |
| `running → completed` | every unit reached a terminal unit outcome, no fatal condition occurred, and the end-of-plan input check (§16.3.3) found no input change |
| `running → cancelling` | `cancel()`; effective at the next unit boundary (§10) |
| `cancelling → cancelled` | the in-flight unit (if any) has settled |
| `queued|planning → cancelled` | `cancel()` before a plan exists: **planning is abandoned** (the current slice finishes, no further enumeration) and the run seals with **planning gap `cancelled`**, zero units, zero results, `coverage: partial`. No partial plan is retained (a half-enumerated plan would differ by timing), and a run cancelled before its definition was resolved is sealed in the pre-resolution representation of §13.1 (`definition: null`, `testRef.semanticDigest: null`) — for either `startRun` overload. Once planning has completed and the state is `running`, the ordinary unit-boundary rules of §10.2 apply. |
| `running → invalidated` | input-changed condition (`placementGeneration` change or `scope-drift`; **not** a `bindingGeneration` change or a binding death, which are gaps + `stale`, §16.3.1) detected at a unit boundary (§16.3, §16.7) |
| `running → failed` | `WorldDisposedError`, `failed:internal-error`, or `maxResultsPerRun` exceeded (§17.1) |

**When several conditions coincide** (a cancel with a disposal, reload, scope drift, capacity failure or post-digest failure; or two of those with each other), the terminal status and the retained secondary reasons are decided by the fixed order of §16.7, not by which callback ran first.

`completed` means **the engine finished its plan** — it says nothing about clash-freeness. Read `coverage`, `integrity` and `freshness` (§8.3) before drawing any conclusion.

### 8.3 Run outcome record

```js
/** @typedef {Object} RunOutcome
 *  @property {'completed'|'cancelled'|'failed'|'invalidated'} status
 *  @property {boolean} environmentSensitive      true iff `environmentSensitiveCauses` is non-empty: the ledger and/or the pair set depend on world/renderer state that is not among the run's declared inputs (§1.3.5–6, §6.4 P1, §15.1). Not a coverage/integrity axis; it qualifies the *repeatability* of the run
 *  @property {Array<'bucket-capacity'|'geometry-readiness'|'placement-contention'>} environmentSensitiveCauses   ascending; `bucket-capacity` iff any unit failed with `CapacityExceededError{kind:'bucket-capacity'}`; `placement-contention` iff any unit ended `failed:stale-placement` (foreign placement-revision aborts exhausted `maxPlacementRetries` while the engine's own `placementGeneration` was unchanged, §7.5); `geometry-readiness` iff the exact count of *examined* `geometry-not-yet-available` diagnostics is > 0 or `unclassifiedOccurrences` > 0 (§12.4)
 *  @property {'full'|'partial'} coverage        'full' iff a plan exists, it has no planning-level gap, and every **leaf** unit was `evaluated` (`split` parents are superseded by their leaves; declared exclusions are not gaps for coverage, but `hierarchyInternal`/`unreachablePopulation` still block the claim, §6.6)
 *  @property {'clean'|'caveated'} integrity     'caveated' iff any completeness-affecting diagnostic — **attributable or not** (§12.2.1) — was recorded in evaluated pair-space, or any diagnostic was received but not examined (`unclassifiedOccurrences > 0`, §7.1/§12.4), or `duplicateEvidenceConflict`
 *  @property {'current'|'stale'|'unverifiable'} freshness    (§16.4)
 *  @property {string[]} reasons                 machine-readable, e.g. 'unit:failed:capacity-exceeded', 'cancelled', 'model-evicted:<modelKey>'
 */
```

Unit outcomes: `evaluated`, `split` (superseded by two children, §6.4), `failed:<cause>`, `skipped:<cause>`, `discarded:<cause>`, `not-run:<cause>` where `<cause>` ∈ `model-evicted | world-disposed | stale-placement | capacity-exceeded | extraction-limit | object-tree-unavailable | internal-error | cancelled | result-cap | run-failed | input-changed | scope-drift | scope-unverifiable | unit-cap | target-too-large`. Every unit also carries `scopeProof ∈ {equal, drift, unverifiable, not-checked}` in the ledger (§16.3), and by invariant I-scope only `scopeProof: equal` units can be `evaluated`. `coverage = 'full'` iff a plan was built, there is **no planning-level gap** (§6.6.2), and no leaf unit is anything other than `evaluated` (hence full coverage *implies* demonstrated scope equality). A run that fails or is cancelled before a plan exists is therefore always `partial` — coverage is never vacuously `full`. A completed run over an empty required pair space is `full` with `emptyOperand: true` (§6.6.4).

**The claim rule (D7).** An engine consumer may present "no clashes found" for a test **only if** `status = 'completed' ∧ coverage = 'full' ∧ integrity = 'clean' ∧ freshness = 'current' ∧ pairSpaceTotal > 0 ∧ ¬emptyOperand ∧ unresolvedSelections = ∅ ∧ unreachablePopulation = ∅ ∧ (hierarchyInternal = ∅ ∨ provenance.hierarchyInternalAcceptance ≠ null) ∧ results = ∅` (§6.2, §6.6.3). **`results = ∅` is part of the single normative predicate (R19-2)**, because the claim is the statement "no clashes found": `assessNoClashClaim.allowed` is exactly this whole predicate, so a completed/full/clean/current run that *contains* clashes is **not** `allowed` (it is a valid, fully-covered *positive* result, presented as such), and `unqualified = allowed ∧ hierarchyInternal = ∅`.
