The last conjunct (H4) makes the unaccepted state **fail closed**: a whole-model self-test whose roots contain fragment-owning descendants silently omits real geometric intersections (§5.6.3), so until a product acceptance record is supplied the helper returns `false` for it. The acceptance value is a host-supplied opaque record reference (`{recordRef: string ≤ 512 code units}`, §18) stored in provenance; the engine cannot verify what it points to (residual trust boundary, stated in OQ-16) — it only makes the unaccepted default mechanically claim-blocking and the accepted state visible and durable. **What an acceptance authorizes (R12 Q2, R16-2):** it lets `assessNoClashClaim` return `allowed: true` *with a qualifier* — it does **not** make `canClaimNoClashes` true — and nothing more; the statement it licenses is **"no clashes found, excluding the hierarchy-internal pairs recorded in `ledger.hierarchyInternal`"**, not an unqualified "no clashes". The exclusion stays visible in `provenance.libraryExclusions` (`hierarchy-internal`) and `ledger.hierarchyInternal`, and a consumer that presents the claim must carry that qualifier; the engine cannot enforce presentation wording (a Panel obligation, OQ-16). Anything else is "no clashes found *in the evaluated, uncaveated region*", and the gap/caveat detail must be reachable. This is the library's "empty `pairs` is not a clean negative" rule (F7, F12) lifted to batch scale, and the engine exposes it as a single helper (`canClaimNoClashes(resultSet, currentFreshness)` — the second argument is **required**; it returns `false` when it is absent or not `'current'`, so a caller can never claim from the stale seal-time `outcome.freshness` alone) (its `results = ∅` conjunct is in the predicate itself, above, not an add-on of the boolean helper). **A boolean cannot carry a qualification, so the API is split (R16-2).** `assessNoClashClaim(resultSet, currentFreshness) → {allowed, unqualified, qualifiers, exclusions, proofMethod}` is the full answer: `allowed` is exactly the claim rule above (including the accepted-`hierarchyInternal` branch); `unqualified = allowed ∧ hierarchyInternal = ∅`; `qualifiers` lists every condition the displayed statement must carry — `{kind:'hierarchy-internal-accepted', recordRef, rootCount, ownerCount}` when the acceptance branch was used — and `exclusions` the declared exclusions (`libraryExclusions`); `proofMethod` is `provenance.scopeProof.method` (`'bracketed-digest'`, OQ-19). **`canClaimNoClashes(…)` returns `true` only when `unqualified` is true** — a run whose claim depends on a hierarchy-internal acceptance yields `canClaimNoClashes === false` and `assessNoClashClaim.allowed === true` with a qualifier, so the natural `if (canClaimNoClashes) show("No clashes found")` can never display an unqualified statement for a qualified result; a consumer that wants to present the qualified statement must call `assessNoClashClaim` and carry the `qualifiers`. Both take the required `currentFreshness` (false/`allowed: false` when absent or not `'current'`) and both have engine-bound async forms (`engine.canClaimNoClashes(runId)`, `engine.assessNoClashClaim(runId)`) that recompute freshness. An engine-bound form, `engine.canClaimNoClashes(runId) → Promise<boolean>` is **asynchronous** because it recomputes `getFreshness` (including the scope re-digest, §16.4) and is the recommended entry point; the standalone synchronous form only accepts an already-verified `Freshness` value. Terminal statuses other than `completed` are never claimable, whatever their axes say. **What the claim's scope proof means (R13-1; policy fixed by R20-4):** every `evaluated` unit carries `scopeProof: 'equal'`, which is *bracketed* equality — the live scope equalled the planning snapshot immediately before and immediately after the call, and provenance records `scopeProof.method: 'bracketed-digest'` (§13.2), never `exact`. A scope change that reverts wholly inside one call (an A→B→A excursion) is undetectable by any caller-side mechanism (F18, §16.3 item 5), so the claim is "no clashes found *given bracketed scope equality*". This was decided in round 2 (a per-call population identity would need a collision-core change, §21.2); the residual is **accepted as standing policy** (OQ-19: a non-gating tightening review, not a gate), and the stored enum value keeps the name `equal` because it is a persisted, digested value — consumers must read it through `provenance.scopeProof.method`.


---

## 9. Progress reporting

### 9.1 Snapshot

```js
/** @typedef {Object} RunProgress
 *  @property {string} runId
 *  @property {'queued'|'planning'|'running'|'cancelling'|'completed'|'cancelled'|'failed'|'invalidated'} status
 *  @property {'queued'|'binding'|'enumerating'|'planning'|'executing'|'finalizing'|'terminal'} phase
 *  @property {number} seq                       monotone per run; every emitted event and snapshot carries it
 *  @property {number} unitsTotal                current LEAF units; may GROW (+1 per deterministic capacity split, §6.4); never shrinks; `split` records are not counted
 *  @property {number} unitsEvaluated
 *  @property {number} unitsFailedOrSkipped      failed + skipped + discarded + not-run
 *  @property {number} pairSpaceTotal            Σ pairSpaceWeight over the plan; CONSERVED by splits
 *  @property {number} pairSpaceEvaluated        Σ weight over `evaluated` units
 *  @property {number} pairSpaceProcessed        evaluated + failed/skipped/discarded (weight the run is done with)
 *  @property {number|null} fractionEvaluated   pairSpaceEvaluated / pairSpaceTotal        (0..1, monotone; null while pairSpaceTotal is 0)
 *  @property {number|null} fractionProcessed   pairSpaceProcessed / pairSpaceTotal        (0..1, monotone; reaches 1 on `completed` with a non-empty plan; null while pairSpaceTotal is 0)
 *  @property {number} resultsFound              distinct PairIds retained so far
 *  @property {number} caveatCount               completeness-affecting diagnostics recorded so far (exact over examined diagnostics)
 *  @property {number} caveatUnclassified        diagnostics received but not examined so far (whole-array overflow, §7.1); together with `caveatCount` an upper bound, never exact
 *  @property {string|null} currentUnitId
 *  @property {number} elapsedMs                 wall-clock since run start; NOT part of any digest
 */
```

### 9.2 Rules

1. **Monotone.** `seq`, `unitsEvaluated`, `pairSpaceProcessed`, `pairSpaceEvaluated`, `fractionEvaluated`, `fractionProcessed`, `resultsFound`, `caveatCount` and `caveatUnclassified` never decrease. `unitsTotal` may increase only through a split; because a split *conserves* `pairSpaceTotal`, the fractions never regress when it happens.
2. **Two fractions on purpose.** A progress bar wants `fractionProcessed` (reaches 1 whenever the run finishes its plan, even with gaps); a completeness readout wants `fractionEvaluated` (< 1 whenever any gap exists). The engine does not collapse them.
3. **Weights are estimates of *work*, not of *time*.** `pairSpaceWeight = |sources| × |targets|` in enumerated elements. The engine provides **no ETA**: unit cost is dominated by candidate density (F14) which is unknown until a unit runs, and M4 recorded no multi-process makespan. Consumers must not derive an ETA from these fractions and present it as one.
4. **Delivery.** `getSnapshot()` is always available and never torn (built at unit boundaries and state changes from committed state only). Events are emitted on every state change, on every unit completion (throttled to at most one `progress` event per `progressMinIntervalMs`, provisional 100 ms — state changes and the terminal event are never throttled), and **the terminal event is emitted exactly once**, after the Result Set is sealed and the first persistence attempt has settled **or the `persistTimeoutMs` wait has elapsed** (§13.5.1); a later `'persistence'` event reports the eventual outcome of a timed-out write.
5. **Listener isolation.** A listener exception is caught, counted (`listenerErrors`), and never propagates into the run. A slow listener lengthens main-thread time it consumes; that cost is the subscriber's, and the engine does not bound it.
6. During `queued`/`planning` the snapshot reports `phase` and the counters that exist (e.g. models enumerated of models total); unit counters are 0 until planning completes.

---

## 10. Cancellation

### 10.1 The constraint (F6)

There is no way to stop an in-flight `findClashes`. No `AbortSignal`, token, or deadline exists; checkpoints only poll lifecycle state. `evictModel`/`dispose()` do stop a call (at its next checkpoint) but **destructively**: `evictModel` permanently tombstones that `Model` object (a reload is required to use the model again) and `dispose()` kills the world. Neither may ever be used as a cancel mechanism. The roadmap §4 deliberately leaves cancellation policy to this layer.

### 10.2 Semantics

`cancel(reason?) → Promise<RunSummary>` — **cooperative, at Work-Unit boundaries**:

| Run state when `cancel()` is called | Effect |
|---|---|
| `queued`, `planning` | Immediately (planning is chunked; the current slice finishes) → `cancelled`. **Planning is abandoned**; sealed with planning gap `cancelled`, **no unit ledger**, zero results, `unitsTotal = pairSpaceTotal = 0`, `coverage: partial` (§6.6.2, §8.2). If resolution has not yet finished (a direct definition not yet normalized/digested, or a stored reference not yet looked up) the set uses the **pre-resolution representation** — `definition: null`, `testRef.semanticDigest: null`, only `testId` retained (§13.1); otherwise it embeds the resolved definition. |
| `running`, unit in flight | → `cancelling`. **The in-flight unit is drained, not aborted or discarded**: it is a complete, valid, atomic evaluation (F7), so its result is merged. No further unit starts. Then → `cancelled`. |
| `running`, between units | → `cancelled` at once (no unit in flight). |
| `cancelling` | Idempotent; the first reason wins. |
| terminal | No-op; resolves with the existing summary. |

- **Cancel latency ≤ one unit's duration** (+ one slice) *to the `cancelled` state and seal*; the `cancel()` promise additionally follows §13.5.1 and may resolve up to `persistTimeoutMs` later. The library bounds neither a single call's duration (F14: one `clearanceQuery` non-preemptible span alone reached ~20 ms; a whole call is far longer) nor makespan, so the *only* lever is `maxSourceFragmentsPerUnit` (§6.4). The engine records `cancelRequestedAt`/`cancelSettledAt` in run telemetry so the latency can be characterized (AC-12) instead of promised.
- **Partial results are retained** (policy decision, roadmap "reject vs partial vs discard"): a cancelled run seals its evaluated results with `status: 'cancelled'`, `coverage: 'partial'`, and every unstarted unit in the (already-built) ledger as `not-run:cancelled`. Retaining is chosen because evaluated units are individually complete and the ledger makes their scope exact; **discard is a separate, explicit, host-initiated operation** (`discardRun`, §13.5.2), never a side effect of cancel; a cancelled run's sealed set is an ordinary resident set (`held` until persisted).
- **Cancel never** evicts, disposes, invalidates placement, or clears library caches.
- **Precedence.** Once a run has *sealed*, that terminal status is absorbing (first seal wins). `cancelling` is **not** terminal, so a requested cancel does not pre-empt anything: when a settling unit or a boundary shows several facts at once, the status is chosen by the fixed order of §16.7 (`failed` > `invalidated` > `cancelled`), and the cancel request is kept as a secondary reason. `freshness` is computed independently either way (§16.4).
- **`engine.dispose()`** cancels every active and queued run (each sealed as `cancelled`, reason `engine-disposed`, **unless a higher-precedence fact of §16.7 is observed at the same boundary — world disposal, a fatal executor error or result-cap ⇒ `failed`, input drift ⇒ `invalidated`**), releases listeners and resident sets, and does not touch persisted results. `dispose()` is **asynchronous**: it synchronously marks the engine disposing (every other method now fails with `EngineDisposedError` — **thrown** from the synchronous entry points, delivered as a **rejection** from the Promise-returning ones — and the world coordinator stops admitting its runs), requests cancellation of every run, and returns a `Promise<DisposeSummary>` that resolves only **after every run has drained its in-flight unit (§10.2) and sealed (as `cancelled`, or per §16.7)** — so the summary is final: `{ droppedUnsaved: Array<{runId, resultSet}>, persistenceInFlight: runId[] }` (the unsaved frozen sets are **handed back**, §13.5.2). For a run still in its `finalizing` window (§13.5.1 item 6) — sealed, persistence write pending, `persistTimeoutMs` not yet elapsed — `dispose()` **forces the window closed** as part of the drain: it settles `done`/`cancel()` and emits the run's exactly-once terminal `state` event **before** it releases listeners, carrying `{state: 'pending', timedOut: true}` (or the final state if the write has already settled), so the terminal-event contract holds even under disposal and no listener is released early. It does not wait for pending persistence writes (they continue detached); it never throws and never drops unsaved sets silently (§13.5.2). Repeated calls return the same promise.

### 10.3 Optional future library seam (not required)

An additive `signal` option on `findClashes` (checked at existing checkpoints) would reduce cancel latency from "one unit" to "one atomic span". It is **not** needed for correctness and is **not** part of this milestone; if ever proposed, it is a collision-core change subject to roadmap §8 (§21). This spec's design is correct with or without it.

---

## 11. Results

### 11.1 `ClashResult` (plain data)

```js
/** @typedef {Object} ClashResult
 *  @property {string} resultId                      canonical string of (testId, semanticDigest, PairId)  (§4.4)
 *  @property {{first: ElementRef, second: ElementRef}} pair
 *  @property {'volume-overlap'|'surface-intersection'|'containment'|'clearance'} mode
 *  @property {{source: ElementRef, target: ElementRef}} evaluatedAs   library orientation the retained evidence came from
 *  @property {Evidence} evidence
 *  @property {{witnessUnavailable?: true, duplicateEvidenceConflict?: true, unreachableOwner?: true, mixedOwnerProvenance?: true}} flags   `unreachableOwner`: an owner of the pair is *unreachable-only*; `mixedOwnerProvenance`: an owner is *mixed* (§6.2 owner classes). Both derive from the recorded plan data, never from the producing unit
 *  @property {string} unitId                        the unit that produced the retained evidence
 */
/** @typedef {{witness: {kind:'surface-crossing'|'containment', point:[number,number,number]} | null}   // volume-overlap
 *          | {distance:number, clearance:number, closestPoints:{first:[number,number,number], second:[number,number,number]}} // clearance
 *          | {}                                                                                      // surface-intersection, containment
 *          } Evidence
 */
```

- **Evidence is orientation-independent in shape.** The library's `clearance` witness is `{sourcePoint, targetPoint}` where `sourcePoint` lies on the library-source operand (README M3). The engine re-keys it to `closestPoints.first/second` using `evaluatedAs`, so the same physical pair yields the same evidence *shape and meaning* whichever element the planner labeled source. Hard-Clash `witness.point` is a single world-space point and needs no re-keying. **Consumers must branch on `mode`**, never assume one witness shape (a hazard already hit once, live, in M3 acceptance).
- Coordinates are exactly the library's Viewer scene/world coordinates; the engine performs no unit or frame conversion (the same trust boundary M2/M3 already rely on).
- `witnessUnavailable: true` mirrors the library's `witness-unavailable` case: the pair verdict is complete, only the point is missing (F12).
- Live `Model` references, `modelId`, `epoch`, `fragmentId` and `geometryIndex` **never** appear in a `ClashResult`.
- **`mode` is the sole persisted discriminator; there is no `type` field.** `ClashResult` is a **closed** persisted type (§13.6), so the executor's per-pair `UnitOutcome.pairs[].type` (§11.2, library orientation) is **projected into `ClashResult.mode`** when the engine merges a unit's pairs, and every stored-set validation, claim derivation and acceptance criterion reads `result.mode` — never `result.type`.

### 11.2 `UnitOutcome` (the executor's plain-data return)

```js
/** @typedef {Object} UnitOutcome
 *  @property {string} unitId
 *  @property {number} attempt
 *  @property {'evaluated'|'failed'|'skipped'|'discarded'} outcome
 *  @property {'equal'|'drift'|'unverifiable'|'not-checked'} scopeProof   §16.3.2; `evaluated` requires `equal` (invariant I-scope)
 *  @property {null | {code: string,                    // a §8.3 cause
 *                     errorName?: string, message?: string,
 *                     capacityKind?: string,           // CapacityExceededError.kind, so the engine can decide split vs gap (§6.4)
 *                     capacityDetails?: {currentSize?: number, missing?: number, requested?: number, limit?: number},   // numeric fields copied from the error's details; for `bucket-capacity` this is the recorded environmental evidence (§6.4 P1)
 *                     modelKeys?: string[],            // affected models (drift/eviction/freshness reasons)
 *                     retryable?: boolean}} failure    null iff outcome === 'evaluated'
 *  @property {{start: Array<{modelKey: string, bindingGeneration: number, placementGeneration: number}>, end: Array<{modelKey: string, bindingGeneration: number, placementGeneration: number}>}} generations   read at attempt start/end (§16.3.1)
 *  @property {Array<{source: ElementRef, target: ElementRef, type: string, witness?: object, distance?: number, clearance?: number}>} pairs   // library orientation, projected
 *  @property {ProjectedDiagnostic[]} diagnostics   per-call, pre-aggregation (below); NOT `NormalizedDiagnostic` (§12.2), which only the engine can build
 *  @property {number} [pairsOverflowLength]        present iff the library returned more than `request.limits.maxPairsPerOutcome` pairs: then `pairs` is `[]` (§7.1)
 *  @property {number} [diagnosticOverflowLength]   present iff the library returned more than `request.limits.maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome` diagnostics: then `diagnostics` is `[]` (§7.1)
 *  @property {Record<string, number>} statistics       numeric library statistics, unit-local
 *  @property {number} durationMs
 */
/** @typedef {Object} ProjectedDiagnostic     what ONE `findClashes` call yields, projected by the executor
 *  @property {'fragment'|'pair'} scope
 *  @property {string} reason
 *  @property {boolean} completenessAffecting       from isCompletenessAffecting(reason) (§12.3)
 *  @property {'element'|'target-model'|'unit'} attribution   attributionOf(diagnostic, unitBindingTable) (§12.2.1) — structural, computable per unit
 *  @property {{modelKey: string|null, dbId: number|null, fragId: number, role?: 'source'|'target'}} [fragment]   // scope 'fragment'
 *  @property {{first: object, second: object}} [fragments]                                                     // scope 'pair'
 *  @property {boolean} [ambiguousModelId]
 */
```

**The two diagnostic DTOs are distinct and neither is optional (R21-C2).** `ProjectedDiagnostic` is the **per-unit, pre-aggregation** shape an executor returns; `NormalizedDiagnostic` (§12.2) is the **stored, post-aggregation** shape and adds exactly the two cross-unit fields (`occurrences`, `unitIds`) that only the engine can compute. The projection is the identity on the shared fields: the engine copies them verbatim and adds the two aggregation fields during cross-unit de-duplication (§12.4). An executor that sets `occurrences` or `unitIds` returns an unknown field and its outcome is malformed (`failed:internal-error`, §7.1); `validateUnitOutcome` therefore checks diagnostics against `ProjectedDiagnostic`, never against `NormalizedDiagnostic`.

The executor performs the `modelId → modelKey` mapping for diagnostics from the unit's own binding table (F12: `modelId` is not unique). Within one unit all participating models are simultaneously live and distinct; if two participating models nevertheless report the same `modelId`, the diagnostic's `modelKey` is `null` and it carries `ambiguousModelId: true` — never a guess — and its `attribution` is `unit` (§12.2.1). The executor preserves the library's `dbId: null` verbatim and returns each unit's diagnostics **before** any de-duplication so the engine can build the per-unit `taints` table.

### 11.3 Canonical ordering and determinism of the set

Results are stored **sorted ascending by `(pair.first.modelKey, pair.first.dbId, pair.second.modelKey, pair.second.dbId)`**, a total order independent of library insertion order, broad-phase choice (TLAS/linear order differs — F3), unit boundaries and execution order. Sorting/merging is done in bounded slices (§17.3).

### 11.4 Reconciliation rules

1. **Unit-local aggregation is the library's.** A source *root* lives in exactly one source chunk of its cell, and **no root — source or target — is ever split by the engine** (target slicing cuts only *between* roots, §6.4); every fragment of a source root and of a target root is therefore inside one unit for each (source chunk, target slice) pair that contains them. Every **owner-level** pair (whose owners lie inside a source root and a target root) is thus evaluated entirely within that one unit, so the library's deterministic `dbId`-level winner selection (witness/`distance` tie-break by `fragmentId`, F3) is unaffected by unit sizing or splitting.
2. **Cross-unit duplicates.** By cell construction over subtree-disjoint roots (§5.6) each required pair is scheduled exactly once, so duplicates are not expected; the rule below is a defensive backstop that also covers a future planner substitution (OQ-5). Rule: keep the record whose `evaluatedAs.source` equals `pair.first` (canonical orientation), else the lowest `unitId` in plan order. If the duplicate records **disagree on hit/miss** (impossible for the deterministic modes, possible for the time-sensitive ones), keep the positive, set `flags.duplicateEvidenceConflict`, and count it as an integrity caveat.
3. **Rank-6 residue (F11).** For `volume-overlap`, a pair that ties through geometry rank 5 (including byte-identical geometry) resolves by the caller's source label. The engine's source/target role assignment for each pair is a **deterministic function of the definition and the sorted enumerations only** (cell rules, §6.3) and never of sizing options, so verdicts remain repeatable; they may differ from the verdict a *different* role assignment would give, and the engine does not attempt to detect or arbitrate that. Invariant **P2** (§15): *verdicts are independent of unit sizing, chunking, capacity splitting, execution order, and broad-phase choice; at the rank-6 residue they additionally depend on the definition-derived role assignment.*
4. A pair present in no unit's `pairs` is **absent**, not "negative" — its status is read from coverage and caveats (§12).

---

## 12. Diagnostics and completeness at batch scale

### 12.1 What must not change

`findClashes` distinguishes *thrown* (whole call fails, no partial result) from *diagnosed* (call resolves; the answer may be incomplete). An empty `pairs` is not "no clash". The batch layer preserves both distinctions and adds the third the library cannot: **a unit that never produced an answer at all** (a gap).

### 12.2 `NormalizedDiagnostic`

This is the **stored, post-aggregation** diagnostic: the `ProjectedDiagnostic` fields of §11.2 copied verbatim from the producing unit, **plus** the two engine-assigned cross-unit fields (`occurrences`, `unitIds`). It is never a `UnitExecutor` return shape.

```js
/** @typedef {Object} NormalizedDiagnostic
 *  @property {'fragment'|'pair'} scope
 *  @property {string} reason
 *  @property {boolean} completenessAffecting       from isCompletenessAffecting(reason)
 *  @property {'element'|'target-model'|'unit'} attribution   how far the diagnostic can be pinned down (§12.2.1) — derived structurally, never defaulted upward
 *  @property {{modelKey: string|null, dbId: number|null, fragId: number, role?: 'source'|'target'}} [fragment]   // scope 'fragment'; dbId is NULL for unattributable diagnostics
 *  @property {{first: object, second: object}} [fragments]                                                    // scope 'pair': each {modelKey|null, dbId|null, fragId}
 *  @property {boolean} [ambiguousModelId]
 *  @property {number} occurrences                  across all units, after de-duplication
 *  @property {string[]} unitIds                    first `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic` (provisional 8) units it appeared in
 */
```

`fragId` is retained as **diagnostic detail only** (session-local; empirically stable on identical-model reloads, not contractual). Durable element attribution uses `(modelKey, dbId)` — for an attributed diagnostic the `dbId` is the fragment's `ownerDbId`.

#### 12.2.1 Attribution classes (unattributable diagnostics are first-class)

The library emits two reasons whose `fragment.dbId` is **always `null`**, both only from whole-model *target* extraction (`extractAllFragments`, F17): `fragment-not-loaded` (checked before any owner lookup) and `fragment-owner-unresolvable` (`fragList.getDbIds` returned `undefined`, or an array for a 2D-style model). In both cases the fragment is **dropped** — no record is created, so it never becomes a candidate, and any clash it would have been part of is silently not found; the diagnostic is the only signal. The model of §12 therefore must not assume a numeric `dbId`. Every diagnostic is classified by a pure function `attributionOf(diagnostic, unitBindingTable)`:

| `attribution` | When (structural test; a diagnostic is `element` only if it passes the `element` test, else `target-model` if it passes that, else `unit`) | Taint scope (§12.5) |
|---|---|---|
| `element` | every fragment operand the diagnostic names has a numeric `dbId` **and** a resolved `modelKey` | the named owner element(s) and, for `scope:'pair'`, that fragment pair |
| `target-model` | `scope:'fragment'`, `role:'target'`, `dbId` is `null`, `modelKey` resolved (i.e. today's two whole-model reasons) | **every required pair whose covering unit's target side includes that `modelKey` as a whole-model scope** — the dropped fragment could belong to any root of that model and pair with any of the unit's sources |
| `unit` | anything else that lacks a numeric `dbId` or a resolved `modelKey` (ambiguous `modelId`, an unrecognized shape, a non-null-but-non-numeric `dbId`) | **every required pair of the unit that reported it** |

The test is on *shape*, not on a reason whitelist, so a future library diagnostic that lacks attribution degrades to `unit` (fail-safe), never to "harmless". Unattributable diagnostics are never dropped or de-numbered into `dbId: 0`/`-1` sentinels: `dbId` stays `null`.

**Taint records are exact and separate from the retained-diagnostic cap.** For every unit the engine records, from that unit's own diagnostics *before* any cross-unit de-duplication or cap, a `taints` table in the ledger with **three** entry kinds, all exact and independent of the global de-duplicated list: `{kind: 'target-model'|'unit', modelKey: string|null, reason, occurrences}` (keyed by `(kind, modelKey, reason)`; tiny) and `{kind: 'element', modelKey, dbId, reason, occurrences}` — one entry per *implicated owner element* per reason (a `scope:'pair'` diagnostic implicates **both** of its owner elements; the engine deliberately taints at owner-element granularity rather than fragment-pair granularity so the record stays small and conservative). Element entries are bounded per unit by `maxElementTaintsPerUnit` (provisional 2,000); when a unit would exceed it — **or when the run's `maxLedgerRecords` discretionary capacity cannot admit an entry of *any* taint kind (element, `target-model` or `unit`; exhaustion contract, §6.5.1)** — the surplus is **not dropped** but collapsed into one `{kind: 'unit', reason: 'taint-overflow'}` entry, stored in a slot reserved for it at the end of planning so it always fits (fail-safe: the whole unit becomes tainted). Retained-diagnostic truncation (§12.4) and the `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic` unit-id cap can therefore never lose a taint. `ledgerDigest` covers the `taints` tables.

### 12.3 The completeness predicate — one place, fail-safe

```text
isCompletenessAffecting(reason) = (reason !== 'witness-unavailable')
```

That single carve-out is the library's documented one (README M2.1): a `volume-overlap` verdict is fully determined before witness capture, so a missing marker point leaves the pair complete. **Every other reason counts**, including all four `clearance-*` reasons (for clearance "the distance is the verdict", so the M2.1 precedent deliberately does not transfer), and **any reason the engine does not recognize** (a future library diagnostic must default to "may be incomplete", never "harmless"). Known reasons today (informative, verified as literals in `collision/`): `fragment-not-loaded` (**unattributable**, `dbId: null`), `fragment-owner-unresolvable` (**unattributable**, `dbId: null`), `geometry-not-yet-available`, `unsupported-geometry-layout`, `rejected-primitive-range`, `geometry-too-large-for-sync-build`, `geometry-aspect-ratio-exceeds-tolerance-model`, `index-references-invalid-vertex`, `crossing-budget-exhausted`, `inconclusive-crossing`, `inconclusive-containment`, `incomplete-component-coverage`, `non-manifold-component`, `unverified-open-component`, `unverified-parity-operand`, `clearance-invalid-transform`, `clearance-anisotropic-scale-unsupported`, `clearance-hard-clash-precheck-inconclusive`, `clearance-distance-unavailable`, and `witness-unavailable` (the one non-affecting reason). The predicate, not this list, is normative; the plan adds a test that fails when `collision/` gains a `reason:` literal the engine's known-reason table lacks (so a new reason is *consciously* classified rather than silently defaulted).

### 12.4 De-duplication, caps, and what stays exact

At batch scale one run can emit orders of magnitude more diagnostics than one query (R1 alone: ~1,709 per single-source query — F12). Therefore:

- **De-duplicate across units**: fragment scope by `(reason, modelKey, fragId)` (a `null`-`dbId` diagnostic is keyed the same way, `fragId` being its only handle); pair scope by `(reason, canonical pair of (modelKey, fragId))`; a diagnostic with a `null` `modelKey` is never merged with one from another unit (its `unit`-attribution is per unit). `occurrences` and the first `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic` unit ids are kept. De-duplication is a *presentation* reduction only: taint is computed per unit beforehand (§12.2.1).
- **Cap retained detail** at `maxRetainedDiagnostics` (provisional 10,000). Beyond it, detail is dropped in **canonical order** (so *which* are kept is deterministic) and `diagnostics.truncated = true`.
- **Exactness, precisely (H7, M8).** *Totals are never capped, and each count says what it counts:* `diagnostics.total` is the exact number of diagnostics received (for a whole-array overflow, its exact `length`). `completenessAffectingCount` is the exact number of **examined** diagnostics for which `isCompletenessAffecting` is true. `unclassifiedOccurrences` is the exact number of diagnostics that were received but **not examined** (whole-array overflow, §7.1) — their classification is unknown. `completenessAffectingUpperBound = completenessAffectingCount + unclassifiedOccurrences` is the only figure that covers them, and it is an *upper bound*, never called exact. **`integrity` is computed from the upper bound** (`caveated` iff it is > 0, or `duplicateEvidenceConflict`), never from the retained list. *Per-reason counts are exact only for retained reasons:* `countsByReason[(scope, reason)]` holds at most `maxDistinctReasons` keys per run, admitted in plan order (unit order, then code-unit order of the keys new to that unit, so admission is deterministic); every occurrence of a key that could not be admitted is added to the run's single `omittedReasonOccurrences` counter. Invariant: `Σ countsByReason + omittedReasonOccurrences + unclassifiedOccurrences = total`. No claim is made about the number of distinct omitted reasons. **Dedicated exact counter:** independent of `countsByReason` retention, the engine keeps one exact run-level count of *examined* `geometry-not-yet-available` occurrences (`diagnostics.geometryReadinessOccurrences`). The `geometry-readiness` cause of `environmentSensitive` is set iff `geometryReadinessOccurrences > 0` **or** `unclassifiedOccurrences > 0` (an unexamined array could hide that reason; fail-safe, and stated as possible rather than certain).
- **Diagnostics are per-call facts, not per-pair facts.** Counting them is not counting affected pairs (README "Diagnostics"): a fragment-scoped diagnostic (e.g. `clearance-invalid-transform`) fires before any pair exists. The engine reports diagnostic counts and, separately, the set of *elements* implicated **plus the unit-level taint tables for diagnostics that implicate no element** (§12.2.1) — an empty implicated-element set with a non-empty taint table is *not* "nothing affected".

### 12.5 The three axes and the element-level view

| Axis | Values | Derived from |
|---|---|---|
| `coverage` | `full` / `partial` | ledger: a plan exists, no planning-level gap, every leaf unit `evaluated` (`split` records excluded, §6.4) (declared exclusions are not gaps, §6.6) |
| `integrity` | `clean` / `caveated` | `completenessAffectingUpperBound > 0` (exact examined count + unexamined overflow, §12.4) or `duplicateEvidenceConflict` |
| `freshness` | `current` / `stale` / `unverifiable` | §16.4 |

Statement matrix (what a consumer may claim):

| coverage | integrity | freshness | May claim |
|---|---|---|---|
| full, `status: completed`, non-empty required space, `¬emptyOperand`, `unresolvedSelections = ∅`, `unreachablePopulation = ∅`, (`hierarchyInternal = ∅` or acceptance recorded) | clean | current | "No clashes found" (when `results` is empty) / "Exactly these clashes" |
| any, with `unresolvedSelections ≠ ∅` or `unreachablePopulation ≠ ∅` | any | any | **No** "no clashes" claim; results and absences are valid only outside the unresolved/unreachable elements (a *mixed* owner is in neither set: its pairs are `covered-caveated`, taint rule 6) |
| any, with `hierarchyInternal ≠ ∅` and **no** acceptance record | any | any | **No** "no clashes" claim; parent/descendant (and sibling-under-root) owner pairs inside the listed roots were never evaluated (§5.6.3). Results found remain valid positives |
| full | caveated | current | Results are correct as reported; **absences are not proven** for any pair `getPairStatus` reports `covered-caveated` (element-attributed caveats, or `target-model`/`unit` taints of the covering unit, §12.5) |
| partial (incl. any planning-level gap) | any | any | Results are correct for evaluated pair-space only; absences inside gaps are **unknown** |
| any status other than `completed`, or empty required space | any | any | **No** "no clashes" claim, ever |
| any | any | stale / unverifiable | Results describe a **previous** model/placement state |

**Pair level (required by comparison, §15.4):** `getPairStatus(resultSet, pairId) → { required, coveringUnitId, unitOutcome, roots: {first, second}, caveats: {reason, count, attribution}[], verdict: 'covered-clean' | 'covered-caveated' | 'gap' | 'not-required' | 'undetermined' }`. It checks that the pair's **roots** (via the ledger's `rootOwners` table) were actually paired in an evaluated unit, then applies the **taint rule**, reports `not-required` for a result whose owner is **unreachable-only** (§6.2 owner classes: absent from the root-owner population, outside the required pair space) and, with reason `hierarchy-internal`, for a pair whose two owners map to the same root (§6.6.3); a pair with a **mixed** owner is *required* (its reachable fragments are in `P`) and is handled by taint rule 6 below, never `not-required`; and reports `undetermined` only when an owner dbId is absent from the ledger's `rootOwners` table (a defensive case: a set produced by a foreign/older writer, or an owner outside every planned root). Element-level status alone can never establish that a *pair* was asked about.

**Taint rule (normative).** Let `u` be the pair's covering unit (unique: each required pair is scheduled exactly once, §6.3) and `T` the model of the pair's *target-side* operand in `u`'s orientation. The verdict is `covered-clean` **only if none** of these holds; if any holds it is `covered-caveated` and `caveats` lists them:
1. `u`'s `taints` table (§12.2.1) has an `element` entry for either of the pair's owner elements **whose `reason` is completeness-affecting** (`isCompletenessAffecting(reason)` is true, §12.3) — the one benign reason, `witness-unavailable`, therefore does not by itself downgrade `covered-clean`, although such an entry is still listed in `caveats` as retained evidence. **The filter is verdict-only:** the per-unit `taints` table of §12.2.1 stays **unfiltered** (it is the evidence base for the ledger and for the other rules, and nothing is dropped from it). This rule reads that exact per-unit table, **never** the capped/de-duplicated retained-diagnostic list or its `unitIds`;
2. `u`'s `taints` table (§12.2.1) has a `target-model` entry for `modelKey = T` **and** `u`'s target scope for `T` was a whole-model scope (an unattributable dropped target fragment can only matter to a whole-model target, and *always* matters to every source of that unit);
3. `u`'s `taints` table has any `unit` entry;
4. the run's `duplicateEvidenceConflict` flag is set for this pair;
5. the Result Set's `outcome.freshness` at seal was not `current` (a mixed/stale-input run never yields clean absences; §16.4);
6. **(H1) either owner of the pair is a *mixed* owner** (§6.2: it owns both tree-reachable and tree-unreachable fragments, recorded in the ledger's `unreachablePopulation[].mixedOwnerDbIds`), regardless of which owner is on the source side, which is on the target side, or which unit covered the pair. The library's owner-level result cannot show which fragment of a mixed owner produced — or failed to produce — a pair, and the unreachable fragments were not evaluated as a source, so no absence involving that owner can be proven clean. `caveats` lists `mixed-owner-provenance` with attribution `element`.
7. **(R12-1) the model of *either* operand has `unattributable.notLoaded + unattributable.ownerUnresolvable > 0` in the ledger's `unreachablePopulation`** (§6.2) — regardless of orientation, unit or which side the model was on. Such fragments have no owner, so no pair can be singled out; every pair involving that model is `covered-caveated` with `caveats` listing `unattributable-unreachable-population` (attribution `target-model`). Like rule 6 this only weakens *absences*; reported clashes stay valid positives.

Rules 2–3 and 7 make unattributable evidence taint *coverage-level* claims rather than disappear for lack of an element: a `fragment-not-loaded` diagnostic in a whole-model target scope makes **every** pair between that unit's sources and that model `covered-caveated`, whatever the element attribution of the rest of the diagnostics. Clashes actually reported in `results` remain valid positives — caveats only weaken *absences*.

`getElementStatus(resultSet, elementRef) → { inRequiredPairSpace, coverage: 'full'|'partial'|'none', caveats: {reason, count, attribution}[], gapCauses: string[] }` is the foundation for a per-element "clean/unknown" indication; it is derived from the ledger (element ∈ a non-evaluated unit's source set or target scope), the `element`-attributed diagnostics' `dbId`s, **and the same taint tables**: an element in the source roots of a unit with any `unit` taint, or in the target scope of a whole-model target with a `target-model` taint, reports those caveats (with their `attribution`) rather than an empty list; a **mixed** owner always reports `mixed-owner-provenance`, and an owner inside a `hierarchyInternal` root reports `hierarchy-internal` (its owner-to-owner pairs within the root are unevaluated). The Panel decides how to present it.

### 12.6 Library statistics

Per-call `statistics` are aggregated per run as *sums of count fields*, `libraryMsTotal` (sum of in-call wall-clock), and `maxUnitMs`. They are informational and **excluded from every digest** (timings are wall-clock and load-dependent). TLAS-specific fields are zero under `'linear'` and are not comparable across broad-phase choices except `broadPhasePairs`/`narrowPhasePairs` (M2 §7.5); `tlasBuildMs` counts only builds a call itself initiated, so summing across units under-attributes shared builds. `blasBuilt`/`blasCacheHits` are currently always `0` in the library (README) and are not reported as facts.

---

## 13. Durable Result Set

### 13.1 Structure

```js
/** @typedef {Object} ResultSet
 *  @property {1} schemaVersion
 *  @property {string} runId
 *  @property {{testId: string, revision: number|null, semanticDigest: string|null}} testRef   `revision`/`semanticDigest` are `null` exactly for a run **sealed before its definition was resolved** — either `startRun` overload, either terminal status (`failed` or `cancelled`), see *Pre-resolution seal* below
 *  @property {ClashTestDefinition|null} definition          full embedded copy (self-describing); `null` iff `testRef.semanticDigest` is `null` (pre-resolution seal)
 *  @property {string|null} derivedFromRunId                 set by rerun (§15.3)
 *  @property {RunOutcome} outcome                           freshness here is the value AT SEAL time
 *  @property {Provenance} provenance
 *  @property {Ledger} ledger                                units (outcome, cause, `scopeProof`, exact per-unit `taints` table with `element`/`target-model`/`unit` entries), `rootOwners` (rows `{rootDbId, ownerDbIds, cls}`, §5.6 item 2), `unresolvedSelections`, `unreachablePopulation` (incl. `mixedOwnerDbIds`), `hierarchyInternal`, planning gaps, splits, gaps, declared exclusions, empty-operand warnings, `budgetExhausted` and first-refusal unit id (§6.5.1)
 *  @property {ClashResult[]} results                        canonical order (§11.3)
 *  @property {{retained: NormalizedDiagnostic[], countsByReason: object, total: number, completenessAffectingCount: number, omittedReasonOccurrences: number, unclassifiedOccurrences: number, completenessAffectingUpperBound: number, geometryReadinessOccurrences: number, truncated: boolean}} diagnostics   `total` exact; `completenessAffectingCount` exact over *examined* diagnostics; `unclassifiedOccurrences` exact count of unexamined ones; `completenessAffectingUpperBound = completenessAffectingCount + unclassifiedOccurrences` (an upper bound, not exact); `countsByReason` ≤ `maxDistinctReasons` keys, exact for those keys only; `Σ countsByReason + omittedReasonOccurrences + unclassifiedOccurrences = total` (§12.4)
 *  @property {object} statistics                            aggregated, informational (§12.6)
 *  @property {{digestVersion: 1, pairSetDigest: string, ledgerDigest: string}} digests   "sha256:<hex>" over canonical JSON (§5.4)
 *  @property {true} sealed
 */
```

- **Pre-resolution seal (R11-4).** *Resolution* is complete when the definition has been obtained (stored-reference lookup, migration and `validateTest` of the returned value, §5.5), normalized and `semanticDigest`-ed — the last step of the first sliced work of `queued` (§5.3). A run that seals **before** that point, for any reason — a stored-reference lookup/validation/migration failure or `test-deleted`/`test-lookup-failed` (§5.5), or `cancel()` (or a `dispose`) arriving while the run is `queued`/`planning` and resolution has not finished (which abandons the remaining resolution work, §8.2, §10.2) — has one representation, the same for both `startRun` overloads: `definition: null` and `testRef = {testId, revision: null, semanticDigest: null}`, where `testId` is the only caller-supplied identity retained (the reference's `testId`, or the `testId` of the *captured* direct definition, §5.3; the un-normalized copy itself is discarded, never embedded, so nothing unnormalized or undigested is persisted). It has planning gap `<cause>` (`cancelled` for a cancel), **no** unit ledger, zero results, `coverage: 'partial'`, and its `ledgerDigest` covers the planning gap alone (§6.6.2); `RunOutcome.reasons` carries `planning:<cause>`. A run that seals **after** resolution (for example a cancel during enumeration/planning) embeds the resolved definition and its `semanticDigest`, per the ordinary rule. Such a pre-resolution set has no question to rerun: `rerun` of it without a `definitionRevision` **rejects** its returned Promise with `InvalidOptionsError('no-embedded-definition')` (§15.3, R21-1); with one it starts a normal stored-reference lookup.
- `pairSetDigest`: digest (format §5.4) over the canonical ordered list of `(resultId, flags)` — **exact discrete content only**; equal digests establish identical pair membership and flags **and nothing about float evidence** (§15.4).
- `ledgerDigest`: digest over the deterministic ledger content (unit ids, source/target sets, outcome kinds, causes, per-unit `scopeProof` and `taints` tables, the `rootOwners` table — **every row's `rootDbId`, `ownerDbIds` and `cls`** (§5.6 item 2) — the `unreachablePopulation` and `hierarchyInternal` tables, planning gaps, and the sizing options) — **excluding** timings, attempt counts, wall-clock, and `provenance.admissionBaseline`. A ledger containing a `bucket-capacity` or `stale-placement` gap is `environmentSensitive`: equal `ledgerDigest`s are still meaningful, but *unequal* digests do not indicate a defect when either run is `environmentSensitive` (§15.1).
- Floating-point evidence (witness points, distances) is **not** digested: cross-machine bit-equality of float evidence is not something the library promises, so the diff (§15.4) compares it by tolerance instead.

### 13.2 Provenance

```js
/** @typedef {Object} Provenance
 *  @property {string} registrySessionId                 nonce of the WORLD-SCOPED registry (§4.3) the run bound against; any engine over the same world shares it (freshness, §16.4)
 *  @property {string|null} libraryVersion             host-supplied; null ⇒ cross-build repeatability is not asserted (§1.3)
 *  @property {'tlas'|'linear'} broadPhase
 *  @property {'deterministic'|'time-sensitive'} determinismClass   from the mode (§15.1)
 *  @property {object} runOptions                      sizing options (folded into ledgerDigest)
 *  @property {Array<{modelKey: string, modelVersion: string, scopeKinds: ('model'|'elements')[],
 *                    elementCount: number,
 *                    scopeDigests: { population: {count: number, digest: string}|null,
 *                                    roots: Array<{dbId: number, subtreeDigest: string}> },   // plan-time, §6.2.1
 *                    bindingGeneration: number, placementGeneration: number}>} models
 *  @property {{method: 'bracketed-digest', digestVersion: 1}} scopeProof   how executed-scope equality with the planning snapshot was demonstrated per unit (§16.3); never 'exact'
 *  @property {Array<{modelKey: string, epochInstanceCount: number}>} admissionBaseline   the collision world's per-epoch instance-bucket size for each touched model **at the moment leases are taken** (`getEpochCollisionInstanceCount`): an observational witness for epoch-cache-dependent outcomes (§1.3.5, §6.4 P1) — a **count only**, so it does not identify cache membership and is not sufficient to reproduce a capacity outcome (§15.1). Recorded, **not digested**; `epoch` itself never appears (§16.5)
 *  @property {null | {recordRef: string}} hierarchyInternalAcceptance   host-supplied product-acceptance reference (§8.3, OQ-16); `null` ⇒ unaccepted ⇒ a non-empty `ledger.hierarchyInternal` blocks the no-clash claim
 *  @property {string[]} libraryExclusions             e.g. ['assembly-rooted-self-exclusion', 'hierarchy-internal' (iff `ledger.hierarchyInternal` non-empty), 'tree-unreachable-population' (iff any)]
 *  @property {false} placementVerified                the engine never reads or hashes model placement transforms
 *  @property {{viewerVersion?: string, userAgent?: string}} [environment]   host-supplied; not digested
 *  @property {string} startedAt                       ISO 8601 wall-clock; not digested
 *  @property {string} finishedAt                      ISO 8601 wall-clock; not digested
 */
```

`placementVerified: false` is deliberate and honest: model placement (federation alignment) affects every geometric verdict, the library cannot verify it (M2 §6.5), and this layer records only a session-local generation counter. A Result Set loaded in a later session therefore has `freshness: 'unverifiable'` for placement (§16.4).

### 13.3 Immutability and sealing

- A Result Set is **sealed exactly once**, at the transition into a terminal state, and is immutable afterwards — **enforced, not merely promised**: sealing includes a *deep freeze* (performed in the sliced main-thread discipline, §17.3) of the set and of every definition the engine stores or exposes, so `done`, `getResultSet`, `getTest` and every store input/output are frozen objects (a caller mutation throws in strict mode / is a no-op and can never change digests, coverage or persisted content). **Ownership at each boundary:** inbound values are captured as detached copies before any asynchronous work (§5.3, R11-1); a `ResultStore`/`ClashTestStore` receives frozen objects and **must not mutate them and must copy on write and on read** (the in-memory reference implementation holds each set as its **canonical JSON string** and parses a fresh copy on read — never native `structuredClone` on external input, §13.6 — so the same string also answers the `maxBytes` byte guard, letting an oversized value be refused *without materializing* it); the engine deep-freezes anything a store returns before exposing it. AC-11 has mutation-attempt tests for `saveTest`, `done`, `getResultSet` and both reference stores. Annotation layers (§14.5) live beside it, never inside it.
- **In-progress runs are volatile in v1.** Nothing is persisted mid-run; a run interrupted by a page reload or crash is lost (no half-written durable state). Resumption is a non-goal (§23); a rerun is the recovery path.

### 13.4 Serialization rules (structured-clone / JSON safety)

A sealed Result Set must round-trip through `structuredClone` and `JSON.stringify/parse` with **semantic equality** (every number is finite and `-0` is normalized to `0` at projection so JSON's `-0 → 0` cannot cause a difference; no bit-level float claim is made). Therefore it contains only plain objects, arrays, strings, finite numbers, booleans and `null`. Forbidden anywhere inside it: `Model` or any Viewer object, functions, `THREE` objects, `Map`/`Set`, `undefined` values, and non-finite numbers (a non-finite evidence value would be treated as unavailable with a diagnostic; the library already guarantees finite witness points or omits the pair). This is the direct, permanent answer to the M1-gate crash (F4): the engine's projection boundary is the `UnitExecutor`, so a live `Model` cannot reach a Result Set, a store, or a worker message. AC-11 asserts it.

### 13.5 `ResultStore` port

```text
put(resultSet, { signal? }) → Promise<void>          atomic AND linearizable across the identity index (§4.2 enforcement point 2): runId check + every modelKey→modelVersion check/claim + test-tombstone check + insert are ONE step, across different runIds too; a set is fully visible or not at all; rejects with IdentityConflictError on a modelKey→modelVersion conflict, and with TestDeletedError for a NEW insertion whose testRef.testId is tombstoned (an identical re-put of an already-stored runId still succeeds)
tombstoneTest(testId) → Promise<void>               permanent, idempotent reservation of a hard-deleted testId (§5.5); independent of delete(runId); survives every retention operation
get(runId, { maxBytes? }) → Promise<ResultSet|null>   // MUST reject ResultSetTooLargeError without materializing when the stored canonical-JSON size exceeds maxBytes (§13.6)
list({ testId?, limit?, cursor? }) → Promise<{ summaries: ResultSetSummary[], next?: cursor }>   // header only: outcome, counts, digests, testRef, models[{modelKey, modelVersion}], times. ORDER, PAGING and CONSISTENCY: see "Listing contract" below
delete(runId) → Promise<void>
```

**Listing contract (R12-5), for `ResultStore.list` and `ClashTestStore.list` alike.** Order is a **total order with a unique tie-breaker**: `ResultStore.list` ascending by `runId` (code-unit order); `ClashTestStore.list` ascending by `testId` (code-unit order; a tombstoned id is not listed). `limit` is an integer 1 ≤ n ≤ 1,000 (default 100; anything else is `InvalidOptionsError`). `cursor` is **opaque and versioned** (a string the store issues, e.g. `v1:` + the last key returned; a malformed/unknown-version cursor is `InvalidOptionsError('bad-cursor')`, never a silent restart). Paging is **keyset, not offset**: the next page is everything strictly after the cursor's key in the total order, so an item present for the whole traversal is returned **exactly once**, with no duplicates and no skips, however inserts/deletes interleave; an item inserted or deleted *during* the traversal may or may not appear (no snapshot isolation is required or promised). A backend that pages by offset does not conform. AC-11 runs the reference stores through interleaved insert/delete/list.

**Listings are untrusted data too (R15-6).** Both stores' `list` return closed summary shapes — `ResultSetSummary = {runId, testRef, status, coverage, integrity, environmentSensitive, counts: {results, units, pairSpaceTotal}, serializedBytes, digests, models: [{modelKey, modelVersion}], startedAt, finishedAt}` and `TestSummary = {testId, latestRevision, archived, name}` — and each **page** is captured as one detached copy and validated before it is returned (strings within the §13.5.2 caps, counts non-negative safe integers, `models` ≤ 20,000 entries (= 2 × the `maxEntriesPerSelection` ceiling of §17.1.1, so the summary of *every* valid run is valid — a run's distinct models cannot exceed the entries of its two selections) with no duplicate `modelKey`, enums exact, unknown keys rejected, plain data only). A page with any invalid entry is rejected whole with `CorruptListingError{issues}` (first 20 `{path, code}`, no values) — never a partially trusted page and never a per-entry silent skip. The engine exposes them as `listTests({limit?, cursor?}) → Promise<{summaries, next?}>` and `listResults({testId?, limit?, cursor?}) → Promise<{summaries, next?}>`; both take the read slots of §13.5.2.

**Ambiguous remote outcomes (R16-3).** A remote backend cannot always know whether a write committed (a connection can drop after the server commits and before the response arrives). The contract is therefore: a `put` that fails with **certainty of no mutation** rejects with any error; a `put` that **cannot tell** must reject with the typed **`PersistenceOutcomeUnknownError`**. A rejection is the *end of the attempt* — persistence state `failed` (the set is `held`, never evicted) with `error.name` recorded — **except** `PersistenceOutcomeUnknownError`, which is *not* the end: the attempt **stays `pending`** while the engine runs **one reconciliation read** `resultStore.get(runId)` (through the read slots, §13.5.2) as **part of the same attempt**, and only then settles it: a stored set with identical canonical content ⇒ `saved` (the write had committed); `null`, a failing `get`, `ReadCapacityError`, or **`reconcileTimeoutMs`** elapsing (provisional 10,000; measured from the moment the reconciliation read starts; the read receives an `AbortSignal` that the timeout aborts, its slot is released, and it is the *only* timer on persistence settlement — `persistTimeoutMs` still bounds only `done`'s wait) ⇒ `failed` with `error.ambiguous: true`. A backend that ignores the abort simply leaves the read running detached; the attempt has already settled. So `done`, `persisted`, `getPersistenceState` and the `'persistence'` event observe **only** the settled outcome — never an intermediate final `failed` that is later corrected — and the §13.5.1 one-shot/agreement guarantees hold unchanged. The `persistTimeoutMs` window of `done` runs from seal exactly as before (if it elapses during the reconciliation, `done` resolves `{state:'pending', timedOut:true}` and `persisted`/the event follow at settlement). Because `put` is **create-only and idempotent for identical content** (§13.5), `retryPersist` (legal from `failed`, §13.5.1) is *always safe* after an ambiguous failure — a retry of a write that had in fact committed simply succeeds idempotently — and `discardRun`'s `resultStore.delete(runId)` (chained after settlement) removes a possibly-committed copy either way. Slot retention is unchanged (`pending` and `failed` both occupy the slot; `failed` ⇒ `held`). No fifth persistence state is introduced. **After `engine.dispose()` (R19-3):** the engine's read coordinator and resident set are gone, so a detached write that later rejects with `PersistenceOutcomeUnknownError` is **not reconciled** — the attempt settles `failed` immediately with `error.ambiguous: true` and `error.disposed: true` (a plain rejection settles `failed` as usual; a resolution settles `saved`), so `persisted` always settles and no disposed-engine state, timer or read capability is retained; a host that needs the truth reads the store itself (`get(runId)`) or retries the write from a set it holds (`retryPersist` is dead with the engine; `put` idempotency makes a direct retry safe). AC-11 injects an ambiguous failure after a real commit and before a real commit.

**`put` versus abort (R15-7).** `ResultStore.put(resultSet, {signal})`: the signal is **advisory**. A `put` that **rejects with anything other than `PersistenceOutcomeUnknownError`** guarantees that *no* insert and *no* identity-index or tombstone mutation occurred; once the insertion has linearized, `put` **must resolve** successfully even if an abort is observed later. The engine therefore never infers durable state from an abort: persistence state follows the promise's settlement only (`saved`/`failed`), and `discardRun`'s chained `resultStore.delete(runId)` runs after the write settles (§13.5.2), so a write that committed just before observing the abort is deleted, never orphaned. AC-11 races the abort before start, during work, immediately before commit and after commit.

**`runId` and create-only persistence.** `runId` is engine-generated as a random, globally collision-resistant identifier (UUIDv4 via `crypto.randomUUID`, or ≥ 122 bits of CSPRNG output) — never a counter — so two sessions cannot mint the same id. `put` is **create-only and atomic**: if `runId` is absent the set is inserted; if present with **identical** canonical content the call succeeds idempotently; if present with **different** content it rejects with `RunIdConflictError` and changes nothing (the conflict check and the insert are one atomic step). The `modelKey → modelVersion` identity index of §4.2 is **independent of result lifetime**: `delete` never removes its entries, so deleting results cannot re-open a key to a different version.

- The plan provides an in-memory reference implementation; **storage backends (IndexedDB, server, file) and retention/quota policy are host/Panel decisions** (§23).
- Persistence failure **never alters the run's outcome**. The Result Set is sealed *before* the persistence attempt.

#### 13.5.1 Persistence state and the `RunHandle` promise contract

A Promise settles once, so the lifecycle is specified as **three distinct observables**, each with exactly one meaning:

**Persistence state** (per Result Set; a value, not a promise): `skipped` (no store configured, or `runOptions.persist === false`) · `pending` (a `put` is in flight) · `saved` (`put` resolved) · `failed` (`put` rejected, incl. `identity-conflict` and `test-deleted`; carries `{name, message}`). `saved`, `failed`, `skipped` are the only *final* states of an attempt; **`timeout` is not a persistence state** — it describes only the *waiting window* below.

1. **`done: Promise<RunSummary>`** — resolves **exactly once** and **never rejects** for any run or persistence outcome (`cancelled`, `failed`, `invalidated` and a failing/hanging store all still *resolve*). It resolves at the first of: (a) the sealed set's first persistence attempt has settled, (b) `persistTimeoutMs` (provisional 5,000) has elapsed since seal, or (c) `engine.dispose()` forced the window closed (§10.2) — and in both cases only after the terminal `state` event has been emitted. `RunSummary = { runId, status, resultSet, persistence: PersistenceSnapshot }` with `PersistenceSnapshot = { state: 'skipped'|'pending'|'saved'|'failed', timedOut: boolean, attempt: number, error?: {name, message} }`. **Timeout is represented as `{state: 'pending', timedOut: true}`** — the wait window expired with the write still in flight; it is a statement about the observer's wait, not a persistence outcome. The sealed `resultSet` carries no persistence information (it is immutable and sealed *before* persistence).
2. **`persisted: Promise<PersistenceOutcome>`** — settles **exactly once**, always by *resolving* (never rejecting), when the **first** persistence attempt settles: `{state: 'saved'} | {state: 'failed', error} | {state: 'skipped', reason: 'no-store'|'persist-false'}` (`skipped` resolves at seal). It has **no timer** and **never reports `timeout`**: if `done` timed out, `persisted` simply remains pending until the detached write settles, and then reports the eventual `saved`/`failed`. If the backend never settles, `persisted` never settles (deliberately not on the lifecycle critical path) and the set stays `held` (§13.5.2) so it is never silently dropped.
3. **Persistence continues after `done`.** The timed-out write is not cancelled or abandoned; it runs detached. The eventual outcome is observable three ways, all consistent: the `persisted` promise; `getPersistenceState(runId)` (a synchronous `PersistenceSnapshot` for late observers of a **resident** run — nonresident ids return `null`, see *Nonresident run ids* in §13.5.2); and a `'persistence'` event delivered to subscribers (subscriptions stay valid after the terminal event **for this event only**).
4. **Later attempts.** `retryPersist(runId) → Promise<PersistenceOutcome>` starts a new attempt for a set whose state is `failed`; each attempt has its **own** promise and `attempt` number; `handle.persisted` remains the first attempt's outcome, never rewritten. No timer applies to a retry.
5. **`cancel(reason?)`** returns the same `Promise<RunSummary>` (same waiting rule) as `done` would; calling it on a terminal run resolves with the existing summary.
6. **Phase during the wait.** From seal until `done` resolves, `getSnapshot()` reports the terminal `status` with `phase: 'finalizing'`; it becomes `'terminal'` when `done` resolves. The terminal `state` event is emitted **once**, at the same point `done` resolves, carrying the `PersistenceSnapshot`.

#### 13.5.2 Resident result sets: a hard memory bound

`sealed` means immutable and complete; `saved` means a store confirmed a durable copy. A sealed set that is not `saved` is **unsaved durable state**.

`maxResidentResultSets` (provisional 4) is a **hard cap that also counts runs in flight**. Each run holds one *slot* from admission (`startRun`/`rerun` accepted, including `queued`) until its resident set is released; `slots = active runs (queued, planning, running, cancelling) + resident sets ≤ maxResidentResultSets`, always. Each resident set is itself bounded by the per-run caps (`maxResultsPerRun`, `maxRetainedDiagnostics`, `maxUnitsPerRun`, the exact taint tables) and annotation snapshots (bounded by count, string length and `maxAnnotationBytes`, §14.5, and released with their set); host-supplied strings recorded in a set are length-capped (`modelKey`/`modelVersion` ≤ 1,024 code units, `libraryVersion` ≤ 256, each `environment` field ≤ 512; over-length ⇒ `ModelBindingError`/`InvalidOptionsError`), so resident engine memory ≤ `maxResidentResultSets` × a constant (plus, transiently, at most `maxConcurrentStoreReads` sets being read through and validated, below) (the engine drops its own references to a `RunSummary`/handle on release; a `RunSummary` the *caller* still holds is caller-owned memory outside this bound); the actual bytes-per-set figure is **not measured** (AC-12 measures it; `retentionReport()` exposes `slotsInUse` and per-set class).

Every resident set has exactly one **class**:

| Class | Condition | Auto-evictable? |
|---|---|---|
| `evictable` | persistence `saved`, no outstanding retain lease | **yes**, least-recently-used first (LRU by seal time, refreshed by `getResultSet`) |
| `retained` | `saved`, with ≥ 1 outstanding lease from `retainResultSet` | no, until every lease is `release()`d |
| `held` | persistence `skipped`, `pending` or `failed` (unsaved durable state — possibly the only copy) | **never** — not by the engine, ever |

**Cap-reached behavior (admission).** At `startRun`/`rerun`, after validation succeeds, synchronously: (1) if a slot is free, admit; (2) else evict the LRU `evictable` set — safe, because a confirmed durable copy exists and `getResultSet` re-reads it — and admit; (3) else **refuse** with `ResultCapacityError{cap, active: runId[], retained: runId[], held: {runId, persistence}[]}` — **thrown** by `startRun` (which returns a `RunHandle`) and delivered as a **rejection** of `rerun`'s returned Promise, the admission step still happening synchronously at the moment validation succeeds (R21-1, §18, AC-11). Refusal is the backpressure: the engine never queues waiting for a slot, never drops a `held` set, and never exceeds the cap. Because queued runs occupy slots, the effective queue depth is bounded by slot accounting; `maxQueuedRuns` (default `maxResidentResultSets − 1`) may only lower it. Sealing converts the run's own slot into the resident set, so **sealing can never fail for lack of a slot**.

**No-store and failed-store mode.** With no store (or `persist: false`) every sealed set is `held`; the engine is a *`maxResidentResultSets`-run engine until the caller intervenes*. That is the intended, visible consequence of "never silently drop unsaved state", not a leak.

**How a caller retains or discards intentionally** (all explicit; none is a side effect of cancel, dispose-by-GC, or another run):
- `retainResultSet(runId) → { release() }` — keeps a `saved` set resident (on a set that is **not** `saved` — a `held` one — it throws `UnsavedResultSetError`: held sets are never auto-evicted, so a retain lease is meaningless for them; on a nonresident id, `ResultNotResidentError`) (a retained set still occupies a slot; a caller that retains every slot starves admissions and gets `ResultCapacityError`, by design).
- `releaseResultSet(runId, { acknowledgeUnsaved? })` — drops the **resident copy only** (durable copy, if any, untouched). Refused with `ResultSetRetainedError` while leases are outstanding, and with `UnsavedResultSetError` for a `held` set unless `acknowledgeUnsaved: true` (the caller asserts they took the set via `getResultSet` and persisted it elsewhere).
- `discardRun(runId)` — release-with-acknowledgement **plus** `resultStore.delete(runId)` (chained after any in-flight `put` settles, so a late write cannot resurrect it; for a hung write the returned promise does not resolve until the write settles). **A set whose `put` is still in flight keeps its slot until that write settles** — `releaseResultSet` and `discardRun` free the slot only at settlement (after `dispose()` the accounting is moot, see below) — so detached writes are *inside* the hard bound: repeated hung writes exhaust slots and yield `ResultCapacityError` (whose `held` list marks them `pending`), never unbounded retained sets. `ResultStore.put(resultSet, { signal })` receives an `AbortSignal` that `discardRun` aborts (a cooperating backend may then settle promptly); a backend that ignores it simply keeps the slot occupied. It is valid **only for a terminal run** (`cancel` first; otherwise its returned Promise **rejects** with `RunNotTerminalError`) and with no configured store it simply releases the resident copy (nothing to delete). It is one of exactly **two** explicit, host-initiated operations that remove unsaved state from the engine — the other is `engine.dispose()`, which **hands the unsaved sets back to the caller** (`DisposeSummary.droppedUnsaved` carries the frozen sets, not merely their ids) so nothing is destroyed unless the caller discards it — and the only one that also deletes the durable copy. The engine never removes a `held` set on its own initiative.
- `retryPersist(runId)` — converts a `failed` `held` set into an `evictable` one once a write succeeds.
- `getResultSet(runId)` — returns the resident set if resident; otherwise reads the store **without making it resident** (read-through, not cached: the caller owns the returned copy). **Transient read work is bounded too (R14-2):** every store read-through — `getResultSet`, and the by-id forms of `rerun` and `getFreshness` — runs through **read slots**: at most `maxConcurrentStoreReads` (provisional 1, ceiling 4) read/validate/freeze operations are in progress at once, further calls wait in a FIFO of at most `maxQueuedStoreReads` (provisional 8, ceiling 64), and a call beyond that rejects `ReadCapacityError{limit}` immediately; a read holds its slot until its validation, digest recompute and freeze finish (or it rejects), so the engine's transient read-side working set is at most `maxConcurrentStoreReads` × one schema-ceiling set, on top of the `maxResidentResultSets` resident bound. Reads accept an optional `signal` (`AbortSignal`) checked at slice boundaries (rejecting `ReadAbortedError`, slot released). The *caller-owned* returned copy is outside the bound, exactly like a held `RunSummary` (§13.5.2).
- **Nonresident run ids (R11-5).** The engine keeps **no** per-run metadata once a run's resident set has been evicted, released or discarded — its bound (`maxResidentResultSets` × a constant) covers everything it retains, and there is deliberately no separate history cache. The run-scoped operations therefore apply to **active and resident** runs only, with these defined results for a nonresident `runId` (one that was never admitted by this engine, or whose set has since been evicted/released/discarded):

  | Operation | Nonresident `runId` |
  |---|---|
  | `getPersistenceState(runId)` | returns `null` (not an error; it never throws for an unknown id). The durable outcome of a nonresident set is the *store's* to report (`getResultSet`/`resultStore.get`). Since only `saved` sets are ever auto-evicted, an evicted set was `saved`; a set released with `acknowledgeUnsaved` or discarded carries no engine-held claim. |
  | `getResultSet(runId)` | read-through from the store; `null` if there is no store or it holds no such set (§13.5.2, unchanged). |
  | `getRun(runId)` | `null` (a resident terminal run still returns its handle, §18). |
  | `retainResultSet(runId)`, `retryPersist(runId)` | fail with `ResultNotResidentError{runId}` — **thrown** synchronously by `retainResultSet` (it returns `{release()}`), delivered as a **rejection** by `retryPersist` (it returns a Promise). `retryPersist` applies only to a resident `failed` set, and a `failed` set is `held`, hence never auto-evicted, so a nonresident id has nothing for it to retry (the caller re-obtains the set with `getResultSet` and persists it with the store, or reruns). |
  | `releaseResultSet(runId, …)` | idempotent no-op (already released). |
  | `discardRun(runId)` | still issues `resultStore.delete(runId)` (idempotent; with no store, a no-op) — an explicit host request to destroy the durable copy is honored regardless of residency. |
  | `getFreshness(runId)` | computed over the set read through from the store (as `getResultSet`); rejects `ResultNotResidentError` if the store holds none. |
  | `annotate(runId, …)` | rejects `ResultNotResidentError` — annotation snapshots live and die with the resident set (§14.5, §13.5.2); a caller holding a set obtained via `getResultSet` uses the standalone `groupBy`/`filter` with its own snapshot. |

  After `engine.dispose()` every method fails with `EngineDisposedError` (§10.2) — thrown by the synchronous entry points, rejected by the Promise-returning ones. A `'persistence'` event subscription stays valid only while its run is resident.

- `engine.dispose() → Promise<DisposeSummary>` — after every run has drained and sealed (§10.2), releases all resident sets, but **never silently**: `droppedUnsaved` returns — as `Array<{runId, resultSet}>`, **the frozen sets themselves, transferring ownership to the caller** (caller-owned memory, outside the bound) — every `held` set with no write in flight (`skipped`/`failed`) that it released, and `persistenceInFlight` names the `pending` ones whose detached write continues. After `dispose()` the engine is dead and its slot accounting moot, so a still-pending write's reference is **backend-owned memory outside the engine's bound** (an explicit weakening; the signal is aborted to let a cooperating backend release it). It never throws and does not wait for writes.

### 13.6 Schema evolution

**Versioning model (chosen, R13-3): aggregate.** `schemaVersion` is an integer carried by exactly **two** persisted top-level types — `ClashTestDefinition` and `ResultSet` — and the `ResultSet.schemaVersion` governs **every nested structure** (`RunOutcome`, `Provenance`, `Ledger`, `diagnostics`, `ClashResult`, `PairId`, `ElementRef`, `digests`), which have no independent version field; a change to any nested shape is a `ResultSet` version bump and migrations are registered per `(type, fromVersion)` for those two types only. The "open for additive optional fields within one version" rule below never applies to a change that alters the meaning of an existing field. A reader rejects a version greater than it understands with a distinct error (`UnsupportedSchemaVersionError`); lower versions go through a registered migration whose output is re-validated. **Open vs closed is defined per type (the earlier blanket "additive fields tolerated" contradicted V1):**

| Persisted type | Unknown fields | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| `ClashTestDefinition` (stored **or embedded in a Result Set**) | **Closed** — rejected at every nesting level (V1). | Any new field requires a `schemaVersion` bump and a migration; a reader never preserves or ignores an unknown definition field. An embedded definition that fails V1 makes the whole Result Set unreadable (`definition-invalid` on read), not partially trusted. |
| `ResultSet` top level, `Ledger`, `Provenance`, `RunOutcome`, `diagnostics` | **Open for additive *optional* fields within one `schemaVersion`.** | A reader preserves unknown fields verbatim when it copies/rewrites a set and does **not** interpret them. Unknown fields are **excluded from `pairSetDigest` and `ledgerDigest`** (digests cover only the documented fields of §13.1), so a newer writer's extra field cannot change a digest an older reader computes. A field that changes the meaning of documented fields, or a *required* new field, requires a version bump. |
| `ClashResult`, `ResultId`, `PairId`, `ElementRef` | **Closed.** | Identity- and evidence-bearing shapes; any change is a version bump. |

Migrations are registered per `(type, fromVersion)`; a missing or throwing migration is `migration-failed` (§5.5). Panel-specific state never enters the sealed core — it goes in annotation layers keyed by `ResultId`.

**Capture first (R15-1, R19-1).** The stability boundary comes *before* migration and before any sliced work, exactly as for definitions (§5.3): **every** Result Set that enters an asynchronous engine operation from outside — a `ResultStore.get`/read-through value, and a **raw set passed by a caller** to `rerun`, `engine.getFreshness`, `engine.canClaimNoClashes` or `engine.assessNoClashClaim` — is first **captured as one atomic, detached, engine-owned copy** by the explicit algorithm below, in a single synchronous, uninterruptible step (so nothing the store or caller holds can change what is captured). **Native `structuredClone` is not used** — it invokes enumerable getters and would violate the no-invocation rule; the capture is the same descriptor-based reflection algorithm as §5.3, generalized to the Result Set: an iterative (explicit-stack, non-recursive) traversal that, for every object, reads its own keys once with `Reflect.ownKeys` and each own property **descriptor** once (an accessor descriptor or a symbol key ⇒ `CorruptResultSetError('accessor')` / `('symbol-key')`, **without invoking any getter**), for every array reads `length` and each index by descriptor once (holes and extra own keys rejected), copies only data values into fresh plain objects, tracks visited source objects (a revisit ⇒ `('cyclic')`), and treats a value that is not plain data (a function, `Map`, `Set`, `Model`, non-finite number, `undefined`) as `('not-plain')`. **Ceilings are charged *before* the corresponding copy is allocated:** depth ≤ 64; each string within its field cap (§13.5.2); every array's length against its schema ceiling (§17.1.1 hard maxima) at the moment it is reached; total captured nodes ≤ the **fixed schema node ceiling** `SCHEMA_MAX_NODES = 35 × maxResultsPerRun_ceiling + 12 × maxLedgerRecords_ceiling + (24 + maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic_ceiling) × maxRetainedDiagnostics_ceiling + 65,536` (errata **A11**, **A13**; the closed shapes bound one `ClashResult` to ≤ 35 nodes — a clearance result with all four flags, counting every object, array and primitive as one node — one retained `NormalizedDiagnostic` to ≤ 24 + `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic` nodes — `fragment` and `fragments` with `role` on all three operands, `ambiguousModelId`, and one node per kept unit id — and one ledger record to ≤ 12; the `_ceiling` values are the §17.1.1 hard maxima) — a data-format property, so **every set any conforming writer may legally produce is within it** and exceeding it is `CorruptResultSetError`; **separately**, an *operational* reader limit `maxCapturedNodes` (an engine limit, provisional 16,000,000, ≤ `SCHEMA_MAX_NODES`) above which a valid set is refused as `ResultSetTooLargeError`, never as corrupt (below and §17.1.1); and the running byte count of **unknown fields** ≤ 64 KiB — so an oversized nested array or a huge unknown field is rejected as soon as the traversal reaches it, before the rest is copied. `schemaVersion` is the **first** key read (a fixed key order) and an unsupported value throws `UnsupportedSchemaVersionError` *before any other property is read or any migration is invoked*; there is no separate preflight read (each property is read exactly once, so there is no preflight/capture disagreement).
