The capture step is **excluded from the sliced-span target** and measured like the definition capture (§17.3, AC-12); it is bounded by the ceilings above; **the atomic synchronous capture is retained deliberately** (a sliced capture cannot give the no-TOCTOU guarantee, and only native immutable ownership transfer could, which a store port cannot promise), so its cost is capped and *measured, not assumed*: AC-12 measures the worst-case capture duration and peak bytes (source graph plus detached copy ≈ 2×) at the schema ceilings on the reference device, the peak enters the §17.1 worst-case memory estimate, and **the operational limits (`maxCapturedNodes`, `maxStoredResultBytes`) and the run defaults they constrain (`maxResultsPerRun`, `maxLedgerRecords`, `maxRetainedDiagnostics`) are frozen only after that measurement, jointly** — if the measured capture stall or peak is unacceptable the plan must lower them together (the cross-field rule below keeps them consistent; a valid set above a lowered *reader* limit then fails with `ResultSetTooLargeError`, never a partial read). **Producer/reader invariant (R21-2, normative):** *an engine never produces a set it cannot read.* At `startRun`/`rerun` option validation (§17.1.1) the run's worst case must fit this engine's reader limits — `35 × maxResultsPerRun + 12 × maxLedgerRecords + (24 + maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic) × maxRetainedDiagnostics + 65,536 ≤ maxCapturedNodes` (A11, A13) **and** `maxCapturedNodes × bytesPerNode ≤ maxStoredResultBytes` (`bytesPerNode` = 32, provisional, calibrated by AC-12) — else `InvalidOptionsError('result-unreadable')`. A set produced by an engine with *larger* limits and read by one with smaller limits is `ResultSetTooLargeError` (valid, not corrupt). **A serialized-size guard sits at the store boundary so an oversized value is refused before any object graph exists:** `ResultSetSummary` carries the stored set's canonical-JSON `serializedBytes`, and the engine calls `ResultStore.get(runId, {maxBytes: maxStoredResultBytes})` (default in §17.1; ceiling 1 GiB, §17.1.1), which **must** reject `ResultSetTooLargeError{runId}` without materializing the value when the stored size exceeds `maxBytes`. **This is a required capability of a conforming `ResultStore` (R20-4b), not an option:** the engine cannot detect a violation at run time (it only ever sees the returned object), so the hard memory guarantee of §13.5.2/§17.1 holds **only for conforming stores**, and conformance is established by AC-11's store conformance test (the test store proves no object graph larger than `maxBytes` is ever constructed) which every store a host ships must pass; a host-supplied store that has not passed it is outside the guarantee. **Exact `ResultSetTooLargeError` predicate:** a *valid* set (i) whose stored size exceeds `maxStoredResultBytes` (store-side), or (ii) that is read by-id for `rerun`/`getFreshness` while its `counts.results`, `ledger` record count or retained-diagnostic count exceeds the *reading engine's* effective `maxResultsPerRun`/`maxLedgerRecords`/`maxRetainedDiagnostics` — never a corruption finding. A store that hands over a graph at the ceilings is the residual cost of accepting external data (a hostile *in-process* source that supplies millions of unknown keys is inside the same trust boundary as §5.3). Migration, validation, digest recomputation and freezing then run in slices **on the clone only**; the store/caller keeps no reference to it, so passing validation *does* prove the frozen object is the validated object. The store's own contract (§13.3: copy on write and on read, return exclusive ownership) is a courtesy that the engine does not rely on. **Uniform entry rules (R19-1):** the capture runs **synchronously inside the call, before the returned Promise's first `await`**, so a caller that mutates its object immediately after the call cannot affect the operation; every failure of capture, migration, validation or the size ceilings (§17.1.1 schema ceilings; `maxStoredResultBytes` applies only to store reads) is delivered as a **rejected Promise** (`CorruptResultSetError`, `UnsupportedSchemaVersionError`, `ResultSetTooLargeError`), never a synchronous throw, so callers handle one shape; caller-supplied raw sets **reserve a read slot synchronously, before capture** (they bound engine-owned capture/validate work although no store I/O occurs), and if **no slot is free** the call is **rejected immediately** with `ReadCapacityError{limit}` — **raw-set calls are never queued**, because queuing would force either capturing the caller's graph early (retaining up to `maxQueuedStoreReads` schema-ceiling graphs, breaking the transient-memory bound) or holding the caller's mutable reference (a TOCTOU); the FIFO of `maxQueuedStoreReads` applies **only to by-id store reads**, whose input is an id, not a mutable graph; and the claim/freshness methods validate the captured clone in full (§13.6) before using any field of it. The **derivation-context** methods (`summarize`, `groupBy`, `filter`, `compareResultSets`, §14.4) also capture each raw set they receive, synchronously and before their first `await` (they are sliced), but do not run the full `validateResultSet` — a set given to them is the caller's own data and they only *derive* from it, never claim; the purely synchronous helpers (`getPairStatus`, `getElementStatus`, the standalone claim helpers given an already-verified freshness) yield nowhere and need no capture.

**Untrusted-read contract for stored Result Sets (R12-3).** A `ResultStore` is a port and everything it returns — `get`, and the read-through of `getResultSet`, `rerun`/`getFreshness` by `runId` — is **untrusted**, exactly as definitions are (§5.5). Before any such set is exposed, frozen, compared, grouped or used for a claim, the engine runs the pure function `validateResultSet(set)` in sliced work (§17.3) and exposes **nothing** from a set that fails it; failure rejects with `CorruptResultSetError{runId, issues}` (first 20 `{path, code}`, no values) — `UnsupportedSchemaVersionError` and `migration-failed` keep their own paths (§13.6 above). It checks: **(1) structure** — plain-data, acyclic, finite numbers, depth ≤ 64, each string within its length cap (§13.5.2), and every array/list and total record count within the **schema ceilings** — the fixed hard maxima of §17.1.1 for the set's `schemaVersion` (`maxResultsPerRun`, `maxUnitsPerRun`, `maxLedgerRecords`, `maxRetainedDiagnostics`, … at their *ceiling* values), which are properties of the data format — **never** the reading engine's configured run options (a valid set produced under a larger configuration must not become "corrupt" under a smaller one) and never the set's self-reported options; a set that is valid but larger than this engine can hold resident is not corrupt: `getResultSet` reads it through without making it resident, and a `rerun`/`getFreshness` by id that would need it resident beyond the engine's configured bounds rejects `ResultSetTooLargeError{runId}`, distinct from `CorruptResultSetError`; **(2) unknown fields** — in the open types of the table above only, and only if they are JSON-safe and their **total serialized size ≤ 64 KiB** (else `CorruptResultSetError`, never preserved); a closed type with an unknown field is rejected; **(3) cross-field invariants** — `definition === null ⇔ testRef.semanticDigest === null`; `outcome.coverage = 'full'` ⇒ a plan exists, no planning gap and every leaf unit `evaluated` with `scopeProof: 'equal'`; when that holds, the set must also satisfy the A10 (§25, §6.3) exact-cover checks — every evaluated leaf's source roots are one contiguous run of one class table and every explicit target entry is one contiguous run, under the canonical root order (`coverage-layout-noncontiguous` otherwise), and the union of the evaluated leaves' source × target rectangles covers every pair of the required pair space `P` (§6.1) exactly (`coverage-pair-uncovered` otherwise); `status: completed` ⇒ the run carries **no cause that §16.7 maps to a higher-precedence terminal status** — none of the fatal run-level causes (`world-disposed`, `internal-error`, `result-cap`, the planning gaps) and none of the input-change causes (`input-changed`, `scope-drift`), and no `cancel-requested`; the **non-fatal unit gaps** a completed run legitimately contains (`unit:failed:capacity-exceeded|extraction-limit|object-tree-unavailable|stale-placement|unit-cap|target-too-large|model-evicted`, `unit:skipped:*`, `unit:discarded:scope-unverifiable`) are **permitted** and imply `coverage: 'partial'`; `invalidated`/`failed`/`cancelled` are checked as the terminal *status*, never as a substring of a reason; `emptyOperand`/`pairSpaceTotal` consistent; each `results[i]` has both owners in the ledger `rootOwners` **or** — only when it carries `flags.unreachableOwner` (a clash against an *unreachable-only* whole-model target owner, §6.2, `getPairStatus` = `not-required`) — has its one non-root owner in that model's `unreachablePopulation.ownerDbIds` (the same rule `validateUnitOutcome` applies at production, §7.1, so the engine's own output always round-trips); a result with a non-root owner that is not listed unreachable, or an `unreachableOwner` flag on a pair whose owners are both root owners, is corrupt; its `ResultId` matches `testRef`/`PairId`; **(R21-4) result-to-ledger linkage:** each result's `unitId` names an existing **leaf** unit (not a `split` parent) whose outcome is `evaluated` with `scopeProof: 'equal'`; that unit's source and target ranges **cover the result's root pair** (both owners map, through `rootOwners`, to roots the unit paired; a whole-model target covers its root owners or, for an `unreachableOwner`-flagged result, its `unreachablePopulation` owner); `evaluatedAs.source.modelKey` equals the unit's `sourceModelKey` and `evaluatedAs` names exactly the result's two refs; and the result's `mode` and evidence shape match the embedded definition's `mode` (§11.1; `ClashResult` carries `mode`, never `type`); `provenance.models` covers every `modelKey` the results/ledger mention; **(4) canonical order and uniqueness** — `results` strictly ascending by `PairId` order (§11.3), no duplicate `ResultId`; and **(5) digests** — `pairSetDigest` and `ledgerDigest` are **recomputed** and must equal the recorded ones (a `digestVersion` the engine does not know is not compared and the set is treated as `unverifiable` for comparison, §5.4, never `current`-claimable). **Residual trust boundary, stated:** validation proves a set is well-formed and self-consistent, **not that it is authentic** — a store that returns a *forged but internally consistent* set (correct digests, consistent ledger) cannot be detected, because digests are content hashes, not signatures. Result-store integrity (and any signing) is the host's; `canClaimNoClashes(resultSet, …)` and the other standalone pure derivations (§18) **do not validate** a set the *caller* built or fetched themselves — only sets the engine obtained through `getResultSet`/`rerun`/`getFreshness` are validated. Freezing (§13.3) runs on the validated copy, so an oversized or cyclic value is rejected *before* it is traversed by the freeze.

---

## 14. Aggregation and grouping foundations

### 14.1 Principle

Roadmap §4: keep result semantics rich enough for downstream grouping/filtering, keep Group By **out of collision core**, keep presentation out of this layer. This section therefore defines only **pure, deterministic derivations over a sealed Result Set** plus an optional attribute snapshot — the data-side foundation, no UI, no state.

### 14.2 Built-in facets (derived from the Result Set alone)

| Facet | Value | Valued |
|---|---|---|
| `mode` | the test's mode | single |
| `pairModels` | `[pair.first.modelKey, pair.second.modelKey]` (already canonically ordered) | single |
| `crossModel` | `first.modelKey !== second.modelKey` | single |
| `elementFirst` / `elementSecond` | the canonical `ElementRef` on that side | single |
| `element` | each member `ElementRef` of the pair | **multi** (a result belongs to two groups) |
| `locationCell(size)` (`size` a required finite number > 0; `NaN`/`Infinity`/≤ 0 ⇒ `InvalidOptionsError`) | `[⌊x/size⌋, ⌊y/size⌋, ⌊z/size⌋]` of the result's location point; `null` if none | single |
| `distanceBucket(width)` (`width` a required finite number > 0, same validation) | `⌊distance/width⌋` (clearance only) | single |
| `caveated` | `getPairStatus(pair).verdict === 'covered-caveated'` — i.e. an `element`-attributed diagnostic on either element **or** any `target-model`/`unit` taint on the pair's covering unit (§12.5) | single |

*Location point*: `volume-overlap` → `witness.point` when non-null; `clearance` → midpoint of `closestPoints.first/second`; otherwise none. **Range safety (R15-5):** every derived bucket index (`locationCell`, `distanceBucket`) must be a **safe integer**; a value that would be non-finite or outside the safe-integer range (e.g. `Number.MAX_VALUE / 1e-300`) makes the derivation reject `FacetRangeError{facetId}` with no partial grouping — never `null`, never a saturated value, so a `GroupId` can never alias the "no location" (`null`) group through `JSON.stringify`. `locationCell` is the deterministic **spatial-grouping foundation** (a fixed-grid partition, a pure function of a recorded coordinate and a caller-chosen `size`); clustering algorithms are out of scope.

### 14.3 Group identity and determinism

- `GroupId` = canonical JSON of `[facetId, params, value]`. It is stable across reruns and sessions, so a later workflow layer can attach state to a `GroupId` or `ResultId`.
- Groups are ordered by `value` (numbers numerically, strings by code unit, arrays lexicographically, `null` last); members appear in canonical result order. Nested grouping is an ordered facet list; a group's identity is its path.
- **Partition vs cover.** A single-valued facet *partitions* the results (each result in exactly one group; group counts sum to the result count). A multi-valued facet *covers* them (a result may appear in several groups; counts do **not** sum to the result count). The API marks facets `multiValued` so no consumer sums counts blindly.

### 14.4 Filters

A filter is a **conjunction** of `{facet, op, value}` clauses with `op ∈ {eq, in, lt, lte, gt, gte, isNull, notNull}`, evaluated purely over results and annotations. No disjunction, no text search, no saved filters (§23).

**Truth rules (R16 Q2).** A facet yields, per result, a *value list* (a single-valued facet: exactly one value, possibly `null`; a multi-valued facet — `element`, `attrEither` — one or more values). A clause is **existential** over that list: `eq v` ⇔ some value equals `v`; `in S` ⇔ some value ∈ `S`; `lt/lte/gt/gte v` ⇔ some (non-null, numeric) value satisfies it, and these four ops are valid only on facets whose values are **scalar numbers** (`distanceBucket`, numeric attributes) — `locationCell` is *array-valued* `[i, j, k]` (§14.2) and has no component selector, so ordering ops on it (and on any non-numeric facet) reject `InvalidOptionsError`; `eq`/`in` on `locationCell` compare the whole array by canonical-JSON equality; `isNull` ⇔ every value is `null` (or the list is empty); `notNull` ⇔ some value is non-null (so `notNull` and `isNull` are exact complements). A `null` value never satisfies `eq`/`in`/ordering. **Unavailable facets** (an attribute facet with no compatible snapshot, §14.5): a clause on it is **false for every result** — never ignored — and the derivation's resolved value carries `unavailable: [{facetId, reason}]` (the `annotation-incompatible:…` reason) so a consumer can tell "no matches" from "could not evaluate"; `groupBy` omits an unavailable facet's level and lists it in the same `unavailable` field. Built-in facets are never unavailable. AC-13 has the full truth table.

**Where the limits live (R17-4).** The derivation functions (`summarize`, `groupBy`, `filter`, `compareResultSets`) need a scheduler and the §17.1.1 derivation limits, which belong to an engine, so they are **not** free-standing pure functions with implicit global state. They are exposed as methods of a **derivation context**: `engine.derivations` (the engine's own context, using its `yieldToScheduler`, `sliceBudgetMs`, `aggregationSliceMaxItems` and `maxConcurrentDerivations`/`maxFacetsPerGroupBy`/`maxFilterClauses`/`maxGroupMemberships`/`maxComparisonEntries`), or a standalone `createDerivationContext({limits?, yieldToScheduler?, now?}) → DerivationContext` (same defaults and validation, §17.1.1). Concurrency is **per context** (never process-wide, never module-global); two contexts have independent limits, and a standalone call without a context is not part of the API. The synchronous pure helpers that need no scheduler or limits (`getPairStatus`, `getElementStatus`, `canClaimNoClashes`, `assessNoClashClaim`) stay standalone.

**Cost and bounds (R13-4).** `summarize`, `groupBy` and `filter` are **asynchronous** (`Promise`-returning) and run in the deadline-aware slices of §17.3 (results are processed `aggregationSliceMaxItems` at a time, yielding between slices); a synchronous form is not offered, because a synchronous call cannot yield. Each accepts an optional `signal` (`AbortSignal`) checked at every slice boundary — abort rejects `DerivationAbortedError` with no partial result and stops all further slices and allocation (merely dropping the Promise does **not** cancel it, so a caller superseding a request must abort it); at most `maxConcurrentDerivations` (provisional 2) run at once and a further call rejects `DerivationBusyError{limit}` immediately (never queues), so obsolete requests cannot accumulate. Derived work is bounded before it is done: a `groupBy` accepts at most `maxFacetsPerGroupBy` (provisional 4) facets and rejects a repeated facet id with the same params as redundant (`InvalidOptionsError`); a filter at most `maxFilterClauses` (provisional 32); and the total number of **derived membership records** (one per result per facet path, so a multi-valued `element` facet contributes 2 per result and nested multi-valued facets multiply) is counted **incrementally, before allocation**, against `maxGroupMemberships` (provisional 1,000,000, ceiling 10,000,000) — exceeding it rejects with `GroupingTooLargeError{limit, observed}` and returns **no** partial grouping (fail-closed, never a silently truncated group list). Bounds and ceilings are in §17.1/§17.1.1; AC-13 has a cap-sized case and the §14.4 truth-rule table (single-/multi-valued, `null`, empty list, unavailable, each op incl. `isNull`/`notNull` complementarity and ordering ops on a non-numeric facet).

### 14.5 Element attributes (optional annotation layer)

Grouping by level/category/system needs Viewer property data. Grouping by attribute is served from a *snapshot* taken while the model is live, so that grouping does not re-query the Viewer for every operation. **Snapshots are deliberately ephemeral**: they live only as long as their resident Result Set, are **not** persisted by `ResultStore`, and are regenerable only while the model is loaded — offline reproducibility of attribute groups is a non-goal (an `AttributeSnapshotStore` would be a separate port, §23):

```text
ElementAttributeSource.getAttributes(binding, dbIds, names, {signal}) → Promise<Map<dbId, Record<string, string|number|boolean|null>>>
engine.annotate(runId, names, {signal?}) → Promise<AttributeSnapshot>   // ONE snapshot per run; each call REPLACES it atomically; last-request-wins via a per-run monotone token (an older call that finishes after a newer one started discards its result and rejects with `AnnotationSupersededError`); never accumulates, so the `maxAnnotationBytes` bound is per run, total
         AttributeSnapshot { schemaVersion, runId, semanticDigest, names, capturedAt, modelVersions, values: { [elementRefKey]: Record<name, scalar> } }   // elementRefKey = JSON.stringify([modelKey, dbId]) — delimiter-safe, like ResultId (§4.4)
```

The snapshot is held **beside** the sealed core (keyed by `runId`, released with it, §13.5.2), is not digested, may be regenerated while the model is live, is **hard-bounded** (`maxAnnotatedNames`; attribute name ≤ 128 code units; string value ≤ 1,024 code units — longer values are truncated with a `…` marker and counted; ≤ `maxAnnotationBytes`, provisional 8 MiB, measured **exactly** as the UTF-8 byte length of the snapshot's canonical JSON encoding (§5.4 encoding: sorted keys, no whitespace; keys, quotes, brackets and separators counted; a value truncated to 1,024 code units is counted *after* truncation, its `…` marker included), accumulated **incrementally before each value is stored** — exceeding it makes `annotate` reject with `AnnotationTooLargeError` and keep nothing), and enables `attrFirst:<name>`, `attrSecond:<name>` (single-valued) and `attrEither:<name>` (multi-valued) facets. Absent a snapshot, attribute facets are simply unavailable (not an error). Attribute *values* are plain scalars only. **Compatibility (a snapshot is never applied on trust):** the standalone `groupBy`/`filter` accept a caller-supplied snapshot, so they verify, before using any attribute facet, that (1) `snapshot.runId === resultSet.runId`, (2) `snapshot.semanticDigest === resultSet.testRef.semanticDigest`, (3) for **every** `modelKey` in `resultSet.provenance.models`, `snapshot.modelVersions[modelKey]` exists and equals that entry's `modelVersion`, and (4) every attribute name the facet/filter references is in `snapshot.names`. On any mismatch the attribute facets are **unavailable** with reason `annotation-incompatible:<runId|semanticDigest|modelVersion:<modelKey>|name:<name>>` — never applied, never partially applied, never an exception — exactly like the no-snapshot case; built-in facets are unaffected. `engine.annotate(runId, …)` stamps `runId`/`semanticDigest`/`modelVersions` from the run it snapshots, so an engine-owned snapshot is compatible by construction. These four keys do not prove that the attribute *values* still describe the live model (they are a snapshot, §14.5); they prove only that the snapshot was taken for *this* result set.

**Annotation jobs are cancellable and bounded (R18-1).** Each `annotate` call is a *job* holding an `AbortController`. A job is **actively aborted** — its `signal` (passed to `getAttributes(…, {signal})` and checked at every slice boundary) fires and its retained bindings/maps are dropped — when it is superseded by a newer `annotate` for the same run, when its set is released, discarded or evicted, on `engine.dispose()`, and when the caller's own `signal` aborts (rejecting `AnnotationSupersededError` / `ResultNotResidentError` / `EngineDisposedError` / `DerivationAbortedError` respectively; a source that ignores the signal is left running detached, holding at most its own memory, and its late result is discarded unread). At most **one** job is in flight per run and at most `maxConcurrentAnnotations` (provisional 2, ceiling 8) **engine-wide**; a further `annotate` rejects `AnnotationBusyError{limit}` immediately (never queues). In-flight annotation memory is therefore at most `maxConcurrentAnnotations × maxAnnotationBytes` and is **added to the §13.5.2/§17.1 memory bound**. The synchronous capture of a resolved `Map` copies only the requested `dbId`s' entries (≤ run element count × ≤ `maxAnnotatedNames` names, bounded incrementally by `maxAnnotationBytes` and aborting the copy the moment the cap is exceeded, `AnnotationTooLargeError`) and is listed with the other measured synchronous exceptions of AC-12(b).

**`annotate` versus lifecycle, and the source's failure modes (R12-4).** `annotate(runId, names)` requires a **resident** set (else `ResultNotResidentError`, §13.5.2) and, at call time, captures that resident set's **residency token** (an engine-internal identity minted each time a set becomes resident). It does **not** take a retain lease (a lease would occupy a slot and starve admissions for the duration of a property fetch): instead the result is **published atomically at completion only if** the engine is not disposed, the run is still resident **with the same token**, no newer `annotate` for the run has started (last-request-wins, `AnnotationSupersededError`), and the recorded `modelVersions` still match the live bindings' `modelVersion`s. Otherwise nothing is stored and the promise rejects — `EngineDisposedError` after `dispose()`, `ResultNotResidentError` if the set was evicted/released/discarded meanwhile (even if re-admitted later under a *new* token, so an annotation can never attach to a different residency), or `AnnotationSupersededError`. A snapshot therefore can never be resurrected for a nonresident run and cannot leak. **`ElementAttributeSource` contract:** `getAttributes` must resolve a `Map`, and the engine **captures it immediately**: in one synchronous pass on resolution it copies exactly the requested `dbId`s' entries (looking each up once; iterating no unrequested entry) into an engine-owned plain structure, so a source that mutates the returned `Map` afterward, or yields between the engine's slices, cannot alter what is validated, counted or published (no ownership transfer is required of the source); anything else (non-`Map`, a rejection, a throw, or a duplicate name in `names`, which is `InvalidOptionsError` before any call) makes `annotate` reject `AttributeSourceError` with **nothing published** — the call is all-or-nothing across models, never a partial snapshot. Within a successful result, a `dbId` absent from the map reads as every requested attribute `null`; entries for `dbId`s that were not requested are ignored; an attribute value that is not a scalar (`string|number|boolean|null`), or is a number that is not finite (`NaN`, `±Infinity`), is replaced by `null` and counted (`-0` is normalized to `0`), so byte accounting and `GroupId` identity always see the same JSON-safe value.

### 14.6 Summary

`summarize(resultSet)` returns exact totals: results by `mode`/`pairModels`/`crossModel`, unit outcomes by cause, gap weight, caveat counts by reason, and the three axes — the data a Panel header needs, without the Panel re-deriving it.

---

## 15. Repeatability, rerun, comparison

### 15.1 Determinism contract per mode (F11)

| Mode | Class | What is promised |
|---|---|---|
| `volume-overlap` | **deterministic** | Same **inputs and same starting admission state** ⇒ same pair set, flags and diagnostic counts/taints, **within one build/runtime/environment envelope** (below). Deterministic operation-count budgets; canonical operand orientation. Residual: rank-6 ties resolve by the definition-derived role assignment (§11.4). |
| `clearance` | **deterministic** | As above (its Hard-Clash precheck reuses `volumeOverlapQuery`; the ~24% inconclusive volume on the M3 fixture is a deterministic property of geometry). |
| `surface-intersection` | **time-sensitive** | The library still lets elapsed time set `aborted`; a verdict near the budget boundary can depend on machine speed. Differences appear as `crossing-budget-exhausted` caveats, not silent flips. |
| `containment` | **time-sensitive** | Same, via the witness term (`SurfaceCrossingWitness`). |

**Environmental inputs (H3, geometry readiness, foreign placement activity).** "Same inputs" means the definition semantics, the sorted enumerations, the sizing options, the library budget constants, **the collision world's cumulative epoch-cache state** (§1.3.5) **the renderer's geometry availability** (§1.3.6) **and concurrent placement activity by callers this layer cannot serialize** (F13). None is derived from the definition, and none is covered by the scope digests (which prove *extraction identity* only). The engine handles them by *recording and qualifying*, not by claiming them away:

- **Epoch cache.** An unrelated earlier query, another engine's run, or a direct `findClashes` caller can pre-populate a touched epoch's instance bucket, and a run that is fine in a fresh world can then fail a unit with `bucket-capacity`. (a) `provenance.admissionBaseline` records the starting per-epoch bucket size — **a count only**: an observational witness that qualifies and explains a capacity outcome, **not a reproduction key**. Admission depends on the bucket's *membership* (count + genuinely-new fragment ids ≤ limit, §1.3.5), which the public library surface does not expose, so two worlds with equal counts but different membership can admit differently (the engine records the count and, when a unit does fail, its `{currentSize, missing, limit}`); this spec does **not** flag a run merely because a bucket was non-empty — a call's verdicts do not depend on admission, and flagging every run in a busy Viewer would make the flag meaningless — it flags a run **when admission actually decided an outcome**; (b) each such gap records `failure.capacityDetails`; (c) cause `bucket-capacity` is added to `environmentSensitiveCauses`. Admission only gates a call, it never changes a call's verdicts, so a run *without* such a gap in a dirty world reproduces the fresh-world pair set and ledger (AC-19 tests this); a run *with* one keeps every evaluated unit's verdicts but not the identity of the gap units.
- **Foreign placement activity (R11-3).** A direct `findClashes` caller (e.g. the Clash Finder extension) that grows the placement state of a touched model bumps its placement revision and aborts an in-flight TLAS unit (F13). The bounded retry (§7.5) absorbs an occasional abort; if it is exhausted the unit is `failed:stale-placement` and the run's `environmentSensitiveCauses` contains `placement-contention`, because the same inputs in a quiet world would evaluate that unit. (The engine identifies this case by the engine-owned `placementGeneration` being *unchanged*; a host-signalled change is `discarded:input-changed` and invalidates the run instead, §7.5.) An `evaluated` unit's verdicts are unaffected by how many attempts it took, so a retry that succeeds adds no cause. **Nothing is promised about which units a rerun would evaluate**, and comparisons involving such a run carry the same `environmentSensitive` note as the other causes (§15.4).
- **Geometry readiness.** A proxy that becomes available (or unavailable) between two runs, with no change to any digested field, changes which pairs are found. The engine keeps a dedicated **exact** count of `geometry-not-yet-available` occurrences (independent of the retained-reason cap, §12.4); if it is > 0 (or `unclassifiedOccurrences > 0`, which could hide it) the run's `environmentSensitiveCauses` contains `geometry-readiness`. Those occurrences already make the affected elements `covered-caveated` and the run `integrity: caveated` — so the run can never be presented as clean — but **nothing is promised about which pairs a rerun would find**, and equal `pairSetDigest`s between two such runs are not expected. The engine does not add a readiness witness to the digests (whether the Viewer exposes a reliable one is unevidenced; OQ-18).

**The determinism contract above holds for runs with `environmentSensitive = false`.** Strict input-only determinism would need collision-core changes (§21.2 (h) for the cache; a readiness seam for the proxy; cross-caller serialization for placement) and is not pursued.

**Scope of the promise.** The determinism promise is stated for one build, one browser/runtime and one device class; cross-machine reproducibility of *discrete* results is expected from the deterministic operation-count budgets but is **separately evidenced**, not established by same-environment reload tests (AC-6). Float evidence is compared by tolerance (§15.4), never by bit equality (§13.1).

`determinismClass` is recorded in provenance. For `time-sensitive` runs the engine **promises no repeatability** whenever a `crossing-budget-exhausted` caveat is present, and a differing rerun is not by itself a defect. This spec does not promote either mode to deterministic — that is a tracked library follow-up (PC design §7.3), not this layer's to fix.

### 15.2 Comparability preconditions

A rerun is *comparable* to a base only to the extent these match; the engine reports each rather than asserting:

`semanticDigest` equal; every `modelKey` has equal `modelVersion`; `scopeDigests` (population digest and per-root subtree digests) equal; `libraryVersion` equal and non-null; same `mode`/`clearance`; **same `pairSemantics` (a hard precondition — a difference makes the sets `incomparable`, §15.4)**; neither run `environmentSensitive` (otherwise gaps — and, for `geometry-readiness`, found pairs — are not comparable evidence, §15.1). **Placement equality is never verified** (`placementVerified: false`); across sessions it is reported `unverifiable`, within one session it is `same-generation` only if no `notifyPlacementChanged` intervened. Sizing options and `broadPhase` do **not** affect comparability (invariant P2), and `broadPhase: 'linear'` is the recommended cross-check arm (§22 AC-4).

### 15.3 Rerun

`engine.rerun(runIdOrResultSet, { definitionRevision?, runOptions? }) → Promise<RunHandle>` (**asynchronous, R21-1**: both a raw set and a by-id set must be captured/read and validated first, §13.6, so errors need a Promise; `startRun` stays synchronous) is `startRun` with the embedded (or a newer) definition and `derivedFromRunId` set. A base set with `definition: null` (pre-resolution seal, §13.1) has nothing embedded to rerun: without `definitionRevision` the returned Promise **rejects** with `InvalidOptionsError('no-embedded-definition')` — like every capture, validation, option and admission failure of `rerun`, it is delivered as a **rejection, never a synchronous throw** (R21-1/R19-1, §18, AC-11). Prior Result Sets are **never mutated**. There is no incremental/partial rerun in v1 (§23): a rerun re-plans and re-executes everything against a fresh input snapshot.

### 15.4 Comparison

`compareResultSets(base, next, opts?) → Promise<Comparison>` (**asynchronous, R15-4**): a single **O(n + m) merge over the two sets' canonical `PairId` order** run in the deadline-aware slices of §17.3 (yielding between slices of `aggregationSliceMaxItems`), with every per-pair `getPairStatus` served from **indexes built once** over each ledger (`rootOwners` and per-unit coverage maps; a per-pair linear ledger scan is non-conforming); `opts.signal` (`AbortSignal`, checked at slice boundaries, `DerivationAbortedError`, no partial output) and the shared `maxConcurrentDerivations` bound (`DerivationBusyError`) apply; total classified entries are counted **incrementally before allocation** against `maxComparisonEntries` (provisional 2,000,000, ceiling 10,000,000) and exceeding it rejects `ComparisonTooLargeError{limit, observed}` with no partial comparison. **The join key is the `PairId`, never the `ResultId`**: `ResultId` embeds `semanticDigest` (§4.4), so the same element pair under a changed mode/clearance/selection has two different `ResultId`s by design, and matching on them would report every result as added+removed. Each entry therefore carries the `pairId` plus whichever `ResultId`(s) exist (`baseResultId`, `nextResultId`; equal when the `semanticDigest`s are equal), so a workflow layer can look up its attached state under the id it used.

```js
/** @typedef {Object} Comparison
 *  @property {{ sameDefinition: boolean, sameModelVersions: boolean, sameEnumeration: boolean,
 *              sameLibraryVersion: boolean|null, baseDeterminismClass: 'deterministic'|'time-sensitive', nextDeterminismClass: 'deterministic'|'time-sensitive', determinismClass: 'deterministic'|'time-sensitive' /* the WEAKER of the two: time-sensitive if either side is */,
 *              placement: 'unverifiable'|'same-generation', notes: string[] }} comparability
 *  @property {null | {reason: 'pair-semantics-changed', base: 'ordered'|'unordered', next: 'ordered'|'unordered'}} incomparable   non-null ⇒ every class below is empty (§15.4 pair-semantics gate, H5); `identityConflicts` is still populated
 *  @property {Array<{pairId, baseResultId, nextResultId}>} unchanged
 *  @property {Array<{pairId, baseResultId, nextResultId, changedFields: string[]}>} changed   present in both; evidence beyond tolerance, flags, mode or clearance differ
 *  @property {Array<{pairId, nextResultId}>} added                       in next only, and base had covered+clean status for it
 *  @property {Array<{pairId, nextResultId}>} addedUnverifiedBase         in next only, but base did NOT cover it cleanly (may have been missed, not new)
 *  @property {Array<{pairId, baseResultId}>} resolved                    in base only, and next covered it cleanly (positive absence)
 *  @property {Array<{pairId, baseResultId}>} removedUnverifiedNext       in base only, but next did NOT cover it cleanly (absence proves nothing)
 *  @property {Array<{pairId}>} identityConflicts                         pairs involving a modelKey recorded with different modelVersions in the two sets (host contract violation, §4.2): classified nowhere else
 */
```

- "Covered cleanly" for a pair means `getPairStatus` (§12.5) returns `covered-clean` in that Result Set — i.e. the pair's roots were actually paired in an evaluated unit and **none** of the taint-rule conditions of §12.5 holds (element-attributed caveat, `target-model`/`unit` taint from an unattributable diagnostic, duplicate-evidence conflict, non-current freshness, **mixed-owner provenance**). **A pair whose covering unit reported an unattributable diagnostic (`fragment-not-loaded`, `fragment-owner-unresolvable`, …) therefore never counts as covered cleanly, and can never be reported `resolved` or `added`** — it lands in `removedUnverifiedNext` / `addedUnverifiedBase`. Element-level status is **not** sufficient (two elements can each be fully covered without their mutual pair ever having been asked about, e.g. after a definition revision adds a new pairing). `undetermined` and `not-required` both classify as unverified.
- **Pair-semantics gate (H5).** Before any join, `compareResultSets` compares the two sets' `pairSemantics` (§4.5, derived from `mode`). **If they differ** (one `containment`, the other `volume-overlap`/`surface-intersection`/`clearance`), the sets are **`incomparable`**: the result is `{ incomparable: { reason: 'pair-semantics-changed', base: 'ordered'|'unordered', next: 'ordered'|'unordered' }, comparability, unchanged: [], changed: [], added: [], addedUnverifiedBase: [], resolved: [], removedUnverifiedNext: [], identityConflicts }` — **no `PairId` is joined, and no pair is classified `unchanged`/`changed`/`added`/`resolved`**, in either direction of the change. (An ordered `(x, y)` and an unordered `(min, max)` `PairId` for the same two elements are different identities, §4.4; reporting one as removed and the other as added, or as `changed`, would be a false statement about geometry.) `identityConflicts` is still reported. Only the two set-level summaries (counts, axes) remain available for display.
- **Within one pair-semantics class** the `PairId` is a valid join key. Ordered class: `(x, y)` and `(y, x)` are distinct pairs by definition (§4.4); a test whose `selectionA`/`selectionB` were swapped therefore reports its converse pairs through the ordinary `added`/`resolved`/`unverified` classes (each verified through `getPairStatus` in the other set), and `comparability.notes` carries `containment-orientation-reversed` when the swapped selections are detected (equal `semanticDigest` of the swapped definition is not required to produce the note). Unordered class: a change of `mode` between two symmetric modes (e.g. `volume-overlap` → `clearance`) or of `clearance` keeps the same canonical unordered `PairId`.
- When the two `semanticDigest`s differ (within one class), `added`/`resolved` are produced only where `getPairStatus` verifies the pair in the *other* set; when `mode` or `clearance` differs, evidence is not comparable and every pair present in both is reported under `changed` with `changedFields: ['mode']` (or `clearance`) rather than `unchanged`. The `unverified` classes exist precisely so the diff never turns a gap or caveat into a claimed resolution — the batch-scale form of "empty ≠ negative".
- `changed` uses tolerances (`distanceTolerance`, `pointTolerance`: finite numbers ≥ 0, provisional defaults `1e-6` world units, overridable via `opts`; unknown option keys, `NaN`, `±Infinity` and negatives reject the returned Promise with `InvalidOptionsError`, A8) because float evidence is not digested (§13.1). An equal `pairSetDigest` (§13.1) short-circuits **only pair membership and flag comparison** (no `added`/`removed`/flag `changed` can exist); it says nothing about evidence, so evidence of every common pair is still compared by tolerance (two runs with the same pairs and flags but distances `0.0417` vs `0.0470` are `changed`).  Equal `pairSetDigest` says **nothing** about whether the two runs asked the same question (two empty result lists hash identically whatever their tests): comparability is always decided from `testRef.semanticDigest` independently, never inferred from `pairSetDigest`.
- **Determinism class of a comparison (R16-5).** The two sets may have different modes (unordered modes compare, §15.4), hence different classes: `comparability` records both (`baseDeterminismClass`, `nextDeterminismClass`) and `determinismClass` as the **weaker** of the two (`time-sensitive` if either is). Every `added`/`resolved` entry carries `timeSensitive: true` iff the weaker class is `time-sensitive`, and `comparability.notes` names which side(s) weaken it.
- If either set is `environmentSensitive` (§8.3), `comparability.notes` says so: differing *gaps* between the two runs are then not, by themselves, evidence of a model change (§15.1); the `unverified` classes already keep such gaps from becoming claimed resolutions.
- A `scopeDigests` mismatch (same `modelKey`, hence same `modelVersion` by §4.2) means the model's *enumerated content* changed between runs (e.g. it finished streaming); the comparison still computes but `comparability.notes` says so. A `modelVersion` mismatch for one `modelKey` is a **host identity-contract violation**, not a note: see `identityConflicts` (§4.2, §15.4).

---

## 16. Stale-model, epoch, and lifecycle interactions

### 16.1 Event matrix

| Event | `queued`/`planning` | `running` | Terminal (sealed) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Model unloaded** (`registry.unregister` + `world.evictModel`) | binding dead ⇒ run `failed` with planning gap `model-not-bound` (or lease invalid at bind) | units touching it: `skipped/failed:model-evicted` (lease pre-check §7.4); other units run; `coverage: partial`; `freshness: stale` (`model-evicted:<modelKey>`) | read-time freshness → `stale` |
| **Model reloaded** (same `modelKey`, new `Model`) | new run binds the new object | run stays on its **snapshot** binding (now dead) ⇒ as above; the engine never re-binds mid-run; `bindingGeneration` bumped | read-time `stale` (`model-reloaded:<modelKey>`) |
| **Fragment population changed** (streaming growth, fragment replaced/unloaded — no host call) — *after seal, read-time `getFreshness` re-digests and reports `stale` (§16.4)* | plan-time digests are taken during planning; a change during planning yields a new digest for that plan, no drift concept yet | pre/post scope digests differ ⇒ `skipped/discarded:scope-drift`, run `invalidated` (§16.3.2); digest unavailable ⇒ gap `scope-unverifiable` | read-time `getFreshness` re-digests the recorded scopes ⇒ `stale` (`scope-drift:<modelKey>`), or `unverifiable` if not computable; the sealed set records `provenance.scopeProof` |
| **Placement changed** (`notifyPlacementChanged`) | new run sees new generation | §16.3 (taint + `invalidated`) | read-time `stale` (`placement-changed:<modelKey>`) |
| **World disposed** | `failed` | `failed:world-disposed`, pending `not-run` | unaffected (sealed) |
| **Foreign growth revision bump** (another caller's extraction) | — | `ModelPlacementChangedError` with unchanged generation ⇒ bounded retry (§7.5) | — |
| **Definition edited/archived/deleted** | no effect on an already-started run once its admission check has passed (a delete *before* that check fails a persisting run, §5.5) | no effect on execution (embedded snapshot); a delete before the set is persisted makes that persistence `failed` (`TestDeletedError`, §5.5) | no effect on the sealed set |
| **New model registered** | binds only if registered **before** the `queued → planning` snapshot; a run naming a still-unbound key at that instant fails `model-not-bound` (a queued run's scope never depends on timing after the snapshot) | out of scope (snapshot) | — |

### 16.2 Placement generation

The library cannot know a model was moved unless the caller says so (`invalidateModelPlacement`, F13), and its revision counter also moves on extraction growth, so it cannot serve as the engine's staleness signal (§1.3). The engine therefore owns `placementGeneration` per binding:

- `engine.invalidateModelPlacement(modelKey)` is the **recommended single call**: it invokes `world.invalidateModelPlacement(model)` and increments `placementGeneration`, so the two halves cannot be forgotten independently.
- A host that calls `world.invalidateModelPlacement` directly **must also** call `registry.notifyPlacementChanged(modelKey)`; otherwise the engine cannot see the change. This is documented as a host obligation, not enforced (the library likewise cannot verify its own caller, M2 §6.5).
- `broadPhase: 'linear'` re-reads placement each call and needs no invalidation for *correctness of one call*, but a mid-run change still yields a **mixed-placement** run, so the same handling applies to both broad-phase choices.

### 16.3 Taint, scope proof and invalidation

At the **`queued → planning` transition** the engine atomically captures, for every model the definition names, the binding object (identity), `modelVersion`, and `(bindingGeneration, placementGeneration)`; this is the run snapshot. It re-reads the generations at each unit's start.

1. If, when a unit completes, any touched binding's **`placementGeneration`** differs from that unit's *start* value, the unit is **`discarded:input-changed`**: its pairs are **not** merged (they may mix placements). Mixed-placement results are treated as worse than absent ones. **`bindingGeneration` is deliberately not an input-change signal (resolves a conflict with §16.1):** a reload (or unload) means the run's snapshot `Model` is gone, and nothing in the run mixes it with a different one, so it is handled by the lease and digest paths — the unit is `skipped`/`failed:model-evicted` (§7.4) or, if it had already finished, `discarded:scope-unverifiable` (the post-check cannot digest a dead binding) — the run **continues** with a gap and `freshness` becomes `stale` (§16.1, §16.4). Only `placementGeneration` and scope drift invalidate a run.
2. **Input-scope proof (bracketed digest).** Executing a unit follows this protocol, in the executor, for **every** scope the unit reads — each whole-model target (population digest), each explicit target root list and the unit's source roots (subtree digests), **and (R20-1) the whole-population digest of every model the unit uses as a source through a `kind:'model'` selection** (a fragment that appears *outside* the instance-tree roots changes no source-root subtree digest, yet changes the model's selection; the population digest is what sees it, so it is bracketed around the source call exactly like a whole-model target's), all per §6.2.1:
   1. **Pre-check.** Immediately before issuing `findClashes`, call `digestScope` for each scope and compare with the *planning-time* digest recorded for it. Any mismatch ⇒ the unit is **not issued** and is `skipped:scope-drift`; a `digestScope` failure (tree unavailable, dead binding, thrown error) ⇒ `skipped:scope-unverifiable`.
   2. **Call.** `world.findClashes` (§7.2).
   3. **Post-check.** Immediately after it settles (resolved, or thrown-and-mapped), compute the digests again and compare with the planning-time digests. Any mismatch ⇒ the unit is `discarded:scope-drift` (pairs and diagnostics **not** merged); a failure to compute ⇒ `discarded:scope-unverifiable`.
   4. Only if both checks are `equal` for every scope is the unit eligible to be `evaluated`; the ledger records `scopeProof: 'equal'`. A retry (§7.5) repeats the whole protocol.

   Deterministic semantics: the comparison is exact string equality of digests, so the outcome is a pure function of the (mockable) enumerator's answers — no thresholds, no counts-only heuristics. An increase, decrease, replacement or status flip of the population is identically drift. `placementCount` is **not** used as evidence (F18).
3. **Invalidation.** `scope-drift` (either check) is an input change exactly like a `placementGeneration` change: at the next unit boundary the run transitions to **`invalidated`** and seals; earlier `evaluated` units are retained and flagged `freshness: stale` (reason `scope-drift:<modelKey>`), everything else is a gap. `scope-unverifiable` is **not** drift: the unit is a gap (`coverage: partial`), the run continues, and a later unit may succeed. (A `placementGeneration` change observed at a unit boundary is handled the same way: if any touched binding's `placementGeneration` differs from the *run snapshot*, the run is `invalidated`.) **The end-of-plan check counts as a boundary:** a `scope-drift` or `input-changed` outcome on the *last* unit yields `invalidated`, never `completed` (§8.2).
4. **Invariant I-scope (normative).** *A unit is `evaluated` only if its `scopeProof` is `equal`.* Since `coverage: full` requires every unit `evaluated` (§8.3), **no `completed ∧ full ∧ current` Result Set can exist unless equality with the planning snapshot was demonstrated for every unit's target scope (and source/explicit scope).** The method is recorded as `provenance.scopeProof = { method: 'bracketed-digest', digestVersion: 1 }`.
5. **Declared residual — the proof is bracketed, not continuous.** Equality is shown at two instants around the call. A population that changes *and reverts* wholly inside one call (an A→B→A excursion during the library's own extraction yields) is undetectable by any caller-side mechanism (F18). The engine therefore records `method: 'bracketed-digest'`, never `'exact'`, so the guarantee is not overstated; making it exact needs an optional additive library change reporting the extracted target population per call (§21.2 (f)). AC-8 pins this boundary with a documented-limitation test.

### 16.4 Freshness

- **Freshness is a bracketed, whole-operation check (R15-2).** Read-time freshness is not a sequence of independent checks (a model already checked could change while a later one is being digested). It is: **(1)** one `registry.snapshotBindings(all modelKeys in provenance.models)` (`snapshot₁`) before any scope is read; **(2)** digest **every** recorded scope of every model (`digest₁`); **(3)** `snapshot₂`; **(4)** digest every scope **again** (`digest₂`) and require each to equal both `digest₁` and the sealed value; **(5)** a **final** `snapshot₃` taken after `digest₂` and immediately before the result is returned. Every touched binding must be alive with equal `bindingGeneration`/`placementGeneration`/`modelVersion` across **all three** snapshots — any touched model whose binding died or whose values differ between any two ⇒ `stale` with the reason (an unrelated registry mutation that leaves every touched binding equal does **not** matter). Placement is the case that needs `snapshot₃`: scope digests exclude transforms, so a `notifyPlacementChanged` landing during `digest₂` is visible *only* through the registry. `current` is returned **only when all brackets agree**; a first-pass/second-pass mismatch, or either differing from the sealed digest, ⇒ `stale` (`scope-drift:<modelKey>`); an uncomputable digest ⇒ `unverifiable`. So a model changed while a later model was being checked is caught by the second pass, without any host notification and without a content-revision witness. The residual is the same declared one as execution (§16.3 item 5): a change that reverts wholly between the two passes is undetectable, and `getFreshness` cost is **two** scope passes plus three registry snapshots, measured in AC-12. `engine.canClaimNoClashes` inherits this exactly.
- **What `current` means.** `freshness: current` establishes that the recorded models are alive, unmoved (by the host's own notifications) and extraction-identical (scope digests) — nothing more. It does **not** establish that a rerun would find the same pairs: renderer geometry availability (§1.3.6) and the world's epoch-cache state (§1.3.5) are outside it, and are reported separately by `environmentSensitive` and by the `geometry-not-yet-available` diagnostics, which already keep the run `caveated`.
- At seal: `outcome.freshness = 'stale'` if any binding died, any generation moved, or any unit ended `scope-drift` (`scope-drift:<modelKey>`) during the run; else `'current'`.
- At read: `getFreshness(resultSet, {registry, enumerator?}) → Promise<Freshness>` is **asynchronous** and recomputes against the current registry **and the live model content**: it re-digests every scope recorded in the sealed `provenance.models[].scopeDigests` (whole-model population and per-root subtrees, §6.2.1) through the `ElementEnumerator` and compares them with the sealed values — a mismatch (e.g. fragments that finished streaming after the run, with no host signal) ⇒ `stale`, reason `scope-drift:<modelKey>`; no enumerator, or a digest that cannot be computed ⇒ at best `unverifiable`, **never `current`**. It then checks: same `registrySessionId` (the registry's own nonce — so a set produced by *another engine over the same world* is verifiable, while a set from a different registry lifetime/session is not), all bindings alive, all generations equal ⇒ `current`; any binding dead / generation moved ⇒ `stale` with reasons; a live binding whose `modelVersion` differs from the recorded one for the same `modelKey` ⇒ `stale` with reason `model-version-conflict:<modelKey>` (a host identity-contract violation, §4.2 — never a silent match); a Result Set from a **different session** (loaded from a store) ⇒ `unverifiable` (placement cannot be checked) unless a `modelVersion` mismatch makes it `stale`.

### 16.5 Epochs

`loadEpoch` never appears in durable data. It exists only in non-persisted run telemetry (`epochByModelKey`) for debugging. Eviction/epoch death is handled entirely through lease checks (§7.4). A model that has been evicted can never be re-admitted (F5), so "retry against the same model" is meaningless — recovery is a reload plus a new run.

### 16.6 Handler safety

`unregister` and any engine hook wired to `BEFORE_MODEL_UNLOAD_EVENT` **never throw** and never `await`: an uncaught throw aborts the Viewer's teardown of every remaining model (M1). They only mark state; runs observe it at the next boundary.

### 16.7 Settlement precedence (normative)

"First terminal transition wins" (§10.2) says nothing about *which* transition is first when one unit settlement or boundary surfaces several facts together — for example a cancel requested while the unit's model is unloaded and re-registered, `findClashes` throws, and the post-check digest also fails. The result must not depend on callback scheduling, so facts are **collected from committed state first, then resolved by this fixed order**. Nothing is decided until the whole set of facts for that settlement is in hand.

**A. Unit outcome.** For one attempt, the primary cause is the highest-precedence fact observed:

| Prec. | Fact | Unit outcome |
|---|---|---|
| U1 | world disposed / `WorldDisposedError` | `failed:world-disposed` |
| U2 | any other unmapped error; `validateUnitOutcome` failure | `failed:internal-error` |
| U3 | executor returned more than `maxPairsPerOutcome` pairs, or merging would exceed `maxResultsPerRun` | `failed:result-cap` |
| U4 | `placementGeneration` moved, or `ModelPlacementChangedError` with a moved generation | `discarded:input-changed` |
| U5 | pre- or post-check scope digest mismatch | `skipped:`/`discarded:scope-drift` |
| U6 | lease dead, `ModelEvictedError`, or binding died/re-registered (`bindingGeneration`) | `failed:`/`skipped:model-evicted` |
| U7 | a required digest could not be computed | `skipped:`/`discarded:scope-unverifiable` |
| U8 | `CapacityExceededError`, `ExtractionLimitExceededError`, `ObjectTreeUnavailableError`, retries exhausted (`stale-placement`), `unit-cap`, `target-too-large` | the corresponding gap cause (§7.5) |
| U9 | none of the above | `evaluated` |

Every *other* fact observed at the same settlement is kept as a **secondary cause** in the unit's ledger record (`secondaryCauses`: **the first 8 distinct** causes, ascending by code-unit order). **The unit-level list may therefore be truncated, deterministically and by design:** a settlement that observes more than 8 distinct secondary facts drops the tail in that fixed canonical order — expected, not a defect, because `RunOutcome.reasons` (below) remains the **complete** record of every observed fact at run level. Secondary causes are diagnostic and **excluded from `ledgerDigest`** (which of them a real timing race exposes can vary; the primary cause is a pure function of the recorded facts). A unit whose primary cause is U3–U8 never merges pairs or diagnostics.

**B. Run status.** After a settlement, and at every boundary (including planning slices, the end-of-plan check, and a pending `engine.dispose()`), let *F* = any unit primary cause in U1–U3 or any planning cause other than `cancelled`; *I* = any unit primary cause U4/U5 or a `placementGeneration` change against the run snapshot; *C* = a cancel or dispose request. Then, in this order:

1. *F* ⇒ **`failed`** (a planning cause other than `cancelled` also wins over a cancel requested in the same slice);
2. else *I* ⇒ **`invalidated`**;
3. else *C* ⇒ **`cancelled`** (in-flight unit drained first, §10.2);
4. else all leaf units terminal ⇒ **`completed`**; otherwise continue.

**C. Pairwise matrix — an in-flight unit settles while a cancel or `engine.dispose()` is pending (merge/discard and final status, both normative).** A unit that is *not* in flight at the request is untouched by it (queued units become `not-run:cancelled`).

| In-flight unit's primary cause (table A) | Pairs/diagnostics of that unit | Ledger record | Final status |
|---|---|---|---|
| U9 `evaluated` (drained) | **merged** (§10.2) | `evaluated` | `cancelled` |
| U8 capacity / extraction-limit / tree-unavailable / retries exhausted | none exist | that gap cause | `cancelled` |
| U7 `scope-unverifiable` | discarded | `skipped:`/`discarded:scope-unverifiable` | `cancelled` |
| U6 `model-evicted` (unload, or unload + re-register) | discarded | `failed:`/`skipped:model-evicted` | `cancelled` (`freshness: stale`) |
| U5 `scope-drift` | **discarded** | `skipped:`/`discarded:scope-drift` | **`invalidated`** |
| U4 `input-changed` (`placementGeneration`) | **discarded** | `discarded:input-changed` | **`invalidated`** |
| U3 `result-cap` | discarded (whole unit) | `failed:result-cap` | **`failed`** |
| U2 internal error / `validateUnitOutcome` failure | discarded | `failed:internal-error` | **`failed`** |
| U1 world disposed | none exist | `failed:world-disposed` | **`failed`** |

Rows are independent of *which* of {user cancel, `engine.dispose()`} was requested (`engine.dispose()` differs only in its `engine-disposed` reason and in cancelling every run). Two or more simultaneous facts on one unit use table A first (the row of the highest-precedence fact), then this matrix. Every row is an acceptance case in AC-21, executed under every ordering of the facts.

`RunOutcome.reasons` lists **every** observed fact (`cancel-requested`, `engine-disposed`, `unit:…`, `model-evicted:<modelKey>`, …), ascending, so a run sealed `invalidated` or `failed` still records that a cancel had been requested. `freshness` (§16.4) and `coverage` are computed from the ledger regardless of which status won. Retained results are those of units whose primary cause is U9 (§10.2 retention rules unchanged); an `engine.dispose()` request is a cancel request with reason `engine-disposed` and is subject to the same order (a world disposal observed at the same boundary is *F*, not *C*). AC-21 covers the combinations.

---

## 17. Memory and performance boundaries

### 17.1 Provisional caps

All values are **provisional** — chosen by reasoning, revisable by measurement, exactly the posture `collision/constants.js` documents. Recorded in provenance; folded into `ledgerDigest` where they shape the plan.

**This table is the single normative source of default values (R21-I1).** Other sections name an option and cross-reference §17.1 rather than restating its number; where a number stated elsewhere in this spec disagrees with this table, **this table governs**. Hard ceilings live in §17.1.1 and are governed the same way.

| Parameter | Default | Basis / evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `maxSourceFragmentsPerUnit` | 200 | Bounds one unit's cost and thus cancel latency (§10.2). M4: whole-Structure source (543 instances) used 26% of the 20,000 clearance cap, so capacity is not the binding reason; latency is, and unit makespan is **unmeasured**. |
| `maxSourceDbIdsPerUnit` | 500 | Bounds extraction walk per call (F9: extraction is per-call, not cached). |
| `maxUnitsPerRun` | 10,000 | Fails `plan-too-large` at planning, before any work. Same-model bisection alone is ~(owner-count − 1) units. |
| `maxResultsPerRun` | 100,000 | **Whole-unit boundary rule:** each unit's results are canonically sorted (§11.3) before merging; if merging a unit would exceed the cap, that **entire unit is discarded** (`failed:result-cap`, with its pair-space weight recorded as a gap) and the run stops (`failed`, reason `result-cap`). The retained set is therefore always a union of whole units in plan order — identical under TLAS and linear, whose library pair order differs. `coverage: partial`. |
| `maxRetainedDiagnostics` | 10,000 | §12.4; counts stay exact. |
| `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` | **10,000** (was 100,000) | Definition size guard (V5). Lowered because element validation is the one synchronous, non-sliceable pass (H6, §5.3, §17.3); the value is **provisional until AC-12 measures it** (the plan must lower it if the measured synchronous validation of two cap-sized selections exceeds `frameBudgetMs` on the reference device). |
| `maxValidationIssues` | 100 | §5.3 — bounds the synchronous issue list; `issuesTruncated` set beyond it. |
| `maxTargetModelsPerUnit` | 4 | §6.4 — target models per unit (H2). Bounds per-call extraction fan-out. Unmeasured. |
| `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit` | 20,000 | §6.4 — total target weight per unit (H2); equals the library's per-epoch ceiling (F9), so any whole-model target the library can cover fits alone. A whole-model target above it becomes a `not-run:target-too-large` gap. Unmeasured; AC-18 characterizes raw in-call diagnostics/latency at this cap. |
| `maxPlacementRetries` | 2 | §7.5. |
| `broadPhase` | `'tlas'` | §7.2 — the only viable broad phase for multi-instance work; `'linear'` is the oracle (its 1,000,000-pair ceiling rejects large plans). Recorded in provenance. |
| `interUnitYield` | `'animation-frame'` | §7.3 — animation-frame-aligned where available, else it degrades to a macrotask; `'macrotask'` forces the latter. |
| `persist` | `true` | §13.5.1 — omitted ⇒ `true`: the set is persisted when a `ResultStore` is configured (states `pending`→`saved`/`failed`, deleted-test admission check §5.5 applies), and with **no** store configured the state is `skipped` (`no-store`) exactly as for `persist: false` (`reason` distinguishes `'no-store'` from `'persist-false'`). |
| `persistTimeoutMs` | 5,000 | §13.5.1 — bounds only how long `done`/the terminal event wait; never the write. |
| `maxEntriesPerSelection` | 1,024 | V4 (§5.3). |
| `maxQueuedRuns` | `maxResidentResultSets − 1` | §7.3, §13.5.2; slot accounting is the real bound, this can only lower it. |
| `maxResidentResultSets` | 4 | §13.5.2 — hard cap on resident sets **plus** in-flight runs; unsaved sets never auto-evicted. |
| `maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome` / `maxDistinctReasons` | 200,000 / 64 | §7.1, §12.4 — legal-length diagnostics beyond these collapse (exact totals, exact counts only for retained reasons, one `omittedReasonOccurrences` count, fail-safe unit taints); *illegal* diagnostics (reason > 128 code units, non-plain, unclassifiable) are rejected, not collapsed. `maxDistinctReasons` also bounds `countsByReason` per run. |
| `maxPlanReferences` | 500,000 | §6.5.1 — **every planning-time record** (`planRecords`: root/owner/unreachable/mixed/digest/`unresolvedSelections`/`hierarchyInternal` rows, planning gaps, initial unit and range records), checked incrementally before allocation. |
| `maxLedgerRecords` | 1,000,000 | §6.5.1 — one run-wide count of every retained plan **and** ledger record (tables, digest rows, unit records, gaps, taint entries); exhaustion collapses element taints into a unit-wide `taint-overflow`, never fails the run. Must be ≥ `maxPlanReferences + 2 × maxUnitsPerRun` (`maxUnitsPerRun` slots are reserved for per-unit `taint-overflow`, the rest is discretionary runtime capacity; §6.5.1 lists the exhaustion rule for every runtime record class). Count bound only; bytes unmeasured. |
| `maxPairsPerOutcome` | `maxResultsPerRun` | §7.1 — pairs one executor outcome may return. |
| `maxElementTaintsPerUnit` | 2,000 | §12.2.1 — per-unit exact element-taint entries; overflow collapses to a unit-wide `taint-overflow` taint. |
| `maxAnnotationBytes` | 8 MiB | §14.5 — exact UTF-8 byte length of the snapshot's canonical JSON, counted incrementally, cap for one annotation snapshot. |
| `maxAnnotatedNames` | 16 | §13.5.2, §14.5 — bounds an annotation snapshot. |
| `progressMinIntervalMs` | 100 | §9.2. |
| `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic` | 8 | §12.2 — unit ids kept per retained diagnostic. |
| `sliceBudgetMs` | 4 | §17.3 — per-slice deadline (half of `frameBudgetMs`). |
| `maxConcurrentStoreReads` / `maxQueuedStoreReads` | 1 / 8 | §13.5.2 — transient read-through/validate/freeze concurrency and FIFO depth; beyond it `ReadCapacityError`. Unmeasured. |
| `maxComparisonEntries` | 2,000,000 | §15.4 — classified entries one comparison may produce (counted before allocation); over-cap ⇒ `ComparisonTooLargeError`, no partial result. Unmeasured. |
| `reconcileTimeoutMs` | 10,000 | §13.5 — bound on the single ambiguous-outcome reconciliation read; on expiry the attempt settles `failed` (`error.ambiguous`). Never applies to an ordinary `put`. |
| `maxCapturedNodes` | 16,000,000 | §13.6 — operational reader limit on captured nodes (≤ `SCHEMA_MAX_NODES`); above it a valid set is `ResultSetTooLargeError`. Cross-constrained with the run defaults (producer/reader invariant, §13.6). Provisional until AC-12. |
| `maxStoredResultBytes` | 536,870,912 (512 MiB) | §13.6 — store-boundary serialized-size guard for external Result Set reads (`ResultStore.get(runId, {maxBytes})`); provisional until the memory calibration (§17.1). |
| `maxConcurrentAnnotations` | 2 | §14.5 — engine-wide in-flight `annotate` jobs (one per run); beyond it `AnnotationBusyError`. Adds `× maxAnnotationBytes` to the memory bound. |
| `maxConcurrentDerivations` | 2 | §14.4 — simultaneous `summarize`/`groupBy`/`filter` runs; beyond it `DerivationBusyError`. |
| `maxFacetsPerGroupBy` / `maxFilterClauses` / `maxGroupMemberships` | 4 / 32 / 1,000,000 | §14.4 — bound derived grouping/filter work before it is done; over-cap ⇒ `InvalidOptionsError` / `GroupingTooLargeError` (no partial result). Unmeasured; AC-13 cap-sized case. |
| `aggregationSliceMaxItems` | 2,000 | §17.3; sized so a slice stays well inside `frameBudgetMs`; **unmeasured** (AC-12 verifies). |

**Memory-ceiling calibration gate (R11-7; R19 Q2).** *No default in this section — including the 16,000,000-node capture ceiling and the 512 MiB stored-result allowance (the table's `maxCapturedNodes` and `maxStoredResultBytes`) — may be implemented as a frozen constant before the AC-12 calibration; until then they ship only as provisional, overridable defaults.* The caps above are *count* bounds; a count bound is a memory bound only once bytes-per-record is known. The worst-case retained size of the resident sets is `maxResidentResultSets × (maxLedgerRecords × bytesPerLedgerRecord + maxResultsPerRun × bytesPerResult + maxRetainedDiagnostics × bytesPerDiagnostic + maxAnnotationBytes)` (plan tables, ledger, results, diagnostics, annotation), **plus the transient terms that are also bounded by engine limits (R21-3):** `maxConcurrentStoreReads × 2 × S_set` (captured clone + source graph), `maxConcurrentDerivations × (2 × S_set + indexes + output records)` (a comparison holds two captured sets), and `maxConcurrentAnnotations × maxAnnotationBytes`, where `S_set = maxCapturedNodes × bytesPerNode`, and at the provisional defaults **no one has shown that this fits any browser tab**. So the defaults are **not frozen** by this spec: at plan time AC-12(c) measures the per-record figures on the reference device and reports that worst-case estimate, and the plan must **lower `maxLedgerRecords`/`maxPlanReferences`/`maxResultsPerRun`/`maxResidentResultSets` until the estimate fits a memory budget the product owner confirms** (no budget is confirmed today — the same open state as the responsiveness target, §17.4), recording the measured figures beside the frozen values. A per-set *byte* limit (`maxResultSetBytes` with estimated-size accounting) was considered and **not adopted**: estimated-byte accounting on plain JS objects is itself unreliable across engines, and calibrated count caps with a hard `maxResidentResultSets` give the same fail-closed behavior with far less machinery; it can be revisited if AC-12 shows counts are a poor proxy.

### 17.1.1 Option validation

**Ownership (R15-3).** Every option below is exactly one of two kinds. **RunOptions** (per run, passed to `startRun`/`rerun`; recorded in `provenance.runOptions`; those that shape the plan — the sizing options — are folded into `ledgerDigest`): `broadPhase`, `maxSourceFragmentsPerUnit`, `maxSourceDbIdsPerUnit`, `maxTargetModelsPerUnit`, `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit`, `maxUnitsPerRun`, `maxResultsPerRun`, `maxRetainedDiagnostics`, `maxPlacementRetries`, `progressMinIntervalMs`, `interUnitYield`, `persist`, `maxPlanReferences`, `maxLedgerRecords`, `maxPairsPerOutcome`, `maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome`, `maxDistinctReasons`, `maxElementTaintsPerUnit`, `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic` (R18-4: it shapes the persisted `NormalizedDiagnostic.unitIds`, so it is a recorded RunOption, not an engine limit; not in `ledgerDigest`, since retained diagnostics are outside both digests). **Engine limits** (constructor `limits`, fixed for the engine's lifetime; **not** recorded in any Result Set and never digested, because they gate operations rather than shape a stored set's content): `maxResidentResultSets`, `maxQueuedRuns`, `persistTimeoutMs`, `maxAnnotatedNames`, `maxAnnotationBytes`, `sliceBudgetMs`, `aggregationSliceMaxItems`, `maxEntriesPerSelection`, `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection`, `maxValidationIssues`, `maxConcurrentStoreReads`, `maxQueuedStoreReads`, `reconcileTimeoutMs`, `maxCapturedNodes`, `maxStoredResultBytes`, `maxConcurrentAnnotations`, `maxConcurrentDerivations`, `maxFacetsPerGroupBy`, `maxFilterClauses`, `maxGroupMemberships`, `maxComparisonEntries`. The constructor's `limits` and the `RunOptions` schema of §18 list exactly these sets; a key in the wrong place is an unknown key and is rejected (`InvalidOptionsError`).

`RunOptions` and the engine limits are validated by one pure function that **collects every issue** and reports `InvalidOptionsError{issues}`. Validation always runs **synchronously** inside the call; only its *delivery* differs by entry point: it is **thrown** from `startRun` (which returns a `RunHandle`) and from `createClashTestEngine` for the limits, and delivered as a **rejected Promise** from `rerun` — and from every other Promise-returning entry point (R21-1/R19-1, §18, AC-11). Rules: a plain object; **unknown keys rejected**; no coercion; every number must be a **finite integer** (`NaN`, `±Infinity`, fractions, strings rejected); enums exact.

| Option | Accepted | Hard ceiling (provisional) |
|---|---|---|
| `broadPhase` | `'tlas'` \| `'linear'` | — |
| `interUnitYield` | `'animation-frame'` \| `'macrotask'` | — |
| `persist` | boolean | — |
| `maxSourceFragmentsPerUnit`, `maxSourceDbIdsPerUnit` | integer ≥ 1 | 20,000 / 5,000 (the library's per-epoch and per-dbId ceilings, F9) |
| `maxUnitsPerRun` | integer ≥ 1 | 100,000 |
| `maxPlanReferences` | integer ≥ 1 | 10,000,000 |
| `maxLedgerRecords` | integer ≥ 1, **≥ `maxPlanReferences + 2 × maxUnitsPerRun`** (guarantees `maxUnitsPerRun` reserved overflow slots **and** ≥ `maxUnitsPerRun` discretionary records at a full plan) | 20,000,000 (cross-field) |
| `maxPairsPerOutcome` | integer ≥ 1, **≤ `maxResultsPerRun`** | (cross-field) |
| `maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome` | integer ≥ 1 | 1,000,000 |
| `maxDistinctReasons` | integer ≥ 1 | 1,024 |
| `maxEntriesPerSelection` | integer ≥ 1 | 10,000 |
| `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` | integer ≥ 1 | 100,000 (was 1,000,000; the synchronous validation pass scales with it, §5.3) |
| `maxValidationIssues` | integer ≥ 1 | 1,000 |
| `maxTargetModelsPerUnit` | integer ≥ 1 | 64 |
| `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit` | integer ≥ 1 | 100,000 |
| `maxResultsPerRun` | integer ≥ 1 | 956,824 (was 1,000,000; erratum A11) |
| `maxRetainedDiagnostics` | integer ≥ 0 | 100,000 |
| `maxPlacementRetries` | integer ≥ 0 | 10 |
| `progressMinIntervalMs`, `persistTimeoutMs` | integer ≥ 0 | 60,000 |
| `sliceBudgetMs` | integer ≥ 1 | `frameBudgetMs` (8) |
| `aggregationSliceMaxItems`, `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic`, `maxAnnotatedNames` | integer ≥ 1 | 100,000 / 64 / 256 |
| `maxResidentResultSets` | integer ≥ 1 | 64 |
| `maxAnnotationBytes` | integer ≥ 1 | 64 MiB |
| `maxElementTaintsPerUnit` | integer ≥ 1 | 100,000 |
| `maxFacetsPerGroupBy`, `maxFilterClauses`, `maxGroupMemberships` | integer ≥ 1 | 16 / 128 / 10,000,000 |
| `maxConcurrentStoreReads`, `maxQueuedStoreReads`, `maxConcurrentDerivations` | integer ≥ 1 / ≥ 0 / ≥ 1 | 4 / 64 / 8 |
| `maxComparisonEntries` | integer ≥ 1 | 10,000,000 |
| `reconcileTimeoutMs` | integer ≥ 1 | 60,000 |
| `maxConcurrentAnnotations` | integer ≥ 1 | 8 |
| `maxCapturedNodes` | integer ≥ 1, ≤ `SCHEMA_MAX_NODES` | `SCHEMA_MAX_NODES` (§13.6) |
| `maxStoredResultBytes` | integer ≥ 1 | 1 GiB |
| `maxQueuedRuns` | integer ≥ 0, **≤ `maxResidentResultSets − 1`** | (cross-field) |

Ceilings exist so a caller cannot defeat a bound by passing a huge value; they are provisional like every cap here. A run's effective options are recorded in provenance (`runOptions`) and folded into `ledgerDigest` where they shape the plan.

### 17.2 Memory ownership

- **Engine-owned:** plain data only — the ledger and plan tables (O(roots + owners + unit records) integers, §6.5.1), results (one small plain record per pair), retained diagnostics (capped), definitions, annotation snapshots. No `Model`, no `THREE`, no geometry. Per-record byte size is **not measured**; AC-12 measures it rather than this spec asserting one.
- **Library-owned (unchanged, not controlled here):** per-epoch instance bucket, geometry/BLAS/connectivity registry, TLAS, placement tables — released only by `evictModel` or `dispose()`; no LRU/TTL (F9). A whole-model run touches every candidate-gated geometry (resolution is lazy and candidate-gated), so the world's retention grows with distinct geometries touched. M4's deterministic typed-buffer metric measured 4–25 MB across real single-call workloads (a **lower bound**; TLAS adds a constant ~807 KB across 3 epochs; per-instance retention 280–388 B). **No long-running, many-query, or leak data exists.** The engine exposes a read-only `retentionReport()` composed from the public accessors (`getEpochCollisionInstanceCount`, `getEpochGeometryDiagnostics`, `getEpochTlasDiagnostics`, `getEpochPlacementDiagnostics`) so a host can observe growth; it takes no action on it.

### 17.3 Main-thread discipline

**Two span classes, deliberately separated (H6).**

1. **Synchronous validation is *excluded* from the 8 ms slice guarantee.** `validateTest`, `saveTest`'s validation step and `startRun`'s validation must *run synchronously* (§5.5; their failures are returned as issues by `validateTest`, thrown by `startRun`, and delivered as a rejection by `saveTest`, A8) and therefore cannot yield mid-scan. They are a single linear pass (§5.3) — no sort, no de-duplication, no digest, and no allocation proportional to input beyond the ≤ `maxValidationIssues` issue list **and the single detached capture copy of the input (§5.3, R11-1)** — bounded, for the schema-shaped part, by `maxEntriesPerSelection` and `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` (both lowered for exactly this reason, §17.1); the enumeration of *unknown* own keys is not bounded by them (trust boundary, §5.3). This spec **does not guarantee** that pass fits in 8 ms on every supported device. It instead (a) fixes its asymptotics and hard ceilings, (b) requires AC-12 to **measure** it (capture copy included) at the ceilings on the reference device and to record the distribution, and (c) makes the ceilings a **plan-time gate**: if the measured p95 exceeds `frameBudgetMs` on the reference device, the plan must lower `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` before the values are frozen. `startRun` also performs O(constant) option validation and slot admission synchronously (§13.5.2), which are not size-dependent.
2. **Everything else the engine controls is sliced and held to 8 ms (design target).**

The engine adds **no other synchronous span longer than `frameBudgetMs` (8 ms)** of its own: normalization and `semanticDigest` computation (moved out of the synchronous path, §5.3), enumeration handling, outcome projection, dedup/merge, canonical sorting, digesting, and sealing all run in **deadline-aware slices**: a slice checks the injected `now` at least every 64 items and yields once `sliceBudgetMs` (provisional 4 ms, half of `frameBudgetMs`) has elapsed, with `aggregationSliceMaxItems` only a secondary ceiling; the 8 ms figure is a design target verified in AC-12's reference environment, not a guarantee on arbitrary devices. Slices yield between slices through the same kind of scheduler seam `collision/scheduler.js` uses. The library's own atomic spans (M4: tasks ~9–25 ms; a single non-preemptible span up to ~20 ms on the M3 fixture, ~680 ms for the synthetic pathological pair) are outside the engine's control and not improved by it. The engine reports its own labeled spans through an optional `spanRecorder` option (same shape as `collision/scheduler.js`'s `setSpanRecorder` callback) so AC-12 can measure them without touching the library's global recorder.

### 17.4 What is known and not known about cost (Verdict D honesty)

Known (F14, single-call, one fixture, one machine): task lengths, candidate density as the cost driver, TLAS as the only viable broad phase for multi-instance work (linear is an oracle and rejects large plans outright), memory lower bounds. **Not known:** wall-clock makespan per unit or per run on the representative runtime; frame-cadence behavior of *sustained back-to-back* queries (M4 measured single calls); input latency (the T-c aggregator defect makes it unusable); any worker message/transfer overhead (S6′ was ruled not to run); performance beyond the measured fixture (M4 §5.7 explicitly forbids extrapolation).
