Consequently **this spec states no throughput, latency, or responsiveness SLO**, and the responsiveness acceptance items are characterization-only (§22). Verdict D means the main-thread status quo is the sole oracle here — it does *not* mean the main thread is proven adequate for batches, nor that a worker is ruled out.

### 17.5 Worker seam (commitment, not adoption)

The one commitment: `ExecutionRequest` and `UnitOutcome` are plain, structured-clone-safe data keyed by `modelKey`/`dbId` (never `Model`, never epochs), and all lifecycle/identity authority stays on the main thread — exactly the shape M4 §9/§20 identify as the compatible seam. This spec does **not** adopt a worker, does **not** claim a worker would or would not help, and starts none of M4 Phase 4.

### 17.6 Hard boundaries of the current library that surface here

0. **The epoch bucket is world state, not run state.** Whether a run hits `bucket-capacity` depends on what any earlier caller admitted (§1.3.5); this is recorded (`admissionBaseline`, `environmentSensitive`), not hidden, and cannot be removed without a collision-core change (§21.2 (h)).
1. A model with more than `maxCollisionInstancesPerEpoch` (20,000) fragments cannot be fully covered (bucket is cumulative per epoch) — reported as a gap.
2. A single requested dbId over `maxFragmentsPerDbId` (5,000) or `maxChildrenPerNode` (10,000) fails extraction — reported as a gap; the engine does not expand it.
3. `broadPhase: 'linear'` rejects any (source-count × target-epoch-instances) over 1,000,000 — the oracle arm is only usable on small plans.
4. `clearance` units are bounded by `maxClearanceNarrowPhasePairs` (20,000) per call — handled by the deterministic split order of §6.4 (sources, then target models, then explicit target roots) down to one source root against one target root or a whole-model sentinel, which is a gap.
5. One run's diagnostics volume can be very large (F12) — capped detail, exact counts.
6. Instance-tree under-enumeration is silent (F15) — inherited, recorded via the plan-time `scopeDigests` (§6.2.1).

---

## 18. Contract sketch (semantics normative, names provisional)

This section fixes the *shape* of the engine boundary so the Panel and the implementation plan agree on semantics. Identifier names may change in the plan; the behaviors in §4–§17 may not without a revision of this spec.

```js
// proposed clashtest/index.js
createClashTestEngine({
  world,                         // CollisionWorld (required)
  enumerator,                    // ElementEnumerator (required)   — §6.2
  registry,                      // ModelRegistry (optional). The world coordinator owns ONE registry per world (§4.3); omitted ⇒ the engine joins it; a different registry ⇒ ModelBindingError('registry-mismatch')
  attributeSource,               // ElementAttributeSource (optional) — §14.5
  unitExecutor,                  // UnitExecutor (optional; default LocalUnitExecutor over `world`) — §7.1. An orchestration seam only: it lets tests inject deterministic failures without faking library-call construction, and it does NOT specify how geometry would reach a non-main-thread executor (§17.5).
  testStore, resultStore,        // ClashTestStore / ResultStore (optional; in-memory reference impls provided) — §5.5, §13.5
  limits,                        // optional { maxResidentResultSets, maxQueuedRuns, persistTimeoutMs, maxAnnotatedNames, maxAnnotationBytes, sliceBudgetMs, aggregationSliceMaxItems, maxEntriesPerSelection, maxExplicitElementsPerSelection, maxValidationIssues, maxConcurrentStoreReads, maxQueuedStoreReads, reconcileTimeoutMs, maxCapturedNodes, maxStoredResultBytes, maxConcurrentAnnotations, maxConcurrentDerivations, maxFacetsPerGroupBy, maxFilterClauses, maxGroupMemberships, maxComparisonEntries } — the ENGINE LIMITS of §17.1.1 (exhaustive; validated there; fixed per engine, not recorded in Result Sets)
  hierarchyInternalAcceptance,   // null | {recordRef: string ≤ 512 code units}; default null = UNACCEPTED, so a non-empty ledger.hierarchyInternal blocks the no-clash claim (§8.3, OQ-16). Recorded in provenance; the engine cannot verify what recordRef points to
  libraryVersion,                // string|null, host-supplied — §13.2
  spanRecorder,                  // (label, ms) => void, optional — §17.3
  now, yieldToScheduler,         // test seams, same convention as CollisionWorld's
}) → ClashTestEngine

ClashTestEngine {
  registry
  // definitions
  validateTest(def) → {issues, issuesTruncated}       // pure; never throws for data errors (§5.3)
  saveTest(def) → Promise<ClashTestDefinition>        // validate + atomic put; caller assigns revision, store enforces latest+1 (§5.5); RevisionConflictError, never overwrites
  getTest(testId, revision?) / listTests({limit?, cursor?}) / listResults({testId?, limit?, cursor?}) / archiveTest(testId) / deleteTest(testId)   // no revision ⇒ latest; archive/delete cover all revisions; deleteTest = resultStore.tombstoneTest THEN testStore.delete (idempotent, fail-closed order) and is the only supported delete (§5.5)
  // runs
  startRun(definition | {testId, revision?}, runOptions?) → RunHandle   // the TWO overloads of §5.5 (a stored reference is the two-field shape {testId, revision?}, §5.3 scope rule (ii)); throws InvalidTestDefinitionError synchronously; all runtime failure surfaces as a terminal status
  rerun(runIdOrResultSet, { definitionRevision?, runOptions? }) → Promise<RunHandle>   // R21-1: capture/read+validation errors reject the Promise (CorruptResultSetError, UnsupportedSchemaVersionError, ResultSetTooLargeError, ReadCapacityError, InvalidOptionsError); run-slot admission (ResultCapacityError, §13.5.2) happens synchronously at the moment validation succeeds and also rejects; the read slot is released on every rejection
  getRun(runId) → RunHandle | null                    // active/queued runs AND terminal runs whose set is still resident (the handle keeps its settled `done`/`persisted`/`getSnapshot`); null once nonresident (§13.5.2)
  getResultSet(runId) → Promise<ResultSet | null>     // resident or via store
  discardRun(runId) → Promise<void>                   // explicit, host-initiated release + store delete; never a side effect of cancel (§13.5.2)
  retainResultSet(runId) → { release() }              // keeps a saved set resident (§13.5.2)
  releaseResultSet(runId, { acknowledgeUnsaved? }) → void   // drop the resident copy only; refuses retained/unsaved sets unless acknowledged
  retryPersist(runId) → Promise<PersistenceOutcome>   // new attempt for a failed set (§13.5.1)
  getPersistenceState(runId) → PersistenceSnapshot | null   // synchronous, for late observers of an ACTIVE/RESIDENT run; null for a nonresident id (evicted/released/discarded/unknown) — the engine keeps no history beyond its resident bound (§13.5.2)
  getFreshness(runIdOrResultSet) → Promise<Freshness> // async; re-digests live scopes (§16.4)
  canClaimNoClashes(runIdOrResultSet) → Promise<boolean>              // async; recomputes freshness (§8.3, §16.4); true only when `unqualified`
  assessNoClashClaim(runIdOrResultSet) → Promise<ClaimAssessment>    // R17-5: same read-through, capture/validation (§13.6) and bracketed freshness (§16.4) as canClaimNoClashes; the only path that returns qualifiers; ClaimAssessment = {allowed, unqualified, qualifiers, exclusions, proofMethod}
  invalidateModelPlacement(modelKey)                  // §16.2 (recommended single call)
  annotate(runId, names, {signal?} = {}) → Promise<AttributeSnapshot> // §14.5 (cancellable job; unknown option keys ⇒ InvalidOptionsError)
  retentionReport() → object                          // §17.2
  dispose() → Promise<DisposeSummary>                 // sync: marks disposing; resolves after all runs drained+sealed (`cancelled`, or per §16.7 if a higher-precedence fact is observed); {droppedUnsaved: [{runId, resultSet}], persistenceInFlight: runId[]} — unsaved sets handed back, not destroyed; never throws; idempotent (same promise)
}

RunHandle {
  runId
  getSnapshot() → RunProgress                         // §9
  subscribe(listener) → unsubscribe                   // events: 'state' | 'progress' | 'unit' | 'persistence' (the last stays valid after the terminal event)
  cancel(reason?) → Promise<RunSummary>               // §10; same value/timing as `done`
  done: Promise<RunSummary>                           // {runId, status, resultSet, persistence: {state, timedOut, attempt, error?}}; resolves ONCE for EVERY terminal status; never rejects; at seal + (first persistence settle OR persistTimeoutMs), timeout = {state:'pending', timedOut:true} (§13.5.1)
  persisted: Promise<PersistenceOutcome>              // resolves ONCE, never rejects, never reports timeout: first attempt's eventual {saved|failed|skipped}; no timer; NOT on the lifecycle critical path
}

// pure derivations (no engine state), also exported standalone:
assessNoClashClaim(resultSet, currentFreshness) → {allowed, unqualified, qualifiers, exclusions, proofMethod}   // §8.3 (R16-2); canClaimNoClashes(resultSet, currentFreshness) → boolean  // true ONLY when `unqualified` (no declared exclusion depends on an acceptance); §8.3 claim rule; currentFreshness REQUIRED, false if absent/not 'current'; also engine.canClaimNoClashes(runId) → Promise<boolean>, which recomputes freshness incl. scope digests
createDerivationContext({limits?, yieldToScheduler?, now?}) → DerivationContext   // R17-4; engine.derivations is the engine's own context
DerivationContext { summarize(resultSet, {signal?}) · groupBy(resultSet, facets, {annotations?, signal?}) · filter(resultSet, clauses, {annotations?, signal?}) · compareResultSets(base, next, {signal?, distanceTolerance?, pointTolerance?} = {}) }   // all Promise-returning; each options object is closed (unknown key ⇒ InvalidOptionsError), sliced, per-context concurrency (§14.4 bounds)
getPairStatus(resultSet, pairId) · getElementStatus(resultSet, elementRef) · getFreshness(resultSet, {registry, enumerator?}) → Promise   // async; re-digests live scopes (§16.4); session identity from registry.registrySessionId; engine.getFreshness(runIdOrResultSet) is the bound form
```

`RunOptions` (all optional; all provisional defaults in §17.1; **validated per §17.1.1**): `broadPhase: 'tlas'|'linear'`, `maxSourceFragmentsPerUnit`, `maxSourceDbIdsPerUnit`, `maxTargetModelsPerUnit`, `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit`, `maxUnitsPerRun`, `maxResultsPerRun`, `maxRetainedDiagnostics`, `maxPlacementRetries`, `progressMinIntervalMs`, `interUnitYield: 'animation-frame'|'macrotask'`, `persist: boolean`, `maxPlanReferences`, `maxLedgerRecords`, `maxPairsPerOutcome`, `maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome`, `maxDistinctReasons`, `maxElementTaintsPerUnit`, `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic`.

Errors the engine's own API surfaces — thrown by its synchronous entry points and delivered as rejections by its Promise-returning ones (A8) (`RunIdConflictError` comes from a `ResultStore`, like `IdentityConflictError`) (never the library's, which are always mapped to unit outcomes): `InvalidTestDefinitionError{issues, issuesTruncated}`, `ModelBindingError` (register conflicts, missing `modelVersion`), `RunQueueFullError` (thrown only when a configured `maxQueuedRuns` lower than slot capacity is reached; otherwise refusal is always `ResultCapacityError`), `ResultCapacityError{cap, active, retained, held}`, `ResultSetRetainedError`, `UnsavedResultSetError`, `ModelKeyVersionConflictError`, `IdentityConflictError` (from a `ResultStore`), `AnnotationTooLargeError`, `EnumerationCancelledError`, `AnnotationSupersededError`, `AnnotationBusyError{limit}`, `AttributeSourceError`, `UnsupportedSchemaVersionError`, `EngineDisposedError` (any method after `dispose()` is called, except `dispose()` itself which is idempotent and non-throwing here — unlike `CollisionWorld.dispose()`), `RevisionConflictError`, `TestDeletedError` (from a `ClashTestStore` or a `ResultStore`), `TestArchivedError`, `RunNotTerminalError`, `ResultNotResidentError{runId}`, `CorruptResultSetError{runId, issues}`, `ResultSetTooLargeError{runId}`, `ReadCapacityError{limit}`, `ReadAbortedError`, `DerivationAbortedError`, `DerivationBusyError{limit}`, `GroupingTooLargeError{limit, observed}`, `ComparisonTooLargeError{limit, observed}`, `FacetRangeError{facetId}`, `CorruptListingError{issues}`, `PersistenceOutcomeUnknownError` (from a `ResultStore`), `StoreNotConfiguredError{store}`, `InvalidOptionsError{issues}`.

Ports summary (all injectable): `ElementEnumerator` (§6.2), `ModelRegistry` (§4.3), `UnitExecutor` (§7.1, constructor option `unitExecutor`), `ElementAttributeSource` (§14.5), `ClashTestStore` (§5.5; incl. `resolveForRun`, `isTombstoned`), `ResultStore` (§13.5; incl. `tombstoneTest`). Every port is injected; the core never imports the Viewer.

---

## 19. Relationship to the current single-query APIs

### 19.1 The single-query surface is unchanged

- `CollisionWorld.findClashes` and every public library contract (query shape, result shape, identity model, diagnostics semantics, the permanent `'linear'` oracle, lifecycle) stay exactly as they are (roadmap §1/§8). The engine calls them; it does not wrap, patch, or subclass them, and reaches no `_`-prefixed member or module-internal.
- The existing `groupBy: 'dbId'` query parameter stays; the roadmap's UI-facing Group-By is this layer's §14, not a `findClashes` parameter.
- `ClashFinderExtension` ("Find Clashes" / "Find Soft Clashes") keeps calling `world.findClashes` directly. It is precisely the degenerate one-element case of this layer (`|A| = 1`, `B` = every loaded model, one unit) and is **not required to migrate**; whether the Panel later reroutes it through the engine is a Panel decision.
- **Concurrency caveat.** The extension's single queries and an engine run share one `CollisionWorld`. The world-scoped coordinator serializes only *engine* runs (§7.3); a concurrent extension query that grows a captured epoch can force a `ModelPlacementChangedError` retry inside the run. This is handled (§7.5) but is a throughput hazard, listed as OQ-11.

### 19.2 Mapping

| This layer | Library call |
|---|---|
| Definition `mode` | `query.mode` (one per unit) |
| Definition `clearance` | `query.clearance` (only for `clearance`; the library has no default) |
| Cell 1/2/3 unit | one `findClashes` with `source` = one model + explicit dbIds, `target = {targets:[…]}` |
| Selection `kind:'model'` as a **target** | `targets` entry with `dbIds` omitted (whole-model sentinel) |
| Selection `kind:'model'` as a **source** | enumerated to explicit `dbIds` by `ElementEnumerator` (no library primitive, F15) |
| `PairId` | derived from `ClashPair.source/target` `(dbId + model→modelKey)`, canonicalized (§4.4); the library's epoch-qualified ordered key is never exposed |
| Result `evidence` | `ClashPair.witness` / `distance` / `clearance`, re-keyed (§11.1) |
| Diagnostics | `statistics.diagnostics`, normalized (§12.2) |

### 19.3 Hard and Soft Clash

One mode per test. A Hard test (`volume-overlap`, or the diagnostic modes `containment`/`surface-intersection`) and a Soft test (`clearance`) are two definitions; the layer never composes them and performs no cross-test de-duplication. Within a `clearance` test the library already excludes any pair that is a Hard Clash under the same scope, semantically (never merely distance-filtered — README M3), and reports `clearance-hard-clash-precheck-inconclusive` when it cannot decide; those diagnostics count toward `integrity` (§12.3). The diagnostic modes are exposed because the library exposes them; whether the Panel offers them, given their time-sensitivity (§15.1), is a Panel decision (OQ-12).

---

## 20. Relationship to the future Viewer Clash Panel

Roadmap §7: the Panel is designed after, or together with, a stable result contract — never by binding UI state to `findClashes` internals. This spec provides that contract and assumes **no UI requirement**.

**The Panel may rely on:** stable `ResultId`/`GroupId` across reruns and sessions; the `RunOutcome` axes and the `canClaimNoClashes` rule (and must not present a bare "0 clashes" without them); `RunProgress` snapshots/events and cooperative `cancel`; sealed immutable Result Sets and their plain-data serializability; canonical result order and the deterministic facet/group/filter derivations; the attribute-annotation layer for level/category grouping; the `ModelRegistry` as the `modelKey → Model` resolver for navigation.

**The Panel owns (this layer defines none of it):** creating definitions from the user's selection (e.g. from `viewer.getAggregateSelection()`, mapped to `modelKey`s through the registry — which is how the shipped extension's "exactly one element" restriction is *not* baked into the engine, per roadmap §4); model→`modelKey` derivation policy; rendering, isolation, `fitToView`, markers and their lifecycle (the existing `ClashFinderExtension` visualization is the reusable reference); result status/approval/assignment/issue workflow and its persistence; saved views, expand/collapse state, sorting UI; the choice of `ResultStore` backend and retention; the default `clearance` value and UX; how partial/caveated/stale outcomes are worded.

**Hand-off rules.** The Panel resolves a `ResultId` → `ElementRef`s → `registry.bindingFor(modelKey).model` on the main thread at display time; the Result Set itself never holds a `Model`. A workflow layer that attaches state to `ResultId`/`GroupId` inherits the identity guarantees of §4.4 and, because a `modelKey` is one immutable model version (§4.2), needs no version reasoning of its own: a version change arrives as a **new `modelKey`** (new `ResultId`s, no aliasing), and a same-key version mismatch is a loud `identityConflicts`/`ModelKeyVersionConflictError`, never a note.

---

## 21. Compatibility, migration, and roadmap traceability

### 21.1 Roadmap §4 constraints → where satisfied

| Roadmap §4 constraint | How this spec satisfies it |
|---|---|
| Stable source/target identity across federated models; don't persist `Model`/`modelId` | §4: durable `modelKey`+`ElementRef`; runtime identities confined to live bindings (D3) |
| Don't rely on diagnostic-only `modelId` | §11.2/§12.2: mapped via unit binding table; ambiguous ⇒ `null`, never guessed |
| Avoid breaking identity redesign for persistent/grouped results | Runtime identity stays swappable under `modelKey`; core unchanged (D1) |
| Don't hard-code single-selected-element UX | §5: many-element, many-model selections; §20 |
| Many-source, many-target, model-vs-model, model-vs-many, many-vs-many, full-model enumeration | §5.2, §6.3 (three-cell decomposition; whole-model source via §6.2) |
| Preserve cancellation/abort path; policy is result layer's | §10 (cooperative at unit boundary; partial retained; no library change) |
| Preserve progress, yielding, batch checkpoints, later worker | §9, §7.3, §17.3, §17.5 (seam only) |
| Rich result semantics for grouping/filtering | §11, §14 |
| No Group By in collision core; existing `groupBy:'dbId'` stays | §14 lives here; §19.1 |
| Panel state, grouping/filter UI, persistent lifecycle, workflow, approval outside collision core | §2.1, §20, §23 |

### 21.2 Architecture-change discipline (roadmap §8)

This spec **changes no roadmap §4 constraint and reopens no settled public contract** in §1 of the roadmap (query shapes, result shapes, identity model, diagnostics semantics, `'linear'` oracle, lifecycle). It therefore needs no §8 five-point justification. The following are **optional, additive** collision-core changes this design would *benefit from* but does not require; each, if ever pursued, is its own reviewed change under §8 and is explicitly out of scope here: (a) a `signal` option on `findClashes` (would cut cancel latency, §10.3); (b) a read-only exported limits object (would let the planner pre-check budgets instead of splitting reactively); (c) an exported library version constant (§1.3); (d) an exported diagnostic-reason registry with a completeness flag (would replace the engine's known-reason table, §12.3); (e) a whole-model *source* enumeration primitive (only if the Viewer adapter's accessor proves inadequate, OQ-4); (g) a typed `CapacityExceededError` kind for the `'linear'` broad-phase ceiling, replacing the message-matched plain `Error` (§7.2); (f) a per-call report of the extracted target/source population identity (fragment ids + owners, or a digest) in `findClashes` `statistics`, which would upgrade the bracketed scope proof (§16.3.5) to an exact one; (h) per-call admission isolation or an explicit epoch-cache reset/inspection seam in `CollisionWorld`, which is what *strict input-only* determinism would need (today the cumulative bucket makes `bucket-capacity` depend on world history, §1.3.5, §15.1); this spec narrows its determinism claim instead and records the environmental input.

### 21.3 Migration

None. There is no existing consumer, stored data, or schema to migrate; the layer is additive and new (`clashtest/`, `test/clashtest/`). Existing acceptance/performance harnesses and `npm test` are untouched, and `npm test` must stay free of any live server or APS credentials (live coverage is script-based, like the existing acceptance and regression scripts). At implementation time: a CHANGELOG entry, a README pointer, and a handoff update. Forward compatibility for this layer's own data is defined in §13.6 (`schemaVersion` = 1).

### 21.4 M4 status

M4 stays closed with Verdict D; no gate is re-run, no Phase 4 spike is authorized or implied, and none of the three tracked follow-ups is a prerequisite. Two are relevant *inputs*: the structured-clone crash (§13.4 excludes its class by construction) and the T-c aggregator defect (why no input-latency claim is made, §17.4).

---

## 22. Acceptance and verification criteria

Tiers: **T1** = `node:test`, fakes/mocks, no Viewer; **T2** = `node:test` differential against a real `CollisionWorld` using the existing fixtures (`test/fixtures/geometry.mjs`, `test/fixtures/mockInstanceTree.mjs`); **T3** = live Viewer, Playwright script run against a running dev server (like `test/acceptance/*.acceptance.mjs`; **never** part of `npm test`). Because responsiveness targets remain unconfirmed (Verdict D), AC-12's live measurements are **characterization, not gates**.

| ID | Tier | Criterion (pass condition) |
|---|---|---|
| AC-1 | T1 | Each V-rule (§5.3) has accept and reject cases **at every field and nesting level** (`revision: 1.5`, `name: null`, `{kind:'model', dbIds:[1]}`, unknown nested properties, oversized strings/lists), the validator reports **all** issues in one pass up to `maxValidationIssues` (an input with more than that many issues yields exactly `maxValidationIssues` issues and `issuesTruncated: true`, and scanning stops), `validateTest`/`saveTest`/`startRun` accept and reject identically, and digests match a fixed set of golden `sha256:` vectors for the canonical-JSON format (§5.4); `semanticDigest` is invariant under key order, whitespace, `name`, `description`, `revision`, and changes with any semantic field; a newer `schemaVersion` is rejected distinctly; **capture at the boundary (R11-1, §5.3):** after `startRun(definition)` and after `saveTest(def)` return, the test mutates the caller's object — a `dbId`, `mode`, `testId`, a pushed/removed entry, `runOptions` — immediately, and again from a `yieldToScheduler` hook while the engine is between slices, and asserts the plan, `semanticDigest`, embedded definition, persisted definition and store input equal those of the value that passed validation (byte-identical to a control run that is never mutated); a definition with an accessor property (the getter is **never invoked** — spy), a symbol key, a sparse array, an array with extra own properties, a non-plain prototype, a `Proxy` whose trap throws or a revoked proxy (`unreadable`), or a cyclic structure is rejected with a V1 issue (never an internal failure, never an escape of a thrown value); a `Proxy` that reports valid data is captured once from what its traps report and later mutation of its target cannot affect the run (no proxy *detection* is asserted); every property descriptor is read at most once (spy on `Reflect`/descriptor access); a definition with 100,000 unknown own keys is rejected with a V1 issue and the test *documents* (does not assert a bound on) the enumeration cost; a definition returned by a fake store or migration that mutates its own object after returning is likewise unaffected; **option validation (§17.1.1)** rejects `NaN`/`±Infinity`/fractional/zero/over-ceiling values and unknown keys with `InvalidOptionsError` listing every issue (e.g. `maxSourceDbIdsPerUnit: 0`, `maxUnitsPerRun: Infinity`, `progressMinIntervalMs: NaN`, `maxQueuedRuns > maxResidentResultSets − 1`); **definition store (§5.5):** `put` accepts only revision 1 for a new test or `latest + 1`, rejecting duplicates, gaps and out-of-order saves with `RevisionConflictError` without overwriting, `get(testId)` returns the latest (also when archived), `archive`/`delete` cover all revisions, and an archived test rejects **stored-reference** runs and new revisions (`TestArchivedError`) while `startRun(definition)` of the same content is still permitted (ad hoc, §5.5); `ClashTestStore` operations are linearizable per `testId` — the reference implementation is tested under interleaved `put`/`archive`/`delete`/`get` so a reader never observes a revision from one point and an archived flag from another (`resolveForRun` is the operation that returns both; a store that reports `live` for a test whose archive committed before the read, or returns `archived` with a definition of another revision, is non-conforming and caught by the interleaving test — the lifecycle is one-way `active → archived` with no restore operation (§5.5), so no un-archive case is tested); `resolveForRun` returns each of `live`/`archived`/`deleted`/`missing` and drives the four planning causes `test-*`, with an archived store definition **not** a schema failure **Tombstone (§5.5):** after `delete(testId)` every `put` for that id — `revision: 1` included — rejects with `TestDeletedError` and changes nothing; the id reads as not found and is not listed; a delete-then-recreate and a `delete`-vs-`put` race (interleavings) never yield a live test under a deleted id and never a state with neither the live test nor the tombstone. **Tombstone bypass paths (NH3, §5.5), engine level, with a `ResultStore` that records every inserted set:** after `engine.deleteTest(T)` — (a) `startRun(directDefinition with testId T)` and `rerun(embedded set of T)` with persistence enabled each seal `failed` with planning gap `test-unavailable` (`test-deleted`), `definition: null`, `testRef.semanticDigest: null`, and the recording fake world sees **zero** `findClashes` calls; (b) the same two starts with `persist: false` run to completion with persistence state `skipped` (`retryPersist` is legal only from `failed`, §13.5.1, so it is **not** used here); the test retrieves each sealed set with `getResultSet(runId)` and calls the configured `ResultStore.put(set)` directly, which rejects `TestDeletedError` with no result inserted and no `modelKey` claimed; and, as the legal `retryPersist` path, a persisting run whose `put` failed for an unrelated reason (injected store error) and is retried **after** `deleteTest(T)` ends `failed` with `error.name = 'TestDeletedError'`; (c) a persisting run admitted **before** the delete whose `put` lands after it seals normally and its persistence ends `failed` (`TestDeletedError`), run outcome unchanged, set `held`; (d) a byte-identical definition re-created under `T` via `saveTest` rejects `TestDeletedError`; (e) an `isTombstoned` lookup that rejects makes a persisting direct run fail closed with `test-lookup-failed`; (f) `deleteTest` step order — if `testStore.delete` fails after `resultStore.tombstoneTest` succeeded, the reservation holds (`put` still rejects) and a retry completes; a `testStore.delete`-only delete (engine bypassed) is shown to be the documented residual (layer 2 blocks a persisting run on that engine, layer 1 does not know); in every path a scan of the `ResultStore` finds **no set newly inserted after the tombstone with `testRef.testId = T`**. |
| AC-2 | T1 | **Exact cover.** Property test (seeded RNG) over 1–4 models with whole/explicit/mixed selections, including same-model and partial `A∩B` overlap: the union of unit pair-spaces equals the required `P`; each required pair (unordered) / ordered pair appears **exactly once**; every same-model unit has disjoint source/target dbId sets; the plan is byte-identical across input reorderings; changing sizing options changes only chunking, never coverage; `maxUnitsPerRun` is enforced at planning. **Hierarchy cases (mock instance tree with fragment-owning parents that have fragment-owning descendants):** whole-model roots absorb nested owners; explicit lists drop listed descendants; `A = B` with nested owners is root-identical and plans; every cross-role nesting fails planning with `hierarchy-overlap` and runs **no** unit; and a differential check proves a naive dbId-disjoint `P → C` query drops the parent-vs-descendant fragment pair (documenting F16) while no *required* root pair is lost. **Scope-digest conformance (§6.2.1):** over mock models the enumerator's population/subtree records equal what the library's own `extractAllFragments`/`extractFragments` return (including both dropped classes), and the digest changes under every mutation class listed in AC-8. **Tree-unreachable population (§6.2):** a mock model with a loaded, numeric-owner fragment (`fragId 42`, owner `900`, an owner that owns **only** unreachable fragments — the *unreachable-only* class; the *mixed* class is AC-17) in no root subtree is reported in `unreachablePopulation` for both orientations — as a whole-model *source* it is flagged unevaluated, as a whole-model *target* a clash against it is retained with `flags.unreachableOwner`, `getPairStatus` is `not-required`, and `canClaimNoClashes` is false in both; duplicated/inconsistent records fail planning. **Mixed reachable/unreachable owner (H1, §6.2 owner classes):** see AC-17. **Unresolved selections (§6.2):** an explicit stale dbId (`not-found`) fails planning with `element-unresolvable` and runs no unit; a real node with zero fragments (`no-fragments`) plans, contributes no pair space, appears in `unresolvedSelections`, and makes `canClaimNoClashes` false even when the run is `completed ∧ full ∧ clean ∧ current`; the with/without-such-element cases are both tested. |
| AC-3 | T1 | **Executor-port conformance:** a stub executor built only from an `ExecutionRequest` (§7.1) can reproduce the default executor's plan and coverage — the engine's split-vs-gap decision uses only `failure.capacityKind`, affected models only `failure.modelKeys`, and `evaluated` is only accepted with `scopeProof: 'equal'`. **Outcome validation (§7.1):** a stub executor returning each of — **(rejection, §7.1 H7.1)** a diagnostic whose `reason` is 129 code units, a 10 KB `reason`, a non-string `reason`, or a non-plain diagnostic (each rejected as `failed:internal-error`, run `failed`, nothing merged — these never reach overflow), `pairs.length > maxPairsPerOutcome` (whole-unit `failed:result-cap`, validated before deep processing), an oversized `failure.message`, a `failure.modelKeys` with 1,000,000 duplicate or non-participant entries, a `generations` array missing, duplicating or adding a participant, an unknown `capacityKind`/`failure.code`, a pair `type` outside the mode's enumeration, an over-length `errorName`, an inconsistent overflow marker (below cap, non-integer, alongside a non-empty array, on a non-`evaluated` outcome), a T2 compatibility test that the library's linear-ceiling error still matches the pinned matcher, unknown statistics keys, a whole-target pair whose owner is in neither `rootOwners` nor `unreachablePopulation`, mismatched `unitId`/`attempt`, `evaluated` with `scopeProof ≠ equal` or non-null `failure`, a pair naming a model outside the unit, a source owner outside the unit's `rootOwners`, a non-finite evidence value, a `Map`/function, evidence of the wrong mode shape — is rejected as `failed:internal-error` (run `failed`) and nothing from it is merged. **Bounded overflow of *legal* diagnostics (§7.1 H7.2, §12.4), separate from the rejection cases above:** (i) 100,000 diagnostics with 100,000 *distinct* reasons each ≤ 128 code units (e.g. `r000001`…): accepted, `total = 100,000` exact, `completenessAffectingCount` exact (all examined), `unclassifiedOccurrences = 0`, at most `maxDistinctReasons` keys retained in canonical order, `omittedReasonOccurrences = 100,000 − Σ retained`, `Σ countsByReason + omittedReasonOccurrences + unclassifiedOccurrences = total`, one unit-wide `diagnostic-reason-overflow` taint, `integrity: caveated`, memory of counts/taints bounded by a constant; (ii) the same input with only benign `witness-unavailable`-class omissions leaves `completenessAffectingCount` unchanged and adds **no** overflow taint; (iii) `diagnostics.length > maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome` (array not iterated: spy on element access) ⇒ `diagnostic-overflow` unit taint with `occurrences = length`, exact `total`, `unclassifiedOccurrences = length`, and **`completenessAffectingCount` unchanged** (never inflated to look exact); run on (1) an oversize array mixing benign `witness-unavailable` and completeness-affecting items, (2) an oversize array of *only* benign items, and (3) an oversize array of only completeness-affecting items: in all three `completenessAffectingUpperBound = count + length`, `integrity: caveated` (fail-safe, documented as conservative for case 2), and `geometry-readiness` is *possible* (set via `unclassifiedOccurrences > 0`); (iv) the retained key set is identical for shuffled diagnostic order and under `tlas`/`linear`. **Projection caps at the executor (R11-6, §7.1):** a fake world returning 300,000 diagnostics (and, separately, more than `maxPairsPerOutcome` pairs) to the default `LocalUnitExecutor` with `request.limits.maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome = 20,000` yields `diagnostics: []` with `diagnosticOverflowLength = 300,000` (and `pairs: []` with `pairsOverflowLength`), a spy proves the executor read no element of the oversized array and constructed no projected object for it, the engine then takes the ordinary `diagnostic-overflow` (resp. `failed:result-cap`) path with exact `total`/`unclassifiedOccurrences`, and a stub executor that projects the oversized array anyway is still bounded by the engine's own `length` check; the exact-cover and `integrity` outcomes are identical whether the marker or the raw array reaches the engine. **Executor trust (§7.1):** a test documents, and the suite pins, that a structurally valid but semantically false `evaluated`-with-no-pairs outcome is accepted (the limit of validation) while every structural inconsistency is rejected. **Library-call conformance** (fake world recording calls): every call uses the `targets` shape, `groupBy:'dbId'`, one mode, `clearance` iff clearance; max concurrent in-flight `findClashes` = 1; no call after cancel/terminal; no call for a dead lease; the engine never calls `evictModel`/`dispose` (its own `invalidateModelPlacement` passthrough excepted). |
| AC-4 | T2 | **Partition and broad-phase independence (P2).** The same test under sizing {1 source/unit, default, all-in-one} × `broadPhase` {`tlas`,`linear`} yields identical `pairSetDigest` and evidence within tolerance, on fixtures with known clashes, including `A = B` (same-model), and partial-overlap cases. and a spy test proves the *synchronous* path does **no** sorting, de-duplication, normalization or digesting (so nothing size-dependent is hiding in it). *(b) Synchronous validation and capture — measured, not sliced:* the **external Result Set capture (§13.6) at the schema ceilings** (worst-case duration, peak bytes, ≈ 2× graph) is measured and recorded as the input to freezing those ceilings; and `validateTest`/`startRun` validation **including the detached capture copy (§5.3)** at the ceilings (two selections of `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` explicit elements, `maxEntriesPerSelection` entries, all-invalid input that fills `maxValidationIssues` and sets `issuesTruncated`, an over-cap `dbIds.length` that costs O(1)) is **not** asserted ≤ 8 ms; T1 asserts linear operation counts (element visits ≤ n, issue list ≤ `maxValidationIssues`) and records a wall-clock distribution on the reference device, which is the plan-time input for freezing `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` (§17.3 gate). *(c) Also gating:* `maxResultsPerRun` discards the whole overflowing unit and stops the run, leaving a union of whole units that is **identical under TLAS and linear**; per-record bytes measured for every record class of the §17.1 worst-case formula, and the resulting worst-case retained-byte estimate is **reported and checked against the product-confirmed memory budget before the defaults are frozen** (§17.1 calibration gate; with no confirmed budget the defaults stay `provisional` and the estimate is recorded, not gated). **T3, informational only:** on the 3-discipline Audubon fixture, record unit count, per-unit ms, run makespan, cancel latency, frame-gap distribution during a run (a T-b1/T-b2 analogue), and `retentionReport()` growth across repeated runs; archive under `test/performance/results/` (git-ignored, like the existing gates). No pass/fail against an unconfirmed target. |
| AC-13 | T1 | **Grouping/filter**: single-valued facets partition and multi-valued facets cover (counts asserted accordingly); facet results are invariant under input shuffling; `GroupId` stable across runs; `locationCell` handles negative and boundary coordinates; `elementRefKey` collision vectors (`{modelKey:'a:1',dbId:2}` vs `{modelKey:'a',dbId:1}`, keys containing quotes/commas/brackets/Unicode/numeric-looking text) never alias; each filter op has a truth table; attribute facets are unavailable, not erroring, without a snapshot. **Annotation compatibility (§14.5):** a snapshot taken for run A applied to run B's set, a snapshot whose `semanticDigest` differs, one whose `modelVersions[modelKey]` differs (a reload with new content under a new key leaves the old key absent) or omits a model in `provenance.models`, and one lacking a referenced attribute name each make the attribute facets `unavailable` with the matching `annotation-incompatible:…` reason — never applied, never partially applied, never thrown; built-in facets are unaffected; an engine-owned `annotate` snapshot is compatible. **Facet range safety (R15-5, §14.2):** `locationCell(1e-300)` over a finite coordinate `1e300`, and `distanceBucket` likewise, reject `FacetRangeError` (spy: no group list returned), no `GroupId` is produced with a non-finite or unsafe component, and a `null`-location group remains distinct from any numeric cell. **Grouping bounds (R13-4, §14.4):** `groupBy`/`filter`/`summarize` return Promises and a spy on the injected scheduler shows they yield between slices of at most `aggregationSliceMaxItems`; a cap-sized set (`maxResultsPerRun` results) grouped by nested multi-valued facets (`element` × `attrEither:<name>`, with a compatible snapshot — **two distinct** multi-valued facets, because a repeated facet id with the same params is rejected `InvalidOptionsError` by §14.4 and could never reach this case) is rejected `GroupingTooLargeError` from the **incremental membership count before** the over-cap allocation (spy: no allocation beyond the limit), returns no partial grouping, and a same-size single-valued grouping succeeds; more than `maxFacetsPerGroupBy` facets, a repeated facet, or more than `maxFilterClauses` clauses reject `InvalidOptionsError`; an aborted derivation stops within one slice, rejects `DerivationAbortedError`, and frees its concurrency slot; a third concurrent derivation rejects `DerivationBusyError`. **Derivation context (R17-4):** two contexts created with `maxConcurrentDerivations` 1 and 8 keep independent limits (a second concurrent call on the first rejects `DerivationBusyError`, the eighth on the second does not), `engine.derivations` uses the engine's scheduler/limits, no module-global state is shared between contexts (tests interleave them), and a call without a context is not exposed. **Derivation memory (R21-3):** a derivation-context call acquires its derivation slot **before** capturing any raw set (a busy context rejects `DerivationBusyError` without capturing), and the §17.1 worst-case memory formula includes `maxConcurrentDerivations × (2 × maxCapturedNodes × bytesPerNode + comparison/grouping output bytes + ledger-index bytes)`, `maxConcurrentStoreReads × (2 × maxCapturedNodes × bytesPerNode)` and `maxConcurrentAnnotations × maxAnnotationBytes`; a peak-memory test runs two simultaneous maximum-shape comparisons (four detached set copies, indexes, `maxComparisonEntries` outputs) and asserts the measured peak against that formula and that a third concurrent call is rejected before capturing. **Method options are exhaustive (R19-4):** `annotate(runId, names, {signal})` and `compareResultSets(base, next, {signal, distanceTolerance, pointTolerance})` accept exactly those keys, reject any other with `InvalidOptionsError`, and honor each (an aborted annotation job is cancelled; a non-default tolerance changes `changed` classification at the boundary; a negative/non-finite tolerance rejects). **Comparison (R15-4, §15.4):** `compareResultSets` returns a Promise, yields between slices (scheduler spy), gives the same output as a synchronous reference implementation on randomized set pairs, is O(n + m) (operation-count assertion: ledger index lookups only, no per-pair ledger scan), a pair of `maxResultsPerRun`-sized sets is rejected `ComparisonTooLargeError` from the incremental count before over-cap allocation (no partial), `signal` abort stops within one slice (`DerivationAbortedError`), and it shares the `maxConcurrentDerivations` slots. **Read backpressure (R14-2, §13.5.2):** with a store holding ten near-schema-ceiling sets, ten concurrent `getResultSet` calls run at most `maxConcurrentStoreReads` validations at once (spy on peak concurrent traversals), queue at most `maxQueuedStoreReads`, the surplus rejects `ReadCapacityError` immediately, an aborted read releases its slot (`ReadAbortedError`), and the by-id `rerun`/`getFreshness` share the same slots. **Annotation jobs (R18-1, §14.5):** ten rapid `annotate` calls for one run abort nine (their `getAttributes` `signal` fires; spy on the source: nine aborted, one completes), a job is aborted by release, discard, eviction, dispose and by the caller's `signal`, a source ignoring the signal has its late result discarded unread and publishes nothing, a call beyond `maxConcurrentAnnotations` rejects `AnnotationBusyError`, peak in-flight annotation bytes never exceed `maxConcurrentAnnotations × maxAnnotationBytes`, and the map capture aborts at the byte cap. **Annotation lifecycle (R12-4, §14.5):** with a controllable `ElementAttributeSource` whose `getAttributes` is pending, (i) evicting the run's set (by a new admission), (ii) `releaseResultSet`, (iii) `discardRun`, (iv) `engine.dispose()`, (v) a newer `annotate`, and (vi) release followed by re-admission of the same `runId` from the store under a **new residency token** each make the pending call reject (`ResultNotResidentError` / `EngineDisposedError` / `AnnotationSupersededError`) and **publish nothing** — no entry exists for a nonresident run and `retentionReport()` shows no leaked snapshot; a `modelVersion` that changed while pending rejects; a source returning a non-`Map`, rejecting after serving one of two models, or a request with a duplicate name yields `AttributeSourceError`/`InvalidOptionsError` with nothing published; an absent `dbId` reads `null` for every requested attribute; unrequested entries are ignored; a non-scalar value becomes `null` and is counted; `annotate` on a nonresident id rejects `ResultNotResidentError`. |

| AC-16 | T1 | **Resident bound and identity enforcement** (§13.5.2, §4.2). With `maxResidentResultSets = N`: slots (active + resident) never exceed `N` under randomized start/cancel/seal/persist/release schedules; admission at a full engine evicts the LRU `evictable` (saved, unleased) set and admits, else throws `ResultCapacityError` **synchronously** naming the blockers (active, retained, held) — never queues, never exceeds `N`, never evicts a `held` (`skipped`/`pending`/`failed`) or `retained` set; a no-store engine refuses run `N+1` until `releaseResultSet(…, {acknowledgeUnsaved:true})` or `discardRun`; `releaseResultSet` on a held set without acknowledgement throws `UnsavedResultSetError`, on a leased set `ResultSetRetainedError`; `discardRun` chains the store `delete` after an in-flight `put` (a late write does not resurrect it); a `failed` set becomes evictable only after a successful `retryPersist`; `getResultSet` of an evicted set reads the store without making it resident; sealing never fails for lack of a slot; `dispose()` returns a `Promise<DisposeSummary>` that never rejects, **forces every `finalizing` run's `done` and terminal event closed with `{state:'pending', timedOut:true}` before releasing listeners (a listener subscribed to a run whose write hangs still receives its exactly-once terminal event on dispose, R13-2)**, and resolves only after every in-flight unit has drained and every run sealed (`cancelled`, or `failed`/`invalidated` when §16.7 says so) (so a dispose during a long unit reports the resulting held set in `droppedUnsaved`, or in `persistenceInFlight` if a write is pending), later calls fail with `EngineDisposedError` (thrown from the synchronous entry points, rejected from the Promise-returning ones), and repeated calls return the same promise; a capacity split that would exceed `maxUnitsPerRun` becomes `failed:unit-cap` (unit records never exceed the cap); `discardRun` on a non-terminal run **rejects** with `RunNotTerminalError` and with no store only releases; **nonresident ids (R11-5, §13.5.2):** after a set is auto-evicted, released (`acknowledgeUnsaved`) or discarded, `getPersistenceState` returns `null` (never throws, no retained per-run record: a 10⁵-run soak leaves the engine's retained metadata bounded by `maxResidentResultSets`), `retainResultSet` **throws** and `retryPersist` **rejects** with `ResultNotResidentError`, `releaseResultSet` is an idempotent no-op, `discardRun` still issues the store `delete`, `getResultSet` still reads through; an unknown id behaves as a nonresident one; **both `startRun` overloads** throw `ResultCapacityError` synchronously when admission fails (never a waiting queue); after successful admission `startRun({testId})` always returns a handle, and an archived/missing test then fails asynchronously — sealing `failed` with planning gap `test-unavailable`, its admitted slot becoming a normal resident set; **Dispose returns unsaved sets (R21-5):** a no-store engine holding four completed sets, `dispose()` resolves with `droppedUnsaved` containing all four frozen sets (ownership transferred, deep-equal to what `getResultSet` returned, none lost), the engine retains none, and a `persisting`-with-write-in-flight run appears in `persistenceInFlight` instead. **dispose with a hung write (R13-2, §10.2):** a run sealed with a never-settling `put` and a subscribed listener, then `engine.dispose()` before `persistTimeoutMs`: the listener receives its terminal `state` event **exactly once** with `{state:'pending', timedOut:true}` and `done` resolves with it **before** listeners are released, `dispose()` still resolves, the write continues detached and a later settle changes nothing observable through the disposed engine; **hung writes (§13.5.2):** with a store whose `put` never settles, repeatedly starting a run and calling `discardRun` exhausts the slots (a discarded set with a pending write keeps its slot) and then throws `ResultCapacityError` listing them as `pending` — resident sets never exceed the cap; aborting the `signal` lets a cooperating store settle and free the slot; a cap-sized many-vs-many planning case (100,000 target roots × many source units) stays within `maxPlanReferences`, and one over it fails `plan-too-large` before allocating; two module copies joining the `globalThis` coordinator share one queue and registry, an incompatible `protocolVersion` throws; `annotate` beyond `maxAnnotationBytes`/name/value limits rejects with `AnnotationTooLargeError` and retains nothing, and over-length host strings are rejected. **Identity:** `register` without `modelVersion` throws `ModelBindingError`; re-registering a known `modelKey` with a different `modelVersion` (also after `unregister`) throws `ModelKeyVersionConflictError`; a reload of unchanged content bumps `bindingGeneration`; a `ResultStore.put` whose `models[]` maps a `modelKey` to a different `modelVersion` than one already accepted rejects `IdentityConflictError`; `compareResultSets` puts every pair involving such a `modelKey` in `identityConflicts` and in no other class; distinct `modelKey`s over identical dbIds produce distinct `ResultId`s. **Combined ledger ceiling, every runtime record class (§6.5.1, M2 round 9):** a synthetic run whose options are `maxUnitsPerRun = N` (small, e.g. 50), `maxPlanReferences = M`, and `maxLedgerRecords = M + 2N` (the minimum the cross-field rule allows), with a plan that stays **within `maxPlanReferences` counting every planning class** (unreachable/mixed owners, digest rows, hierarchy rows, planning gaps and `N/2` initial units included, leaving unit-record room for splits) so that planning **succeeds** and the executor is actually reached; discretionary capacity is then `D ≥ N`. The run is driven by a fake executor whose output vastly exceeds `D`, producing, in randomized order and across all units, (i) `maxElementTaintsPerUnit` **element** taints, (ii) **`target-model`** taints (many reasons, many target models), (iii) **`unit`-kind** taints of every reason including `diagnostic-overflow`/`diagnostic-reason-overflow`/`statistics-overflow`, and (iv) repeated capacity failures that request **splits** (with their child unit and range records), plus retries, secondary causes and cancels. Asserts: the engine's own record counter never exceeds `maxLedgerRecords` at any instant (checked after every admitted record) and no allocation occurs beyond it; every unit's `taint-overflow` is always admitted (the reserved slot is consumed in place, `used` unchanged); once `D` is exhausted every further element / `target-model` / `unit` taint collapses into that unit's `taint-overflow` and every pair of that unit is `covered-caveated`, **never** `covered-clean`; a split that no longer fits is **not performed** — the unit becomes `failed:unit-cap` with `capDetail: 'ledger-records'`, its weight is a gap (refusals for the unit-record cap, `capDetail: 'unit-records'`, are exercised too), `pairSpaceTotal` and progress are conserved, and no half-recorded split exists; inline fields (outcome/cause/`secondaryCauses`/attempts/cancel conversion) still update after exhaustion (they allocate nothing); the run stays `completed` (or the status §16.7 dictates), **never `failed` because of the budget**; `ledger.budgetExhausted` and the first-refusal unit id are recorded and covered by `ledgerDigest`; the whole run is deterministic under input reordering; a plan whose `planRecords` would exceed `maxPlanReferences` (counting **all** planning classes) fails `plan-too-large` before any unit runs, and the end-of-planning invariant `planRecords + maxUnitsPerRun ≤ maxLedgerRecords` is asserted and holds for every plan that passed (it is unreachable under validated options, so it is checked by an internal-consistency test, not by an option-level failure); option validation rejects `maxLedgerRecords < maxPlanReferences + 2 × maxUnitsPerRun`. **Stored-reference validation (§5.5):** a store returning (i) a definition failing `validateTest`, (ii) a `schemaVersion` above the engine's, (iii) a lower version whose registered migration throws, or (iv) a lower version with no migration seals `failed` with planning gap `definition-invalid` / `schema-unsupported` / `migration-failed` (iii and iv), `definition: null`, `testRef.semanticDigest: null`, the three causes mutually distinguishable in the sealed planning gap, **no invalid definition, offending value or field-level issue detail persisted or exposed on the sealed set**, validation work bounded (`maxValidationIssues` scan bound, §5.5) and performed before any planning or collision execution, the admitted slot becoming an ordinary resident set. |
| AC-17 | T1 + T2 | **Mixed reachable/unreachable owner (H1, §6.2, §12.5 rule 6).** Mock model `M` (real `CollisionWorld`, mock instance tree): owner `900` owns fragment `41` (in root `100`'s subtree, tree-reachable) **and** fragment `42` (loaded, numeric owner, in no root subtree); owner `950` owns only unreachable fragment `43`; owner `200` is root-only. **(a) Classification:** `900` → *mixed* (recorded in `unreachablePopulation[].mixedOwnerDbIds`), `950` → *unreachable-only*, `200` → *root-only*; identical under input reordering and under both broad phases. **(b) Both orientations:** run `{A={model M}, B={model X}}` (M as source) **and** `{A={model X}, B={model M}}` (M as whole-model target): for every pair with owner `900` on **either** side — present in `results` *or* absent — `getPairStatus` is `covered-caveated` with `mixed-owner-provenance` and is **never** `covered-clean` and **never** `not-required`; a retained result for such a pair carries `flags.mixedOwnerProvenance` (and the result `900↔y` that only fragment `42` can produce is retained, not dropped, with that flag); `950`'s pair is `not-required` with `flags.unreachableOwner` (target orientation) or unevaluated (source orientation); `200`'s pairs are unaffected. **(c) Claim:** `canClaimNoClashes` is `false` in every case (`unreachablePopulation ≠ ∅`), standalone and engine-bound. **(d) Comparison:** a base holding `900↔y` and a next with no such result lands in `removedUnverifiedNext`, never `resolved`; a next with a new `900↔y` and a base with none lands in `addedUnverifiedBase`, never `added`. **(e) Property test** over randomized owner/fragment assignments: no pair with a mixed owner on either side is ever `covered-clean`, and `not-required` is assigned only to unreachable-only owners. **(g) Unattributable unreachable fragments (R12-1, §6.2, §12.5 rule 7):** mock model `M` also has a fragment `44` of class `N` and a fragment `45` of class `U`, each in no root subtree (the tree silently omits them), with no numeric-owner unreachable fragment at all (so `count = 0`). Run both orientations (M as whole-model source; M as whole-model target) **and** a same-model self-test (`A = B = {model M}`): `unreachablePopulation` lists `M` with `unattributable = {notLoaded: 1, ownerUnresolvable: 1}` in **every** case (orientation-independent — the source-orientation run has no library diagnostic to rely on: the recording fake world sees none), `canClaimNoClashes` is `false`, `libraryExclusions` contains `tree-unreachable-population`, and `getPairStatus` is `covered-caveated` with `unattributable-unreachable-population` for **every** pair with an endpoint in `M` and never for pairs not touching `M`; a control with `N`/`U` fragments that *are* tree-reachable records no `unattributable` count; the record is identical under input reordering and both broad phases. **(f) Documented library fact (F3):** a differential shows the library's owner-level result for `900` is identical whether `41` or `42` produced the hit, i.e. no fragment identity is recoverable — the reason the taint is conservative. |
| AC-18 | T1 + T2 (+ T3 characterization) | **Target fan-out bounded before the first call (H2, §6.4).** **(a) Exact cover under slicing:** property test (seeded) over 1–6 target models with whole/explicit/mixed target scopes and deliberately small `maxTargetModelsPerUnit`/`maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit`: every planned unit has ≤ `maxTargetModelsPerUnit` target models **and** target weight ≤ `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit` (a target root or whole-model sentinel whose *own* weight exceeds `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit` is **never** placed in a unit — see (b)), no root is ever split, a single *source* root heavier than `maxSourceFragmentsPerUnit` forms its own unit (packing target), the union of unit pair-spaces equals the required `P` **exactly once**, and the plan is byte-identical across input reorderings; changing only the target caps changes chunking, never coverage or `pairSetDigest` (T2, real `CollisionWorld`). **(b) Over-cap target entry (a whole-model sentinel *or* a single explicit root heavier than a deliberately lowered `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit`):** every unit that would carry it is created at planning as `not-run:target-too-large` (never issued — the recording fake world sees zero calls for it), `coverage: partial`, `pairSpaceTotal` includes its weight as a gap, `canClaimNoClashes` false. **(c) Plan guard:** a target-heavy federation whose slicing would exceed `maxUnitsPerRun` or `maxPlanReferences` fails `plan-too-large` before any call. **(d) Diagnostic stress (T2, real `CollisionWorld` with mock models):** a federation of many target models whose whole-model extraction emits thousands of `fragment-not-loaded`/`fragment-owner-unresolvable` diagnostics; the recording fake world asserts that **no single call's `targets` exceed the per-unit caps**; the executor records, per unit, the raw diagnostic count received from the library and the outcome's retained size, and the test asserts the count is ≤ the unit's own (source + target) fragment weight plus the library's per-call budgets and that projection stays within `maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome`; a **negative control** — a test mechanism, not an engine requirement (erratum **A12**) — bypasses the planner and issues one direct, un-sliced real `CollisionWorld.findClashes` call carrying every target whole-model sentinel (a call §6.4 item 3 never plans, at any cap) and shows it receives at least the federation's aggregate diagnostic volume — documenting the fan-out the target-side slicing prevents; an engine run at the option ceilings stays sliced per §6.4 item 3 and preserves coverage and `pairSetDigest`. The spec asserts no absolute raw-diagnostic bound; this test *measures* it. **(e) T3, informational:** on the Audubon fixture record per-unit target weight, per-unit ms and cancel latency for a maximum-weight target unit. |
| AC-19 | T1 + T2 | **Epoch-cache state is an explicit input (H3, §1.3.5, §6.4 P1, §15.1).** **(a) Dirty world, no capacity pressure:** the same definition, registered models and options run on (i) a fresh world and (ii) a world into which an *unrelated* `findClashes` (different source/targets, and a direct caller) has already admitted fragments below the capacity limit: identical `pairSetDigest` and `ledgerDigest`, both `environmentSensitive = false`, and `provenance.admissionBaseline` **differs** (recorded, not digested). **(b) Dirty world under capacity pressure (T1 with an injected `CapacityExceededError{kind:'bucket-capacity', details}` or T2 if ≥ 19,000 synthetic instances are practical):** the same definition on a fresh world and on a world whose bucket is near `maxCollisionInstancesPerEpoch`: in the dirty world at least one unit is `failed:capacity-exceeded` with `failure.capacityKind = 'bucket-capacity'` and `failure.capacityDetails` `{currentSize, missing, limit}`, `RunOutcome.environmentSensitive = true`, `coverage: partial`, `canClaimNoClashes` false; **every unit evaluated in both worlds has identical pairs**; `compareResultSets(fresh, dirty)` notes `environmentSensitive` and classifies no gap-covered pair as `resolved`. **(b′) Equal count, different membership (R11-2):** two dirty worlds whose touched epoch holds the *same* instance count (e.g. `limit − 200`) but different membership — in world 1 the unit's 200 fragments are already cached (no genuinely-new ids), in world 2 all 200 are new — record **identical** `admissionBaseline`; world 1's run has no `bucket-capacity` gap and reproduces the fresh-world pair set and ledger, world 2's unit is `failed:capacity-exceeded`/`bucket-capacity` with `capacityDetails` and `environmentSensitive = true`. The test asserts the flag follows the *outcome*, not the baseline, and documents that equal baselines do not imply equal outcomes (the baseline is observational). **(c) Scope of AC-6:** the determinism assertions of AC-6 are made only for `environmentSensitive = false` runs; an `environmentSensitive = true` run is never used as an equality baseline. **(d)** A `call-wide-unique-fragment-precheck`/`whole-model-enumeration` failure (input-only) does **not** set `environmentSensitive`. **(d′) Foreign placement contention (R11-3, §7.5, §15.1):** in T2 a real `CollisionWorld` whose placement revision is bumped by an injected direct-caller growth on every attempt of one unit (engine `placementGeneration` unchanged) exhausts `maxPlacementRetries`: that unit is `failed:stale-placement` with `failure.attempts = maxPlacementRetries + 1`, `environmentSensitiveCauses` contains `placement-contention`, `coverage: partial`, `canClaimNoClashes` false; the same definition in a quiet world evaluates the unit; every unit evaluated in both has identical pairs; `compareResultSets(quiet, contended)` notes `environmentSensitive` and classifies no gap-covered pair `resolved`; an abort that clears on a retry adds no cause and equals the quiet result; and a host-signalled `notifyPlacementChanged` (generation changed) instead yields `discarded:input-changed`/`invalidated`, never `placement-contention`. **(e) Geometry readiness (§1.3.6, §15.1):** in T2 (real `CollisionWorld`, mock viewer whose `getRenderProxy` is controllable) run the same definition twice where a needed fragment's proxy is unavailable in the first and available in the second, with fragment count, loaded flag, owner and `geometryId` — hence every scope digest — **unchanged**: the first run records `geometry-not-yet-available` for that fragment, `geometryReadinessOccurrences ≥ 1`, `environmentSensitiveCauses` contains `geometry-readiness`, `integrity: caveated`, the affected element `covered-caveated`, and `canClaimNoClashes` false; the second finds the clash; both scope-digest checks pass in both runs and `getFreshness` of the first is `current`, and the test documents that `current` does not mean a rerun would match; a run whose proxies were all available has `geometry-readiness` absent; `compareResultSets` of the two notes `environmentSensitive` and never reports the absent pair as `resolved`. |
| AC-20 | T1 + T2 | **Hierarchy-internal exclusions flow through the claim helper (H4, §5.6.3, §6.6.3, §8.3).** Mock tree: root `100` owns fragments and has fragment-owning descendants `101`, `102` whose geometry overlaps. **(a) Unaccepted default:** `A = B = {model X}` (whole-model self-test) ⇒ `ledger.hierarchyInternal` lists root `100` with `ownerCount = 3` and `libraryExclusions` contains `hierarchy-internal`; the run is `completed ∧ full ∧ clean ∧ current` with **empty `results`** (the `101↔102` intersection is provably absent — F16), yet `canClaimNoClashes` is **`false`** through **both** the standalone helper (given `'current'`) **and** `engine.canClaimNoClashes(runId)`. **(b) Accepted:** the same run on an engine created with `hierarchyInternalAcceptance: {recordRef}` ⇒ `provenance.hierarchyInternalAcceptance` recorded, `assessNoClashClaim` returns `allowed: true, unqualified: false` with a `hierarchy-internal-accepted` qualifier (`recordRef`, `rootCount`, `ownerCount`) and `proofMethod: 'bracketed-digest'`, **while `canClaimNoClashes` is `false`** (R16-2: the boolean never licenses an unqualified statement for a qualified result); a set sealed **without** acceptance stays unclaimable even when read through an engine that has one (acceptance is per sealed set, never retroactive). **(c) Scope of the table:** `A={model X}`, `B={model Y}` (root only in `A`) ⇒ `hierarchyInternal` empty; a root in `A∩B` with a single fragment-owning node ⇒ empty; an explicit non-owning parent with two owning children in both `A` and `B` ⇒ listed; `getPairStatus(101,102)` = `not-required`, reason `hierarchy-internal`, in both configurations. **(d)** `recordRef` longer than 512 code units or a non-string ⇒ `InvalidOptionsError`; the claim truth table (§12.5) is exercised with `hierarchyInternal ≠ ∅` × acceptance {absent, present} × every other axis. |
| AC-21 | T1 + T2 | **Settlement precedence (§16.7, §8.2, §10.2, §16.3).** Table-driven over the **combinations** of facts observable at one unit settlement or boundary — {cancel requested, `engine.dispose()` requested, world disposed, `ModelEvictedError`/binding unloaded, unload + re-register (`bindingGeneration` bump), `placementGeneration` moved, pre/post scope drift, `scope-unverifiable`, `CapacityExceededError`, `validateUnitOutcome` failure, `result-cap`} taken pairwise and in the specified triples (including the review's scenario: cancel + unload/re-register + thrown `ModelEvictedError`/`WorldDisposedError` + failed post-digest): the unit's primary cause is the highest-precedence fact of table A, its merge/discard behavior and the run's final status match **every row of matrix C** (with a cancel *and*, separately, an `engine.dispose()` pending), every other observed fact appears in `secondaryCauses` up to its cap — the **first 8 distinct** causes in canonical order, with a case carrying more than 8 distinct secondary facts asserting that the truncation is deterministic and that `RunOutcome.reasons` still lists every fact (§16.7) — the run status follows order B (`failed` > `invalidated` > `cancelled` > `completed`), `RunOutcome.reasons` lists every observed fact (a `failed`/`invalidated` run still records `cancel-requested`), and the outcome is **identical under randomized callback/microtask scheduling of the same facts** (the fake world/executor delivers the facts in every order). A planning-slice variant: world disposal or an enumeration failure together with a cancel seals `failed`, not `cancelled`. **Consistency (resolves §16.1 vs §16.3):** a reload of unchanged content (new `Model`, same `modelKey`) mid-run yields gaps + `freshness: stale` and the run **continues** (never `invalidated`), whereas `notifyPlacementChanged` mid-run yields `invalidated`. `secondaryCauses` is absent from `ledgerDigest` (equal digests across schedulings that differ only in secondary causes). |

**Required verification at implementation time** (mirrors the repo convention): `npm test` green with the new T1/T2 suites; the live T3 scripts run once against the dev server; the `docs/handoffs/latest.md` update follows `CLAUDE.md`'s Agent Handoff Convention.

---

## 23. Explicit non-goals

1. Any UI: the Viewer Clash Panel, and any Viewer rendering, isolation, `fitToView`, marker or overlay work.
2. Result status, approval, assignment, comments, issue-management integration, or any workflow state.
3. Persistence **backend** implementations, synchronization, multi-user sharing, quotas/retention policy (only the `ResultStore`/`ClashTestStore` ports and in-memory reference implementations).
4. Worker/off-main-thread execution, transferables, `SharedArrayBuffer`, worker-boundary topology, or **any** M4 Phase 4 work (S5′–S8′). Only the plain-data seam of §17.5.
5. Any change to collision-core contracts, modes, tolerances, budgets or constants (§21.2 lists optional additive changes that are *not* pursued here).
6. User-tunable geometric tolerances or penetration thresholds (the library has declared detection limits; they are not knobs).
7. Resumption, pause, incremental or partial rerun, continuing a cancelled run; persisting in-progress runs (§13.3).
8. Assembly roll-up of results, or a durable per-element identity beyond `(modelKey, dbId)` (`ElementRef` is closed in schema version 1, §4.4/§13.6; such an identity would be a future `schemaVersion` bump, OQ-2).
9. Composite multi-mode tests, suites/batches of tests, scheduling, recurring runs.
10. Group-By/filter **UI**, saved views, text search, disjunctive filters, clustering beyond the fixed-grid `locationCell`.
11. ETA, throughput, latency or responsiveness SLOs (§17.4).
12. Cross-session placement verification, automatic re-binding of a reloaded model into a running run, dynamic federation-wide operands (a Panel expands "all models" into explicit entries), property/predicate-based selections.
13. Fixing the library's time-sensitive modes, the tracked M4 follow-ups, or `blasBuilt`/`blasCacheHits` reporting.
14. **Nested (ancestor/descendant) selection across the two roles**, parent-vs-own-descendant testing, and partial-subtree selection — all need an exact-owner extraction scope in collision core (OQ-15); v1 fails planning with `hierarchy-overlap` instead.
15. Many-vs-many geometry acceleration (TLAS-vs-TLAS `bvhcast`, incremental TLAS): correctness-first decomposition only.
16. Reconciling results across **model versions** (a changed model gets a new `modelKey`, §4.2): carrying `ResultId`-attached workflow state from an old key to a new one needs a host-supplied element mapping and is a host/Panel concern; the engine only guarantees the ids cannot alias.
17. Persisting attribute annotation snapshots or making attribute grouping reproducible offline (§14.5); an `AttributeSnapshotStore` port would be a separate design.

---

## 24. Open questions and risks

| ID | Question / risk | Owner | Blocks spec? |
|---|---|---|---|
| OQ-1 | **Must be settled before integration acceptance (not before the spec):** how is `modelKey`/`modelVersion` derived in the Viewer host (e.g. document URN + derivative version for the key; a translation revision or content hash for `modelVersion`)? The engine treats both as opaque, but the §4.2 contract (one key ⇔ one immutable version) is only as strong as the witness; a constant or label as `modelVersion` defeats detection. | host/Panel | No |
| OQ-2 | Is a durable per-element identity (e.g. a Revit unique id) needed for continuity *across* `modelKey`s (i.e. across model versions)? `(modelKey, dbId)` is sound only within one immutable version (§4.2); a re-translation gets a new key and therefore no carried-over `ResultId`s until a host-supplied element mapping exists. | product | No |
| OQ-3 | Default `clearance` and its UX (the library has none; the extension's `0.05` is provisional). | Panel/product | No |
| OQ-4 | **Pre-planning feasibility gate for the Viewer adapter (not for this spec):** implementation planning of the Viewer adapter must not be authorized until a minimal live check on the acceptance fixture has recorded, in a `docs/research/` artifact, (a) that the tree-node owner (source rule) and `fragList.getDbIds` owner (whole-target rule) agree for every fragment (`ownerMismatch.count === 0`, AC-15 (g)), (b) the source-side use of the already-resolved `fragList.getDbIds` mapping (distinct-owner enumeration, `fragmentCount`) and the tree-readiness signal, and (c) the fixture's `unreachablePopulation` and `mixedOwnerDbIds` counts. **Fallback, decided now:** if (a) fails, planning already fails closed per affected model (`owner-mapping-mismatch`, §6.2) — v1 is then narrowed to content for which the two mechanisms agree and the supported-content statement is recorded; if that narrowing is not acceptable to the product owner, the remedy is a collision-core exact-owner extraction scope under roadmap §8 (OQ-15), *not* a change to the durable identity model. The library's "Mode 2" under-enumeration is inherited and silent. | plan | No (blocks implementation planning of the adapter) |
| OQ-5 | Unit-count and per-unit-overhead cost of same-model bisection (cell 3b) is **unmeasured**; a block-triangular scheme may be cheaper. AC-2 constrains any substitute. | plan | No |
| OQ-6 | Models over 20,000 fragments are uncoverable under `maxCollisionInstancesPerEpoch`; raising it is a collision-core decision (roadmap §8). | product/core | No |
| OQ-7 | Whether to pursue the optional additive library changes in §21.2. | milestone owner | No |
| OQ-8 | Sizing defaults (§17.1) are reasoned, not measured; no multi-process makespan exists. This now includes `maxTargetModelsPerUnit`/`maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit` (H2) and the lowered `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` (H6). Calibrate via AC-12 T3 and AC-18. | plan | No |
| OQ-9 | Is an animation-frame-aligned inter-unit yield sufficient to avoid the frame starvation M4 measured per query? Characterize; M4 never measured batches. | plan | No |
| OQ-10 | `ResultStore` backend, quota and retention policy. | host/Panel | No |
| OQ-11 | The world-scoped coordinator serializes engine runs only; direct `findClashes` callers (e.g. the Clash Finder extension) bypass it. Decide between a shared lock/lease the extension also honors and documented "unsupported for reliable batch throughput" (interim behavior: bounded retry, §7.5). | plan/Panel | No |
| OQ-12 | Whether the Panel exposes `containment`/`surface-intersection` given their time-sensitivity. | Panel | No |
| OQ-13 | After `invalidateModelPlacement`, the first run pays the placement refresh + TLAS rebuild (M4 R6/refresh: 57–69 ms main-only task, product confirmation of that workload still pending under Verdict D). A rerun-after-move flow will hit it once per rebuilt epoch. | product | No |
| OQ-14 | Digest *implementation* (async `SubtleCrypto`, which needs a secure context, vs a sliceable pure-JS SHA-256) given the ≤ `frameBudgetMs` span rule (§17.3); the format itself is fixed (§5.4). | plan | No |
| OQ-15 | Exact-owner (non-recursive) extraction scope in collision core, to support nested selections and parent-vs-descendant tests; and how common fragment-owning nodes with fragment-owning descendants are in the target Revit content (unmeasured). | product/core | No |
| OQ-16 | **Product acceptance of the hierarchy-internal exclusion** (§5.6.3, H4): a whole-model self-test omits clashes between fragment-owning nodes inside the same topmost root (F16 makes them unevaluable). **Unresolved; the interim behavior is fail-closed, not a deferral:** while no acceptance record is supplied, any non-empty `ledger.hierarchyInternal` makes `canClaimNoClashes` false (§8.3). To resolve it, the product owner must record — in a repository artifact that `hierarchyInternalAcceptance.recordRef` can name — (a) the measured `hierarchyInternal` counts (roots and owners) per model on the acceptance fixture (AC-15 (b) records them), (b) at least one concrete example root per class (parent/descendant; sibling owners under a non-owning root), and (c) an explicit statement that omitting those pairs is an accepted limitation of a "no clashes" claim. The engine can verify none of this (it stores an opaque reference); acceptance is therefore a residual trust boundary. Removing the exclusion instead needs the exact-owner extraction scope of OQ-15. | product | No (interim: claim blocked) |
| OQ-17 | **Must be settled before integration acceptance:** which host component owns *all* model-transform changes and is obliged to call `engine.invalidateModelPlacement` (§16.2)? A direct `world.invalidateModelPlacement` without `notifyPlacementChanged` leaves a result wrongly `current` — a documented host obligation, unenforceable by the engine. | host/Panel | No |
| OQ-18 | Does the Viewer expose a reliable, cheap witness of render-proxy/geometry readiness (per fragment or per model) that could be folded into the scope digests (§1.3.6, §15.1)? Until evidenced, readiness is a recorded environmental input (`geometry-readiness` cause), not a digested one. | plan | No |
| OQ-19 | **Policy (R20-4, one policy only):** the bracketed scope-equality proof (§16.3 item 5) **is** accepted for an unqualified "no clashes found" — this is the design's standing policy since round 2, not an open gate: `canClaimNoClashes` may be `true` with `proofMethod: 'bracketed-digest'` exposed and the residual stated in §8.3, and **nothing waits for a decision record**. The question left for the product owner is a *later tightening* review (requiring an additive per-call population identity from collision core, roadmap §8, §21.2, would make the residual vanish); a tightening, if decided, changes the claim rule through the ordinary spec-revision process. It is **not** a prerequisite in the table below. | product (review, non-gating) | No |

**Readiness is divided (R18-5).** *Core engine* (definitions, planner, ledger, identity, stores, results, derivations) and *Viewer adapter* (`clashtest/viewer/`, the `ElementEnumerator`/`ModelRegistry` Viewer bindings) are separate readiness scopes: only the adapter is blocked by OQ-4. Whether an implementation authorization covers the core only or the core-plus-adapter milestone is the **product owner's** decision, not made here (nothing is authorized).

**Named pre-integration prerequisites (R16 Q4).** The open questions that gate a *downstream step* rather than this spec, with owner, required artifact and pass/fail criterion; none is authorized or satisfied by this document.

| Prerequisite | Gates | Owner | Required artifact | Pass / fail |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OQ-4 live owner-mapping and tree-readiness evidence | Viewer-adapter implementation planning | project owner (runs the check); product owner approves any narrowed content envelope | `docs/research/` artifact from the Audubon fixture (AC-15 g, OQ-4 a–c) | pass: `ownerMismatch.count === 0` and readiness signal recorded; fail: v1 narrowed to agreeing content (recorded) or a roadmap §8 exact-owner scope (OQ-15) |
| OQ-1 `modelKey`/`modelVersion` derivation | integration acceptance | host/Panel owner | written derivation + a test that a retranslation changes `modelVersion` and never reuses a `modelKey` | pass: a changed model can never keep its key/version pair; fail: any constant/label used as `modelVersion` |
| OQ-17 placement-invalidation duty | integration acceptance | host/Panel owner | every transform/alignment path calls `invalidateModelPlacement`/`notifyPlacementChanged` (inventory + test) | pass: no unrouted path; fail: results may report `current` over moved models |
| OQ-16 hierarchy-internal acceptance | any *qualified* claim presentation | product owner | recorded product decision (`recordRef`) | pass: recorded; until then the fail-closed default of §8.3 applies |

None of these blocks the *specification* (OQ-4 is the one that blocks a downstream step — implementation planning of the Viewer adapter, by its own text; whether the core engine is planned before that evidence exists is a sequencing decision for implementation planning, not made here): each has a defined interim behavior in the sections cited (for OQ-16 the interim behavior is a fail-closed claim block, not an assumption of acceptance), and none changes a decision in §0.
