## 25. Review disposition

Independent review: `codex-review spec`, medium effort, request `20260920-034058-96286-27495`, verdict "Requires major revision". Every finding was verified against the repository before a disposition was chosen; none was accepted on Codex's authority alone.

| # | Finding | Verified against | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | Hierarchical dbIds invalidate the exact-cover proof | `extractFragments` in `ViewerModelAdapter.js` (explicit-stack subtree walk); `isSourceOwnFragment` | **Accepted — real defect.** Added F16, §5.6 (roots contract), root-level pair space (§6.1), `resolveRoots` port (§6.2), plan-time `hierarchy-overlap` rule, declared `hierarchy-internal` exclusion. Codex offered three options; chose its third (treat subtrees as indivisible) because the first two are collision-core changes this milestone must not require; the extraction-scope change is recorded as OQ-15. **D1 ("no core change required") stands, at the cost of a narrower supported selection shape** (nested cross-role selection is a stated non-goal). Property + differential tests added to AC-2. |
| C2 | Whole-model targets violate the run-start snapshot | `findClashes` re-extracts whole-model targets per call (`extractAllFragments`) | **Accepted.** Chose "call-time scope with drift detection" over "explicit immutable target lists" (which would trade the cheaper whole-model walk for thousands of per-root walks): §5.2, §16.3 drift guard using the public `getEpochPlacementDiagnostics().placementCount` against the run-start `fragmentTotal`. **Superseded in round 2:** the `placementCount` guard was replaced by per-unit bracketed scope digests (§6.2.1, §16.3, F18). |
| C3 | `canClaimNoClashes` can approve failed / non-claimable runs | §8.3 vs §6.6 as drafted (coverage vacuously `full`) | **Accepted.** Claim rule now requires `status: completed`, non-empty required pair space, no `emptyOperand`; pre-plan failure/cancel is always `coverage: partial` via planning-level gaps (§6.6.2). AC-10 extended to every terminal status. |
| C4 | `ResultId` aliases semantically different tests | §4.4/§5.4 as drafted | **Accepted.** `ResultId` now includes `semanticDigest`; cross-question carry-over must be an explicit reconciliation. (This reverses a deliberate draft choice that favored implicit carry-over; safety of attached workflow state outweighs it.) |
| C5 | Rerun comparison uses element coverage where pair coverage is required | §12.5/§15.4 as drafted | **Accepted.** Added `getPairStatus`; comparison uses it exclusively; `undetermined` maps to unverified; AC-10 has the `a↔b` counterexample. |
| I1 | Snapshot boundary contradiction; `unregister` identity | §5.2 vs §16.1 as drafted | **Accepted.** Boundary fixed at `queued → planning`; `unregister` checks object identity (§4.3). |
| I2 | "One run per world" not enforceable | §7.3 as drafted (engine-local queue) | **Accepted in part.** Added a world-scoped coordinator so multiple engines share one queue; the guarantee is explicitly scoped to engine runs because direct `findClashes` callers cannot be serialized (OQ-11). |
| I3 | Cancellation before a plan exists | §8.2/§10.2 as drafted | **Accepted.** Chose "abandon planning, seal with planning gap `cancelled`, no unit ledger" (deterministic, timing-independent). |
| I4 | Persistence settlement unbounded; eviction unprotected | §13.5 as drafted | **Accepted.** `persistTimeoutMs`, detached write, `RunHandle.persisted`, evict only after confirmed `saved`. |
| I5 | Validation contract incomplete | §5.3 as drafted | **Accepted.** Full field/nesting matrix, collect-all, normalization-after-validation, one shared function. |
| Q1 | Durable digest format deferred | §5.4/§13.1 as drafted | **Accepted.** Format fixed (`sha256:` over canonical JSON, `digestVersion: 1`); only the implementation stays a plan choice. |
| Q2 | Result-cap boundary undefined | §17.1 as drafted | **Accepted.** Whole-unit discard rule; canonical per-unit sort. |
| Q3 | `UnitExecutor` not injectable; worker seam overclaimed | §7.1/§18 as drafted | **Accepted.** `unitExecutor` constructor option; §17.5/§18 state it is an orchestration seam and does not specify geometry delivery to another thread. |

### Round 2 — independent Work review (five findings)

Each was verified against the repository (`collision/viewer/ViewerModelAdapter.js`, `collision/CollisionWorld.js`) before acceptance; verification of the library facts was done by a read-only subagent and is recorded as F17/F18.

| # | Finding | Verified against | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | Whole-model target drift detection (`placementCount`) cannot prove target-scope equality | `getEpochPlacementDiagnostics`/`_recordPlacement`: cumulative, write-once per fragmentId, never shrinks; `emptyStatistics()` exposes no per-call population | **Accepted; contract replaced (F18, §6.2.1, §16.3).** Per-unit *bracketed scope digests*: the executor digests the live scope (whole-model population incl. both dropped classes; per-root subtrees; `[fragId, status/owner, geometryId]` records) immediately before and after the call and compares by exact equality with the planning-time digests carried in `ExecutionRequest.materialized.scopeExpect`. Pre mismatch ⇒ `skipped:scope-drift`; post mismatch ⇒ `discarded:scope-drift`; uncomputable ⇒ `scope-unverifiable` (fail closed, gap). Drift invalidates the run. **Invariant I-scope:** only `scopeProof: equal` units can be `evaluated`, so `completed ∧ full` implies demonstrated equality. Declared residual (A→B→A within one call) recorded as `method: 'bracketed-digest'`; exactness needs optional library change §21.2 (f). `placementCount` is no longer evidence. Tests: AC-2 (digest conformance vs the library's own extraction), AC-8 (replacement/flip/growth/shrink/unavailable, property test, residual pinned). |
| W2 | `dbId: null` diagnostics (`fragment-not-loaded`, `fragment-owner-unresolvable`) must taint pair status | `extractAllFragments` hard-codes `dbId: null` and drops the fragment (F17); no other reason lacks a numeric dbId | **Accepted (F17, §12.2.1, §12.5).** `NormalizedDiagnostic.dbId` is `number|null`; structural `attribution` ∈ `element | target-model | unit` (unknown shapes degrade to `unit`, fail-safe); exact per-unit `taints` tables independent of the retained-detail cap; **taint rule** in `getPairStatus` (a `target-model` taint on a whole-model target, or any `unit` taint, makes every pair of that unit `covered-caveated`); `getElementStatus` and the `caveated` facet use it; `compareResultSets` therefore can never report `resolved`/`added` on tainted pairs. AC-10 extended. |
| W3 | Durable IDs must be model-version safe | `dbId` stability is per model version (§4.4) | **Accepted; option A chosen (§4.2).** A `modelKey` names one immutable model version forever; `modelVersion` is now mandatory (content witness); enforcement at `ModelRegistry.register` (`ModelKeyVersionConflictError`), `ResultStore.put` (`IdentityConflictError`, durable/cross-session) and compare/rerun/freshness (`identityConflicts`). A changed model gets a new key, hence new `ElementRef`/`PairId`/`ResultId`. Option B (version inside every id) rejected: same host-honesty dependency, longer ids, version-pinned definitions. Residual: a host reusing key **and** version for different content is undetectable (OQ-1). AC-16. |
| W4 | Resident-result limit must bound memory | §13.5 as drafted: "never evict unsaved" + "no-store retains all" = unbounded | **Accepted (§13.5.2).** Hard slot cap counting in-flight runs; classes `evictable`/`retained`/`held`; admission evicts LRU evictable else refuses with `ResultCapacityError` (never queues, never drops unsaved); explicit `retainResultSet`/`releaseResultSet`/`discardRun`/`retryPersist`; read-through `getResultSet`; sealing cannot fail for lack of a slot; `dispose()` reports dropped/in-flight. AC-16. |
| W5 | `RunHandle.persisted` semantics contradictory | AC-11 vs §13.5 as drafted | **Accepted (§13.5.1).** `done: Promise<RunSummary>` resolves once, never rejects, at seal + (first persistence settle or `persistTimeoutMs`); timeout = `{state:'pending', timedOut:true}`; `persisted` resolves once with the eventual `saved|failed|skipped`, never reports `timeout`; persistence continues detached; `getPersistenceState`, `'persistence'` event, `retryPersist`. `timeout` is not a persistence state. AC-9/AC-11 rewritten. |

### Round 2 — `codex-review spec` (medium), request `20260920-140117-6555-20172`

Verdict "Requires major revision"; 4 critical + 5 further findings, each checked against the spec text and the repository before acceptance.

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| X1 | Comparison has no valid join key (`ResultId` embeds `semanticDigest`) | **Accepted.** §15.4: join by `PairId`; entries carry `pairId` + `baseResultId`/`nextResultId`. AC-14. |
| X2 | `pairSetDigest` short-circuit hides evidence changes | **Accepted.** §13.1/§15.4: equal digest short-circuits membership and flags only; evidence still compared. AC-14. |
| X3 | `UnitExecutor` not implementable from its port | **Accepted.** §7.1/§11.2: `ExecutionRequest` (mode, clearance, broad phase, run snapshot, `scopeExpect`); structured `failure{code, capacityKind, modelKeys, …}`, `scopeProof`, `generations`; responsibility split stated. AC-3. |
| X4 | Synchronous `dispose()` contradicts cooperative cancellation | **Accepted.** `dispose(): Promise<DisposeSummary>` resolves after all runs drained and sealed; does not wait for writes; idempotent. §10.2, §13.5.2, §18, AC-16. |
| X5 | `maxUnitsPerRun` does not bound runtime splits | **Accepted.** §6.4: cap counts all unit records; a would-exceed split becomes `failed:unit-cap`. AC-9. |
| X6 | Owner→root mapping incomplete (explicit roots) | **Accepted.** §5.6/§6.2: complete `ownerDbIds` per root, ledger `rootOwners` (bounded by fragments in scope); `undetermined` only defensively. |
| X7 | `RunOptions` unvalidated | **Accepted.** §17.1.1 validation table, ceilings, cross-field rule, `InvalidOptionsError`. AC-1. |
| X8 | Item-count slicing does not guarantee 8 ms | **Accepted (softened claim).** §17.3: deadline-aware slices via injected clock (`sliceBudgetMs`), item cap secondary, 8 ms a reference-environment design target. |
| X9 | Definition-revision persistence underspecified | **Accepted.** §5.5: caller-assigned, store-enforced `latest + 1`; `RevisionConflictError`; archive/delete per test; `TestArchivedError`. AC-1. |

### Round 3 — `codex-review spec` (medium), request `20260920-140833-13494-11910`

Second pass on the round-2 revision. Verdict "Requires major revision"; 4 critical + 5 further findings, all checked and accepted (the shared-registry and unresolved-dbId findings were checked against `ViewerModelAdapter.js` `extractFragments`, which returns an empty map without a diagnostic for a dbId with no fragments).

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Y1 | Per-world coordinator shared but registry/placement generations engine-local | **Accepted.** §4.3/§7.3/§18: the coordinator owns the sole per-world `ModelRegistry`; a different registry ⇒ `ModelBindingError('registry-mismatch')`. AC-8 two-engine test. |
| Y2 | Capacity-split parents have no terminal state | **Accepted.** §6.4/§8.3/§9: normative `split` outcome, weight transferred once to leaves, coverage/progress over leaves, `unitsTotal` = leaves vs `unitRecords`, cancel/cap behavior. AC-9. |
| Y3 | `runId` uniqueness/create-only persistence unspecified | **Accepted.** §13.5: random UUIDv4 `runId`, create-only atomic `put` (idempotent on identical content, `RunIdConflictError` otherwise); identity index survives `delete`. AC-11. |
| Y4 | Empty/invalid explicit dbIds could yield a clean negative | **Accepted.** §6.2: `resolveRoots` returns `unresolved` (`not-found` fails planning `element-unresolvable`; `no-fragments` is a claim-blocking `unresolvedSelections` entry); claim rule extended. AC-2. |
| Y5 | Injected `UnitOutcome` unvalidated | **Accepted.** §7.1 `validateUnitOutcome`; violations ⇒ `failed:internal-error`. AC-3. |
| Y6 | `canClaimNoClashes` may rely on stale seal-time freshness | **Accepted.** Required `currentFreshness` argument (false if absent) and engine-bound `engine.canClaimNoClashes(runId)`. AC-10. |
| Y7 | Memory bound excludes unbounded strings | **Accepted.** §14.5/§13.5.2/§17.1.1: name/value/total-bytes limits (`maxAnnotationBytes`, `AnnotationTooLargeError`) and length caps on host-supplied strings. AC-16. |
| Y8 | `startRun(defOrRef)` sync vs async | **Accepted.** §5.5/§18: two overloads; a stored reference always returns a handle and lookup failure is a terminal planning gap `test-unavailable`. AC-16. |
| Y9 | Annotation snapshots claimed offline-reproducible but not persisted | **Accepted (claim removed).** §14.5: snapshots are deliberately ephemeral; `AttributeSnapshotStore` is a non-goal. |

### Round 7 — full independent design review (2026-09-20), authority: `docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-design-review.md`

Verdict "revise spec before implementation planning": 0 Critical, 7 High (blocking), 5 Medium, 4 Low. Each High finding was checked against the repository (`collision/CollisionWorld.js`: owner-level pair key `${sourceEpoch}:${sourceOwnerDbId}:${targetEpoch}:${targetOwnerDbId}` at the aggregation step; per-call `extractFragments`/`extractAllFragments` over **every** `targets` entry with diagnostics pushed before any projection; `admitCollisionInstances` / `_preflightCollisionInstanceAdmission` testing `currentSize + missing` against the cumulative `_instanceCache` bucket) and against this document before a disposition was chosen. All seven are accepted; none was rejected.

| # | Finding | Verified against | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | Tree-unreachable attribution unsafe for owners with both reachable and unreachable fragments | `CollisionWorld.js` aggregation (owner-level key, no fragment identity retained) | **Accepted; conservative taint chosen over failing planning** (§6.2 owner classes, §12.5 taint rule 6, §11.1 flags). Owners are classified *root-only* / *unreachable-only* / *mixed* from recorded plan data; **`not-required` is reserved for unreachable-only**; a mixed owner's pairs are required but never `covered-clean`, in either orientation; `mixedOwnerDbIds` recorded in the ledger. AC-17 (both orientations, comparison, property test, F3 differential). |
| H2 | Target fan-out bypasses outcome/diagnostic bounds | `CollisionWorld.js` target extraction loop and diagnostic accumulation | **Accepted.** §6.4 target-side bounds: per-unit `maxTargetModelsPerUnit` (4) and `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit` (20,000), slicing only *between* roots, whole-model sentinel above the cap becomes a pre-call `not-run:target-too-large` gap; explicit before/during/after accounting of where each bound applies and an honest statement that the engine cannot bound *inside* the core call beyond the caps. AC-18 (exact cover, cap enforcement, negative control, measured diagnostic stress). |
| H3 | P1 ignores cumulative epoch-cache state | `admitCollisionInstances` / preflight | **Accepted; determinism narrowed, not core changed.** Starting admission state is an explicit environmental input (`provenance.admissionBaseline`, per-gap `capacityDetails`, `RunOutcome.environmentSensitive`); P1/AC-6 apply to `environmentSensitive = false` runs; strict input-only determinism recorded as optional core change §21.2 (h). AC-19 (dirty world with and without capacity pressure). D1 amended. |
| H4 | Hierarchy-internal exclusion yields a clean claim without accepted semantics | spec §5.6.3/§8.3; OQ-16 | **Accepted; fail-closed until acceptance is recorded.** `ledger.hierarchyInternal` (roots of `A∩B` with ≥ 2 owners) blocks `canClaimNoClashes` unless `hierarchyInternalAcceptance` is supplied; the acceptance's required contents (fixture counts, examples, explicit statement) are in OQ-16; the engine cannot verify the reference (stated trust boundary). AC-20 through both claim helpers; AC-15 (b) tests both configurations. |
| H5 | Cross-mode comparison cannot join on `PairId` | spec §4.4/§15.4 | **Accepted; incomparable chosen over a new comparison key.** A `pairSemantics` change returns `incomparable` with every class empty (§15.4 gate, §4.4, §15.2); ordered `(x,y)`/`(y,x)` are distinct pairs by definition. AC-14 covers both orientations of change and the swapped-selection case. |
| H6 | Synchronous validation conflicts with the 8 ms gate | spec §5.3/§5.5/§17.3, AC-12 | **Accepted; hybrid of the review's options.** Validation is excluded from the sliced 8 ms guarantee, is a single linear pass with a bounded issue list (`maxValidationIssues`), has *lowered* ceilings (`maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` 10,000), and normalization/digesting moved into sliced work; the ceiling is a plan-time gate fed by AC-12's measurement, not an asserted guarantee. AC-12 rewritten into sliced-gating and synchronous-measured parts. |
| H7 | Diagnostic overflow rules contradict AC-3 | spec §7.1/§12.4, AC-3 | **Accepted.** Illegal data (reason > 128 code units, non-plain, unclassifiable) is *rejected*; legal-length high-cardinality data *overflows* with exact `total`/`completenessAffectingCount`, exact per-reason counts only for retained reasons, one `omittedReasonOccurrences` count and fail-safe unit taints (§7.1, §12.4, §13.1). AC-3 split into rejection cases and bounded-overflow cases. |

**Incidental, not a remediation of the Medium/Low findings:** the L1 (duplicate split-child ids) and L3 (§5.6 "every fragment in one root") wording fixes were made in passing because the same sentences were edited for H2/H1; AC-15 (b)/(c) now state selection `B` (part of L4); §15.1's "any machine/load/reload" promise was narrowed to one environment envelope because the H3 text made the old wording self-contradictory (touches the review's M4 subject only for that sentence). **Not addressed in this round (open, non-blocking):** M1 (terminal-condition precedence), M2 (plan-memory accounting), M3 (viewer-adapter feasibility gate timing, OQ-4), M4 (the remaining determinism-coverage gaps), M5 (annotation-input compatibility keys), L2 (cell-1 wording — partly reworded with H2, not separately verified), L4 for AC-15 (d)–(g), and the review's "trust boundaries not mechanically established" list beyond H4's acceptance reference.

### Round 8 — follow-up independent `codex-review spec` (medium), request `20260920-153241-74421-15823`, plus the original Medium findings

Verdict "requires major revision" (4 High, 5 Medium). It did not list round 7's H1–H7 as unresolved (and did not explicitly confirm them). Each finding below was checked against the spec text; H-3 was also checked against `collision/CollisionWorld.js` (`resolveGeometryRecord`, `RenderProxyUnavailableError`).

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| C-H1 | Identity index not atomic across different `runId` writes | **Accepted.** `put` is one linearization point covering runId check + every `modelKey→modelVersion` check/claim + insert (§4.2 point 2, §13.5). AC-11 races different runIds against a deliberately non-atomic store. |
| C-H2 (= original **M1**) | Combined terminal-condition precedence undefined; §16.1 vs §16.3 tension | **Accepted.** §16.7 fixed orders: unit primary cause U1–U9 with `secondaryCauses` (not digested); run status `failed` > `invalidated` > `cancelled` > `completed`; all facts listed in `reasons`. **Design decisions made here (flag for owner review):** `bindingGeneration` change/binding death is a *gap + stale* (run continues), only `placementGeneration` change and scope drift invalidate; `invalidated` outranks a pending cancel. AC-21. |
| C-H3 | Geometry/render-proxy readiness absent from digests, determinism, freshness | **Accepted in part.** Verified: the library already emits completeness-affecting `geometry-not-yet-available`, so no false clean claim is possible. Chosen contract: readiness is a recorded environmental input (`environmentSensitiveCauses: geometry-readiness`, exact `geometryReadinessOccurrences`), determinism is narrowed, `current` freshness is stated to prove extraction identity only. A readiness witness in the digests was **not** adopted (unevidenced; OQ-18). AC-19 (e). |
| C-H4 | Hard delete leaves `testId` reusable → `ResultId` aliasing | **Accepted.** Permanent tombstone, `TestDeletedError`, same-linearization-point write, cross-store residual stated (§5.5). AC-1. |
| C-M5 (= original **M2**) | Memory bound omits ledger structures | **Accepted.** One run-wide `maxLedgerRecords` (count of every retained plan+ledger record), fail-closed collapse to `taint-overflow`, cross-field reserve rule (§6.5.1, §17.1, §17.1.1). AC-16 combined-ceiling test. Count bound only; bytes unmeasured. |
| C-M6 (= original **M5**) | Annotation inputs lack compatibility validation | **Accepted.** Snapshot carries `runId`/`semanticDigest`; `groupBy`/`filter` verify runId, semanticDigest, every `modelVersion`, and attribute names, else attribute facets are `unavailable` (§14.5). AC-13. |
| C-M7 (= original **M3**) | Viewer-adapter feasibility deferred too late | **Accepted at spec level; evidence still outstanding.** OQ-4 is now a pre-planning gate for the *adapter* with the fallback decided (fail closed per model → narrow v1 → roadmap §8 core change, never an identity change). The live check itself has **not** been run (out of scope for a spec remediation). |
| C-M8 | Stored-reference validation failures have no planning outcome | **Accepted.** Every store read is validated; new planning causes `definition-invalid`, `schema-unsupported`, `migration-failed`; sealed set has `definition: null` (§5.5, §6.6.2, §13.1). AC-16. |
| C-M9 | Schema evolution vs strict V1 | **Accepted.** §13.6 per-type open/closed table; unknown fields excluded from digests. AC-11. |
| Rem-M7 | Explicit-root target-cap exception contradicts the per-unit cap and AC-18 | **Accepted; hard-bound semantics chosen** (the review's first option). `maxTargetFragmentsPerUnit` is a hard bound: a whole-model sentinel **or** a single explicit root heavier than it becomes a pre-call `not-run:target-too-large` gap (§6.4). The *source* cap remains a packing target (default 200 ≪ a legal root's 5,000), stated explicitly. AC-18 (a)/(b) rewritten. |
| Rem-M8 | Whole-array overflow labeled "exact" without inspection | **Accepted.** Unexamined diagnostics go to `unclassifiedOccurrences` (exact count of *unexamined*), never into `completenessAffectingCount`; `completenessAffectingUpperBound` is the only figure covering them and is called an upper bound; `integrity` is computed from it; `geometry-readiness` set as *possible* via `unclassifiedOccurrences > 0` (§7.1, §12.4, §8.3, §9.1, §13.1). AC-3 (iii) covers mixed / all-benign / all-affecting oversize arrays; AC-10/AC-6 updated. |
| Rem-NH1/NH2 refinements | Review's minimum corrections beyond the first draft of the fixes | NH1: AC-11 now races **both completion orders** and a **partial-overlap multi-key case** with all-or-nothing index/result state. NH2: §16.7 gains **matrix C** (in-flight unit primary cause × pending cancel/dispose → merge/discard, ledger record, final status), `engine.dispose()`'s "sealed `cancelled`" is now qualified by §16.7 (§10.2, §18, AC-16), AC-21 runs every matrix row under every fact ordering. |
| Orig. **M4** | Determinism promise broader than acceptance | **Accepted.** Envelope narrowed in round 7; AC-6 now also compares canonical diagnostic counts and per-unit taints; cross-machine equality is explicitly not asserted. |

**Not addressed (not requested this round):** Low findings L2 (cell-1 wording — partly reworded in round 7) and L4 for AC-15 (d)–(g). **Provisional and unmeasured:** `maxLedgerRecords` (4,000,000), and every sizing default already listed in §17.1.

### Round 9 — round 8 verification review (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round8-review.md`)

The review confirmed NH1, NH2 and seven of the eight Medium dispositions resolved (including the two §16.7 policy choices, which it judged explicit and testable) and left two findings, both accepted after checking against the spec text; neither needs a collision-core change.

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| NH3 (High) | Hard-delete tombstone bypassed by direct definitions and embedded-definition reruns, which never consult `ClashTestStore`; `ResultStore` had no test-lineage contract | **Accepted; shared-authority option chosen** (the review's first alternative). The durable authority is the `ResultStore`, the one place every durable `ResultId` passes: a permanent test tombstone set (`tombstoneTest`), checked inside `put`'s single linearization point (all-or-nothing with the identity index), new insertions under a tombstoned `testId` reject `TestDeletedError`; an engine admission check (`isTombstoned`, fail-closed `test-lookup-failed`) stops persisting runs early; `engine.deleteTest` writes the result-store tombstone first, then the definition-store tombstone. Archive stays advisory for direct definitions; **delete is enforced on every persistable path**. The lineage-id alternative was not adopted (it would change `ResultId` and the definition schema). Residual trust boundaries stated (bypassing `deleteTest`; each `ResultStore` needs its own tombstone; cross-store `testId` uniqueness). §5.5, §4.2, §4.4, §13.5, §13.5.1, §16.1, §18, AC-1 (engine-level, six sub-cases), AC-11 (store-level). |
| M2 (Medium) | `maxLedgerRecords` exhaustion defined only for further element taints | **Accepted; both of the review's options combined.** `maxUnitsPerRun` slots are reserved at end of planning for each possible leaf's `taint-overflow` (provably always fits); a per-class table covers element / `target-model` / `unit` taints (collapse into `taint-overflow`), split child + range records (split refused → `failed:unit-cap`, `capDetail: 'ledger-records'`), inline fixed-size unit-record fields (allocate nothing), separately-capped classes, and planning-only classes (`plan-too-large`). Deterministic canonical admission order; fallbacks only widen taint or turn a split into a gap; `ledger.budgetExhausted` recorded and digested. §6.5.1, §12.2.1, §6.4, §17.1, §17.1.1, AC-16 (all runtime classes, budget invariant checked after every record). |

**Not addressed (not requested):** OQ-4 live Viewer evidence (still an external pre-planning gate for the adapter), Low findings L2 and L4 for AC-15 (d)–(g), and all provisional/unmeasured defaults. **A further independent review is required before implementation planning.**

### Round 10 — round 9 verification review (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round9-review.md`)

The review confirmed the round 9 NH3 and M2 designs resolved (no Critical/High) and left two Medium defects; both accepted after checking the spec text. No design decision changed; both are consistency corrections.

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R9-M1 | AC-1(b) invokes `retryPersist` on a `persist: false` (`skipped`) set, contradicting §13.5.1/§18 (`failed` only) | **Accepted; the API contract was kept, the acceptance case fixed** (the review's first option). `retryPersist` stays legal only from `failed`. AC-1(b) now fetches the sealed set via `getResultSet` and calls the configured `ResultStore.put` directly (expects `TestDeletedError`, nothing inserted or claimed), and tests the *legal* `retryPersist` path with a run whose `put` first failed for an unrelated injected reason and is retried after `deleteTest`. §5.5 layer 1/2 wording no longer lists `retryPersist` as an entry point for a `skipped` set. Broadening `retryPersist` was not chosen (it would ripple through §13.5.1, §13.5.2, §18 and AC-11). |
| R9-M2 | `D ≥ maxUnitsPerRun` proof assumed `planRecords ≤ maxPlanReferences`, but `maxPlanReferences` counted only table entries + range records; AC-16's minimum-budget fixture could fail in planning | **Accepted; first option chosen.** `maxPlanReferences` now bounds **every** planning-time record class (`planRecords`), so `planRecords ≤ maxPlanReferences` holds by construction and the proof (`planRecords + maxUnitsPerRun ≤ maxLedgerRecords − maxUnitsPerRun`) is sound. The direct end-of-planning check remains as an asserted internal invariant (unreachable under validated options; a violation is `failed:internal-error`). AC-16's fixture is specified (`N` small, `M`, `maxLedgerRecords = M + 2N`, plan within `M` counting all classes, `N/2` initial units) so planning succeeds and runtime exhaustion is reached; the option-level `plan-too-large` case is now the `maxPlanReferences` overrun. §6.5.1, §17.1, AC-16. Note: the 2,000,000 default now covers a larger set of record classes and remains provisional/unmeasured. |

**Not addressed (not requested):** OQ-4 live Viewer evidence, Low findings L2 and L4 for AC-15 (d)–(g), provisional defaults. **A further independent review is required before implementation planning.**

### Round 11 — internal `codex-review spec` (medium) of the round 10 draft, request `20260920-202255-26792-26384` (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round10-internal-codex-review.md`)

The internal reviewer (not the independent acceptance gate) reported 1 Critical, 3 High, 3 Medium and three questions against draft SHA-256 `22bb434a…`. Every finding was checked against the spec text; claims about library behavior were checked against `collision/CollisionWorld.js`. **All were accepted at least in part; none required a collision-core change.**

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R11-1 (Critical) | Validated definitions are not snapshotted before asynchronous normalization/digest; `saveTest`, store and migration results have the same gap | **Accepted.** A real ownership gap (only sealing froze anything). Capture rule (§5.3): one pass reads each property once into a detached copy and validates the copy; applies to `startRun` (definition + `runOptions`, reference), `saveTest` (before the promise returns), store/migration results, `rerun` inputs; V1 requires plain data (no accessors/`Proxy`); the copy is O(input) inside the already-excluded synchronous span and is measured with it (§17.3, AC-12). Chosen over making admission asynchronous (would break synchronous error delivery, §5.5). AC-1 mutates after return and during yields. |
| R11-2 (High) | `admissionBaseline` records a count, but admission depends on membership | **Accepted in part.** The reviewer's option 2. The library admits on `currentSize + genuinely-new ids` (verified) and the public surface exposes no membership, so a count cannot reproduce an outcome; the spec had over-described it as "the explicit environmental input". It is now an **observational witness, not a reproduction key**. Not adopted: flagging every run with a non-empty bucket (would flag nearly every run in a busy Viewer; a successful call's verdicts do not depend on admission, and a run is flagged when a `bucket-capacity` gap actually occurs) and a collision-core membership digest (would need a §21.2 change for no verdict benefit). AC-19 (b′) covers equal count / different membership. |
| R11-3 (High) | Foreign placement-revision exhaustion is an unrecorded environmental input | **Accepted.** Verified: a foreign growth bump aborts a TLAS unit; retries exhausted ⇒ `failed:stale-placement` while `environmentSensitive` stayed `false`. New cause `placement-contention` (engine `placementGeneration` unchanged; a host-signalled change remains `invalidated`); a retry that succeeds adds nothing. Expected/actual library revisions are **not** recorded (`failure.attempts` is; the library revision is a moving target the engine cannot attribute). §7.5, §8.3, §13.1, §15.1, AC-8, AC-19 (d′). |
| R11-4 (High) | Cancellation before definition resolution cannot satisfy the Result Set schema | **Accepted.** The schema allowed `definition: null` only for a *failed stored-reference* run, yet §5.5 layer 2 already produced it for direct definitions (`test-deleted`) — an existing inconsistency. Single **pre-resolution representation** for both overloads and both terminal statuses (`definition: null`, `testRef = {testId, revision: null, semanticDigest: null}`, retained `testId` from the captured value; nothing unnormalized is persisted); `rerun` of such a set needs `definitionRevision`. Chosen over requiring resolution to finish before cancel seals (would break the cancel-latency contract). §13.1, §8.2, §10.2, §5.5, §15.3, AC-7. |
| R11-5 (Medium) | `getPersistenceState` is sync for late observers but the engine drops evicted/released runs | **Accepted.** Defined for active/resident runs only; `null` for a nonresident id; `retainResultSet`/`retryPersist` throw the new `ResultNotResidentError`; `releaseResultSet` idempotent; `discardRun` still deletes durably; `getResultSet` reads through. No history cache (bound unchanged). Also answers question 2. §13.5.1, §13.5.2, §18, AC-16. |
| R11-6 (Medium) | Executor caps are checked only after oversized arrays are materialized | **Accepted in part.** `ExecutionRequest.limits` and executor overflow markers (`pairsOverflowLength`, `diagnosticOverflowLength`) keep an oversized array from being projected or transferred; the engine still re-checks. **Not claimed:** bounding the library's own raw arrays — `findClashes` has already built them (F12); that volume is bounded only by the pre-call unit caps (§6.4) and measured by AC-18, as before. §7.1, §11.2, AC-3. |
| R11-7 (Medium) | Record-count limits do not establish a usable memory ceiling | **Accepted in part.** The bytes-per-record figure was already unmeasured and stated so; now the worst-case formula is written down and AC-12(c) reports it against a **product-confirmed memory budget before the defaults are frozen** (none is confirmed today, so the defaults stay provisional). A `maxResultSetBytes` byte-accounting knob is **not adopted** (unreliable estimation on JS objects; calibrated counts give the same fail-closed behavior). §17.1, AC-12. |
| Q1 | Is `UnitExecutor` trusted or structurally validated? | **Clarified (§7.1).** A trusted semantic port; the engine validates well-formedness only and cannot detect a structurally valid false clean negative, like a lying `ElementEnumerator`/`ModelRegistry`. AC-3 pins the limit. |
| Q2 | Which handle/persistence operations remain legal after release/eviction/discard? | **Answered by R11-5** (table in §13.5.2). |
| Q3 | Must OQ-4 evidence precede planning of the whole module or only `clashtest/viewer/`? | **No change.** Already scoped: OQ-4 gates the Viewer adapter and the `ElementEnumerator` owner-mapping claim (§1.3.4, §6.2, AC-15 g); the core's plan/ledger/identity contracts are defined over the enumerator *port*. Whether to plan the core before the evidence exists is a sequencing choice for implementation planning, which remains unauthorized. |

**Not addressed (not requested):** OQ-4 live Viewer evidence, Low findings L2 and L4 for AC-15 (d)–(g), provisional defaults. **This was an internal review; the internal loop continues and independent Work review is still required before implementation planning.**

New `unreachable.unattributable = {notLoaded, ownerUnresolvable}`, recorded per model in `unreachablePopulation`, claim-blocking through the existing conjunct and taint rule 7 (every pair touching the model is `covered-caveated`), orientation-independent; AC-17 (g) covers both orientations and a same-model self-test. **Not adopted:** a mandatory tree-readiness witness for explicit scopes — none exists in the Viewer (F15, OQ-4); the residual is stated in §6.2 and the claim's meaning for explicit scopes is qualified accordingly. |

One concrete `Reflect.ownKeys` + own-descriptor algorithm (no getter is invoked, sparse/extra-key arrays rejected, throwing/revoked proxies reported as `unreadable`), an explicit trust boundary (in-process caller; untrusted input must first pass a structured-clone/JSON boundary), and the cost claim corrected to include own keys of visited objects. Unknown-key enumeration is documented as **unbounded by the ceilings**, not fixed. §5.3, §17.3, AC-1. |
| R12-3 (High) | No untrusted-read contract for stored Result Sets | **Accepted.** `validateResultSet` (structure and ceilings from the engine's own configuration, bounded JSON-safe unknown open fields ≤ 64 KiB, cross-field invariants, canonical order/uniqueness, **recomputed digests**), typed `CorruptResultSetError`, nothing exposed on failure, freeze only after validation. Forged-but-self-consistent sets are an admitted residual (digests are not signatures); standalone pure derivations remain non-validating. §13.6, §18, AC-11. Answers question 1. |
| R12-4 (Medium) | `annotate` races eviction/release/discard/dispose | **Accepted; retain-lease alternative not adopted** (it would occupy a slot for the duration of an arbitrary property fetch and starve admissions). Residency token + atomic publish-if-unchanged, defined rejections, all-or-nothing `ElementAttributeSource` contract, no leak. §14.5, §18, AC-13. Answers question 4. |
| R12-5 (Medium) | Store pagination not deterministic | **Accepted.** Total order (`runId` / `testId`, code-unit), `limit` 1–1,000, opaque versioned keyset cursors, exactly-once for items present throughout, no snapshot isolation promised. §13.5, AC-11. |
| Q2 | Does a hierarchy acceptance authorize the literal claim? | **Clarified (§8.3):** it authorizes the helper only; the licensed statement is qualified by the recorded `hierarchyInternal` exclusion; wording is a Panel obligation (OQ-16). |
| Q3 | OQ-4 evidence before the broader implementation plan? | **No change** (as Round 11 Q3): OQ-4 gates the Viewer adapter and the enumerator claim; implementation planning remains unauthorized. |

### Round 13 — internal `codex-review spec` (medium) of the round 12 draft, request `20260920-204249-45015-26623` (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round12-internal-codex-review.md`)

Reported against draft SHA-256 `a769a7e6…`: 1 Critical, 4 High, 3 Medium, 4 questions. No collision-core change is required by any disposition.

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R13-1 (Critical) | Bracketed digests cannot support an unqualified "no clashes" claim (A→B→A excursion inside one call) | **Dispositioned — already a declared, decided residual (§16.3 item 5, round 2); made explicit at the claim and escalated as a product question.** The reviewer's two options are a collision-core per-call population identity (an additive core change under roadmap §8, deliberately not pursued since round 2) or making the claim conditional. The claim rule (§8.3) now states that "no clashes found" is *given bracketed scope equality*, cites `provenance.scopeProof.method = 'bracketed-digest'`, and **OQ-19** records the product question (interim: acceptable, method recorded). **Not adopted:** renaming the persisted, digested `scopeProof: 'equal'` value to `bracket-equal` (churn across every ledger/digest/test reference for no behavioral change; consumers are directed to `provenance.scopeProof.method`); making every claim false (would make `canClaimNoClashes` unreachable for a residual that needs a change and revert within one call — streaming monotonically *adds* fragments — and that the single-query library APIs share). |
| R13-2 (High) | `dispose()` contradicts the terminal-event/`done` contract | **Accepted (the reviewer's second option).** `dispose()` forces each `finalizing` run's window closed — settles `done`, emits the exactly-once terminal event with `{state:'pending', timedOut:true}` — *before* releasing listeners; the write continues detached. §10.2, §13.5.1 (new cause (c)), §18, AC-16. Answers question 3. |
| R13-4 (High) | Grouping specified as both synchronous and sliced; unbounded multi-valued facet multiplication | **Accepted.** `summarize`/`groupBy`/`filter` return Promises and slice; `maxFacetsPerGroupBy` (4), `maxFilterClauses` (32), and an incremental, pre-allocation `maxGroupMemberships` (1,000,000) with fail-closed `GroupingTooLargeError` (no partial result). §14.4, §17.1, §17.1.1, §18, AC-13. |
| R13-3 (High) | Nested schema migration cannot be implemented (no nested version fields) | **Accepted; aggregate model chosen** (answers question 2): one `ResultSet.schemaVersion` governs every nested structure; only `ClashTestDefinition` and `ResultSet` carry versions and migrations. §13.6, AC-11. |
| R13-5 (High) | `ClashTestStore.get` cannot expose archive state | **Accepted.** New linearizable `resolveForRun(testId, revision?) → {status: live|archived|deleted|missing, definition, latestRevision}` maps to the four planning causes; the definition schema stays closed; `list` summaries carry the archived flag. §5.5, §18, AC-1. |
| R13-5b (Medium) | `validateResultSet` ceilings tied to the reading engine's options | **Accepted.** Structural ceilings are the fixed schema maxima of §17.1.1; a valid-but-large set is read through, and `rerun`/`getFreshness` by id that would need it resident reject the distinct `ResultSetTooLargeError`. §13.6, AC-11. |
| R13-6 (Medium) | "Atomic" multi-model binding snapshot has no API | **Accepted.** `ModelRegistry.snapshotBindings(modelKeys)` — one synchronous all-or-nothing read with a `registryRevision`. §4.3, §5.2, AC-8. |
| R13-7 (High) | Viewer feasibility evidence (OQ-4) still absent | **No change; already specified.** OQ-4 already gates *only* the Viewer adapter (and says so — "not for this spec"), specifies the pass criterion (`ownerMismatch.count === 0`, AC-15 g), the fail-closed behavior (`owner-mapping-mismatch`), the decided fallback (narrow v1 to agreeing content, product owner decides; otherwise a roadmap §8 collision-core exact-owner scope, OQ-15), and its owner. This is an external evidence gate, not a spec defect; it is carried in the handoff. Answers question 4 in part (OQ-1 owns `modelKey`/`modelVersion` derivation; the placement-invalidation duty is §16.2/OQ-11). |
| R13-8 (Medium) | Attribute-size enforcement not deterministic | **Accepted.** Exact UTF-8 byte length of the canonical JSON (§5.4 encoding), truncation counted after the fact, incremental accounting before storing. §14.5, §17.1. |
| Q1 | Will the product authorize an unqualified negative under bracketed equality? | **Escalated as OQ-19** (interim: yes, residual stated). |

The reviewer's preferred option is taken: the stored-result invariant now equals the production rule (owner in `rootOwners`, or — only with the flag — in that model's `unreachablePopulation.ownerDbIds`); a flagged result with two root owners is corrupt. §13.6, AC-11 (save → evict → read-through case added). |

Answers questions 1 and 3 (OQ-4/OQ-1/OQ-17 owners are host/Panel/product, unchanged). |

### Round 15 — internal `codex-review spec` (medium) of the round 14 draft, request `20260920-205504-56220-8570` (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round14-internal-codex-review.md`)

Reported against draft SHA-256 `a403b078…`: 2 Critical, 3 High, 2 Medium, 3 questions. All accepted; none needs a collision-core change.

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R15-1 (Critical) | Sliced Result Set validation has a mutation/TOCTOU hole (stored and caller-supplied sets) | **Accepted — the same class as R11-1, not extended to Result Sets by round 12.** Capture-first: O(1) `schemaVersion` and top-level-length preflight (so no migration runs on an unsupported version), then one atomic `structuredClone`-equivalent detached copy, then migration/validation/digest/freeze in slices on the clone only. The reviewer's option 3; the clone is excluded from the slice target and measured. Store-side "copy on read" is a courtesy, not relied on. §13.6, AC-11, AC-12. |
| R15-2 (Critical) | Read-time freshness is not a coherent snapshot | **Accepted.** Verified: §16.4 digested scopes and then checked the registry without bracketing. Now: `snapshotBindings` → digest all scopes → `snapshotBindings` (touched-model change ⇒ `stale`) → digest all again and require equality with pass 1 and the sealed values; `current` only when both brackets agree; cost two passes; the A→B→A residual is restated. §16.4, AC-8, AC-12. Answers question 2 in part: `canClaimNoClashes` keeps the round-2 interim (true when the axes qualify; OQ-19 unresolved) — treating it like the hierarchy-internal case would make the claim unreachable for a residual needing a revert inside one call, and the hierarchy case differs in that omitted *pairs* are known, not merely undetectable. |
| R15-4 (High) | Result comparison is unbounded and synchronous | **Accepted.** `compareResultSets` returns a Promise; O(n+m) merge over canonical order with ledger indexes built once; `signal`; shared derivation-concurrency bound; `maxComparisonEntries` with all-or-nothing `ComparisonTooLargeError`. §15.4, §17.1, §17.1.1, §18, AC-14/AC-13. |
| R15-3 (High) | Configuration ownership contradictory/incomplete | **Accepted.** One exhaustive split: RunOptions (per run, recorded, sizing options digested) vs engine limits (constructor, fixed, not recorded, never digested); constructor `limits` and `RunOptions` lists match it; a misplaced key is an unknown key. The reviewer's seven-column table was not reproduced (the ceilings/defaults already live in §17.1/§17.1.1; ownership is the missing dimension). §17.1.1, §18. |
| R15-6 (High) | Store listings lack an untrusted-read contract | **Accepted.** Closed `ResultSetSummary`/`TestSummary` shapes; each page captured and validated; an invalid entry rejects the page whole (`CorruptListingError`, no silent skips); `listTests`/`listResults` completed and put behind read slots. §13.5, §18, AC-11. |
| R15-5 (Medium) | Spatial facet arithmetic can yield non-finite/aliased `GroupId`s | **Accepted.** Bucket indices must be safe integers, else `FacetRangeError` (no partial grouping); a `GroupId` can never alias the `null` group. §14.2, AC-13. |
| R15-7 (Medium) | `put`-vs-abort semantics unspecified | **Accepted.** Abort is advisory; rejection ⇒ no mutation of any kind; once linearized, `put` resolves; the engine infers nothing from an abort and `discardRun`'s delete is chained after settlement. §13.5, AC-11. |
| Q1 | Who owns the OQ-4 gate; must core planning wait? | **Unchanged** (host/Panel/product; sequencing left to implementation planning, unauthorized). |
| Q3 | Behavior with no/only one store configured | **Answered** (§5.5): `StoreNotConfiguredError`; `deleteTest` performs each configured step and errors only if neither is configured. |

**Not addressed (not requested):** OQ-4 live Viewer evidence, Low findings L2 and L4 for AC-15 (d)–(g), provisional defaults. **This was an internal review; independent Work review is still required before implementation planning.**

### Round 16 — internal `codex-review spec` (medium) of the round 15 draft, request `20260920-210316-63016-12453` (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round15-internal-codex-review.md`)

Reported against draft SHA-256 `2b02944d…`: 0 Critical, 4 High, 1 Medium, 4 questions (one of them, Q4, rated High, re-raising OQ-1/4/17). All accepted; none needs a collision-core change.

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R16-1 (High) | Untrusted Result Set capture is not safely or boundedly specified (`structuredClone` invokes getters; preflight can disagree; only two lengths checked before cloning) | **Accepted — a defect in round 15's own text.** Verified: native `structuredClone` invokes enumerable getters. The capture is now the §5.3 reflection algorithm generalized (iterative, descriptor-only, cycle-aware, `not-plain` rejection), ceilings (depth, string, array, node count, unknown-field bytes) charged *before* allocating the copy, `schemaVersion` read first and rejected before anything else, no separate preflight. `structuredClone` is never called on external input. §13.6, AC-11. |
| R16-2 (High) | The no-clash helper loses a mandatory qualification (boolean vs hierarchy acceptance) | **Accepted.** `assessNoClashClaim → {allowed, unqualified, qualifiers, exclusions, proofMethod}`; `canClaimNoClashes` is `true` only when `unqualified` (no acceptance-dependent exclusion), so the natural boolean use cannot display an unqualified statement for a qualified result. The reviewer's further ask — resolving OQ-19 before any claim — is **not** done: OQ-19 is a product decision; the interim (round 2) stands, with the proof method exposed as `proofMethod`. §8.3, D7, §18, AC-15 (b), AC-20 (b). |
| R16-3 (High) | The persistence acknowledgement contract is impossible for a remote backend | **Accepted; the reviewer's `unknown`-outcome option in a lighter form.** A typed `PersistenceOutcomeUnknownError` for an indeterminate write, one automatic `get`-based reconcile (identical content ⇒ `saved`), and — because `put` is create-only/idempotent — `retryPersist` from `failed` is always safe; no fifth persistence state, slot retention unchanged. Restricting the port to local stores was not chosen (storage backends are a non-goal, §23). §13.5, AC-11. |
| R16-4 (High) | Valid runs can produce invalid listing summaries (256-model cap vs definitions) | **Accepted — an inconsistency introduced in round 15.** Summary `models` ceiling is 20,000 (= 2 × the `maxEntriesPerSelection` ceiling), so every valid run's summary is valid; limit-and-one-above acceptance cases. §13.5, AC-11. |
| R16-5 (Medium) | Comparisons across modes have no determinism class | **Accepted.** `baseDeterminismClass`, `nextDeterminismClass` and a `determinismClass` defined as the weaker; `timeSensitive` per entry iff the weaker is time-sensitive; the note names the weakening side. §15.4, AC-14. |
| Q1 | What does `validateTest` return/throw? | **Answered:** `{issues, issuesTruncated}`, never throws for data errors; `saveTest`/`startRun` throw. §5.3, §18. |
| Q2 | Truth rules for multi-valued/unavailable facets | **Answered:** existential clauses, complementary `isNull`/`notNull`, ordering ops numeric-only, an unavailable facet is *false for every result* and reported in `unavailable`. §14.4, AC-13. |
| Q3 | Non-finite annotation numbers | **Answered:** replaced by `null` and counted; `-0` ⇒ `0`. §14.5. |
| Q4 (rated High) | When are the integration gates resolved? | **Restated as a named-prerequisites table** (owner, artifact, pass/fail) for OQ-4, OQ-1, OQ-17, OQ-16/19. Resolution is external evidence/decisions, not a spec edit; nothing is authorized. §24. |

### Round 17 — internal `codex-review spec` (medium) of the round 16 draft, request `20260920-211118-69383-30495` (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round16-internal-codex-review.md`)

Reported against draft SHA-256 `a4f2db94…`: 1 Critical, 3 High, 1 Medium, 3 questions. All accepted; none needs a collision-core change.

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R17-1 (Critical) | Read-time freshness not bracketed around the whole operation (no registry snapshot after the second digest pass) | **Accepted — a gap in round 15's own protocol.** Placement changes are invisible to scope digests, so only a snapshot after `digest₂` can see one landing during it. Protocol is now `snapshot₁ → digest₁ → snapshot₂ → digest₂ → snapshot₃`, every touched binding compared across all three, `current` only when all agree; cost stated. §16.4, AC-8 (the after-second-digest-before-return case). |
| R17-2 (High) | Ambiguous-write reconciliation contradicts one-shot persistence semantics | **Accepted — a contradiction from round 16.** Reconciliation is part of the same attempt: the attempt stays `pending` through the single reconcile read and settles once (`saved` or `failed`, `error.ambiguous`); no intermediate final `failed` is ever observable; timeout behavior and read-slot failure specified. §13.5, AC-11. |
| R17-3 (High) | External Result Set capture permits a massive uninterruptible stall | **Accepted in part.** The atomic synchronous capture is *kept* (a sliced capture cannot give the no-TOCTOU guarantee, and a store port cannot promise exclusive ownership transfer), so it is capped and **measured**: worst-case duration and peak (~2× graph) at the schema ceilings in AC-12, fed into the §17.1 memory estimate, with the schema ceilings frozen only after measurement and a `ResultSetTooLargeError` if lowered below a valid stored set. The reviewer's alternative (serialized-byte boundary) was not adopted: it moves, not removes, the parse cost. §13.6, §17.1, AC-12. |
| R17-4 (High) | Standalone derivations cannot enforce engine-owned limits | **Accepted (the reviewer's option 2, with an engine-owned instance).** `DerivationContext` (`engine.derivations` or `createDerivationContext`), concurrency per context, no module-global state; pure helpers needing no scheduler stay standalone. §14.4, §17.1.1, §18, AC-13. |
| R17-5 (Medium) | `assessNoClashClaim` missing from the engine contract | **Accepted.** `engine.assessNoClashClaim(runIdOrResultSet)` beside `canClaimNoClashes`, same read-through/validation/freshness. §18, AC-8. |
| Q1 | `locationCell` numeric ordering has no component selector | **Answered:** it is array-valued; ordering ops reject on it; `eq`/`in` compare the whole array. §14.4. |
| Q2 | Attribute-source `Map` ownership | **Answered:** captured in one synchronous pass on resolution over exactly the requested `dbId`s; no ownership transfer required. §14.5. |
| Q3 | Prerequisites enforced? | **Unchanged:** the §24 prerequisites table stands; nothing is authorized. |

**Not addressed (not requested):** OQ-4 live Viewer evidence, Low findings L2 and L4 for AC-15 (d)–(g), provisional defaults. **This was an internal review; independent Work review is still required before implementation planning.**

Answers question 1. §13.5, §17.1, §17.1.1, AC-11. |

### Round 19 — internal `codex-review spec` (medium) of the round 18 draft, request `20260920-212147-79302-5443` (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round18-internal-codex-review.md`)

Reported against draft SHA-256 `aaecc3eb…`: 0 Critical, 3 High, 2 Medium, 3 questions; the reviewer confirmed `collision/` is unchanged since the spec's baseline and found no new exact-cover or false-clean defect in the planner. All accepted; none needs a collision-core change.

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R19-1 (High) | Capture-and-validation boundary not exhaustive (raw sets to `getFreshness`, `canClaimNoClashes`, `assessNoClashClaim`) | **Accepted.** Universal rule: capture synchronously before the first `await`, failures as rejected Promises, caller-supplied sets use the read slots, claim/freshness validate in full; derivation-context methods capture (no claim, no full validation); purely synchronous helpers need none. §13.6, AC-11. |
| R19-2 (High) | `assessNoClashClaim.allowed` can be implemented without checking that results are empty | **Accepted — an inconsistency between the predicate and later prose.** `results = ∅` moved into the single normative predicate; `allowed` is exactly it; `unqualified = allowed ∧ hierarchyInternal = ∅`. AC-10-level cases (non-empty results with every other conjunct true ⇒ both helpers deny). §8.3, AC-15/AC-20-adjacent cases in AC-11's claim block. |
| R19-3 (High) | Ambiguous persistence settlement after `dispose()` undefined | **Accepted (the reviewer's second option).** After dispose an ambiguous rejection settles `failed` (`ambiguous`, `disposed`) with no reconciliation, no retained timer/read capability; a resolution settles `saved`; the host reconciles itself. §13.5, AC-11. |
| R19-4 (Medium) | Contract sketch omits accepted method options | **Accepted.** `annotate(…, {signal?})`, `compareResultSets(…, {signal?, distanceTolerance?, pointTolerance?})`, closed option objects. §18, AC-13/AC-14. |
| R19-5 (Medium) | Default persistence policy not stated | **Accepted.** `persist` defaults to `true` (persist when a store exists; `skipped`/`no-store` otherwise). §17.1, AC-11. |
| Q1 | Implementation authorization scope | **Product owner's decision** (as Round 18; §24 readiness split). |
| Q2 | Memory gate before freezing 16 M nodes / 256 MiB | **Clarified:** no default may be frozen before AC-12 calibration; they ship only as provisional, overridable defaults. §17.1. Budget itself remains a product input. |
| Q3 | Must OQ-19 be resolved before an unqualified claim UI ships? | **Open product decision (OQ-19);** the spec's interim stands and `proofMethod` is exposed so a UI can qualify the statement. |

**Not addressed (not requested):** OQ-4 live Viewer evidence, the product-approved memory/stall budget, OQ-19, Low findings L2 and L4 for AC-15 (d)–(g), provisional defaults. **This was an internal review; independent Work review is still required before implementation planning.**

### Round 20 — internal `codex-review spec` (medium) of the round 19 draft, request `20260920-212650-83795-16541` (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round19-internal-codex-review.md`)

Reported against draft SHA-256 `84db4163…`: 1 Critical, 4 High, 1 Medium, 3 questions. All accepted; none needs a collision-core change.

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R20-1 (Critical) | Whole-model source population is not bracketed | **Accepted — a real false-clean path.** Verified against the spec: §16.3.2 bracketed whole-model *target* populations and source-root subtree digests, but a `kind:'model'` source's *population* was digested only at planning and at read-time freshness, so a fragment outside every root subtree could appear during a run and revert before claim-time freshness without changing any bracketed digest. Now every unit using a `kind:'model'` source also brackets that model's whole-population digest (pre- and post-check); explicit-list sources have no population digest and are unchanged. The A→B→A-inside-one-call residual is unchanged and still declared. §16.3.2, AC-8. |
| R20-2 (High) | Raw-set capture conflicts with queued read-slot semantics | **Accepted (the reviewer's recommendation).** A raw set reserves a slot synchronously before capture or is rejected immediately with `ReadCapacityError`; only by-id store reads queue FIFO. §13.6, §13.5.2, AC-11. |
| R20-3 (High) | Valid completed partial runs fail their own read-back validation | **Accepted — a defect in round 12's invariant.** `completed` now forbids only fatal, input-change and cancel causes and *permits* the non-fatal unit gaps (which imply `coverage: partial`); statuses are checked as status, never as substrings. Save → evict → read-back test for every non-fatal gap outcome. §13.6, AC-11. |
| R20-4 (High) | OQ-19 both permits and blocks unqualified claims | **Accepted; the first policy chosen** — the round-2 interim *is* the standing policy (it was decided then, accepted with the method recorded, and `proofMethod` is exposed); OQ-19 becomes a non-gating tightening review and is removed from the prerequisites table. Making it a gate would make `canClaimNoClashes` unreachable for a residual that requires a change-and-revert inside one call. **This is a judgment call the product owner may reverse** (a reversal changes the claim rule through a spec revision). §8.3, §24. Answers question 3 of round 19 as well. |
| R20-4b (High) | Store-side byte guard is both mandatory and optional | **Accepted (option 1).** A required capability of a conforming store, proven by a store conformance test; the memory guarantee is stated as holding only for conforming stores. §13.5, AC-11. |
| R20-5 (Medium) | `broadPhase` and `interUnitYield` defaults unspecified | **Accepted.** `'tlas'` and `'animation-frame'` (degrading to a macrotask); table-driven omitted-option test. §17.1, AC-1. |
| Q1 | `retainResultSet` on a `held` set | **Answered:** `UnsavedResultSetError` (held sets are never evicted, so a lease is meaningless). §13.5.2, §18. |
| Q2 | `getRun` for a terminal resident run | **Answered:** returns the handle while resident, `null` once nonresident. §13.5.2, §18. |
| Q3 | Implementation authorization scope | **Product owner's decision** (Round 18/19). |

**Not addressed (not requested):** OQ-4 live Viewer evidence, the product-approved memory/stall budget, Low findings L2 and L4 for AC-15 (d)–(g), provisional defaults. **This was an internal review; independent Work review is still required before implementation planning.**

### Round 21 — internal `codex-review spec` (medium) of the round 20 draft, request `20260920-213243-89190-2834` (`docs/reviews/2026-09-20-federated-clash-test-and-results-round20-internal-codex-review.md`)

Reported against draft SHA-256 `68173693…`: 0 Critical, 4 High, 1 Medium, 3 questions; the reviewer found no new false-clean path in the core planner. All accepted; none needs a collision-core change.

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R21-1 (High) | `rerun(ResultSet)` has an impossible error-delivery contract | **Accepted (the reviewer's preferred option).** `rerun → Promise<RunHandle>`; read-slot reservation before capture, run-slot admission synchronously at validation success, every failure rejects and releases; mutation-after-call safe. `startRun` stays synchronous. §15.3, §18, AC-11. |
| R21-2 (High) | Valid writer limits can create unreadable Result Sets | **Accepted.** Fixed schema ceiling `SCHEMA_MAX_NODES` (derived from the producer hard maxima and closed-shape node weights) vs. operational `maxCapturedNodes`; normative producer/reader invariant enforced at option validation (`InvalidOptionsError('result-unreadable')`); provisional run defaults lowered (`maxPlanReferences` 2,000,000→500,000, `maxLedgerRecords` 4,000,000→1,000,000, `maxStoredResultBytes` 256→512 MiB) so the defaults satisfy it; maximum-shape produce→persist→evict→read→rerun tests. The numbers remain provisional and unmeasured (AC-12 calibrates `bytesPerNode` and freezes them jointly). §13.6, §17.1, §17.1.1, AC-11. |
| R21-3 (High) | Hard memory estimate omits derivation inputs, indexes and outputs | **Accepted.** Derivation slot before capture; the §17.1 formula gains the transient read/derivation/annotation terms (`S_set = maxCapturedNodes × bytesPerNode`); a two-simultaneous-maximum-comparison peak test. §14.4, §17.1, AC-12/AC-13. |
| R21-4 (High) | Stored-result validation does not link results to producing units | **Accepted.** Result-to-ledger linkage: an existing evaluated `equal` leaf unit whose ranges cover the pair, matching orientation, mode/evidence agreement; negative read-back cases. §13.6, AC-11. |
| R21-5 (Medium) | Disposal contradicts the unsaved-state retention guarantee | **Accepted (the reviewer's "return the sets" alternative).** `dispose()` returns `droppedUnsaved` as the frozen sets (ownership transferred) so nothing is destroyed; "only operation that destroys unsaved state" restated as two explicit host operations. §10.2, §13.5.2, §18, AC-16. |
| Q3 | Should swapping `selectionA`/`selectionB` preserve `semanticDigest` for unordered modes? | **Decided: no — a different question,** because role assignment drives the rank-6 tie-break residue and cell orientation; pinned by a golden-vector pair. §5.4, AC-1. |
| Q1, Q2 | Authorization scope; product memory budget | **Unchanged** (product owner; no budget exists — defaults stay provisional). |

**Not addressed (not requested):** OQ-4 live Viewer evidence, the product-approved memory/stall budget, Low findings L2 and L4 for AC-15 (d)–(g), provisional defaults. **This was an internal review; independent Work review is still required before implementation planning.**

The §13.6 closed-shape bound for one `ClashResult` was 24 nodes, but under the capture meter (every object, array and primitive is one node; keys are free) a legal `ClashResult` costs 20 to 35 nodes — an ordinary volume-overlap result with a witness already costs 26, and a clearance result carrying all four flags, which the schema and `validateResultSet` accept, costs 35. The formula therefore under-counted, and option validation could accept results-heavy options whose legal sets the same engine then refused as `ResultSetTooLargeError`, violating R21-2. **Change 1:** the result factor becomes **35** in the `SCHEMA_MAX_NODES` formula, in the closed-shape bound, and in the producer/reader invariant. **Change 2 (reconciliation):** under factor 35 the byte half of the invariant (`maxCapturedNodes × bytesPerNode ≤ maxStoredResultBytes`, so at most ⌊1 GiB / 32⌋ = 33,554,432 nodes) makes the former 1,000,000 `maxResultsPerRun` ceiling unreachable by any engine configuration; the ceiling becomes **956,824** = ⌊(33,554,432 − 12 × 3 − 65,536) / 35⌋, the largest value accepted with the smallest legal ledger (`maxLedgerRecords` = 3) and no retained diagnostics. `SCHEMA_MAX_NODES` becomes 274,754,376 (was 265,265,536); the provisional-default worst case becomes 15,685,536 (was 14,585,536), still ≤ the 16,000,000 `maxCapturedNodes` default, so **no §17.1 default changes**. The ceiling stays provisional (G-CAL) and moves with `bytesPerNode`. The ledger and diagnostic factors (12) are **not** changed by this row; they are known to be unsound and remain open owner gates (D-8b, D-8c) in the evidence record. No schema shape, error code, capture rule or reader limit changed (the `results` array's schema limit follows `maxResultsPerRun`, so a set with 956,825–1,000,000 results is no longer an accepted shape; no such set can be produced under the corrected ceiling). |

AC-18 (d)'s negative control read "(caps set to their ceilings, all target models in one unit) shows the un-sliced call receives a diagnostic volume proportional to the whole federation". Read as an engine requirement, that asks the engine to plan every whole-model target sentinel into one unit, which §6.4 item 3 forbids at any cap ("a whole-model sentinel is not partitionable … it is placed alone in a slice"), and (d)'s federation is exactly a whole-model one. **Clarified:** the single un-sliced call is a **test mechanism** that documents the fan-out the target-side slicing prevents: a direct call that bypasses the planner, against the same real `CollisionWorld`. It is **not** an engine requirement and does not override §6.4 item 3. An engine run at the option ceilings stays sliced and keeps coverage and `pairSetDigest`. "Proportional to the whole federation" becomes "at least the federation's aggregate diagnostic volume", which is what the control measures (the un-sliced count ≥ the sum of the sliced per-call counts); proportionality across federation sizes is not claimed. **Nothing else changed:** (d)'s invariant (no single call's `targets` exceed the per-unit caps), its per-unit raw-count and `maxDiagnosticsPerOutcome` assertions, the caps, §6.4 item 3, the engine's behaviour and every test are unchanged. |
| A13 | §13.6 (`SCHEMA_MAX_NODES` formula, the closed-shape diagnostic bound, and the producer/reader invariant) | **Owner-authorized bounded erratum (2026-09-24)** — owner ruling D-8b/D-8c ("perform bounded evidence checks now … if the current factor is unsound and the evidence yields a single conservative fixed replacement without schema/interface changes, apply the same class of bounded erratum used for D-8"; evidence: `docs/reviews/2026-09-24-federated-clash-test-core-p7-d8-node-factor-evidence.md` §11). The §13.6 bound of one retained `NormalizedDiagnostic` was a flat 12 nodes, but under the capture meter a legal diagnostic costs 12 (producer minimum; 7 by the schema) to 24 + *u* nodes, *u* = its `unitIds` count ≤ the run's `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic` (§12.2): the schema and `validateResultSet` accept `fragment` and `fragments` together, `role` on all three operands and `ambiguousModelId` (24 + *u*; 32 at the default *u* = 8, 88 at the ceiling 64), and today's producer reaches 16 + *u* (a pair-scope diagnostic merged across *u* units). The formula therefore under-counted, and option validation could accept diagnostic-heavy options whose legal sets the same engine refused as `ResultSetTooLargeError` (reproduced end to end), violating R21-2. **Change:** the diagnostic term becomes `(24 + maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic) × maxRetainedDiagnostics` in the producer/reader invariant, and `(24 + 64) × maxRetainedDiagnostics_ceiling` in `SCHEMA_MAX_NODES` — the unitIds count is bounded by a run option exactly as the results count is (the A11 principle: counts by the run's options, per-record shapes by the schema/validator maximum). `SCHEMA_MAX_NODES` becomes 282,354,376 (was 274,754,376); the provisional-default worst case becomes 15,885,536 (was 15,685,536), still ≤ the 16,000,000 `maxCapturedNodes` default, so **no §17.1 default changes**; the `maxResultsPerRun` ceiling 956,824 (derived with no retained diagnostics) are unchanged and still reachable; the two diagnostic ceilings (`maxRetainedDiagnostics`, `maxUnitIdsPerDiagnostic`) are unchanged. (The ledger-side ceilings `maxLedgerRecords`/`maxPlanReferences` were not reachable at their ceilings before this erratum either; that is gate D-8b, not changed here.) A flat 88 was rejected: it is looser for every run below the ceiling and would put the defaults at 16,445,536 > 16,000,000. The **ledger** factor (12) is **not** changed: the ledger term is unsound in *form* (unit records persist expanded root ids while the record budget charges one record per range; no fixed or options-only replacement keeps the defaults within 16,000,000) and remains an open owner decision (**D-8b**). No schema shape, error code, capture rule, option, default or reader limit changed. |
| A14 | §6.5.1 (the `maxPlanReferences` and `maxLedgerRecords` record-class lists, and the split row of the runtime-class table) | **Owner-authorized bounded erratum (2026-09-24)** — owner ruling #5 on gate **D-8b** ("Proposed form" 1 of the evidence record; evidence: `docs/reviews/2026-09-24-federated-clash-test-core-p7-d8-node-factor-evidence.md` §11.2, §12). **D-8b: unit-range charged per expanded id.** The budget counted a unit's range records one per `{table, start, end}` range, but the sealed `UnitRecord` stores the **expanded** root ids (`sourceRootDbIds`, `targets[].rootDbIds`), and target ids repeat in every unit that targets them; persisted ledger nodes per charged record were therefore unbounded, so `12 × maxLedgerRecords` did not cover the ledger and option validation accepted ledger-heavy options — including the provisional defaults — whose sealed sets the same engine then refused as `ResultSetTooLargeError` (reproduced end to end), violating R21-2. **Change:** a unit's range records are counted one per expanded root id its persisted record carries (source ids and explicit target ids, a split child's copied target ids included) plus one per whole-model target sentinel, for initial units, `target-too-large` gaps and split children alike. A unit record then costs at most 12 nodes per charged record (17 + 8 secondary causes + *s* + Σ(3 per sentinel, 4 + *n* per explicit entry) ≤ 12 × (1 + *s* + sentinels + Σ*n*), since every unit has *s* ≥ 1 and at least one target entry; its reserved overflow slot stays free for its `taint-overflow` entry), so exhaustion (`plan-too-large`, or a refused split) arrives **before** the persisted ledger can outgrow the formula's ledger term. **Nothing else changed:** no schema shape, persisted field, record class, option, default, ceiling, cross-field rule, error code or `SCHEMA_NODE_FACTORS` value, and no persisted field or serialization changed (a run whose runtime admissions — taints, splits — are unaffected by its larger `planRecords` seals a byte-identical ledger; a run nearer the budget may collapse taints or refuse a split earlier, which is the conservative fallback of the table above). Plans with many ids per range are refused earlier (at the defaults, about 500,000 persisted unit-record ids per run). |
| A15 | §6.5.1 (one sentence after the `maxPlanReferences` guard) | **Owner-authorized bounded erratum (2026-09-24)** — owner ruling #5 on gate **D-10** ("producer-side `plan-too-large` guard"; evidence: `docs/reviews/2026-09-24-federated-clash-test-core-p7-d10-root-cap-evidence.md` §6). **D-10: per-model root cap.** The schema caps each model's `provenance.models[].scopeDigests.roots` and `ledger.rootOwners[].owners` at the `maxEntriesPerSelection` hard ceiling (10,000), and every union root of a model is one row of each, but no planning rule bounded a model's union roots: `maxPlanReferences` (≈3 records per root) first refuses at ≈166,000 roots at the defaults, and the per-selection `maxExplicitElementsPerSelection` does not bound a per-model union. A whole-model scope over a model with more than 10,000 roots therefore sealed `completed` and persisted a set the same engine's reader rejected as corrupt (`array-too-long`), violating R21-2. **Change:** a model with more than that many union roots fails planning `plan-too-large` (reported budget class `root-table`, an existing planning record class) before any of its root rows is built or charged; the run seals `failed` with that planning gap. **Nothing else changed:** no schema shape or ceiling, persisted field, record class, `overflowClass` value, option, default, cross-field rule or error code; a model with at most 10,000 union roots plans exactly as before. Scopes over larger models must be narrowed to at most 10,000 roots per model. |

**Deliberately not done here** (implementation plan or a focused follow-up, per the convergence review §2): the closed `ClashTestReference` schema and the deterministic overload-discrimination rule for partial/mixed-shape objects (R21-I3); the annotation source-map pre-allocation bound (R21-C3); and every other item already listed as plan work.
